### Step 2.8 — Instagram, Messenger, web widget
- [x] Add each plugin (`instagram`, `messenger`, `web_widget`) to `OFFICIAL_CHANNEL_PLUGINS`, each running its contract suite with `{ official: true }` (Instagram and Messenger in PR 1c, the web widget in PR 2a)
- [x] `MetaChannelPlugin` base class; Instagram and Messenger plugins with capability declarations (PR 1c: `libs/channels/meta` base, `libs/channels/instagram`, `libs/channels/messenger`, both in `OFFICIAL_CHANNEL_PLUGINS`; text, media by url, `is_echo`, reactions both ways, ad `referral`, 24 h window with 7 days after an ad)
- [x] **Web widget, server side (owner, 2026-10-29; PR 2a):** `libs/channels/web-widget` (`WebWidgetPlugin`, declares `TEXT` only and has no messaging window; `connect({ siteOrigins })` makes a site key `pk_widget_…` that is the `connectionId`, keeps the normalized origins in `metadata.allowedOrigins` and no credentials). The visitor token is an HMAC-SHA256 over `{siteKey, visitorId, exp}` (1 hour) whose key is derived from `JWT_SECRET` with a purpose label, so no new variable exists. The token is to the widget what the signature is to a provider: `validateWebhookSignature` reads it from the header `x-widget-token`, and `handleWebhook` reads `{visitorToken, clientMessageId, content}` and emits `message_received` with `externalId` `<visitorId>:<clientMessageId>`, so that sending twice stores once and two visitors cannot collide. Api module `widget`: `POST /widget/bootstrap` (token, store name, `away` from the business hours, the last 50 messages of that visitor: text that reached them, not notes, system lines, queued or failed replies, nor deleted messages), `POST /widget/messages` (queued through the new `InboundEventQueue`, shared with the provider webhook, so the worker stores, routes and announces it like any other), per-request CORS against `allowedOrigins` (the preflight, which carries no key, is answered for the origin that asks and grants nothing; the request that follows checks the list and only then lets the browser read the answer), limits of 30 and 60 a minute per address, and the `/widget` socket namespace (token in `auth`, a listed origin, 5 sockets per visitor, closed when the token expires) with `WidgetDeliveryListener`, which pushes a reply the channel took (`MESSAGE_SENT`) to the room of its visitor as `widget.message` (id, `from` visitor or team, text, date; nothing about agents). Cases in `tests/api/widget.spec.ts`, and "a widget site key" in `tenant-isolation.spec.ts` (a key from the site of another store, and a visitor id that exists in two stores). **Left for later:** files from the visitor and `POST /widget/upload-url` (Step 3.4), typing and read receipts (the typing task of Step 3.2: `TYPING_INDICATOR`, `CUSTOMER_TYPING` and `READ_RECEIPTS` are declared only then), the pre-chat form and linking by email (Step 3.3), and the bundle (PR 2b). **Known limits:** the site-key lookup and the history read run with no store context, so Step 7.1 turns them into the `SECURITY DEFINER` function it already lists; the connection is read from the database on every call (cache it if it shows in the P99); a visitor past 5 tabs is refused, not rotated, and the count is looked at in the handshake, so a burst of connects at once can pass it by a few; a visitor token cannot be revoked, so after a disconnect only the replies stop at once (the listener asks for an active connection), while a removed origin (also by `PUT .../widget/origins`, which refuses new calls at once but does not close the sockets already open) or a copied token lives until the token expires (1 hour); the listener reads one message for every `MESSAGE_SENT` of any channel, which is cheap (one row by id) and can be narrowed with a marker in the bridged payload if outbound volume grows; the generic webhook answers 404 for `web_widget` by id, so a second channel with a door of its own would need a flag on the plugin instead of another `!==`; the token key is derived from `JWT_SECRET`, so rotating it ends every open widget session and the `.env.example` placeholder is accepted outside staging and production exactly as it is for the access tokens
- [x] `apps/widget` bundle (PR 2b): vanilla TS built by Vite under 40 KB gzipped, launcher, panel with the store name, message list, composer, the "we're away" banner from `away`, i18n from `Store.defaultLocale`, no inline script or style (it must work under a strict CSP of the merchant), the `visitorId` in `localStorage`, bootstrap at load, a socket on `/widget` that asks for a new token and reconnects when the old one expires, the embed snippet in Settings → Channels, and a way to change the origins of a connection without changing its site key (`connect` makes a new key each time, so today adding a domain would break the snippets already published; an update of `metadata.allowedOrigins` for the connection of a store is needed, with the error of the bad entry named by its position). **Built in PR 2b (2026-10-29):** Vite 8.3.5 library mode, one IIFE `widget.js` of about 25 KB gzipped (`scripts/check-widget-size.ts` fails the build over `WidgetLimits.BUNDLE_MAX_GZIP_BYTES`; `treeshake.moduleSideEffects: false` keeps the zod schemas of the `@platform/core/*` barrels out of it). The snippet is a script tag with `data-site-key`, `data-api-url` and an optional `data-locale`; the language is the snippet's, then `Store.defaultLocale` (now in the bootstrap as `store.locale`), then `navigator.language`, in the two languages the widget has (es, en), kept in `apps/widget/src/i18n.ts`. Styles are a constructable stylesheet in a shadow root, so no `<style>` or style attribute exists; a browser without constructable stylesheets gets no widget. `GET /channels/connections/:id/widget` returns the site key, the origins and the snippet (`WIDGET_SCRIPT_URL` in the env), and `PUT /channels/connections/:id/widget/origins` replaces the origins and keeps the key (the bad entry is named by its position, audited as `channel.widget_origins_changed`); both are drawn by Step 3.6. Seen working in Chrome under `default-src 'none'; script-src 'self'; connect-src <api>` against a stand-in server with the contract of the api; the api and the widget together in a browser are covered by `tests/api/widget.spec.ts` only. **Known limits:** a visitor who sends from two tabs sees the other tab's message after the next history read, not at once (the socket carries replies of the team only); the unread dot counts nothing and clears on opening
- [x] Origin of a conversation, channel-agnostic (owner's idea, 2026-10-28): an optional `origin` on the message of `ChannelEvent` (`kind` ad, post or other; `sourceId`, `sourceUrl`, `title`, `body`, `media` with its type and either a provider media reference or a url, plus a thumbnail; `clickId`) that each plugin fills from what its provider sends (the `referral` of a Click-to-WhatsApp ad on WhatsApp; `referral.ads_context_data` on Instagram and Messenger, whose page must also subscribe to `messaging_referrals`). It lands in the first PR of this step so that Instagram and Messenger are born with it; WhatsApp is mapped there too, and the webhook schema keeps validating each part alone. The core stores it as `ConversationOrigin` (conversation, message, the fields above), because one conversation can come from several ads: the same `sourceId` in a row is one entry, the first is the first contact and the latest is the one the thread shows. The provider's raw `referral` is kept in a JSON column. A new tenant-scoped table: migration with its rollback, a case in `tenant-isolation.spec.ts`. Open questions that the docs and a real ad must answer first: `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md`, "WhatsApp plugin build details", item 14
- [x] Several attachments in one inbound message (owner, 2026-10-28): `ChannelEvent.message.media` becomes an array `media[]` (SDK, `channelEventSchema`, `insertInboundMessage`, one `fetch-media` job per media, WhatsApp emits an array of one). The core stores one message with N `Media`
- [x] Messages written in the provider's own app (owner, 2026-10-28): a new SDK event `message_echo` (customer's `channelIdentity`, the message with its `media[]`, `externalId`) that the core stores as a sent message of an external author (`AuthorType.EXTERNAL`, no `authorId`). It is deduplicated against our own sends by `channelMessageId` (the provider echoes those too), and it neither opens the messaging window nor raises the unread count nor is routed. A conversation that is resolved, snoozed or archived is reopened, as a reply of an agent does (owner, 2026-10-28), never spam; a customer with no conversation gets one, unassigned and read, announced as created. It retries while one of our own replies to that customer has no provider id yet, and stores it as the owner's on the last attempt. This replaces "an echo is dropped and counted" of `TDD_10 §6.7`. **Built in PR 1b:** the core, the SDK and schema, the worker case, and the WhatsApp mapping of `smb_message_echoes` (Meta's reference, read 2026-10-28: `message_echoes[]`, `from` the business, `to` the customer; `revoke` and `edit` are skipped; a reaction becomes an external reaction). That field only exists for a number in Coexistence, which needs Embedded Signup (Phase 2), so it is proved by unit tests until a real number does (a box in Step 7.3). **Built in PR 1c:** Instagram and Messenger map `is_echo` (an echo whose sender is not the account is skipped). **Still open:** the app must be subscribed to the `smb_message_echoes` field (the rest of this line is a box of Step 7.3, "Subscribe the WhatsApp app to `smb_message_echoes`")
- [x] Reactions (moved here from an orphan decision of `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md`, "WhatsApp plugin build details", item 11, which no step carried): the `message_reactions` table, the SDK event `message_reaction`, `message.reaction` in real time, reactions returned with each message, WhatsApp emitting them instead of dropping them, Instagram (`message_reactions`) and Messenger mapping them, and the `REACTIONS` capability declared by the plugins that map them. **Built in PR 1b:** the table with its rollback (a customer by identity, an agent by user, the owner of the provider's app as one `external` reactor), the event and its schema, `MessageReactionsService`, `message.reaction`, `reactions` in each message of the thread, and WhatsApp emitting them and declaring `REACTIONS`. **Owner, 2026-10-28: an agent reacting is in the MVP too, built in PR 1b-2:** `PUT /messages/:id/reaction` (`AuthGuard` + `TenantGuard` + `MESSAGES_SEND`; body `{ emoji }`, null takes it back), `ChannelPlugin.sendReaction` (required by `REACTIONS`, implemented by WhatsApp), the job `SEND_OUTBOUND_REACTION` on the outbound queue, `MessageReaction.deliveryStatus` / `deliveryError` (queued, sent or failed, with a code of ours; shown in each reaction of the thread so that nobody believes the customer saw what the channel refused), and the `messageReaction` case in `tenant-isolation.spec.ts`, which removes the exemption. The core accepts any message that has a `channelMessageId` and a channel that declares `REACTIONS`; the plugin refuses what its provider does not allow. **Known limits:** a reaction taken back that the channel refuses is logged, not shown (its row is gone); a reaction left `queued` because Redis was down is queued again by the same sweep as the replies (`OutboundRequeueScheduler`, with the version it had) and marked failed after an hour, so it never stays "sending". two jobs of the same reaction that run at the same time (an emoji and the next one, a few milliseconds apart) are not ordered, so the provider can end with the older emoji while the thread shows the newer one as sent; the version guard only stops the older job from settling. **Built in PR 1c:** Instagram and Messenger map the customer's reactions (`reaction{action, emoji, mid}`; `unreact` takes it back) and send an agent's (`sender_action` `react` / `unreact`), both declaring `REACTIONS`. The reaction of the account itself is not read: it comes back as the echo of our own send. **Accepted for the MVP (owner Step 7.3 if one is ever hit):** the "Known limits" above are commitments, not open work.
- [x] **Delete and edit a message (owner, 2026-10-28; PR 1d, after 1c):** the core stays channel-agnostic and the plugin decides what its API allows. (1) **Delete is for everyone only:** there is no "delete for me", because in a team inbox it would show the agent a thread the customer does not have (a junk or abusive inbound message is handled with the `spam` or `archived` status of the conversation, a wrong reply with a correction message). Capability `DELETE_MESSAGE`, optional `ChannelPlugin.deleteMessage`, the same queued, sent or failed state as a reaction, shown in the thread; a channel without the capability answers 409 and the inbox hides the action. The permission `MESSAGES_DELETE` exists and has no handler on purpose until now. (2) **Edit reaches the customer or does not happen:** capability `EDIT_MESSAGE`, optional `editMessage`; without it the core answers 409, never an edit that only the inbox shows, and the inbox offers "send a correction" (a new message). Revisions are kept (`MessageRevision`, tenant-scoped, with its isolation case and rollback). (3) **Internal notes** are working data shared by the team, so anyone who can see notes (`CONVERSATIONS_VIEW` and `MESSAGES_INTERNAL_NOTE`) can edit one; the author stays the author, every edit keeps a revision (who, when, the text before) and the note shows "edited by"; an edit carries the version it saw and a stale one answers 409 `RESOURCE_CONFLICT` with the current text, so nobody overwrites a change they did not see. Deleting a note is for its author or `MESSAGES_DELETE`. Edits and deletes are pushed in real time (`message.edited`, `message.deleted`, as `message.reaction` is), so a teammate sees the change without reloading. (4) **Inbound:** SDK events `message_deleted` and `message_edited` for the customer and for the provider's own app (the echo of WhatsApp that carries `revoke` and `edit` is skipped today), applied by the core whatever the author. (5) **A message the customer deleted** shows "the customer deleted this message" to the team; the original text is kept for a short time (store preference, 30 days by default) for disputes, shown only with an explicit permission and every view audited, then a job purges it. (6) **Permissions (owner, 2026-10-28):** no `MESSAGES_EDIT`. A user with `MESSAGES_SEND` edits and deletes their own message to the customer (when the channel can); `MESSAGES_DELETE` (only `admin` has it) deletes the message of another team member; nobody edits the message of another person, a correction is a new message; nobody deletes a message of the customer, it becomes the tombstone of (5). Every delete is audited with who and when **Built in PR 1d (2026-10-29):** the capabilities `DELETE_MESSAGE` and `EDIT_MESSAGE` and the optional hooks `deleteMessage` / `editMessage` (the registry requires the hook of a declared capability); no official plugin declares them, because Meta offers no API to delete or edit a sent message (WhatsApp Cloud API, Messenger and Instagram: nothing in Meta's list of webhook events or send endpoints, read 2026-10-29), so the outbound path is proved with the contract plugin of the suite and a plugin that can (a remote one) needs no change in the core. `PATCH /messages/:id` and `DELETE /messages/:id`, `GET /messages/:id/deleted-content`; `MessageRevision` (migration with its rollback, which also gives the new permission to the Admin roles that exist); `Message.version`, `editedAt/By`, `pendingContent` + `editStatus`, `deletedAt/By`, `deleteStatus`, `changeRequestedAt`, `contentPurgedAt`; jobs `SEND_OUTBOUND_EDIT` and `SEND_OUTBOUND_DELETE` on the outbound queue (a sweep queues again after 2 minutes and fails after an hour, as for reactions); `message.edited` and `message.deleted` in real time (a note only to who may read notes); permission `MESSAGES_VIEW_DELETED` (Admin only) for the kept text of a deleted message, every read audited (`message.deleted_content_viewed`) and every delete audited (`message.deleted`); the purge is an hourly cron (`PURGE_DELETED_MESSAGES`) with `DeletedMessageRetention.DAYS` = 30, which also clears the text of the revisions; inbound SDK events `message_edited` and `message_deleted` applied by `InboundMessageChangesService` whatever the author (the lookup of the target message is shared with reactions in `InboundMessageTargets`); Messenger maps `message_edits` and the `is_deleted` flag of `messages` (both directions: the customer's, and the owner's own echo). **Decisions made while building:** (a) an edit of a reply the customer has changes nothing in the thread until the channel takes it (`pendingContent`, `pendingEdit` in the view), and one the channel refuses leaves the text as it was; (b) a deletion the channel has not taken leaves the message shown with `deleteStatus` queued; (c) the retention is a constant, not a store preference (Phase 2, as the profile staleness); (d) `deletedContent` answers 404 for a message that was not deleted or whose text is purged, and for a note to who may not read notes; (e) an agent's deletion of their own reply needs `MESSAGES_SEND`, the deletion of another person's `MESSAGES_DELETE`. **Bulk deletion measured (2026-10-29):** a customer who deletes many messages at once is one `message_deleted` event and one job for each message; 100 messages of one customer with 20 jobs at a time take about 0.5 s in `tests/api/inbound-message-changes.spec.ts` and leave 100 tombstones and 100 `message.deleted` events, so no batching was built; the cap of 100 events per request only splits the queueing in batches, it drops nothing. The client of Step 3.1 must debounce its invalidation of the thread. **Not verified with a real payload:** the `message_edits` shape of Messenger (`message_edit{mid, text, num_edit}`, taken from Meta's reference) and the `is_deleted` of `messages` (a box in Step 7.3); Instagram's `message_edit` event (changelog of 2026-09) carries no text as far as known, so the plugin skips it, and Instagram's unsend arrives through `is_deleted`. **Follow-ups, none blocking:** (1) the search over message text (`search_vector`) is generated from `content`, so a deleted message stays searchable until it is purged: the search of Step 5.1 must filter `deleted_at IS NULL`; (2) the media of a deleted message stays until Step 3.4 decides what the purge does with files; (3) the thread of Step 3.2 draws the tombstone, "edited", the pending state and the failed one, and the `version` it sends back when editing a note; (4) `editedById` and `deletedById` have no foreign key to `users`, like an audit trail: the user may be removed and the name goes with the member list; (5) editing a note keeps its `mentions` as they were (no one is notified again, and a removed mention stays): decide with the thread of Step 3.2 whether an edit recomputes them; (6) one request at a time for a message: asking to delete a reply whose edit waits answers 409 (and the other way round), so each settles on its own; (7) the purge takes the oldest due rows first across all stores, which is fair enough for one owner and becomes a per-store batch in Phase 2
- [x] **Discard a reply that failed for good (owner, 2026-11-07; PR feat/discard-failed-message):** a reply of the team whose send failed and that the channel is KNOWN not to have taken can be thrown away from the thread. (1) `POST /messages/:id/discard` (204, twice is once), with the permission to send it again (`MESSAGES_SEND`, or `MESSAGES_SEND_TEMPLATE` for a template). (2) Only a message of the team (not a note or a customer's) with `deliveryStatus` failed, no `channelMessageId` and `failureDefinitive = true`. The worker sets `failureDefinitive` when it settles a failure: true for a refusal of the provider (even one retried to the end, a throttle) and for a check that failed before anything was sent; false for a timeout or a network error, a plugin that says `outcomeUnknown`, an answer that made no sense, a failure reported by a receipt, and any failure where an earlier attempt had an unknown outcome; null for a failure from before this existed (such a reply cannot be thrown away, only sent again). A reply that may have gone answers 409 `MESSAGE_DISCARD_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`. (3) Nothing is said to the channel and no note is added to the thread. The row gets `discardedAt` and, like any deleted one, `deletedAt`, `deletedByType` agent and `deletedById`; every read of the thread leaves it out (the thread, the quote of a reply, the target of a reply, the preview of the conversation, the export; the widget already left out the deleted). (4) The text is kept with the retention of the deleted ones (`DeletedMessageRetention`, 30 days, read with `MESSAGES_VIEW_DELETED`): it is the agent's own words, nothing of a customer, and it lets a supervisor recover what was written if a reply was thrown away by mistake. (5) The files of the reply are removed (their rows); the hourly sweep of stray files takes the objects from the bucket within a day. (6) The audit has `message.discarded` (who, when, the kind of message and the code of the failure, never the text) once per discard. (7) Realtime: the event `message.deleted` of the deletion that already exists; the clients read the thread again and the reply is not in it. (8) Resending a failed reply keeps the doubt: a failure that was definitive starts the new attempt clean, one that may have gone (or from before this existed) leaves the message marked, so a later clean refusal does not make it discardable. (9) A failure is definitive only if the plugin AFFIRMS it, never by default: `PluginError.refused` (the provider answered that it did not take the request: set by the Meta plugins for a throttle, a rejected token, a closed window, a template that is not approved and any other answer of the provider that is not an outage) or a code of a local check made before anything was sent (a channel that takes no files, a file too big or of a type it does not take, a connection that is not there, a file that is gone). A plugin that fails without saying so, `CHANNEL_MESSAGE_FAILED` included, leaves a reply that can be sent again and not thrown away. (10) The worker reads the mark when an attempt begins and writes it (false while queued) BEFORE the provider is asked, so a process that dies mid-send or a job that stalls leaves the doubt; a clean refusal with nothing before it that may have gone takes the mark off, and the failure that ends the send is definitive only then.
- [x] 7-day window after an ad (Instagram and Messenger; Meta, changelog of 2026-09-28: up to 7 days from the first message after a Click-to-Messenger or Click-to-Direct ad, 24 h from any other entry point): `CustomerIdentity.adWindowOpenedAt` (migration with rollback) set by `InboundMessageService` when that first message carries an ad `origin`; `MessagingWindowPolicy` gains `extendedHoursAfterAd` (168); `stateOf` takes the later of `lastInboundAt + hours` and `adWindowOpenedAt + extendedHoursAfterAd`. Meta says "up to", so a send refused for a closed window must map to `CHANNEL_WINDOW_EXPIRED`; the exact error code and subcode need a real ad (Step 7.3)
- [x] Contract tests for all three (`tests/contracts`: `instagram-`, `messenger-` and `web-widget-plugin.contract.spec.ts`)
- [x] **PR split (owner, 2026-10-28; 1c-2 added 2026-10-29; 2 split into 2a and 2b 2026-10-29), eight PRs, one per session:** 1a (merged, #65) = `media[]`, `origin` (`ConversationOrigin`), `adWindowOpenedAt` and WhatsApp adapted to them; 1b (merged, #66) = `message_echo` and `message_reaction` with WhatsApp mapping both; 1b-2 (merged, #67) = an agent reacts (endpoint, plugin send, isolation case); 1c (merged, #69) = `MetaChannelPlugin`, Instagram and Messenger (text, media, `is_echo`, reactions both ways, ad `referral`, capabilities in the connection views, contract tests; Instagram through Instagram Login on `graph.instagram.com`, checklist item 12 (moved to the unified login on `graph.facebook.com` on 2026-11-05, same item); typing and read receipts wait for the typing task of the interface milestone); 1c-2 (merged, #70) = name and photo of an Instagram or Messenger customer (below); 1d (merged, #71) = delete and edit a message (above); 2a (merged, #72) = web widget, server side (above); 2b (merged, #73) = web widget bundle
- [x] Channel capabilities to the browser (owner, 2026-10-28; built in PR 1c, `capabilities` in `ChannelConnectionView` from `PluginRegistry.capabilitiesOf`): the connection views (`GET /channels/connections` and `/:id`) return the `capabilities` that the plugin of each connection declares (`ChannelCapability` constants), so the web app decides which actions to draw (react, edit, delete, templates, typing) without knowing any channel by name; a remote plugin declares its own through its manifest. They are static per plugin: if a capability one day depends on the connection (a permission granted), the view widens then
- [ ] **For a future third-party plugin that answers customers who come from an ad (owner, 2026-10-28; nothing of it is built now):** (1) the `origin` of the message travels in the `message.received` webhook to plugins (`TDD_10 §6.5`, not built yet: only the `TRIGGER_PLUGIN_WEBHOOK` queue name exists) together with `messageId` and `conversationId`; it is per message, not per conversation, because one conversation can receive several ads. (2) Scope: the origin goes in `messages:read` (owner decision), with `kind`, `sourceId`, `title`, `body` and `media`, which identify the ad; `clickId` and `sourceUrl` stay out of the payload until a plugin needs them, with a scope of their own. The step that builds `§6.5` carries this. (3) Note: `Events.MESSAGE_SENT` carries only `storeId`, `conversationId`, `messageId` and `visibility` (`MessageStatusPayload`), not `authorType` or `createdAt`, so a plugin cannot tell that a human took over after a given message. Enrich that payload in the step that builds `§6.5`; do not build it now
- [x] **Name and photo of an Instagram or Messenger customer (owner, 2026-10-29; built in PR 1c-2):** the webhooks carry only the scoped id, so the customer shows as a number. Optional `ChannelPlugin.fetchProfile(ctx, channelIdentity)` returning `{ name?, username?, avatarUrl? }`, required by a new capability `CUSTOMER_PROFILE`. Instagram: `GET graph.instagram.com/<IGSID>?fields=name,username,profile_pic` (name may be null). Messenger: `GET /<PSID>?fields=first_name,last_name,profile_pic`, which needs the "Business Asset User Profile Access" feature requested in the Page settings (an owner step, documented in the connect view). A worker job `FETCH_CUSTOMER_PROFILE` on a queue of its own, `customer-profiles` (5 at a time, 3 attempts, exponential 60 s; a provider that answers slowly fills it and delays no customer message; the heavy queue has no consumer until Step 3.4 and one added now would swallow the `fetch-inbound-media` jobs that wait there, and the inbound queue is where messages wait), one per identity (job id), queued when the identity is created and again when `profileFetchedAt` is older than 7 days (a constant in `libs/core`), always after the message is stored, never inside the webhook and never blocking it. **Stored in new columns of `CustomerIdentity`** (migration with its rollback; the table is already covered by the isolation spec): `username`, `avatarUrl`, `profileFetchedAt`, `profileRetryAfter`, `profileFetchError` (a code of ours). No index: staleness is decided from the identity row an inbound message already reads, and no sweep exists. `displayName` takes the name; a read that finds something replaces name, username and photo link together (the stored link expires), and one that finds nothing keeps what was stored; `Customer.name` is set only while it is null, because the customer's own name is the agents'. A failure retries three times with backoff; when they run out, `profileFetchError` is written, and `profileFetchedAt` too only if the failure was a refusal, so that a refused read is not asked again until the profile is stale; an outage is no answer, so it sets `profileRetryAfter` (one hour ahead) and leaves the date of the last real read alone, and the connection view can tell the owner what is missing. **The photo is a reference only:** no storage exists until Step 3.4 and a plugin never stores anything (the core stores what `fetchMedia` and `fetchProfile` hand over); Meta's urls are signed and expire, so Step 3.4 carries the avatar download through the SSRF-safe fetch. **Built:** `fetchProfile` returns null when Meta has no profile to show (an empty object, or error 2018218 of an account made with a phone number), which is stamped like a read and never retried; `ProfileRequester` (after a stored customer message) and `ProfileFetcher` (the job) in `apps/worker/src/inbound`, `CustomerProfileService` in `libs/core/messaging`, and the `CUSTOMER_PROFILE` capability mapped to Step 3.2 in `scripts/frontend-coverage-map.ts`. Display fallback, drawn in Step 3.2: name, then `@username`, then "Instagram customer ····1234", with an initials avatar
- [x] Follow-ups of the profile fetch found in the review of PR 1c-2, none blocking: (1) the requester repeats the connection and identity lookups that `handleMessageReceived` already did, two indexed queries per Instagram or Messenger message; return the identity from the service and check `needsProfileRead` on it first **(done, checked 2026-11-07: `ProfileRequester` checks `needsProfileRead` on its own identity read before queueing)**; (2) the plugin call is not aborted when the 10 s timeout fires, because `ChannelContext` carries no `AbortSignal`: add one to the SDK and to `MetaGraphClient` **(done in PR `fix/profile-read-abort`: `ChannelPlugin.fetchProfile` takes an optional `signal`, `ProfileFetcher` aborts the signal it hands over when `withTimeout` gives up or the read fails, `MetaGraphClient` joins it to its own limit, and the Meta plugin hands it over; covered by tests of the client, the plugin and `tests/api/customer-profile.spec.ts`; other `ChannelPlugin` methods still take no signal: the SDK has no use for one until a call is slow enough to need it)**; (3) Step 3.4, when it stores the avatar, must fetch it through the SSRF-safe fetch with an https-only, public-host check, and Step 3.2 must draw the stored link with the CSP `img-src` of the app (a link the plugin chose is untrusted) **(checked 2026-11-07, nothing to build: the server never fetches the link, it stores it after `isPlainHttpsUrl`; the web draws it with `RemoteImage` (`referrerPolicy="no-referrer"`, initials when it fails) under `img-src` limited to `*.fbcdn.net`, `*.cdninstagram.com` and `platform-lookaside.fbsbx.com` (`CUSTOMER_AVATAR_IMAGE_HOSTS`, `apps/web/lib/security-headers.ts`); the widget draws no customer photo. If a step ever downloads the photo, it uses the SSRF-safe fetch)**; (4) a username the channel stops giving stays until a read gives another (the photo link is the only field replaced as a whole) **(accepted for the MVP; the box in Step 7.3 "Left by Step 2.7 and 2.8" keeps it)**
- [ ] Phase 2, SaaS follow-ups of the profile fetch, recorded so they are not lost: (1) the 7-day staleness becomes a store preference (`libs/core/platform/config/store-preferences.ts`, default 7) once stores are not all the owner's; (2) a budget for profile calls per Meta app across tenants (Graph rate limits count per app), with the job spreading its calls instead of bursting when many tenants import a backlog; (3) the Messenger "Business Asset User Profile Access" step disappears for stores that connect through Facebook Login for Business, which grants it in the flow; (4) an erased customer (`Customer.erasedAt`) clears `username` and `avatarUrl` too, and the job skips it
- [ ] Phase 2, recorded so it is not lost: WhatsApp Embedded Signup (it needs the platform to be a Tech Provider, business verification and App Review) and Facebook Login for Business for accounts of other people. **Facebook Login (classic) for Messenger and Instagram, for the owner, moved to Step 7.8 (owner, 2026-11-05).** Messenger buttons and templates (`sendButtons`, `BUTTONS`; `TDD_10 §5.2` lists them, but no screen of the MVP sends them, so 1c does not declare them). The `HUMAN_AGENT` message tag (a human answers up to 7 days after the last customer message; never for bots or promotion; needs the "Human Agent" permission from App Review)

**TDD:** `TDD_10 §5.2`, `§5.4`, `§5.5`
**Done when:** all four channels deliver into the same unified inbox, and the widget works
on a page with a strict CSP.

## Milestone 3 — Inbox interface

### Step 3.1 — Frontend foundation
- [x] **Stabilize `tests/api/realtime.spec.ts` first, in a fix PR of its own (found in PR 1c-2, 2026-10-29):** in the whole integration run it fails one or two tests, a different pair each run, and it does so on a clean `main` too (four runs at `d186cff`); alone it sometimes passes 69 of 69. The ones seen: `hears an event of the bridge whose ids come in capitals`, `hears an event of the worker, which holds no sockets, through the bridge`, `does not take a user out of a room on the word of an event that the conversation does not bear out`. All three publish to `EVENTS_BRIDGE_CHANNEL` and wait for a socket to hear it. **Hypothesis, not proved:** (1) `EventsBridge.onModuleInit` subscribes inside the `ready` handler and nothing waits for it, so `createTestApp` can return before the api listens; (2) an event is passed on by the one instance that claims it in Redis, so an instance left open by an earlier test of "across instances" can claim it and the socket of the test never hears it. **How to close it:** reproduce in a loop (the spec alone, 20 runs) with a log of which instance claims each event; if (1), make the bridge expose a promise that settles when the subscription is acknowledged (it also fixes the order of a real boot) and have the suite await it; if (2), close every extra instance in a `finally`; if it is neither, say what it was. Do not widen waits or add retries to hide it. **Done when:** `realtime.spec.ts` passes 20 runs in a row alone and the whole integration suite passes three runs in a row. Step 3.1 relies on realtime, so it starts after this. **Closed:** it was both. (1) The bridge subscribed in the `ready` handler and nothing waited for it: `EventsBridge.subscribed` settles when Redis acknowledges, and `createTestApp` awaits it. (2) An api left running on the laptop (`dist/apps/api/main.js`) listened to the same channel and claimed events: `assertNoOtherBridgeListener` stops the suite and names the cause. Proved with 20 runs of the spec alone and three whole integration runs (1057 tests each)
- [x] Next.js App Router structure with `/s/[storeId]`, `/org`, `/account`, `/invitations/[token]`, `proxy.ts`. A user with no membership is redirected to the "no access" screen, not `/onboarding` (DEFERRED). The top bar shows an entry only for a page that exists at this point of the build (Inbox in Step 3.2, Customers in 3.3; Orders and Analytics join with Steps 4.4 and 5.2), and the store switcher shows the name and the role of each store, with no counts of open conversations (they need one call per store; Phase 2, `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §10 item 39) **PR B (2026-10-29):** `proxy.ts` (CSP with a nonce only), the root layout, `/no-access`, `not-found` and `error` are built; `/s/[storeId]`, `/org`, `/account`, `/invitations/[token]`, the `/` redirect and the redirect to "no access" arrive with the auth flow (PR C), because their layouts validate the membership. **PR C is split (owner, 2026-10-29):** C1 is the auth flow, the client and CORS; C2 is `/s/[storeId]`, `/org`, `/account`, `/invitations/[token]`, the top bar and the store switcher. **Closed (PR C2):** the membership is validated in the browser, not on the server: the access token lives in the memory of the page, so the layouts of `/s/[storeId]`, `/org` and `/account` are client shells (`SessionGate` reads `auth.me`); a store the person does not belong to, or `/org` without `ORG_MANAGE_STORES` in any store, goes to `/no-access`, and the api refuses the requests anyway. `StoreProvider`/`useStore()` give the store, the role and `can(permission)`. The top bar has the brand, the store switcher (name and role of each store, no counts) and the account menu (profile, sign out); it has no entry for a page that does not exist yet, so the links of Inbox, Customers, Orders and Analytics join with their steps. `/org/stores` lists the stores of the organization and `/account/profile` shows the profile read-only (the api has no route to change it). `/invitations/[token]` handles the three cases through `/auth/accept`, which keeps the refresh token of a new account in the cookie: no account (the name creates it), an account signed in as the invited address, and a session for another address (told to sign out); an account with no session is sent to the login by the api's `login_required` refusal. **The last store is remembered by the store shell, but `/s/<id>/inbox` is a 404 until Step 3.2.** A file under `app/` that is three folders deep cannot import with `../../../` (lint), so the client component of such a route lives one level up (`app/org/stores-list.tsx`, `app/account/profile-card.tsx`, `app/invitations/invitation-card.tsx`, `app/login/verify-magic-link.tsx`, `app/s/store-shell.tsx`) and the page only renders it
- [x] **The memberships and permissions of the user (found by the TDD_09 pass of the alignment audit, owner, 2026-10-29; server side):** `GET /auth/me` returns the profile only, and `organizations.stores` returns stores with no role, so the browser cannot know what the user may do. Widen `auth.me` with `memberships: { storeId, storeName, role, permissions }[]` (one source for the store switcher, the "no access" screen, the last-store redirect, the navigation that hides what the role cannot open and every action that appears only with its permission). The permissions of a request are still decided by the server; this only lets the interface hide what would be refused. Cover it in `tests/api` (a user sees only their own memberships) and in the isolation suite **Closed:** `auth.me` returns `memberships: { storeId, storeName, role, permissions }[]`, active ones only, by store name; covered in `auth.controller.integration.spec.ts` and in the isolation suite (`the memberships of the account`)

- [x] Auth flow with token refresh; `apiClient` setting `X-Store-Id` **Closed (PR C1):** the refresh token lives in an httpOnly cookie that the Next server sets (`app/auth/{consume,refresh,logout}`, cookie `__Host-platform_refresh` in production, `SameSite=Lax`, `Path=/`, and an `Origin` check on every handler); the access token (15 min) lives in the memory of the page and a reload trades the cookie for a new one. `createBrowserSession` shares one refresh among callers and across tabs (`navigator.locks`), because the api revokes the whole family when a refresh token is presented twice; `createApiClient` asks for a token before the first request, sends `Authorization` and `X-Store-Id`, and retries once after `AUTH_TOKEN_EXPIRED`. The api answers the browser with CORS for `FRONTEND_URL` only (`webCors`, headers `authorization`, `content-type`, `x-store-id`, no credentials). Before drawing ticks, give the queued one an event of its own (`message.queued`) and carry the `clientMessageId` of the request in `MessageQueuedPayload`, so that the optimistic bubble matches **Closed:** `message.queued` is a realtime event of its own with `clientMessageId` (the idempotency key of the request); `message.sent` no longer carries that field
- [x] **Relay the inbound events to the browser (server side; found by the second pass of the frontend alignment audit, owner, 2026-10-28):** the worker publishes `Events.MESSAGE_RECEIVED` and `Events.CONVERSATION_CREATED` and neither is in `BridgedEvents` nor forwarded by `RealtimeEventsListener`, so a message from a customer or a new conversation never reaches an open inbox (the comment of `BridgedEvents` says they join "when cut down to ids", and no step did it). Without this task the "Done when" below ("two browser sessions see each other's messages instantly") cannot pass for a customer's message **Closed:** both events are bridged and forwarded; the payloads carry `visibility` and the bridge schema cuts the text (`contentPreview`, `firstMessageContent`). `conversation.updated` is removed (domain and realtime), recorded in `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §3 (`TDD_02 §1`, `TDD_06 §3` realtime events); covered with real sockets in `tests/api/realtime.spec.ts`
- [x] The realtime client, moved from Step 2.4 (`TDD_06 §3`, `§4`): connection manager with a fresh token on every reconnect and the connection status in the header, `useRealtimeSync` that invalidates `QueryKeys` (debounced), and the offline queue with terminal-error handling and the `idempotencyKey` that `POST /messages` already honours. Join the conversation room of the open thread (`conversation.join`) and send the heartbeat every 30 seconds with `{ active: true }` when the user did something. Read the presence of the team (`PresenceService.getStorePresence`, built in Step 2.4 with no route yet) by adding its route here (**route done, server side:** `GET /presence`, `CONVERSATIONS_VIEW`, covered in `tests/api/realtime.spec.ts`; the client that reads it is still to do); the Team status widget that draws it is a task of Step 3.2. A refused connection arrives as `connect_error` with the code in `error.data.code` (not as `auth.error`, which is only for a socket that was already connected); on `auth.error` or a server disconnect, reconnect with a fresh token **Closed (PR D):** `apps/web/lib/realtime/` holds the connection (`openRealtimeConnection`: the `auth` callback runs on every attempt; socket.io does not reconnect after a middleware refusal or a server hang-up, so the retry is ours, with backoff and a fresh token after `AUTH_TOKEN_EXPIRED` or `auth.error`; `AUTH_TOKEN_INVALID` ends the session only if a fresh token was also refused, `TENANT_ACCESS_DENIED` at once), the status store (`useSyncExternalStore`, no zustand), the heartbeat (on connect and every `SocketLimits.HEARTBEAT_INTERVAL_SECONDS`, `active` from real pointer/key activity), the invalidation table and batcher, `attachRealtimeSync` (the `useRealtimeSync` of the TDD, attached in the same effect that opens the socket so that no first connection is missed), `OfflineQueue` (a message that waited longer than `QUEUE_AUTO_SEND_WINDOW_MS`, 10 minutes, is not sent on its own: it is marked `OfflineQueueErrors.EXPIRED` and the agent confirms it with `resend`, because a reply to a customer cannot be taken back and the conversation may have moved on) with its `createFlushScheduler` (a pass that leaves something waiting is tried again with a growing wait, because the socket may stay up while a send fails), and the hooks `useConversationRoom` and `useTeamPresence`; `RealtimeProvider` opens one socket per store inside `StoreShell`, and `ConnectionIndicator` draws the amber dot and the red banner. `libs/api-client` gains `sendMessage`, `fetchTeamPresence` and `QueryKeys.presence`/`messages.all`. Covered by unit tests (a real socket.io server for the connection) and by `tests/api/realtime-client.spec.ts` against the real api: a message queued while the network is down is sent once on reconnect, two sockets of a store hear each other, the presence is read. **The `e2e-tests` cases of "Done when" that drive this in a browser are in the PR E2b (below)**

### Step 3.2 — Inbox
Tasks 2 to 7 were added by the TDD_09 pass of the frontend alignment audit (owner, 2026-10-29): each names a view or a route that the interface of this step reads and the server does not give yet. Build the server side of each one first, with its test in `tests/api`, then draw it.
- [x] **Delete the test page of Step 3.1 and create the `lighthouse` job (owner, 2026-10-30; the deletion is PR B3a, the `lighthouse` job is PR B3c, owner 2026-10-30: the cases of `realtime.spec.ts` need the offline queue, which only the composer drives). Done in PR B3a:** the cases of `realtime.spec.ts` drive the inbox (offline queue, two sockets) and the test page, its harness, the `E2E_HARNESS` branch and `web:build-e2e` are gone. **Done in PR B3c:** the `lighthouse` job (pull requests that can affect the web app; optional, not a required check) runs `nx run web-e2e:lighthouse`: a Playwright config of its own (`playwright.lighthouse.config.ts`, the servers shared with the e2e one through `playwright.shared.ts`) whose spec opens Playwright's Chromium with a debugging port and gives it to the `lighthouse` module (13.6.0, a root dev dependency), in place of the `lhci autorun` that `TDD_03 §4` used to name, because the inbox is behind the login: `/login` signed out, and `/s/{store}/inbox` after a magic link read from Mailpit, with a new sign-in before each run, because the session was lost in some runs (the likely cause is the rotation of the refresh token with Lighthouse cutting the load short; not confirmed in the api logs), and a new sign-in before each run removed it. Lighthouse's own run wipes the cache, so each run is a cold load; the budgets are asserted on the median of three (`lighthouse/budgets.ts`). The budgets of `TDD_08 §4.4`: FCP, LCP, TTI, CLS as written; **Total Blocking Time < 200 ms stands in for INP**, which needs a person; the initial script transfer is held at a **ceiling per page** (login 260 KB, inbox 340 KB, owner 2026-10-30) because the app weighs 244 KB and 322 KB today against the 200 KB of the TDD: the ceilings come down in Step 7.7. The per-route 100 KB budget is not measured (a lab run cannot tell the shared chunks from the route's own). The steps that install Node, the dependencies, the migrated database and Chromium moved to the composite action `.github/actions/web-stack`, used by `e2e-tests` and `lighthouse`; the services stay in each job, since an action cannot declare them.

Instagram and Messenger are left out of PR 1c on purpose (owner, 2026-10-28) and implement both here: Meta's `sender_action` `typing_on` / `typing_off` / `mark_seen` on `POST /<id>/messages` (Messenger `message_reads`, Instagram `messaging_seen`), declaring `TYPING_INDICATOR` and `READ_RECEIPTS` only then. The web widget (Step 2.8, PR 2a) declares neither `TYPING_INDICATOR`, `CUSTOMER_TYPING` nor `READ_RECEIPTS` for the same reason: it adds them here, with the events on its `/widget` namespace **Server side done (PR 3.2-A3, owner 2026-10-30; the interface that draws and sends them is B4):** Instagram and Messenger declare `TYPING_INDICATOR` and `READ_RECEIPTS` (`sender_action` `typing_on` / `typing_off` / `mark_seen`, the last one for the whole conversation, so the message id is not used). The typing of an agent leaves the agents' gateway to `AgentTypingRelay`, which does the first start of each 5 s slot and a stop that follows a start that went (calls for one conversation run in order, a start first asks the database whether the agent may still send, and only an active connection is told): a channel with the plugin capability gets `SEND_OUTBOUND_TYPING` on the outbound queue (one attempt, not kept and with no job id, carrying the time of the event: `TypingSender` in the worker drops a job older than 10 s or overtaken by a newer one of its conversation, and only logs a failure), and the widget visitor gets `widget.agent_typing` `{ active }` on the socket. **Meta sends no typing of the customer** (Messenger and Instagram have no such webhook; checked with the docs of both), so `CUSTOMER_TYPING` is declared by the widget alone: the visitor's socket sends `widget.typing`, once in 2 s for each visitor (all their tabs) and 600 a minute for each site, and the api emits `customer.typing` (`{ conversationId }`) to the room of the conversation (not for an archived or spam one), api only, nothing stored. **Before running more than one api or worker instance:** the thinning of typing and the two limits of the widget live in the memory of each process, so with several instances each lets a signal through; move them to Redis (`SET NX PX`) then. The MVP runs one of each. **Left for PR B5, on purpose (owner, 2026-10-30):** the widget bundle does not send or draw typing yet, and the widget declares no `READ_RECEIPTS`; both are the next task below. **The widget side is PR B6b; the inbox side is PR B7:** the composer announces a reply being typed (`useAgentTyping`, `TypingAnnouncer`: a start at most once in `TypingTiming.AGENT_START_GAP_MS`, a stop when the agent is idle, empties the box, sends or leaves; never for a note, a slash command or a closed window), and the line under the thread (`typing-line.tsx`, `useTyping`, `TypingTracker`) draws "the customer is typing" from `customer.typing` (it lapses after `TypingTiming.CUSTOMER_LAPSE_MS`, or when the customer's message arrives) and the teammates from `typing.start` / `typing.stop`, except the person on another tab. The read state toward the channel was already sent by `POST /conversations/:id/read`, which `useMarkRead` calls (`ReadReceipts.queueFor`, `widgetRead.notify`)
- [x] **WhatsApp shows "typing" to the customer (owner, 2026-11-04, real test with Meta; PR `feat/whatsapp-typing-indicator`):** `WhatsAppPlugin` declares `TYPING_INDICATOR` and implements `sendTypingIndicator` as Meta documents it (<https://developers.facebook.com/documentation/business-messaging/whatsapp/typing-indicators>): the read receipt of the newest message of the customer plus `typing_indicator: { type: 'text' }`. Meta ends it when the agent answers or after about 25 seconds and has no call to take it away, so a stop (`active: false`) calls nothing. `TypingParams.lastInbound` (provider id and ISO time) is read by the worker (`typing-inbound.ts`, inside the store, the newest live message of the customer); the plugin shows nothing without it, nor once the 24 hour window is closed. Because the indicator also marks that message as read, the worker offers no message when the store switched `sendReadReceipts` off. A refusal of Graph is logged by `providerCode` and `providerSubcode` only. Known limit, accepted: while the indicator is on, the customer sees the message as read even if the agent never opened the conversation (the same effect as the blue ticks the agent would send by opening it); a store that does not want that switches the receipts off and WhatsApp then shows no indicator. Known limits, accepted: `TypingSender.deliver` itself has no unit spec because its module imports the environment (CI has no `DATABASE_URL`); the pieces that decide (`typing-inbound.ts`, the plugin) are tested and the wiring is covered by the real test with Meta; `receivedAt` is the time the message was stored, not the one Meta sent, so a delayed webhook can cost one refused call (logged by provider code).

- [x] The list of conversations when a quiet message arrives (owner, 2026-10-29): the preview of each row is the newest message of the thread by date, so the server already answers it; the client reads `message.queued` and changes the text of that row in place, without moving it (the order of the list is `lastMessageAt`, which a quiet message does not change) **Done (PR B2):** `message.queued` already makes the `conversations` key stale (`invalidation.ts`, covered by `invalidation.spec.ts`), and the refetched row carries the new preview in the same place, because the order is `lastMessageAt`; nothing else was needed

Read receipts: the worker cannot reach a visitor's socket, so the read route of the api (where `ReadReceipts.queueFor` is called) also emits a new `widget.read` to the visitor's room when the conversation belongs to the widget, the widget draws the "seen" mark on the messages of the visitor, and the plugin declares `READ_RECEIPTS` only then (with a `markAsRead` that does nothing, as `sendTypingIndicator` does). Each piece gets its case in `tests/api/widget.spec.ts` **Done in PR B6b (#101):** `Session.typing()` sends `widget.typing` at most once in `WidgetLimits.CUSTOMER_TYPING_GAP_MS` and only on a live socket; `Session.onAgentTyping` drives the "the team is typing" line of the panel (a polite `role=status` line under the list), cleared by a stop, by a team reply, by the socket going down, or after `WidgetLimits.AGENT_TYPING_LAPSE_MS` (25 s, in core with the other widget limits). `WidgetSocketEvents.READ` (`widget.read`, `{ messageId }`) is emitted by the new `WidgetReadNotifier` (api, `WidgetDeliveryModule`) from the read route and from the bulk `mark_read`, next to `ReadReceipts.queueFor`; it names the latest text message of the visitor, honors the store's `sendReadReceipts` switch, and only a conversation of an active widget connection is looked into. The widget marks that message and every earlier one of the visitor as "Seen" (`ChatLog.markSeenUpTo`; the marks survive a re-read of the history). Cases in `tests/api/widget.spec.ts` ("read receipts") and unit cases in `apps/widget`. **Left on purpose:** a visitor who reloads the page loses the "Seen" marks until the next read; the task right below closes it. Also known: `widget.read` names the latest message of the visitor when the notifier runs, not at the instant of the read, so a message that lands in between can be marked seen early; it closes with the task below by reading `lastReadAt`

No line in the thread for a reaction. `message.reaction` carries only ids, so the client invalidates the thread; if a notice such as "Ana reacted" is wanted, widen that event first (`action`, `emoji`, `authorType`, `authorId`) **Drawn read-only in PR B2** (`reaction-chips.tsx`, with who reacted when the chip is opened and the pending or failed state of an agent's reaction); the actions are PR B4 **Done in PR B4:** the picker is the six quick emoji of `QUICK_REACTIONS` plus a dialog for any other single emoji (checked with `isWholeEmoji`, the rule of the api), taking back one's own reaction from the same menu or by choosing it again; offered only when the channel declares `REACTIONS`, the viewer holds `MESSAGES_SEND` and the message has a copy in the channel (`lib/inbox/message-actions.ts`, the same rules as the api, covered in its spec)
- [x] The actions of an agent on a message (owner, 2026-10-28; the server side is built in Step 2.8): react with an emoji picker and take it back (`PUT /messages/:id/reaction`), showing the reaction as waiting or failed with the reason until the channel answers; "send again" on a reply that failed (`message.failed`; the route `POST /messages/:id/resend` is built, PR 3.2-A2: it puts the same message back on its way as `queued`, so the bubble and its `clientMessageId` stay, and a text needs the window open again while a template does not; `Message.resentAt` is when the sweep starts counting its age); and edit and delete (built in PR 1d of Step 2.8). Every action appears only if the channel of the conversation declares its capability (from the connection view) and the user holds the permission. **No official plugin declares `EDIT_MESSAGE` or `DELETE_MESSAGE`** (Meta offers no such API for WhatsApp, Messenger or Instagram, Step 2.8), so these two actions stay hidden for every official channel until a plugin that can is installed; the interface is still built and tested with the contract plugin. For a note the actions need no capability. **Draw what the thread receives from PR 1d:** the tombstone of a message the customer deleted ("the customer deleted this message") with the kept text behind `MESSAGES_VIEW_DELETED` (`GET /messages/:id/deleted-content`, every read audited), "edited" with `pendingEdit` and `editStatus`, `deleteStatus` queued or failed with its reason, and the `version` that an edit of a note sends back (a stale one answers 409 with the current text). Decide here whether an edit of a note recomputes its `mentions` (Step 2.8, follow-up 5) **Drawn in PR B2, read-only:** the tombstone (without the kept text), "edited", `pendingEdit` and `editStatus`, `deleteStatus` queued or failed with its reason, and the failed ticks; the actions, the capability checks and `deleted-content` are PR B4 **Done in PR B4:** a bar beside the bubble (hover, keyboard focus, always on a touch screen) with react and a menu of resend, edit, edit history, view deleted text and delete (`message-actions-bar.tsx`, dialogs in `message-action-dialogs.tsx`); the capability comes from the channel directory (`ChannelCapability` moved to `libs/core/domain` so the browser can ask for it by name, and the SDK re-exports it), delete asks first, the kept text is read when its dialog opens and never cached (every read is audited), and a stale save of a note shows the current text and its version instead of overwriting. **Decision, edit of a note and `mentions`:** `PATCH /messages/:id` takes no mentions, so an edit neither notifies nor changes who the note names; the highlight marks only the names still written in the text. The cases with a channel that declares the capabilities (react, edit and delete of a reply, resend) are for PR B5 with the fixtures of the composer cases below, because the e2e fixtures have no such channel; the note case runs in `thread.spec.ts`
- [x] Internal notes in the thread (owner, 2026-10-28; the data is built in Step 2.3, and the edit with its revisions in Step 2.8, PR 1d): a note is not on the left or the right like the messages of the customer and of the agents; it sits on the right, on the side of the team like an agent's message, with the avatar and name of its author and the time, in a soft amber background from theme tokens with a dashed amber border, and the label "internal note" (owner, 2026-10-29, which replaces the centered note without label of 2026-10-28: the color is never the only signal, and the author and the order of the conversation read better on the side of the team). A note written by a plugin shows its nickname and the "automatic" mark (below). It shows "edited by" when a teammate changed it with a link to the revisions, and the `@` mentions highlighted; edit and delete appear on hover, according to the permissions; another agent's edit arrives in real time and a stale save shows the current text. In the composer, the note tab tints the field amber. This replaces "amber left border, Internal label" of `TDD_09 §4`. **The "automatic" mark** (drawn in Step 5.6, when a plugin can write a message) shows on every message whose author is a plugin of integration (`AuthorType.SYSTEM` with `authorPluginId`), next to its nickname, so that a plugin cannot pass as a person; it does not show on an agent's message (also one a plugin sent for them, which shows "sent through <plugin>" on hover), on the lines of the system ("resolved"), or on a message the owner typed in the provider's own app (`external`, a channel event, not a plugin call) **Drawn in PR B2** (amber tokens `--note*` in `globals.css`, dashed border, label, author, time); the edit, the revisions link and the mentions highlight are PR B4, the amber composer tab is PR B3, the "automatic" mark is Step 5.6 **Drawn in PR B4:** the edit and delete from the menu, "edited by <name>" and the edit history dialog (the link to the revisions is the menu entry), and the mentions highlighted **Left: the "automatic" mark,** owned by Step 5.6 (task "Messages from a plugin", below), because nothing can write as a plugin before it.

The result is one toast with the number changed and the reason of each kind of failure; **Undo** puts every conversation back to what it was (`lib/inbox/bulk.ts`: one step per previous state, only for rows ticked by id, never for a filter, and not for archiving), and a conversation whose old state cannot be set again (snoozed, archived) is left as it is. **Known limit:** the Undo of the toast restores the state read when the action ran and does not check what happened since, so an Undo left on screen can overwrite a later change by another agent or a reply of the customer; accepted for the MVP (a faithful Undo would need the server to refuse a change made over a newer one), to be reconsidered with the visual pass of Step 7.7. "Select all matching this filter" is offered only when a filter is in the address (the server refuses a filter that names nothing) and sends the filter, not the ids; more than `BulkLimits.MAX_ITEMS` ticked by id is refused in the bar. A row has a snooze button (hover, focus, touch) and the key `s`. On a phone the focus goes to the `h1` of the conversation when it opens, and back to its row when the agent leaves it (the back button, Escape; on a desktop too). A row resolved with `e` without opening it is resolved as the bulk bar resolves, with no reason and no closing message. Cases: `chords.spec.ts`, `labels.spec.ts`, `help-labels.spec.ts`, `list-navigation.spec.ts` and `apps/web-e2e/specs/inbox.spec.ts` (the cases named "shortcuts:", in the session of that file: the magic link is limited to `MagicLinkThrottle` per address, and a new spec that signs in as `admin@test.com` went over it). **Not built here, each with the step that owns it (the constants stay in `Shortcuts`, outside `AvailableShortcuts`, until then):** `/` and Cmd/Ctrl+K have nothing to open yet (Step 5.1 registers them with `useShortcut`); `g o` (Step 4.4) and `g c` (Step 3.3) wait for their pages; `Shift+O` (Step 4.4) and `Shift+C` (Step 3.3) wait for the order dialog and the profile drawer. When one of them is built, add its name to `AvailableShortcuts` and its label to both message files (`help-labels.spec.ts` fails otherwise). **Known limits:** `a`, `e` and `s` act on the row that has the focus before the open conversation, so after `j` moves the focus to another row they reach that row while `r` still writes to the conversation on screen (accepted: the focus is the cue, and the toast of an action on one conversation names the customer, "Resolved: Ana", with its Undo; a clearer ring for the focused row waits for the visual pass of Step 7.7); the row snooze button sits over the bottom right of the row and can cover a tag chip on a narrow list, to be looked at in the visual pass of Step 7.7

- [x] **Closing message that waits for the Undo (owner, 2026-10-30; PR B3b-2, right after B3b):** B3b sends the closing message first and resolves after, so an Undo reopens the conversation but cannot take back a message the customer already has. Move it to the server so that resolving is instant and durable (closing the tab loses nothing) and the closing message keeps the ten seconds of the Undo. `PATCH /conversations/:id/status` takes an optional `closingSnippetId` (only with `RESOLVED`); the api resolves, then stores the closing message as `queued` and hands it to the `outbound` queue with a delay of `ResolveUndo.DURATION_MS`, without reopening (the status is already set and the later send does not touch it). `OutboundSender` sends it only if the conversation is still `RESOLVED` by the same resolution (`resolvedAt` not after the message); otherwise it deletes the row (never sent, so it leaves no trace; a `failed` bubble after an Undo would be wrong, and a message left `queued` would be requeued by the sweep). The web stops sending the closing message and only asks to resolve. Cases: `tests/api` (resolve with the closing message, Undo before the send, a reply of the customer before it, resolving twice), a worker integration case, and the e2e case of PR B3b changed to wait for the message **Done in PR B3b-2:** `ConversationClosingService` (`libs/core/messaging`), the delay in `OutboundQueue.enqueue`, the check and delete in `OutboundSender`; the web only sends `closingSnippetId`
  - [x] **A clean thread after an Undo (owner, 2026-11-08, "like a Ctrl+Z"; PR `fix/clean-thread-on-undo`):** an Undo that carries `undoesResolution`, within its window (10 s + 2 s of grace, checked on the server) and from the person who resolved, deletes the system line "resolved" of that resolution (with `message.deleted`) and writes no "undone" line; `conversation.resolved` and `conversation.resolve_undone` go to the audit log with actor, address and time (the controller logs them). `resolved_at` and the reason are cleared as any reopening does, so the analytics (which read the rows) and the first-answer date are as if it never happened. A late Undo, or one by someone else, writes the usual lines. **Known limit:** the `platform_conversations_resolved_total` metric is defined but nothing increments it today, so there is no counter to restore; when one exists it must not count a resolution that was undone inside its window. **Known limits, written here (rule 12; Step 7.7 owns them):** only the single-conversation route does this (the bulk resolve and its Undo keep the two lines, and are not audited as `conversation.resolved`); the audit write is fire-and-forget like every audit in the api, so a crash between the commit and the write loses the pair; the log cannot tell a silent retraction from a late visible Undo (both are `resolve_undone`).
  - [x] **The Undo takes the queued closing message off the thread at once (owner saw it linger, 2026-11-08; PR `fix/undo-removes-closing-message`):** the reopening that carries `undoesResolution` deletes, in the same transaction and under the same lock of the conversation, the closing message of that resolution that is still `queued` and not marked as in flight (`failureDefinitive` null), with `message.deleted`. One that the worker already claimed or sent is not touched; a reopening of a person without the flag does not reach into the thread (the worker still discards it when it finds it).

One that the worker already claimed or sent is not touched; a reopening of a person without the flag does not reach into the thread (the worker still discards it when it finds it).
  - [x] **A clean thread after an Undo (owner, 2026-11-08, "like a Ctrl+Z"; PR `fix/clean-thread-on-undo`):** an Undo that carries `undoesResolution`, within its window (10 s + 2 s of grace, checked on the server) and from the person who resolved, deletes the system line "resolved" of that resolution (with `message.deleted`) and writes no "undone" line; `conversation.resolved` and `conversation.resolve_undone` go to the audit log with actor, address and time (the controller logs them). `resolved_at` and the reason are cleared as any reopening does, so the analytics (which read the rows) and the first-answer date are as if it never happened. A late Undo, or one by someone else, writes the usual lines. **Known limit:** the `platform_conversations_resolved_total` metric is defined but nothing increments it today, so there is no counter to restore; when one exists it must not count a resolution that was undone inside its window. **Known limits, written here (rule 12; Step 7.7 owns them):** only the single-conversation route does this (the bulk resolve and its Undo keep the two lines, and are not audited as `conversation.resolved`); the audit write is fire-and-forget like every audit in the api, so a crash between the commit and the write loses the pair; the log cannot tell a silent retraction from a late visible Undo (both are `resolve_undone`).
  - [x] **Race closed (QA 69, P3; PR `fix/closing-message-undo-race`):** the worker decided "the resolution still stands" on an unlocked read, so an Undo that committed between that read and the provider call (~11.5 s of the 12 s) answered 200 and the closing message still reached the customer. The check and the mark of "may have gone" are now one step under the lock of the conversation that the Undo takes (conversation, then message; no network call inside; the retry and the idempotency after the mark are those of before). **Known limit, by design (Step 7.x, sends to customers):** an Undo that arrives after that mark (past the 10 s + 2 s of grace) does not take the message back, because a message already on its way to a customer cannot be withdrawn, and the lock is not held while the provider is asked. A retry after an attempt that may have reached the provider is not judged again and its message is never deleted. **Debt (found in review, not new; Step 7.x):** a delayed job and a sweep duplicate for the same message that run together can both pass the claim (the mark cannot tell a live attempt from a retry); the key is the same for both, and a lease column on the message would make the claim exclusive.

- [x] **A snoozed conversation that wakes up is marked and raised in the list (owner, 2026-11-08; API half, PR `feat/snooze-wake-attention`):** a generic mark with a reason (`conversations.attention_reason/_at/_note`, `AttentionReasons.SNOOZE_WOKE`) that the sweep puts when the snooze ran out (not when a person wakes it) and that opening the conversation (`POST /conversations/:id/read`) or leaving OPEN takes off; the list orders by (`attention_at`, `last_message_at`, `id`) with a 3-part cursor (the old 2-part one still works), served by `conversations_store_list_order_idx`; `attention: {reason, at, note} | null` on the item and the detail; a reminder (`snooze_note`, 140 characters, `POST /conversations/:id/snooze` takes `note`) kept with the snooze, copied to the mark on waking and to the system line `woken` (`metadata.note`), dropped on any way out of SNOOZED; new realtime event `conversation.attention_changed` (reason only, never the note). A snoozed conversation nobody owns already told the team (`NotificationsInboxListener.onSnoozeExpired`); a test pins it. No counter of the panel counts unread or attention, so none changes. A note too long (or with a NUL) is `VALIDATION_FAILED` (400). **Known limits, written here (rule 12; Step 7.7 owns them):** (a) opening the conversation needs `CONVERSATIONS_VIEW` and `CONVERSATIONS_RESOLVE`, so a viewer who cannot resolve sees the mark until someone who can opens it; (b) the order puts `attention_at` first, so a list filtered by status or assignee sorts its matches instead of walking an index (the 10,000-row timing test still passes; add a composite if it ever does not); (c) `messages.metadata.note` and `attention_note` hold agent text: the retention and erasure jobs of Step 5.x must scrub them with the rest of a conversation; (d) the two new indexes are raw SQL (the precedent of the `raw_sql` migration) and are created without `CONCURRENTLY`, which is fine at this size.
