- [x] **Assign on the first reply (owner, 2026-11-08; API half, PR `feat/assign-on-reply`):** an agent who holds `CONVERSATIONS_ASSIGN` and sends a reply (text, file or template, never a note) to a conversation nobody has takes it, in the transaction of the reply and by the same atomic update as taking it by hand (`assignIfFree`), with its `assigned` line (`reason: on_reply`, `AssignmentReasons`) and `conversation.assigned`; a resolved or archived conversation that the reply reopens is taken too; an owned one never changes hands. The store setting `assignOnReply` (`store-preferences.ts`, read as on when absent, so no migration and the stores that exist are on) turns it off; `GET/PUT /settings/preferences` carry it. The closing message of a resolution does not come through here and takes nothing. The agent is not notified of what they took (`assignedBy` is the assignee). **Web half (session 12):** the switch "Assign me when I reply" in the settings of the inbox and the text of the line `assigned` with reason `on_reply`. **Known limit:** the first reply of an agent without `CONVERSATIONS_ASSIGN` (an Agent preset) leaves the conversation in the queue as before; whether they should take it too is a product call for the owner. **Accepted trade-offs (review, rule 12; Step 7.7 owns them):** a supervisor who answers one message of the shared queue to help becomes its owner (the switch of the store is the answer, and a person can release it); the reopening of a reply publishes `conversation.status_changed` with the owner of before (null) and `conversation.assigned` right after, so a socket limited to its own conversations may see the row flicker; the preference is read inside the lock of the conversation (one indexed read, only for an unowned conversation and an agent who may assign); the assignee must be an active member holding `CONVERSATIONS_VIEW` and `MESSAGES_SEND` (`assigneeCanWork`, taken before the conversation lock as `assign` does). The `reason` of a system line is still a free string shared by status and assignment reasons.
- [ ] **A text under each image (owner, 2026-11-08; plan approved by the coordinator; three PRs):** **[Superseded in part by the cut of the text under each file (owner, 2026-11-09): only a channel with `CAPTION_PER_FILE` carries a text under a file; the state, the echo and the resend of a text apart per file, and the route by file, are gone (PR `refactor/drop-per-file-caption-state`); the columns `media.caption_state` and `media.caption_channel_message_id` are dropped by migration `20261116100000`.]** (a) this PR: `POST /messages` takes `captions` (by place; `content` stays as the text under the first file and `captions` wins), `media.caption/caption_state/caption_channel_message_id` (migration `20261115120000_media_caption`, rollback drops them), the views and `api-client` carry `caption` and `captionState` per file, the idempotency key now covers the texts, and a text under a file other than the first is refused unless `OUTBOUND_CAPTION_PER_FILE_ENABLED` is on (off until (b)). (b) worker, one job per step chained (file 1, text 1, file 2...; the next step is queued by the one that finished, state in the database, the sweep resumes from the first step not sent, a step in flight is `tainted`, each step with its own timeout and the pace of the connection, the window checked at every step, an audio text goes apart as text, the echo of a text finds `media.caption_channel_message_id`, resend of one text by file; measure 10 files x 2 steps and put the figure in the PR). (c) plugins, session 31: the worker calls `sendMedia` once per file with that file's text; WhatsApp carries it natively, the web widget plugin ignores it (the visitor reads it from the api) and Messenger/Instagram keep sending it as a message of their own inside `sendMedia`, answering `CAPTION_EXTERNAL_ID` / `CAPTION_STATE` (no new capability: the worker does not read one). New in (c): the widget history and the live push carry `attachments[].caption`, the text under EACH file (the first one's too; null for a message from before the places), the files in the order the agent chose (`position`, then upload); when the first file is not given to the visitor (scan pending or infected) the text of the message is not shown under another file; the script draws each text under its file when the api sends any, else the text after the files as before, and one malformed `caption` never drops the reply (bundle +~70 bytes, 28 888 of 40 960 gzipped); each plugin has a spec for two files with a text each. **Debt:** the live-push listener (`widget-delivery.listener.ts`) has no spec of its own; it shares the select and the view with the history, which are tested (7.7). Web (composer, one bubble with each file and its text) = session 12 after (a). **Debts:** `Message.content` is still the only text that search, export, draft and preview read, so the texts under files 2 and later are not searched (7.7); the columns `messages.caption_*` stay for the first file until a later step retires them with `content`; an audio text is refused as before until (b) sends it apart. **Accepted in review (rule 12):** the replay of an idempotency key returns the original message without re-validating the texts, by design of every replay; the negative audit assertion in `conversations.spec.ts` waits a fixed 500 ms (the audit writer has no flush; a deterministic one is due in 7.7); the diagnostic of an unresolved quote counts the customer's messages also for an owner's echo. The text under the FIRST file lives in `messages.content` (an edit settles there) and the view shows it as the caption of place 0.
  - [x] **(b) The worker (PR `feat/caption-steps-worker`):** **[Superseded in part by the cut of the text under each file (owner, 2026-11-09): only a channel with `CAPTION_PER_FILE` carries a text under a file; the state, the echo and the resend of a text apart per file, and the route by file, are gone (PR `refactor/drop-per-file-caption-state`); the columns `media.caption_state` and `media.caption_channel_message_id` are dropped by migration `20261116100000`.]** one job still sends the message, in order, file by file; the state of each file and of each text is in the database (`media.delivery_status`, `media.caption_state`, `media.caption_channel_message_id`), a file already `sent` is never sent again, the sweep resumes from the first file not sent, an attempt that may have reached the provider is `tainted` and not judged again, and the locks are taken for state only, never across a call. **Decision (rule 12), differs from the plan of the coordinator in one point:** the best rejected alternative was one job per step chained (next step queued by the one that ends). It was rejected because the loop already gives order, resume and tainted semantics from the database, and a chain would have moved the in-memory aggregate of the message (`allRefused`, `anySent`, the definitive flag) into a second place to rebuild from rows on every step, in the most delicate code of the sends; if a deployment shows the single job too long, the split is a follow-up. Worst case measured: ten files with ten texts that go after them (twenty calls) add 80 ms of the core around a provider that answers at once (`outbound.spec.ts`); the provider's own time is bounded per call (205 s for a file with its text, 20 s for a text after it), so 10 files are at most about 37 minutes, under the hour after which a reply is given up. The pace is that of the files today (one call after the answer to the previous, no parallelism). Each file carries its own text (`media.caption`; the first file's is the message's): Meta channels keep sending it as a message of its own inside `sendMedia` and the worker keeps its id and state on THE FILE the moment it is answered (the echo finds it), WhatsApp and the widget carry it natively, and a kind with no place for a text on the channel (an audio on WhatsApp) is sent without it and the text follows with `sendMessage`. Before the first file leaves, every text is checked; the window is checked at every file after the first (a closed one fails the rest with `CHANNEL_WINDOW_EXPIRED`, in order). `POST /messages/:id/media/:mediaId/resend-caption` sends only that text again (from `failed` or `unconfirmed`, never automatic, the sweep ends a stale request as `unconfirmed`). `OUTBOUND_CAPTION_PER_FILE_ENABLED` stays off by default and on in the e2e. **Long job, with evidence (coordinator's condition):** BullMQ 6.3.4 defaults apply to the outbound queue (no override in `apps/worker`): `lockDuration` 30 s renewed every 15 s (`lockRenewTime` = half) by a timer for as long as the process lives, and the stalled check every 30 s only takes a job whose lock expired (a dead process or a blocked event loop; the send only awaits network, so the loop is free), with `maxStalledCount` 1; a job that is active is not added again by the sweep (same job id), so the sequence cannot be duplicated while it works. Head of line: the queue runs 20 jobs at once per process, so a sequence of 37 minutes holds one slot of 20 and does not hold up other messages of the same connection (jobs are not serialized per connection). A resume after a death is tested (files 1 and 2 sent, the text of the first in doubt, a worker that comes back sends only file 3 and its text). **Debt with owner (Step 7.3):** if a long sequence ever blocks the queue of a connection, split the job into steps queued one by one (the chain N to N+1 the coordinator asked for first); not done now because the loop already gives order, resume and the tainted rule from the database. **Review of (b) (high), fixed:** one text request at a time per message (the version of the message is the request, two at once left the first job without its turn); an apart text is marked in doubt BEFORE its file goes, so a death between the file and the text leaves it `unconfirmed` and the agent can send it again; a failure to write the state of a text after its file went is logged and never makes the file fail; the echo of an unconfirmed text of a file other than the first finds it; the text of the first file is the message's (an edit settles there) in the resend and its state is also written on the message; the time of a request is cleared only when no other text, edit or deletion of the message waits; a resend needs the file to have gone. Left (7.7): the sweep settles each waiting text in its own transaction (N+1 for a message with many texts in doubt). **Debts (older note):** an apart text whose job died between its file and the text is now resendable (see above), but still not resent by itself (the file is `sent` and the text has no state; the agent sees the file with no text); logging never carries a text, only its length; the plugins (c) and the screen are next.
- [x] **Debts of the review of #293 and #296, paid in this PR (owner of both: this session):** the audit entries `conversation.resolved` / `conversation.resolve_undone` are written from what the service did, after the commit (`StatusOptions.afterTransition`), never from the request flag, so a refused or empty request logs nothing, and the Undo says `silent: true/false` (the log can now tell a silent retraction from a late visible Undo); the quote that finds no message in an owner's echo is logged again, with the shape of the id only, as the customer's is. The audit write stays fire-and-forget like every audit of the api, so the pair is still lost if the process dies between the commit and the write (7.7).

Moving the socket out of the first load does not help, because the job counts what loads until the page is quiet. **Decision:** `lighthouse` stays advisory (not in the `main` ruleset): it measures a lab run whose result moves by a few KB between runs, and a required check that flakes blocks every PR. ### Step 3.4 — Media
- [x] **PR E is four PRs (decided 2026-11-01, rule 12: one PR cannot carry the server work, the thread, the gallery with the center and the ad media and be reviewed):** **E1** server surfaces (files in `MessageView`, `MEDIA_*` events, `FILE_UNAVAILABLE`, `missing.txt`, gallery counts, CORS check); **E2** the thread, in two PRs (decided 2026-11-01, rule 12): **E2a** the web side (composer upload with progress, attachments, placeholder, lightbox) and **E2b** the "file unavailable" marker and the caption debts, which need the server and the plugins; **E3**, in three PRs (decided 2026-11-01, rule 12: the gallery, the exports center and the bulk export are three screens with three kinds of test): **E3a** the gallery (a dialog of the customer drawer, not a page: `TDD_09 §7` draws it as a dialog with a close button), its lightbox with arrow keys, "Download all" and the debt of the stale lost-file marker; **E3b** the exports center (the `EXPORT_READY` notification and realtime refresh stay in Step 3.5, as the exports task says) and the link from the toast of "Download all"; **E3c** the bulk `EXPORT_CSV` with `conversationIds` and the CSV buttons; **E4** the media of an ad (fetch with a pinned address, origin panel). Tasks below that say "PR E" belong to the one of these that the line names
- [x] Pre-signed upload with progress, purposes and key prefixes; `StorageHealthIndicator` (`HeadBucket`) joins `/health/ready` with the S3 client (`TDD_04 §4`) **Done in PR A:** `libs/core/storage` (`@platform/core/storage-module`: `StorageService` over the S3 client, `StorageModule`), `StoragePlatformModule` (global, from the env, in `HttpPlatformModule` so the api and the worker both have it), `StorageHealthIndicator` in `/health/ready`, `POST /media/upload-url` and `POST /media/confirm-upload` (`MEDIA_UPLOAD`; the key must be exactly the one signed for that media id and name, a key of another store is `FILE_KEY_OUT_OF_SCOPE`, a repeated confirmation answers with the same media and queues the scan again under the job id `scan-file-<media id>` if the first attempt died before queueing it; the client uploads to a staging key `stores/<id>/uploads/...` and the confirmation copies the file to `StorageKeys.message` and deletes the staging one, because the signed URL outlives the confirmation and the uploader could otherwise overwrite a file after it was scanned), `sanitizeFilename`, and SeaweedFS as a service of every CI job that starts the api (integration, security, security-scan, e2e, lighthouse); the container answers `/healthz` before it creates the bucket, so the integration and security jobs run `scripts/wait-for-bucket.ts` before they need it (security-scan retries the 503 of readiness with `curl --retry`, e2e and lighthouse wait on `/health/live`). The scan job is queued but its consumer is PR B, so a confirmed media stays `pending` until then; the isolation suite has its own cases for the upload routes and `Media` leaves the exemptions when `GET /media/:id` joins the generic list
- [x] Scan-then-process worker chain (ClamAV, sharp variants as keys) **Done in PR B1:** `apps/worker/src/media` (`ClamavClient`: INSTREAM over TCP, a file without a verdict is never taken for a clean one; `MediaPipeline.scan`: infected means the object is deleted first and the row marked `infected`, clean means `clean` and, for an image, `PROCESS_IMAGE` under the job id `process-image-<media id>`; `MediaPipeline.processImage`: three WebP variants of `ImageVariantSpecs`, orientation applied and metadata dropped, `limitInputPixels` against decompression bombs, an image the decoder refuses fails for good and is served as a download without variants; a failure of the bucket is retried; a repeated scan job of a clean image queues the variants again). **Known limits (rule 12):** the variants of an animated GIF are its first frame (the original stays a download; animated WebP is a task for Step 7.x polish); the image is decoded once per variant, so the processor runs 2 jobs at a time to bound memory (a shared decode is a task if the worker memory budget of Step 7.3 needs it) and `HeavyProcessingProcessor` (one processor for the queue, told apart by job name; the fetch of inbound media joins it in PR B2). The worker integration suite runs the chain against a real ClamAV (the EICAR string), SeaweedFS and Postgres, so the `integration-tests` job has a `clamav` service (it waits for its signature database). **The worker image carries `sharp`:** a native binary cannot be bundled, so `tools/rspack/nest-bundle.cjs` takes `nativePackages`, and the Dockerfile installs `sharp` on Alpine in a stage of its own (`native-worker`; the api gets an empty one)
- [x] Sanitize the file name of an inbound media where the file is written and where `Content-Disposition` is built (**Content-Disposition done in PR B1:** `attachmentDisposition` quotes and RFC 5987-encodes the name; **the inbound file done in PR B2:** `InboundMediaFetcher` runs `sanitizeFilename` once, for the key and the row) (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md`, "WhatsApp plugin build details", item 12): the WhatsApp plugin already cuts it to a last path segment, but the name is the customer's and this step owns what is done with it
- [x] **(PR B2)** The `fetch-media` processor (moved from Step 2.7; it also fetches the `url` of an ad's media (`ConversationOrigin.mediaUrl`, `thumbnailUrl`) that Instagram, Messenger and WhatsApp send, so before any server-side fetch of a link that came from outside it must refuse private, loopback and link-local addresses, `httpUrl` does not): `InboundMessageService` already queues `fetch-media-<message id>-<index>` (one job per media of the message); it calls `plugin.fetchMedia`, stores and scans the file, and must skip a message that already has its media **Done in PR B2:** `InboundMediaFetcher` (`apps/worker/src/media`) on `HeavyProcessingProcessor` (job `FETCH_INBOUND_MEDIA`, which now carries `index`). The media id is a hash of the message and the place (`inboundMediaId`), so a retry finds its row (and only queues the scan again) and never makes a second file; the type is the one the file server declared (the declared one only when it says `application/octet-stream`) and must be an allowed type, the size is held to the limit of its type while it is read, and the file enters `pending` and goes to the scan like any upload, with `customerId` set for the gallery. A reference that is a link goes through `assertPublicHttpsUrl` first (https, default port, no credentials, every address its host resolves to public: `isPublicAddress`). A file the core refuses (type, size, empty, unsafe link) is dropped with a warning, a failure the plugin calls retryable is retried and then dropped. **Known limits (rule 12):** the plugins already follow only Meta hosts and no redirects, so the address check before the call is a second layer and cannot see a DNS answer that changes between the check and the call; the fetch of an ad's media, the marker for a file that was dropped and the pinned address are tasks of PR E (two lines below); the file is held in memory while it is stored, a task of Step 7.3
- [x] Signed download with variants **Done in PR B1:** `GET /media/:id` (`MEDIA_VIEW`, store from the tenant context): `FILE_NOT_READY` (409) until clean, `FILE_INFECTED` (403) for an infected file, the signed URL of the original and of each variant (a `variants` value that is not a key is no variant, it does not fail the answer). `Media` joins the generic list of `tenant-isolation.spec.ts` and leaves `scripts/isolation-exemptions.ts`; a resource with no change route has no `modify` (`IsolationResource`). **Done in PR E1:** `MEDIA_PROCESSED` (the scan found it clean, and again when its variants exist) and `MEDIA_INFECTED` are published by `MediaPipeline` for a file that belongs to a message, bridged (`BridgedEvents`, `bridged-payloads.ts`: ids, visibility and `internal`, no text) and sent by `RealtimeEventsListener` to who may read the message (`RealtimeEvents.MEDIA_PROCESSED` and `MEDIA_INFECTED`; the web refreshes the thread scope). A file with no message yet (still in the composer) announces nothing: the composer asks `GET /media/:id` itself (PR E2). **Not done:** the `MEDIA_INFECTED` notification of `TDD_07 §6.5` waits for the notification service (task added to Step 3.5); the worker logs a warning with the media and store ids when a file is infected
- [x] Files from a web widget visitor (owner, 2026-10-29; the widget of Step 2.8 is text only): `POST /widget/upload-url` (`ApiRoutes.widget.upload`, mapped to Step 2.8 in `scripts/frontend-coverage-map.ts` and not built), `mediaId` in `POST /widget/messages`, `ChannelCapability.IMAGE` and `DOCUMENT` declared with a `sendMedia` that works, and the attachment drawn by the bundle. The visitor is anonymous, so the upload is limited by the visitor token, the size and type of `TDD_07 §6`, and a quota per visitor and per site **Done in PR F1 (server, visitor to store):** `POST /widget/upload-url` (`ApiRoutes.widget.upload`, the visitor token authenticates it, `WidgetThrottle.UPLOAD` per address, `WidgetLimits.UPLOAD_URLS_PER_VISITOR_PER_HOUR` per visitor and `UPLOAD_URLS_PER_SITE_PER_HOUR` per site, type and size as any upload; the answer is `{ uploadUrl, uploadId }`; the file goes to the staging key `uploads/<uploadId>/<name>` and the api records in Redis, for `WidgetLimits.UPLOAD_TTL_SECONDS`, the visitor, name, type and size it signed). **There is no confirm call and no `mediaId`** (decision of F1, rule 12; the plan said `mediaId`): a `Media` row needs a message and a customer, and a confirmation without a message would leave rows nobody owns. `POST /widget/messages` takes `uploadIds` (at most `UPLOADS_PER_MESSAGE`; text, files or both); `WidgetUploads.attachmentsOf` checks each one against the record (same visitor, `head` size and type equal to what was signed, claimed by one message only; every failure is `FILE_UPLOAD_FAILED`) and hands the plugin `attachments` built from the record, never from the request (the body schema refuses unknown keys since A9 of Step 7.9). The event carries `media` with the upload id as `ref`; the worker's `InboundMediaFetcher` runs unchanged and `WebWidgetPlugin.fetchMedia` reads the object under `uploads/<uploadId>/` of the store of the connection, so type and size are checked again on the bytes, the file is copied to the messages area, scanned and tied to the customer like any file. The staging object is not deleted by the fetch; the orphan cleanup removes it after `UNCONFIRMED_UPLOAD_TTL_HOURS`. The preflight of `upload` is answered like the other two routes. The capabilities `IMAGE` and `DOCUMENT` are **not** declared yet: they promise that an agent can send a file to the visitor, which F2 builds. **Done in PR F2 (server, store to visitor):** `WebWidgetPlugin` declares `IMAGE` and `DOCUMENT` and its `sendMedia` answers `SENT` with the idempotency key as the id, like `sendMessage`: the file is already clean when `POST /messages` queues it (`requireFiles`), so there is nothing to upload to anyone; `WidgetDeliveryListener` now passes on a reply of any visible kind (text, image, video, audio, document) with its files. `WidgetMessageView` gains `attachments` (`{ id, filename, contentType, size }`, never a link) and `content` is the caption, **an empty string when the message is only files** (rule 12: not `null`, so the bundles already on the stores' sites do not crash; they draw an empty bubble until they are updated). A message with neither text nor a clean file (still scanned, infected) is not shown, in the history or live. The three places that decide what a visitor may read (the history, the live delivery and the download) share one rule, `visitorMessagesWhere` (their own messages, and the store's that the channel took; no note, system line, deleted message or reply still waiting or failed) and `WIDGET_FILES_SELECT` (a file of purpose `message` that is `clean`; the picture of an ad never). **`POST /widget/download-url`** (`ApiRoutes.widget.download`, `WidgetThrottle.DOWNLOAD`, preflight answered like the others): body `visitorToken` and `mediaId`; the token names the site and the visitor, the file must hang from a message `visitorMessagesWhere` lets that visitor read, of the store of the site, and anything else (another visitor's, another store's, a note, a deleted or unsent message, an ad, a missing id) is the same 404. The file must be `clean` (`WIDGET_FILES_SELECT`, the rule of the history), so a pending or infected file is the same 404 and the visitor learns nothing of the scan; the link is signed by the unchanged `MediaService.urlsFor` (`MediaModule` now exports it) and the original is always an attachment download; the answer is `{ url, filename, contentType, previewUrl }`, where `previewUrl` is the medium WebP variant for an image and `null` otherwise. The readers of "Seen" (`readUpTo`) count a message of files. The history query asks for something to show (a text or a clean file) so that files still being scanned do not use the 50 slots of the window, and `sendMedia` refuses video and audio. Cases: `the files of a conversation` and one socket case in `tests/api/widget.spec.ts`, the plugin spec. **Left (PR F3):** the bundle: the paperclip, the upload with progress, the attachment drawn (it asks `download-url` when the visitor opens it), the field error of the phone below, inside the 40 KB gzip budget. **Known limits of F2 (rule 12):** (1) only a file the scan found clean is listed, so a file the visitor just sent appears in the history after its scan, not at once (the bundle shows it from its own state meanwhile); (2) an agent can send images and documents to a visitor, not video or audio (the visitor can send them; declaring them is a task of Step 7.7 with the player); (3) the download throttle (60 a minute) is per address like the other public routes, with no per-visitor counter, and the bundle asks one link per file it opens or previews, so F3 must ask on demand (not one call per history message) and a preview link in the history answer is the fix if that is not enough (Step 7.3, with the other abuse limits of public routes); the media row is read twice per link (the ownership check and `urlsFor`), accepted; (4) a store site that still serves a bundle from before F3 draws a file-only message as an empty bubble. **Known limits of F1 (rule 12, from its review):** (1) the per-visitor cap keys on a `visitorId` the client chooses at the bootstrap, so a script can mint visitors and drain the per-site cap (`UPLOAD_URLS_PER_SITE_PER_HOUR`) for real visitors; the address throttle (`WidgetThrottle`) bounds the rate, and a per-address hourly counter is a task of Step 7.3 with the other abuse limits of public routes; (2) a message must omit `content` when it has only files (`content: ''` is refused as an empty text); the bundle of F3 omits it. (3) the record of an upload and its claim last `UPLOAD_TTL_SECONDS` (1 hour): a resend of an already queued message after that is refused instead of answered again; (4) a message takes its type from its first file, so a PDF followed by an image is a `DOCUMENT` with an image among its media (the inbox draws each file by its own type; the gallery filters by the file); (5) the api checks size and type of the staged object but does not pin its etag as `confirmUpload` does, so bytes swapped through the still-valid signed URL before the fetch are possible; the worker checks type and size again on the bytes it reads and scans the copy, so nothing unscanned is ever served. A file of the visitor shows in the inbox as soon as the worker has it; until the scan ends it is `pending`, like any file **Done in PR F3 (the bundle, 27.2 KB gzip of 40):** a paperclip beside the text field picks files (`accept` is the allowed types; `refuseFile` applies the rule of the api, `refuseUpload`, before any byte leaves, and at most `UPLOADS_PER_MESSAGE` stay staged, each removable), `Session.send(content, files)` writes the message at once and puts the files up one after the other (`POST /widget/upload-url`, then an XHR `PUT` for the progress, shown as a bar that moves in place), then names them in `uploadIds` of `POST /widget/messages`, omitting `content` when there is only files. A retry puts up only what is missing and sends under the same message id; an upload the api no longer vouches for (`FILE_UPLOAD_FAILED`) is put up again. A file is drawn as a name and a size; its link is asked from `POST /widget/download-url` only when the visitor opens it (one call per click, never per history message, so the 60/min throttle is not met) and followed as a download, never shown on the page. The rules `refuseUpload`, `maxBytesFor` and `UploadRefusals` moved to `upload-rules.ts` (re-exported by the same barrel) because they sat beside the zod schemas and importing them put the validation library in the bundle (51 KB gzip). Cases: unit specs of the api client, log, session, panel and `upload.ts`, and two cases of `widget.spec.ts` (e2e, the strict-policy site now lists the bucket host in `connect-src`: a store that sets one must do the same, task in Step 3.6). **Known limits (rule 12, from its review):** (1) a file the visitor just sent is drawn without a link until the history is read again (the answer of `POST /widget/messages` carries no media ids), so it cannot be opened for up to the renewal of the token; (2) the link is followed with a click after an await, which Safari may block (the same debt as the gallery tile, Step 3.5); (3) the `File` objects of a message that never succeeds stay in memory until the page closes. Task in Step 3.5: have the messages answer return the ids of the media, which fixes (1). The e2e job has no ClamAV, so the case checks the upload and the send, not the file reaching the inbox; the scan and the inbox side are covered by the integration suites
- [x] Media gallery with lightbox, bulk ZIP export, and the route it reads, `GET /customers/:id/media` (`ApiRoutes.customers.media`, by `Media.customerId`, which no earlier step builds): filters by type with their counts, a search by file name, and "Download all" that creates the `MEDIA_ZIP` export and needs `MEDIA_DOWNLOAD_ALL` (`TDD_09 §7`) **Server done in PR D2:** `GET /customers/:id/media` (`CUSTOMERS_VIEW` + `MEDIA_VIEW`; `type` = `all | image | video | audio | document`, `q` = a part of the file name with `%` and `_` as text, a cursor page; the answer carries `counts` for every tab under the search, whatever tab is open, and for each file its id, tab, name, type, size, date and the channel of its conversation, never a link or a key: the link is asked from `GET /media/:id`). Only `clean` files are offered (a file not scanned yet appears when its scan ends, an infected one never), and only those of conversations the viewer could open in the inbox (`visibleTo`; nothing archived or spam without `CONVERSATIONS_ARCHIVE`; no `CONVERSATIONS_VIEW` means no file; a file with no message only for who sees it all). The ZIP freezes the requester and their view permissions in the row (`viewer`), as the conversations CSV does, so it holds what that member could see; the assignment of each conversation is read when the worker runs; a customer of another store or absorbed by a merge is a 404 (a merge moves `Media.customerId` to the survivor). `POST /media/download-all` (`CUSTOMERS_VIEW` + `MEDIA_VIEW` + `MEDIA_DOWNLOAD_ALL`, 202 with the export; body `customerId` and the optional `type` and `q` of the gallery) refuses an empty selection (`EXPORT_EMPTY`) and one over `GalleryLimits.ZIP_MAX_FILES` (500) or `ZIP_MAX_BYTES` (1 GiB) (`EXPORT_TOO_LARGE`) before it creates the row; the worker holds the same ceilings, because files can arrive between the request and the ZIP, and fails the row for good with `EXPORT_TOO_LARGE`. The ZIP is made by `ZipExportWriter` (`yazl`): the files are read from the bucket one at a time and the ZIP is uploaded as it is written, so memory does not grow with the files; the names inside are cleaned again and repeated ones get `(2)`, `(3)` (`ZipEntryNames`); only text and PDF are deflated. It holds the gallery **as it is when the worker runs**, not when the member asked (a merge in between moves the files to the survivor and the ZIP of the absorbed customer fails with `EXPORT_EMPTY`). The job is `GENERATE_EXPORT` like every export: the TDD's `GENERATE_MEDIA_ZIP` was never needed and its constant is gone (the row says the kind; `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` wins). A media ZIP is listed and downloaded only by who still holds all three permissions. ****Interface done in PR E3a:** `MediaGallery` (`apps/web/components/customers/media-gallery.tsx`), a dialog opened from a button of the customer drawer for who holds `CUSTOMERS_VIEW` + `MEDIA_VIEW`, loaded on demand (the inbox budget has no room for it): tabs with the counts of the first page, a search by file name (debounced), a grid with download and, for images and videos, the lightbox with arrow keys (`MediaLightbox` takes `onPrevious` and `onNext`), "Load more", and "Download all" for who holds `MEDIA_DOWNLOAD_ALL`, which toasts that the ZIP is being prepared. **Done in PR E3b:** the toast of "Download all" has a "View exports" action that goes to the exports center. **Not done:** each image tile asks its own signed link (`GET /media/:id`), so a page of images makes one call per tile, as the thread does (a thumbnail link in the list answer would avoid it; owner: Step 3.5); the download of a tile opens the signed link in a new tab after an await, which Safari may block (the fix needs the link to be a same-tab attachment download; owner: Step 3.5)
- [x] `mediaIds` on `POST /messages` (the upload comes first, `TDD_09 §4`): the media is checked against the store and the conversation, and linked to the message in the same transaction **Done in PR C:** the body for `IMAGE`, `VIDEO`, `AUDIO` and `DOCUMENT` takes `mediaIds` (exactly one, `MediaMessageLimits.MAX_FILES`) and an optional `content`; `MessagesService.requireFiles` runs under the conversation lock and after the replay check: the file must be of this store, uploaded by the sender (`uploadedBy`; another member's upload, another store's and a missing one are the same 404), `purpose` message, not yet attached (409), `clean` (`FILE_NOT_READY` 409, `FILE_INFECTED` 403) and of the type the message says (`mediaMessageTypeOf`, else 400), and the channel must declare the capability of the type (`CHANNEL_CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED` 409, asked of the plugin registry by the route). `linkFiles` sets `Media.messageId` and `customerId` (the gallery) in the same transaction with `messageId: null` in the condition, so two conversations racing for one file give it to the first. A repeated key with other files is 409. Permission: `MESSAGES_SEND`
- [x] A media message is one message with its text under it (owner, 2026-10-28): `POST /messages` with an image, video, audio or document `type`, `mediaIds` and `content` as the caption, in the same `idempotencyKey` and message, never a media message followed by a text one. The sender hands the caption to `sendMedia` (`SendMediaParams.caption`, which the WhatsApp plugin already sends within its limits: 1024 characters, none on audio); a channel that cannot carry a caption on a type is refused for good before the send, as WhatsApp does for audio. Inbound already works that way (WhatsApp stores the caption of the media as the `content` of the same message). `replyToMessageId` applies to a media message as it does to text. **Albums (decided 2026-10-28):** inbound, a channel that brings several attachments in one message (Instagram, Messenger) becomes one message with N media (`Media.messageId` is not unique, so the model needs no change), and WhatsApp keeps one message per media; outbound, the MVP sends one media per message with its caption. **Echo of a caption (found in PR 1c, owner 2026-10-29):** Instagram and Messenger cannot carry a caption in the message of the file, so their plugins send it as a second message and answer `metadata.captionExternalId`; Meta echoes both. The sender must keep that id (the `Message` row has one `channelMessageId`) and the echo check of `InboundMessageService` must know it, so that the caption is not stored again as an `external` message; every other echo still enters as `external`. **Phase 2:** `mediaIds` with several entries, split by the core into consecutive sends when the channel takes one per send, the caption on the first, and a place for the `channelMessageId` of each part (`Message.channelMessageId` is one column today) **Done in PR C (outbound):** `OutboundSender` signs the download of the one clean file (`StorageService.downloadUrl`, 15 minutes, a fresh link per attempt) and calls `plugin.sendMedia` with the text as `caption` (none when blank), the reply target and the message id as the key; a plugin without `sendMedia`, a file that is missing, not clean or not alone fails the message for good (`CHANNEL_CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED`, `CHANNEL_MESSAGE_FAILED`); a caption a plugin cannot carry (WhatsApp audio, too long) is refused by the plugin before the call, as before. The `captionExternalId` the plugin returns is read from the result (`ChannelResultMetadata.CAPTION_EXTERNAL_ID`, a constant now) and kept in `Message.metadata` by `MessageStatusWriter`; `InboundMessageService` looks an echo up by `channelMessageId` and then by that key, so the second message of Instagram and Messenger is a duplicate. **Not done, owned by PR E:** the thread does not draw the file yet (see the task below)
- [x] **The composer sends a file (PR E2a):** the paperclip of a reply (never of a note) picks one file; `refuseAttachment` checks the type and size with the same rule as the api (`refuseUpload`) before any byte leaves the browser; `useAttachment` asks `POST /media/upload-url`, `PUT`s to the signed URL with XHR for the progress, calls `POST /media/confirm-upload`, then asks `GET /media/:id` every 1.5 s (40 tries) until the file is clean (`waitUntilClean`; `FILE_INFECTED` drops it, a minute without verdict is `FILE_NOT_READY`), and only then enables Send, which posts one message with `mediaIds` and the text as caption under one idempotency key per attachment. A file does **not** go through the offline queue (an upload needs the network); while it uploads or is scanned nothing is sent, so the text cannot leave without its file. `NEXT_PUBLIC_STORAGE_URL` (required, in the web env schema, `.env.example`, the Dockerfile and the CI build) joins `connect-src`, `img-src` and `media-src` of the CSP. **Known limits (rule 12):** (1) **closed in PR E2b:** the paperclip is hidden when the channel declares no kind of file, the picker offers only the kinds it declares (`acceptFor`), and a file of a kind it does not take is refused before the upload with `CHANNEL_CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED` (`channelTakesFile`; the api still checks it); (2) the hook has no unit test because `apps/web` has no DOM test setup: its pure parts (`attachment-rules`, `waitUntilClean`) are tested and the refusal path has an e2e case; the real upload path (needs ClamAV in the e2e job) is tested by the api and worker suites; (3) one file per message (`MediaMessageLimits.MAX_FILES`); (4) removing the chip right after the `PUT` cannot cancel the confirmation already in flight, so that file stays a row with no message until the orphan cleanup takes it; (5) a file whose scan died for good answers `FILE_NOT_READY` like a slow one, so the composer waits its minute before failing; both close with the terminal state for an unscannable file of Step 3.5

(2) **Done in PR E2b:** a caption a channel cannot carry (WhatsApp audio, over 1024 characters, the text limit of Instagram and Messenger) is refused by `POST /messages` in `requireCaptionFits` from the limits the plugin declared (`ChannelLimitsView`, Step 7.9 A2), before anything is queued. (4) **Done in PR E2b:** the id of the message that carried the caption is the column `messages.caption_channel_message_id`, indexed with the conversation (migration `20261103120000_message_caption_channel_id` copies the old `metadata.captionExternalId` and removes it; rollback script included), and the echo lookup reads the column.

(3) The thread cannot re-fetch a customer file that was lost (`unavailableFiles`); the dead-letter queue is the only way and a button needs the provider's media id to be kept in the message (owner: Step 3.5). - [x] The exports center, server and interface (`exports.list`, `exports.byId`, `exports.download`; no earlier step builds these three routes, found by the second pass of the audit): every export of the store (media ZIP, customers CSV, conversations, analytics) with its state, progress and a signed download, and the realtime or notification when one is ready. Created here because this step builds the first export **Server done in PR D1:** `ExportsModule` (`apps/api/src/modules/exports`): `GET /exports`, `GET /exports/:id`, `GET /exports/:id/download` (`@RequireAnyPermission` of `CONVERSATIONS_EXPORT`, `CUSTOMERS_EXPORT`, `MEDIA_DOWNLOAD_ALL`, a new decorator of the permission guard: one of the list is enough, `@RequirePermissions` still needs all of its own). **A member sees and downloads only the exports they asked for**, and only of the kinds their role may still make; another member's, another store's and a kind the role lost are the same 404 (an export is personal data on its way out, so "every export of the store" was narrowed on purpose). `request()` takes the store lock and refuses a fourth active export (`MAX_ACTIVE_PER_STORE`, 429; a `pending` row older than `ACTIVE_WINDOW_MINUTES` no longer counts), creates the row (`expiresAt` in 7 days: `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §10 item 25 wins over the 14 of `TDD_07 §7`, `DataRetentionPolicies.EXPORTS` is 7 now) and queues `GENERATE_EXPORT` under `generate-export-<id>`; a queue that refuses marks the row `failed`. The worker (`apps/worker/src/exports`): `ExportsProcessor` (queue `exports`, one at a time), `ExportGenerator` (reads the kind, filters and requester from the row, never from the job; a row that is not `pending` is left alone; filters that do not fit fail the row for good), `CsvExportWriter` (`StorageService.putStream`, multipart upload that is aborted when the source fails, so no half file stays), the audit entry with `ExportAuditActions` and the filters, `ExpiredExportsCleaner` + `ExpiredExportsScheduler` (`Schedules.CLEAN_EXPIRED_EXPORTS`, hourly: deletes the object of a `ready` export past its date and marks the row `expired`, gives up on a `pending` row older than `PENDING_GIVE_UP_HOURS`). The download is a signed link with `Content-Disposition: attachment` and the type of the format, asked each time. `Export` leaves the isolation exemptions. **Interface done in PR E3b:** the page `/s/:storeId/exports` (`ExportsPage`, `apps/web/app/s/exports-page.tsx`) and its entry in the store nav, shown to who holds any of `CONVERSATIONS_EXPORT`, `CUSTOMERS_EXPORT` or `MEDIA_DOWNLOAD_ALL` (`mayOpenExports`): a table of kind, state, file, rows, date asked and date kept until, a cursor "Load more", a Download button for a `ready` row that asks the signed link each time and goes to it in the same tab (the link answers `attachment`, so the page stays; this also avoids the pop-up block a new tab meets in Safari), and the code of a failed export translated. A kind or state the page does not know draws as "Other" or "Unknown". The list asks again every `EXPORT_POLL_MS` (5 s) only while a row is `pending`; asking for a ZIP makes the list stale so the new row is there when the person arrives. The export kinds, formats and states moved from `libs/core/platform/queues` to `libs/core/domain/exports` (the web may not import the platform layer; the queues path re-exports them). Cases: `export-state.spec.ts`, `exports-api.spec.ts` and the e2e case of the gallery, which follows the toast link to the center. **Not done, owner Step 3.5:** the polling gives way to the `EXPORT_READY` realtime event; `AppPaths.store.exportDownload` (`/s/:storeId/exports/:id`, the `actionPath` of the notification) has no page yet, so the notification task must add one that opens the center on that row or point `actionPath` at the center; no filter by kind or state and no progress bar (the row carries no progress, only `pending`); **Done in E3c:** the CSV buttons (the customers page, which exports the search it shows, and the inbox bulk bar, in "More"). **Also not done:** the `EXPORT_READY` notification and realtime refresh wait for the notification service of Step 3.5 (task added there); rows of `failed` and `expired` exports stay (they hold no personal data, only the filters), a purge of old rows is a task of Step 7.3

The interface asks no confirmation (a file changes nothing) and its toast leads to the exports center. (2) The assignment of each conversation is read when the file is made, not when it was asked: Step 7.1, task "Exports leave the platform with personal data". **Decision (E1, rule 12):** the export row freezes filters, not ids, so a selection by ids needs `conversationIds` in the filters (checked against the visibility of the requester, capped at `BulkLimits.MAX_ITEMS`) and the same scope rule in the worker; that is PR E3's first task

- [x] **Purposes a client may upload for (found building PR A):** `ClientUploadPurposes` (`libs/core/domain/storage/upload-schemas.ts`) holds only `message`. An upload purpose is not added before the code that checks who may attach it: `product` with Step 4.1 and `order` with Step 4.3 (both need a `parentId` that belongs to the store, `TDD_07 §6.3`), `avatar` with Step 3.6 (the key has no store, `StorageKeys.avatar`, so the `confirmUpload` prefix check is by user there)
- [x] **Orphan uploads (PR B2, done):** `OrphanMediaCleaner` and `OrphanMediaScheduler` (`Schedules.CLEAN_ORPHAN_MEDIA`, hourly, one replica) delete the stray objects, requeue the scans that were lost (a finished job with the same id is removed first, or it would swallow the new one), drop a `pending` row whose file is gone once it is past `UNCONFIRMED_UPLOAD_TTL_HOURS`, and delete the file of an `infected` row. It lists `ORPHAN_LIST_PAGES_PER_RUN` pages per run and resumes from a cursor in Redis; the infected rows are looked at for `INFECTED_CLEANUP_WINDOW_HOURS` after their verdict. **Known limits:** a `pending` row whose scan fails for good is queued again every hour (a terminal state for it is a task of Step 3.5, with the notification of an unscannable file; until then 500 such rows can crowd out the rows behind them, the batch is oldest first), and the requester of an upload lives only in Redis (accepted: the member asks again). Speed of a full turn is a task of Step 7.3. The original task: a scheduled job in the worker deletes the objects under `stores/*/uploads/` and under `stores/*/messages/` with no `Media` row (a confirmation that died between the copy and the row leaves one at the final key; deleting it at the time could destroy the file of a concurrent confirmation of the same media) older than `UploadLimits.UNCONFIRMED_UPLOAD_TTL_HOURS` (the signed URL lets a member leave a file in the bucket and never confirm it), and the `pending` rows whose scan never ran, and the object of an `infected` row (the scan marks the row first and deletes the file second, so a failure in between leaves the file)
- [x] **Downloads never trust the name (PR B1, review finding of PR A):** the extension of `Media.filename` is the client's and may not match its declared type (`x.html` declared `image/png`). Every signed download sets `ResponseContentType` from `Media.contentType` and `ResponseContentDisposition: attachment` (inline only for the image variants, which are re-encoded WebP), so that nothing is ever served as active content by its extension
- [x] **Upload quota (PR B2, with the orphan cleanup; done):** 100 signed URLs per user and 300 per store per hour (`SIGNED_URLS_PER_USER_PER_HOUR`, `SIGNED_URLS_PER_STORE_PER_HOUR`), counted in Redis by hour, answered with 429 `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`. `upload-url` records who asked (`CacheKeys.upload.requester`, `PENDING_UPLOAD_TTL_SECONDS`) and `confirm-upload` answers `FILE_UPLOAD_FAILED` to anyone else, also for a media that already exists; a Redis that lost the record makes the member ask again. The original task: `upload-url` persists nothing, so a member with `MEDIA_UPLOAD` can fill the bucket with files that are never confirmed until the cleanup runs. Add a per-store (and per-user) limit of signed URLs per hour in Redis, and `UploadLimits` constants for it. The same step persists who asked for each signed URL (a pending-upload record, or an HMAC in the `mediaId`) so that `confirm-upload` accepts only that user: today any member holding the ids could confirm another's upload and be recorded as its `uploadedBy`. **MED-15 (QA, 2026-11-07):** the hourly quota is the per-person cap of `POST /media/upload-url` (a per-minute one would be weaker than it, so none was added); its 429 now carries `retryAfterSeconds` (the seconds left of the hour). One batch of the multi-file composer (10 files) uses 10 of the 100 asks of the hour, so a person who sends more than ten such batches in an hour waits for the next one: raise `SIGNED_URLS_PER_USER_PER_HOUR` if real use shows it. The composer does not read `retryAfterSeconds` yet, so a person over the limit sees the generic message: a web task (draw the wait, as the team page does).
- [x] **Ad media fetched and stored (PR E4; debt of PR B2, which left it out):** `ConversationOrigin.mediaUrl` and `thumbnailUrl` are stored as links only, and nothing fetches them. Fetch them in a job of the heavy queue, store them as `Media` (scan and variants like any file) and draw them in the origin panel. The fetch is done by the core, not by a plugin, so it must call `assertPublicHttpsUrl` (`apps/worker/src/media/public-url.ts`), follow no redirect, and **pin the address it resolved** for the request (an agent with a fixed `lookup`), which closes the DNS change between the check and the call that B2 left open **Done in PR E4:** `Media.originId` and `originSlot` (migration `20261104120000_media_origin`, with its rollback; purpose `MediaPurpose.ORIGIN`; the row goes with its origin, `ON DELETE CASCADE`, and the orphan cleanup removes the object). `InboundMessageService.queueOriginFetches` queues one `FETCH_ORIGIN_MEDIA` job per slot (`OriginMediaSlots`: `thumbnail`, `media`) after the commit, only for an origin row the message created (the same ad again makes no row and no job), under the job id `fetch-origin-media-<origin id>-<slot>`; the job carries ids only and `OriginMediaFetcher` (`apps/worker/src/media`) reads the link from the row. The request is `pinnedHttpsGet`: `resolvePublicHttpsUrl` checks every address of the host, the socket is opened to the checked address through a fixed `lookup` (the certificate is still checked against the name of the link), no redirect is followed (3xx, 4xx are final; 429 and 5xx are retried), identity encoding, and the body is read with the size limit of an image (10 MB) and must declare an image type. The file is stored under the messages area of the bucket with a deterministic media id (`originMediaId`), `pending`, with no message and no customer (so the gallery and the media ZIP never see it), and queued to the scan and the variants like any file. `GET /conversations/:id/origins` carries `mediaFile` and `thumbnailFile` (`{ id, status }` for `pending` and `clean`; an infected file is left out, a file of another store is never matched); the card draws the signed link of the thumbnail (`GET /media/:id`), or the media when it is an image, and **never the address of the ad** (it expires and tells the ad's CDN who is looking), and the open conversation asks the origins again every 3 s while a picture of an origin under 5 minutes old is `pending`. Cases: `origin-media-fetcher.service.spec.ts`, `pinned-https-get.spec.ts`, `origin-picture.spec.ts`, two in `conversation-history.spec.ts`, two in `inbound.spec.ts` and one in `thread.spec.ts` (e2e). **Known limits (rule 12):** (1) a video of an ad is not fetched (it is the largest file that comes in and nothing draws it); the card shows the thumbnail, and a player is a task of Step 7.7 (visual polish); (2) origins recorded before this PR have no picture and are not backfilled here; the one-off script is the task "Backfill of ad pictures" of Step 7.3; (3) the pin covers the fetch the core makes; the fetch of a customer's file by a plugin (`mediaRef` as a link) keeps the address check of B2 and the plugin's own host list; (4) the file is held in memory while it is stored, as every fetch is (Step 7.3); (5) a picture that was dropped (refused type, unsafe link, 404) leaves no marker: the card has no picture, the origin keeps its title and body; (6) no event announces that the picture of an ad is ready, so the open conversation asks again every 3 s for 5 minutes after the origin was recorded (measured on the clock of the browser against the time of the message) and then waits for the next load: a `MEDIA_PROCESSED` for origin files, invalidating the origins, is a task of Step 3.5 (realtime and notifications). A generic `application/octet-stream` answer is accepted when the first bytes are an image; a link that fails its certificate or does not resolve is dropped without retry
- [x] **A customer file that could not be kept (PR E2b; debt of PR B2):** `InboundMediaFetcher` drops a file it refuses or cannot fetch and the message shows nothing. **Done in PR E2b (decision, rule 12):** the worker, and not `InboundMessageService`, records it when the job gives up: `UnavailableFileMarker` adds `{ index, code }` to `metadata.unavailableFiles` of the message (codes `MediaUnavailableCodes`: `refused`, `unsafe_link`, `fetch_failed`, `channel_cannot_fetch`; under the message row lock, because an album is several jobs on one row; one entry per place) and publishes `MEDIA_UNAVAILABLE`, which refreshes the open thread. Writing a "file expected" count when the fetch is queued was rejected: a file still being fetched would read as lost until the job ends, and the count adds nothing the give-up record does not say. A retryable failure is recorded only on the last attempt. `MessageView.unavailableFiles` carries the list (empty for a deleted message) and `MessageMedia` draws one line per entry, with a text for each code (en and es). The message type keeps its icon placeholder out of the way when a line is drawn
- [x] **Bucket CORS (PR E1):** the browser `PUT`s to the signed URL on the bucket host, so the bucket must allow the origin of the web app with `PUT` and the `content-type` header. **Verified in E1:** SeaweedFS 4.48 (development and CI) answers the preflight for `PUT` with `content-type` for any origin and exposes `ETag`, so nothing is configured there. **Left:** the CORS rule of the production S3 bucket (the origin of the web app, `PUT`, `content-type`, expose `ETag`) is a task of Step 7.3. **Added in PR F1:** the visitors of the web widget `PUT` from the sites of the stores, not from the web app, so the rule of the production bucket must also allow those origins (the signed URL is what authorizes the write, so `*` with `PUT` and `content-type` is enough there)
- [x] **A file missing from a media ZIP is told (debt of PR D2 of Step 3.4; done in PR E1):** `ZipExportWriter` adds a `missing.txt` entry (`MEDIA_ZIP_MISSING_ENTRY`, one name per line, no prose so it needs no language) when it leaves out a file that is no longer in the bucket; a ZIP in which every file is gone still fails with `EXPORT_EMPTY`
- [x] **Gallery counts only on the first page (debt of PR D2 of Step 3.4; done in PR E1):** `GET /customers/:id/media` runs the per-tab `groupBy` only without a cursor and answers `counts: null` on the pages that follow; the gallery page (PR E3) keeps the counts of the first page
- [x] **Accepted limits of the media ZIP (PR D2 of Step 3.4, owner not asked, rule 12):** (1) the ZIP holds the gallery as it is when the worker runs, so a merge between the request and the job moves the files to the survivor and the ZIP of the absorbed customer fails with `EXPORT_EMPTY`: the center (PR E) shows that code and the member asks again from the survivor; (2) the assignment of each conversation is read when the worker runs, while the requester's permissions are frozen in the row. No change planned; revisit with the conversations export limits of Step 7.3
- [x] **The phone of the pre-chat form (debt of PR D2 of Step 3.3, owner 2026-11-01; PR F):** the widget form checks the phone by characters only and the api drops a phone it cannot read without saying so. Make the api answer with a field error the bundle shows, cheaper than shipping `libphonenumber-js` in the bundle (26.5 KB gzip of the 40 KB budget) **Server done in PR F1:** with the form on, `POST /widget/messages` answers 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED` with `metadata.issues` `[{ path: 'profile.phone', code: 'invalid_phone' }]` (`WidgetFieldIssues`) for a phone `normalizePhone` cannot read, before any file is claimed or anything is queued; a store with the form off still drops the profile as before. **Left (PR F3):** the bundle reads that issue and shows `invalidPhone` under the phone field **Done in PR F3:** the bundle reads `metadata.issues` (`WidgetApiError.phoneRefused`); when the api refuses the phone the message stays (failed), the form comes back with `invalidPhone` under the phone input (`aria-invalid`), and the messages held back are sent when the visitor submits it again (`Session.setProfile`). e2e: a case of `widget.spec.ts`

**TDD:** `TDD_07 §6`, `§7`
**Done when:** an image uploads, is scanned, generates variants, and is served through
signed URLs; a `confirmUpload` with a foreign store prefix returns `FILE_KEY_OUT_OF_SCOPE`; a message sent with `mediaIds` shows the attachment in the thread, and one naming media of another store is refused.

### Step 3.5 — Notifications
- [x] Notification service with `messageKey` + params, preference resolution, quiet hours from `UserNotificationSettings`, and its routes: `notifications.list`, `unreadCount`, `markRead`, `markAllRead` and `preferences` (PR A of Step 3.5; `NotificationsService.send` saves the row and emits `NOTIFICATION` to the sockets of the user in that store). **Decisions of PR A:** (1) these routes belong to the member and not to a role, so they carry the new `@MemberOfStore()` (the permission guard lets in an active member of the store of the header; `route-protection.spec` refuses it next to a permission or an exempt route); (2) `markRead` answers 404 when none of the ids is the caller's, and skips the foreign ones otherwise; (3) quiet hours read the hour with `Intl` (`libs/core/domain/notifications/quiet-hours.ts`), not `date-fns-tz`, which the repository does not have. **Left to later PRs of this step (written here, not only in the PR):** `send` does not queue web push (PR C adds `SEND_WEB_PUSH` and the `pushSubscription` route) nor the email digest (PR D adds `QUEUE_EMAIL_DIGEST`); `crossStoreEnabled` and `crossStoreSupervisorOnly` are stored and returned but nothing reads them yet: the notification types of the MVP are all raised inside one store, so the setting takes effect in the first step that notifies across stores (owner: PR E draws the switches; no step uses them before Phase 2). **Review findings left on purpose (PR A, owner: PR F of this step):** (1) `sendToStore` reads the settings and the business hours of the store once per recipient, and `send` could run the preference and settings reads together: hoist the store's hours out of the loop when a type reaches a large team; (2) the follow-store branch of quiet hours repeats `WidgetAway.isAway` (hours missing or unreadable means open): extract one `isStoreClosed(storeId, at)` for both; (3) `PUT preferences` upserts the type rows one by one inside the transaction: batch them if the page ever sends the whole matrix at once
- [x] **The picture of an ad is ready (debt of PR E4 of Step 3.4):** `MediaPipeline` publishes `MEDIA_PROCESSED` only for a file that belongs to a message, so the origin card polls for 5 minutes instead of being told. Publish it for a file of an origin with the conversation's visibility, bridge it, and have the web invalidate the origins of that conversation; then drop `refetchInterval` from `useOrigins` **Done in PR F2 of Step 3.5:** `MediaPipeline.announce` falls back to the message that carried the ad (`ConversationOrigin.messageId`, unique) when the file has no message of its own, so the existing `MEDIA_PROCESSED`, `MEDIA_INFECTED` and `MEDIA_UNSCANNABLE` reach whoever may read that message and the web refreshes the thread scope, which holds `QueryKeys.messages.origins`. No new event, payload or bridge entry. `useOrigins` no longer polls (`originsNeedRefresh`, `ORIGIN_REFRESH_MS` and its window are gone). **Decisions (rule 12):** (1) the event is the message's, not a new origin event: the visibility rule (`visibility`, `internal`) is the one of the conversation and nothing widens; (2) an infected picture of an ad now also raises the `MEDIA_INFECTED` notification of PR B1 to who may read the conversation, which is right for a file that came from outside; (3) a picture that was dropped (refused type, unsafe link, 404) still announces nothing and leaves the card as it was. **Known limit:** a client that was offline when the scan ended shows the placeholder until its next load of the conversation. Case: two in `media-pipeline.events.spec.ts`
- [x] **An infected file (debt of PR E1 of Step 3.4, done in PR B1 of Step 3.5):** `MEDIA_INFECTED` reaches the open thread but nobody is notified. Add a `MEDIA_INFECTED` notification type with its constants and send it (`TDD_07 §6.5`) to the members who may read the conversation of the message and add the audit entry, from the same event **Decisions of PR B1:** (1) `NotificationsEventsListener` (api) hears `MESSAGE_MENTION` and `MEDIA_INFECTED` and decides who is told when it runs, not when the event was raised, with the new `conversationReaders` filter (`libs/core/messaging`), which `requireMentionable` now uses too: one rule for the check of a mention, the mention notification and the infected one; (2) `AppPaths` moved to `libs/core/domain` (the api may not import `api-client`) and `api-client` re-exports it, so the web is unchanged; (3) an infected file that belongs to no message has no event and so no notification: the composer shows its state (the worker only announces files of a message); the audit entry `AuditAction.MEDIA_INFECTED` is written by the same listener with a system actor. **Left on purpose (owner: PR F):** the audit entry is one per message, not per file (the event carries no media id); `sendToUsers` writes for all recipients at once, fine for a team of the MVP, to be batched with the PR A findings if a store grows
- [x] **An export that is ready (debt of PR D1 of Step 3.4, done in PR B2a of Step 3.5):** `ExportGenerator` marks the row `ready` and audits it, but sends nothing. Send `NotificationTypes.EXPORT_READY` to `Export.requestedBy` with `{ filename, type }` and the path of the download (`TDD_07 §7`), and publish `Events.EXPORT_READY` (its payload type does not exist yet) so that the exports center refreshes without a reload; do the same for a failed export. **Also here (debt of PR E3b):** drop the polling of `useExportList` (`EXPORT_POLL_MS`) for the event, and give `AppPaths.store.exportDownload` (`/s/:storeId/exports/:id`, the `actionPath` of this notification) a page, or point the `actionPath` at the center `AppPaths.store.exports` **Decisions of PR B2a:** (1) `ExportGenerator` and `ExpiredExportsCleaner` (the give-up of a stale `pending` row) publish `Events.EXPORT_READY` / the new `Events.EXPORT_FAILED` after the row settles, only from the call that settled it, with ids and the kind (`ExportSettledPayload`); both cross the bridge (`bridged-payloads.ts`), and a failure to announce is logged, never fails the job; (2) the api turns them into `RealtimeEvents.EXPORT_UPDATED` (`{ exportId }`, to the tabs of the requester only) and into `NotificationTypes.EXPORT_READY` / the new `EXPORT_FAILED` (`NotificationsEventsListener`), which reads the row, checks it is in the state of the event, belongs to that requester in that store, and that the requester is still an active member whose role makes that kind (`ExportTypePermissions`, moved from `ExportsService` to `libs/core/domain/exports` so the listener and the service share one rule); (3) the `actionPath` is the center, `AppPaths.store.exports`, so `AppPaths.store.exportDownload` was removed instead of given a page; (4) the web drops `EXPORT_POLL_MS` and `hasPendingExport`: `EXPORT_UPDATED` and a reconnect invalidate `QueryKeys.exports.all`. **Left on purpose (review of PR B2a):** settling the row and announcing it are two steps without an outbox, so a worker killed between them loses the notification and the live refresh; the page no longer polls, so such a row shows `pending` until a reload or a socket reconnect (owner: Step 7.3, with the purge of old export rows; the `pending` give-up of the cleaner settles it within 24 h); the texts of `notifications.export.failed` were added to both message files next to `ready`.
- [x] **"Export CSV" of a whole filter (debt of PR E3c of Step 3.4):** the bulk action by filter stops at `BulkLimits.MAX_ITEMS` (500) and the toast says the file was cut. This step already reworks the exports flow (the `EXPORT_READY` event), so it also lets the bulk bar export every conversation of a filter: the export row keeps the filter instead of ids (the plain `GET /conversations/export` already works so), and the "select all matching" text stops promising 500 for this one action. Close it with a case that exports more than 500. **Done in PR F2 of Step 3.5:** `POST /conversations/bulk` with `export_csv` and a `filter` no longer resolves ids: `ConversationBulkService.exportFilter` counts the matches (`ConversationsService.countMatching`, the rule of `idsMatching`, which also refuses the archived history to who may not archive) and writes the row with the filter and the frozen viewer, as `GET /conversations/export` does. The answer is `{ succeeded: [], failed: [], truncated: false, exported: <n> }` (`BulkResult.exported`, optional in `bulkResultSchema`); a filter that matches nothing writes no row (`exported: 0`). A selection by ticked ids is unchanged. The web counts `exported` in `summaryOf`, the toast no longer says the file was cut (`exportTruncated` is gone) and the texts of "select all matching" say that Export CSV is the one action without the limit. **Found by the 501-conversation case and fixed here:** `conversationsExportSource` paged with `id > last` and also with `cursor` + `skip: 1`, which dropped the first conversation of every page after the first (a file of more than 500 conversations lost one per page, also in `GET /conversations/export`); the keyset alone is correct. Cases: `exports every conversation of a filter, past the limit of the other bulk actions` and `writes no file when a filter matches nothing` in `tests/api/exports.spec.ts`, and one in `bulk.spec.ts` (web)
- [x] (PR B1 of Step 3.5) The mention notification (`NotificationTypes.CONVERSATION_MENTIONED`, from `Events.MESSAGE_MENTION`) goes only to who may open the conversation and read its notes; Step 2.3 already refuses a mention of anyone else, but a role may change after the note is written
- [x] Notification center UI rendering keys (PR D of Step 3.5): `NotificationCenter` in the top bar of the store area (bell, `99+` badge, a sheet with the tabs All / Unread / Mentions, "Mark all read", load more); the text is `messageKey + .title/.body` with `notifications.unknown` for a key the page does not have; a click marks the row read and goes to `actionPath` only if it is a path of the app (`safeActionPath`). **Decisions of PR D:** (1) a sheet and not a popover, to reuse the focus trap of the existing primitive; (2) "Mark all read" marks every notification of the store, whatever the tab, and is disabled with nothing unread; (3) the link "Notification settings" of `TDD_06 §8` arrives with the preferences page (PR E), since a link to a page that does not exist would be a 404. **Left on purpose (owner: PR F):** the relative time of an open panel does not tick; a message whose ICU params do not match the server's relies on next-intl's own error handling; a mark-read refetches the cached tabs, not only the active one. **Not run locally:** `notifications.spec.ts` (e2e) could not run on this machine, where the worker fails to render any email (`renderToPipeableStream is not a function`) even on a clean `main`; CI runs it
- [x] **Web Push, the server (PR C1 of Step 3.5; PR C2 below draws the browser side):** `NotificationsService.send` queues `SEND_WEB_PUSH` (one job per notification, `jobId` = name + notification id) when the type's `push` is on and the user is not in quiet hours; the worker's `NotificationsProcessor` hands it to `WebPushSender`, which writes the title and body in the person's locale from the `notifications.<type>` messages (`push-payload.ts`), sends to every registered browser through `WebPushGateway` and drops a subscription the push service answers 404 or 410 for. Routes `POST /notifications/push-subscription` and `POST /notifications/push-subscription/remove` (`@MemberOfStore()`, 204; a POST and not a DELETE with a body, which proxies drop). **Decisions of PR C1:** (1) the endpoint is a url the worker will call, so it is accepted only on the host of a known push service (`PushServiceHosts`, exact hosts and suffixes in `libs/core/domain/notifications/push-constants.ts`), never a host the caller names (SSRF); a browser on a push service that is not listed needs its host added there; (2) the keys of a registration must be base64url of 65 and 16 bytes (`isBase64UrlOfBytes`) and the VAPID pair of 65 and 32, so a bad key stops at the door and not as a retry storm; the wait for the queue is bounded by `PushLimits.ENQUEUE_TIMEOUT_MS`; `PushSubscription` belongs to the person and not to a store (no `storeId`, no RLS), so there is no `tenant-isolation.spec` case; the isolation of one person from another (a foreign endpoint is not removed, the same browser moves to the next person who registers it) is in `tests/api/notifications.spec.ts`; (3) at most `PushLimits.MAX_SUBSCRIPTIONS_PER_USER` browsers per person, the least recently used dropped; `send` queues no job for a person with no browser registered; each push has a `REQUEST_TIMEOUT_MS`; a refusal that will not pass (401, 403) is logged as an error; (4) a failure of the push service that may pass (429, 5xx, network) fails the job, which the queue retries 5 times; the retry repeats the push to the browsers that already got it, and the shared `tag` makes the device replace it instead of showing it twice; a failure that will not pass (400, 401, 403, 413) is logged and not retried; (5) a queue that is down loses the push and never the action that raised the notification (logged); (6) the keys travel with each call (`vapidDetails`), not through the global state of `web-push`, and `VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` / `VAPID_PRIVATE_KEY` are now required together (`env-schema`); without them the worker sends nothing and says nothing. **Left on purpose (owner: PR F):** registering is not one transaction (two registrations at once may leave one row over the cap until the next); an endpoint already registered by another person moves to the caller without proof (the endpoint is an unguessable url only that browser knows); a subscription the push service refuses for good (400, 401, 403, 413) is logged as an error and kept, with nothing that expires it (drop it after N failures, or by `lastUsed`); the VAPID keys are checked for format but not required in a deployed environment (decide with the deploy config of Step 8); each retry of a failed push repeats it to every browser (one job per browser would stop that); `send` reads the subscriptions once in the api and again in the worker; a push is sent to a person who was suspended in the store between the notification and the job (seconds, text of the notification only); nothing yet reads `lastUsed` to expire a subscription that is alive but never used
- [x] **Web Push, the browser (PR C2 of Step 3.5):** Serwist service worker (`apps/web/sw.ts`), the push and notification-click handlers only; the caching rules of `TDD_09 §16.3` and the manifest are Step 8.3, which adds them to the same file **Web image (left by PR E1):** `NEXT_PUBLIC_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` is read by `env-schema.ts` but is not a build argument of `apps/web/Dockerfile`, and Next.js inlines it at build time, so add it as an `ARG`/`ENV` there and to the `security-scan` leg of `web` in this task, or the image ships without push **Decisions of PR C2:** (1) `apps/web/sw.ts` is built by `@serwist/turbopack` (the route `app/serwist/[path]/route.ts`, served as `/serwist/sw.js` with `Service-Worker-Allowed: /`; Next 16 builds with Turbopack, which the webpack plugin of `@serwist/next` does not serve); `esbuild` is a pinned dev dependency because the route imports it by name; the worker imports nothing from the `serwist` runtime yet (Step 8.3 adds the caching), and `libs/core/domain/notifications/notification-constants.ts` now imports `../status-enums` instead of the barrel `../index`, which had put zod and all of the domain in the worker (455 KB, now 5 KB); (2) the worker is registered by the prompt, not on every page (`lib/push/push-browser.ts`), so no person pays for it until they say yes; `SerwistProvider` is not mounted (its `reloadOnOnline` and cache-on-navigation belong to Step 8.3); (3) `proxy.ts` leaves `/serwist/sw.js` (and its map) out of its matcher: a browser fetches the worker with no page around it and its update check must not be sent to the login; (4) the body of a push is validated before it is shown (`parsePushPayload`), and the click opens only a path of the origin (`targetPathOf`: `//host`, other origins and `javascript:` go to `/`); (5) the prompt (`usePushOffer`, toast with Turn on / Not now) is asked once per browser after a conversation is resolved (`StorageKeys.PUSH_OFFERED`), never when the permission is denied, and a browser that already allows it registers again silently on each resolve (another person may have used it); (6) signing out removes the push registration of this browser (`unsubscribeFromPush`, best effort, with the last store id since the route needs `X-Store-Id`), so a shared computer does not show the next notifications of the person who left; (7) `sw.ts` has its own program, `apps/web/tsconfig.sw.json` (webworker types, no dom), excluded from the one of the app: the `type-check` target of `web` runs both, and `eslint.config.js` points the parser at it for that one file, so worker globals never leak into the pages; (8) `NEXT_PUBLIC_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` is a build argument of `apps/web/Dockerfile` and of the `web` leg of `security-scan`, optional like its schema. **Left on purpose:** (a) the notification has no `icon` or `badge`: the files arrive with the manifest of Step 8.3 (owner: Step 8.3), the browser draws its own meanwhile; (b) there is no e2e of a real push (a headless browser has no push service): the handlers and the click are unit-tested, and the first real delivery is checked by hand with a VAPID pair (owner: PR F, which also closes the step); (c) a session lost by an expired refresh does not remove the registration (only the sign-out does); the server drops a dead one when the push service answers 410 (owner: PR F with the other subscription expiry debts); (d) the switch "notifications on this device", for a person who pressed Not now or turned it off, is drawn by the preferences page of the task below, with `subscribeToPush` and `unsubscribeFromPush`
- [x] **A file that cannot be scanned (debt of PR B2 of Step 3.4, done in PR B2b of Step 3.5):** `OrphanMediaCleaner` queues again, every hour, a `pending` row whose scan fails for good. Give the row a terminal state after a number of tries (a `MediaStatus` value, with its constants), stop requeuing it, and notify the team of the store (`MEDIA_INFECTED` is the model, `TDD_07 §6.5`) **Decisions of PR B2b:** (1) the number of tries is the attempts of the scan job (`isLastAttempt`): on the last one `MediaPipeline.scan` writes the new `MediaStatus.UNSCANNABLE` (a `status` string, so no migration and no rollback script), deletes the file and still throws, so the job ends in the dead-letter queue; the hourly cleanup only looks at `pending`, so it never queues the row again, and its infected-files pass also deletes the file of an `unscannable` row if the delete in the scan failed; (2) a failure that is the bucket's (`the file to scan is not in the bucket`) is not given up: the row stays `pending` and the cleanup drops it as before; (3) `Events.MEDIA_UNSCANNABLE` (bridged), `RealtimeEvents.MEDIA_UNSCANNABLE` (refreshes the thread), `NotificationTypes.MEDIA_UNSCANNABLE` (in-app only, no push: nothing is unsafe, the sender must send it again), `AuditAction.MEDIA_UNSCANNABLE`, all raised only for a file of a message, exactly as the infected one; (4) `GET /media/:id` and `POST /messages` answer `FILE_UNSCANNABLE` with 410, so the composer stops at once instead of waiting its minute (closes limit (5) of the composer task of Step 3.4), and the thread draws the file as discarded. **Left on purpose:** a daemon that is down for longer than the retries of the job turns every file uploaded in that window into `unscannable` (the member uploads them again); the retry policy is the queue's, to be revisited with the alerts of Step 7.3. **Review findings left on purpose (PR B2b, owner: PR F of this step, with the PR A and B1 findings):** (1) `report.infectedFilesDeleted` of the cleanup also counts the files of `unscannable` rows, and neither kind of row is ever removed: rename the counter and purge old rows; (2) the "tell each person once" check of `MEDIA_INFECTED` and `MEDIA_UNSCANNABLE` reads and then writes, so two concurrent deliveries could notify twice: use a unique key or an upsert; (3) the 410/403 status branch of a media is written out in `MediaService.urlsFor` and `MessagesService`: extract one `assertMediaServable(status)`. **Flaky on main, not caused by this PR (owner: its own fix PR, before Step 3.5 closes):** `media-pipeline.integration.spec` ("queues its variants once", "makes the three WebP variants") and sometimes the cleaner's "queues the scan again" fail with `job could not be removed because it is locked by another worker`, because the `WorkerModule` of the test runs the processor that takes the job before `job.remove()`; fix by pausing the queue or not registering the processor in those specs **Done in PR F3 of Step 3.5:** the flaky media specs now drop their queued job through `dropJob` (`apps/worker/src/testing/drop-job.ts`), which ignores only the `locked by another worker` error; it did not reproduce on this machine (3 runs green) and `compile()` starts no worker, so the cause is a worker of another process on the shared Redis, and the assertions stay strict. Finding (3), `assertMediaServable(status)` in `libs/core/messaging/src/messages/media-guard.ts` (not in the `errors` barrel, which the web bundles), replaces the two copies in `MediaService.urlsFor` and `MessagesService`
- [x] Preferences page (PR E of Step 3.5): `/account/notifications` (`AppPaths.account.notifications`), generated from the types the api returns and grouped by `NotificationGroupTypes` (`libs/core/domain/notifications`; the org-level data-request types are in no group and never appear): the matrix In-app / Push / Email / Sound of one store, the push switch of this browser (`usePushSwitch`; `denied` shows a text that says to allow it in the site settings), quiet hours (range, time zone, follow the store), the digest choice and the two cross-store switches; each change saves at once with an optimistic update.
