## Milestone 4 — Commerce

Inventory is robust from the first release (`TDD_16_Inventory.md`): warehouses that serve
several stores, a movement ledger, reservations, transfers between warehouses and physical
counts. Orders depend on it, so it is built first.

- [ ] **Before Milestone 4 starts (QA round 2, 2026-11-06, rule 12):** run the full round of the case matrix (416 cases, `qa-reportes-2026-11-05/matriz-casos.md`) with `/qa-team`. Round 2 covered what was built up to #190 and the connection UX; Milestone 4 adds orders and inventory on top, so the matrix is run complete first, and the P1/P2 it finds are fixed before the first Step of this milestone.

### Step 4.1 — Products and variants
- [x] Products + variants CRUD (store-scoped; `freeShipping` flag, `TDD_17 §4.13`; `trackInventory`; `lowStockThreshold` is optional, empty means no low-stock alert, `TDD_16 §4.4`). **Built in PR A (`apps/api/src/modules/products`):** `GET/POST /products` (cursor page, newest first, `?active=`), `GET/PATCH/DELETE /products/:id`, `POST /products/:id/variants`, `PATCH/DELETE /products/:id/variants/:variantId`. Reading asks for `products:view`, creating a product or a variant for `products:create`, changing either for `products:edit`, deleting either for `products:delete`. Migration `20261119100000_product_cost_and_optional_threshold` (with rollback): `cost_cents` on products and variants (a REFERENCE cost per unit in USD cents, null on a variant = the product's; the cost of a unit in stock is the one the movement carries, see Step 4.2), `low_stock_threshold` nullable with no default, a unique `(product_id, sku)` on variants, and CHECKs against negative amounts. Prices and costs are whole USD cents with a ceiling (`ProductLimits.MAX_AMOUNT_CENTS`). A product or variant that an order or a stock movement names (a product with files too) is not deleted (409 `has_history`): it is archived with `active: false`. The SKU of a product is unique in its store (index), the SKU of a variant in its whole store (checked under `lockStoreScope`), and a product SKU is never compared with a variant SKU. A change of price (product or variant) is audited with the old and the new value (`PRODUCT_PRICE_CHANGED`); the events `product.created|updated|deleted` are published (`ProductChangedPayload`) for the search cache of PR B. `imageMediaId` is read only here: it is written by the upload purpose `product` (PR C) and drawn by the catalog page (PR D)
- [x] Debts of PR A (rule 12): (a) **cost is readable by everyone who may view products:** `costCents` and `effectiveCostCents` come back on every read behind `products:view` (agents and viewers hold it); the owner is the only user of the MVP, but before a second kind of staff sees the catalog, cost needs its own permission (`PRODUCTS_VIEW_COST`, constants in `permissions.ts`, preset roles, the views dropping the field without it): Step 4.2, where cost becomes part of stock, or the finance sub-milestone, whichever comes first; (b) a product with files is not deleted (409 `has_history`, count `media`), decided and indexed in PR A (see the debt of PR C above); (c) the unique indexes of variants are guarded by the product row lock and the SKU advisory lock, not by catching `P2002`: a future writer that does not take `lockProduct` (an import, Step 5.x) must map the violation to the same 409 answers; (d) only price and cost changes are audited, not `sku` or `active`; (e) `order_items` has no index on `product_id`, so the history count of a delete (and the foreign key check) scans it while the product row is locked: add `@@index([productId])` and `@@index([variantId])` to `OrderItem` in the migration of Step 4.3, which builds the orders; (f) a file that is infected or unscannable does not keep a product from being deleted, and is left with no product (never served) **Owners written (PR D):** (a) Step 4.2 (cost permission, see its task); (b) closed by design (see PR C); (c), (d), (f) Step 7.7 (catalog hardening); (e) Step 4.3 (indexes of `order_items`).
- [x] Texts of the 409 answers of the catalog (for PR D, the web): the client reads `error.metadata.conflict` (`ProductConflicts`: `sku_in_use`, `variant_name_in_use`, `too_many_variants`, `has_history`) and `error.metadata.orderItems|inventoryMovements` for the count; each needs an i18n text in `es` and `en`, and `has_history` offers "archive" instead **Done in PR D:** `products.conflicts.*` in `es` and `en` (`ProductError` reads `metadata.conflict`; `has_history` offers "Archive product" with the counts of order lines, stock movements and pictures).
- [x] Product search with sub-200ms autocomplete. **Built in PR B:** `GET /products/search?q=&limit=&includeArchived=` (`products:view`, store of `TenantGuard`), by a part of the name or the sku of a product and of the name or the sku of its variants (`ILIKE`, case-insensitive), answering `{ data: [{ id, name, sku, priceCents, imageMediaId, active, matchedVariant }] }` (`matchedVariant` is the variant the text matched, with its own and its effective price, or null). Order: an exact sku, then names or skus that start with the text, then the rest, then by name. A product that matched by itself (its name or its sku) comes with `matchedVariant: null`; the variant is named only when it is the reason the product is in the answer, and among several the one whose sku is the text comes first. Archived products and variants are left out unless `includeArchived=true`. `ProductSearchConfig`: a text shorter than 2 characters (Unicode characters, after trimming) answers `200` with an empty list and never reaches the database, at most 100 characters (400 above), default 8 rows, ceiling 20 (400 above). The text is always a bind parameter and the wildcards of LIKE (`%`, `_`, `\`) are escaped, so `%`, `_` and `\` find nothing and `10%` finds only a name that contains `10%`. Migration `20261120100000_product_search_trigram_indexes` (with rollback) adds trigram GIN indexes on `products.sku` and on the name and the sku of the variants (the name of a product and `pg_trgm` came with the first migration; the rollback drops only the three indexes and never the extension). Measured over 5 000 products and 3 000 variants: the statement runs in 4 to 8 ms in Postgres and `tests/api/products-search-performance.spec.ts` asserts p95 < 200 ms through the whole api for nine kinds of text; **no cache** was needed (the cache key and the `product.*` invalidation exist in `cache-keys.ts` for the day a catalog is large enough to want it)
- [x] Debts of PR B (rule 12): (a) the search is not ranked by similarity or tolerant to typos (`pg_trgm` `similarity`, `SearchConfig.SIMILARITY_THRESHOLD` of `TDD_08 §5.2`) nor does it search the description (the `search_vector` column is there for it): add both if the owner asks for a fuzzier autocomplete, with the global search of Step 5.1, which also reads `search.products`; (b) at 5 000 products the planner reads the catalog of the store through `(store_id, active)` and filters; trigram indexes on `name` and `sku` take over as the catalog grows (checked with `EXPLAIN`, not forced); (c) the rank of the statement repeats a correlated `EXISTS` on the variants several times per candidate and compares `lower(sku)` with `ILIKE`: one lateral aggregate (`bool_or` of exact, prefix and contains) would compute it once and keep the two folds from ever disagreeing, if a catalog grows until the statement shows in a profile; (d) `escapeLike` lives in the customers support file of `core/messaging` and the `ESCAPE '\\'` of the statement assumes its character: move it to a neutral text module of `core/domain` with the escape character as a constant when a third search uses it; (e) the test of speed runs on the machine that runs the suite: its limit of 200 ms is the contract, not a benchmark of the hardware **Owners written (PR D):** (a) Step 5.1; (b), (c), (d), (e) Step 7.7 (catalog hardening).
- [x] **To design with the owner when this step starts (nothing is decided, and `TDD_09` has no product screen):** the catalog interface. The order drawer of Step 4.4 only picks variants, so someone has to create them: a products page (list, search, create and edit a product with its variants, images through Step 3.4, stock summary from Step 4.2). Decide its scope here, in this step, and add it as tasks before building
  - Decided by the owner (2026-11-10) and built in PR D: `/s/[storeId]/products`, a list with a panel on the side. Table (name, SKU, price, variants, state) with a filter (all, active, archived), a search box (from two characters it asks `GET /products/search` after a pause, and a product also answers for the name or SKU of a variant; with fewer it narrows the rows already loaded), "Load more", and "New product" (`products:create`). A click opens a sheet with three tabs: Details (every field of the product, archive/restore by `PATCH active`, delete that answers the 409 `has_history` with counts and offers to archive), Pictures (upload with the purpose `product`, the cover chosen among the product's own clean pictures) and Variants (an editable table: add, edit, delete). Actions are hidden by `products:view|create|edit|delete` (and `media:upload`, `media:view` for pictures). Money is typed and read as whole US cents, with no float (`lib/products/money.ts`). Two api additions made here because the screen cannot work without them: `GET /products/:id/images` and `imageMediaId` in `PATCH /products/:id` (the cover must be a live `product` file of the same product, answered 404 otherwise). The panel loads on demand; the page measures 277 KB of script in `lighthouse` (ceiling 300 KB, `ScriptCeilingsKb.PRODUCTS`). The top bar wraps its links on a phone now (five do not fit one line at 360 px).
- [x] **Upload purpose `product` (left by Step 3.4):** add it to `ClientUploadPurposes` together with the check that the `parentId` is a product of the store (`TDD_07 §6.3`); a purpose is never added before the code that checks who may attach it
  - Done in PR C: `product` is a purpose of `POST /media/upload-url` and `/media/confirm-upload` with `parentId` (a UUID, required for it and refused for `message`). The product must be one of the store of the header (a product of another store and one that does not exist answer the same 404); the member needs `PRODUCTS_EDIT` besides `MEDIA_UPLOAD` (checked on both routes, `PURPOSE_PERMISSIONS`); only jpeg, png and webp up to 5 MB (`ProductImageUpload`); the row keeps `productId`, and a retry that names another product is refused.
  - [x] **Debt, owner Step 4.1 PR A/D (closed in PR A):** `@@index([productId, storeId])` on `Media` (product first: the foreign key looks rows up by `product_id` alone) is in the migration `20261119100000_product_cost_and_optional_threshold` (with its rollback). `Media.product` is `onDelete: SetNull`; PR A decided that a product with files is NOT deleted (409 `has_history`, count `media`) and is archived instead, so no picture is ever left without its product. Removing a product's files together with it stays a possible later feature (the media service would delete them in the same transaction); it is not a debt while archiving is the way.
  - [x] **Debt, owner Step 4.1 PR D:** the web upload of a product picture (the `parentId` and the 5 MB / three-type rule in `apps/web/lib/media/attachment-rules.ts`) is drawn with the Images tab; `Product.imageMediaId` (the cover) is set by the product API, which must check that the media is a `product` file of the same product. **Closed in PR D:** the Pictures tab uploads with the purpose `product` (`refuseAttachment` with the rules of the purpose, then the signed url, the bucket and the confirmation); `imageMediaId` of the product api checks that the file is a live `product` file of the same product.

### Step 4.2 — Warehouses and stock
- [x] `Warehouse`, `StoreWarehouse`, `InventoryLevel`, `OrderItemAllocation` models, the changes to `InventoryMovement` and `StoreSettings`, and the raw-SQL constraints and partial uniques of `TDD_16 §2` (with rollback). **Built in PR A1** (migration `20261121100000_inventory_schema`, one additive migration with its rollback, tried twice on a copy; `StockTransfer` and `StockCount` come with Steps 4.5 and 4.6). Beyond `TDD_16 §2`, decided under rule 12: (a) **the tenancy is a rule of the database:** `store_warehouses` names the organization twice (composite foreign keys `(organization_id, store_id)` and `(organization_id, warehouse_id)`), so a store can never be linked to a warehouse of another organization; `inventory_levels` and `inventory_movements` reference `products(store_id, id)`, so stock can never name a product of another store; and one trigger (`check_stock_row_belongs_together`) on levels, movements and allocations refuses a warehouse that is not of the store's organization (a level is not a link: a store may keep stock in a warehouse it was unlinked from, `TDD_16 §4.7`), a variant that is not a variant of the row's product, and an allocation of an order line of another store; (b) **the ledger is append-only in the database:** `platform_app` and `platform_worker` have no UPDATE, DELETE or TRUNCATE on `inventory_movements` (as with `audit_logs`; the security suite checks the privileges, so a later `GRANT ... ON ALL TABLES` fails the build), and nothing deletes it by cascade: a store or a warehouse with stock cannot be deleted (`TDD_16 §4.9`, the foreign keys restrict it); the idempotency key is unique **inside a store** (`(store_id, idempotency_key)`), not across the platform, so tenants never share a namespace; warehouse codes are unique ignoring case (`uq_warehouses_code_lower`) and a code or a name has no stray spaces and keeps the lengths of `InventoryLimits`; (c) `InventoryLevel.valueCents` (BigInt, USD cents) holds what the units on hand are worth at their cost: the weighted average is `valueCents / onHand`, kept by `InventoryService` in the same transaction as the level (PR A2), and FIFO lots can later be built from the receipts (`unitCostCents` of the movement) with no change to either table; (d) variants and products are never deleted or turned into the product by a delete while stock refers to them (`ON DELETE RESTRICT`), and `ProductsService` answers 409 `has_history` with the count `inventoryLevels` for a product or variant that has a level, even at zero; (e) CHECKs: `reserved >= 0`, movement quantity `> 0`, cost and reserved-after `>= 0`, allocation quantity `> 0`, priority `>= 1`, default threshold `>= 0`; (f) the migration locks `inventory_movements` and REFUSES to run if it has rows (nothing wrote to it before this step), instead of inventing a warehouse for them; its rollback is destructive (it drops the stock tables), and says so
- [x] Check that the inventory constants born in Step 0.3 cover `TDD_16 §3`; add what is missing. **Done in PR A1:** every entry of the table of `TDD_16 §3` exists (permissions and role presets, events, audit actions, error codes, notification types, job, schedule, metric, routes, paths, query keys, scopes). Added: `PRODUCTS_VIEW_COST` (below), `InventoryMovementEffects` (what each type does to `onHand` and `reserved`, read by the engine, the kardex and the reconciliation), `InventoryMovementsThatBringUnitsIn` (the types that carry a unit cost), `InventoryManualMovements`, `InventoryLimits`, `DefaultWarehouse`, `FIRST_WAREHOUSE_PRIORITY`, `ProductHistoryTables.INVENTORY_LEVELS`
- [x] **Unit cost on every stock movement (finance requirements of 2026-11-10, `project_finance_module_requirements`):** `InventoryMovement` gets `unitCostCents` (whole USD cents, null when the movement has no cost, e.g. a reserve), written by receipts, returns and opening stock, so that a weighted-average cost is computed from movements now and FIFO lots can be added later without migrating data. **The column and `InventoryLevel.valueCents` are in PR A1; writing and using them is `InventoryService` (PR A2).** `Product.costCents` / `ProductVariant.costCents` (Step 4.1) are only the reference cost that prefills a receipt; they never override the cost a movement carries **Closed in PR A2** (the service writes and averages the cost, and refuses a cost-less receipt where it is needed).
- [x] `InventoryService`: transactional, idempotent movements (row lock, `onHandAfter`/`reservedAfter`), reserve/release/issue/restock, manual adjustments with mandatory reason, low-stock alert, kardex. **Built in PR A2** (`libs/core/inventory`, `@platform/core/inventory`; routes in `apps/api/src/modules/inventory`): `move`, `moveMany` (sorted by warehouse, product, variant so two orders never deadlock) and `reserve`/`release`/`issue`/`restock`, each taking the caller's transaction when it has one. The pure rules of a movement are in `movement-math.ts` (coverage threshold 90%). A key is serialized per store (advisory lock), the level row is created and locked (`FOR UPDATE`), the rules run, level and ledger change together and `inventory.moved` leaves after the commit; the same key with the same request answers the original (`replayed`), with another request it is 409 `idempotency_key_reused`. Rules decided here (rule 12): a movement that **adds** stock or **reserves** needs a warehouse that is linked to the store and on (`INVENTORY_WAREHOUSE_NOT_LINKED`, `INVENTORY_WAREHOUSE_INACTIVE`), one that takes stock out does not (what is stranded in an unlinked warehouse can still be written off); a product with `trackInventory` off is `INVENTORY_PRODUCT_NOT_TRACKED` for what adds stock or promises it, while what it still holds can be released, shipped or written off; release or issue beyond what is reserved is `INVENTORY_RESERVATION_EXCEEDED` even with negative stock on; `TRANSFER_OUT` always needs real available stock; a receipt with a cost fixes it, units that leave take the average of the level and record it (`valueCents / onHand`, rounded half up, the last unit empties the value), units that come in with no cost take the current average, and a deficit is covered before any unit carries value. **Routes** (`GET /inventory/levels` with `warehouseId`, `productId`, `variantId` and `lowStock`; `GET /inventory/movements`; `GET /inventory/kardex` for one item in one warehouse; `GET /products/:id/inventory`; `POST /inventory/adjustments`, 201 and 200 for a retry): `inventory:view` reads, `inventory:adjust` writes, the cost fields come only with `products:view_cost`, every adjustment is audited once (`INVENTORY_ADJUSTED`)
- [x] Warehouses CRUD and store links with priority (organization-scoped, `ORG_MANAGE_WAREHOUSES`). **Built in PR B** (`apps/api/src/modules/warehouses`): `GET/POST /warehouses`, `GET/PATCH /warehouses/:id`, `PUT /warehouses/:id/stores`. The organization is the one `TenantGuard` resolved from `X-Store-Id` (never the body), reading asks `inventory:view`, writing `org:manage_warehouses`. Decided under rule 12: (a) **no DELETE route** (`TDD_16 §6` lists none): a warehouse is switched off with `PATCH {active:false}`, refused with `INVENTORY_WAREHOUSE_HAS_STOCK` (`metadata.reason = deactivate`) while any level of any store of the organization has units on hand, reserved or a negative balance (the TDD says `onHand > 0`; reserved and negative are held to the same rule because a warehouse that still owes or owns units is not empty), and its stock is read, never written, by this module; (b) the code is ASCII `[A-Za-z0-9][A-Za-z0-9._-]{0,19}`, kept as typed, unique per organization ignoring letter case (`409 RESOURCE_ALREADY_EXISTS`, `metadata.conflict = code_in_use`), also under two requests at once (the unique index of the migration decides); (c) **`PUT /warehouses/:id/stores` takes the complete list** `{ stores: [{ storeId, priority? }] }`: a store left out is unlinked (refused, with nothing changed, when that store has units reserved there, `metadata.reason = unlink`, `TDD_16 §4.7`), a priority puts the warehouse at that place of the store's list and shifts the others, one without keeps its place (a new link goes to the end), and every touched store is renumbered `1..n` in the same transaction (the unique `(store, priority)` is DEFERRABLE), so a reorder is one call and two requests on the same store take turns (advisory lock per store, taken in sorted order); a store of another organization, or one that does not exist, is the same 404 and writes nothing; a switched-off warehouse takes no new link (`409`, `conflict = inactive`) but may lose them; (d) an organization holds at most `WarehouseLimits.MAX_PER_ORGANIZATION` (200) warehouses, checked under an organization lock; (e) every list is a cursor page oldest first, and every change is audited (`WAREHOUSE_CREATED`, `WAREHOUSE_UPDATED` with the fields and the before/after of name, code and active, or `change: 'stores'` with what was added, removed and the final order)
- [x] **Debts of PR D1 (rule 12):** the Settings area weighs 333 KB of script on its own (the page of Settings → Inventory measured 333.34 KB; its own chunks are about 7 KB, the rest is the shell and the settings navigation with its dialog), so its Lighthouse ceiling is `ScriptCeilingsKb.INVENTORY_SETTINGS` = 350; split the shared weight with the other ceilings (**Step 7.7**)
- [x] **For `InventoryService` (PR A2) and orders (Step 4.3), the rules the warehouse routes rely on (rule 12, found in PR B):** (a) a movement that puts units into a warehouse (`RECEIPT`, `ADJUSTMENT_IN`, `TRANSFER_IN`, `COUNT_IN`, `RETURN_IN`) and a reservation must take the warehouse row `FOR SHARE` in its transaction and refuse an inactive warehouse, because the deactivation locks it `FOR UPDATE` and then reads the levels: without it a receipt can slip in between that read and the commit; (b) a reservation must also take the `store_warehouses` row of its (store, warehouse) `FOR SHARE` and refuse when there is none (`INVENTORY_WAREHOUSE_NOT_LINKED`), because an unlink deletes the row first and then reads `reserved`; (c) the allocation of an order skips inactive warehouses (Step 4.3) **Done in PR A2 for `InventoryService`:** a movement that adds units or reserves takes the product row, the warehouse row and the link of the store `FOR KEY SHARE` (it keeps out the `FOR UPDATE` of a deactivation and the `DELETE` of an unlinking, but not a rename or a reorder) before it takes the level `FOR UPDATE`, and refuses an inactive or unlinked warehouse; what takes stock out reads them with no lock, so stock stranded in a warehouse that is off or unlinked can still leave it. `tests/api/inventory-engine.spec.ts` holds the races with a lock taken by hand (the competing transaction says when it holds it), in both directions; the cases against the real deactivation and unlinking code of the warehouses module (the routes, racing a receipt and a reservation of the real engine, twelve rounds each) are in `tests/api/warehouses.spec.ts`, added by PR C. The orders (Step 4.3) still own: allocating by priority skipping inactive warehouses
- [x] **Debts of PR B (rule 12), each with its owner:** (a) `Events.WAREHOUSE_CREATED` and `WAREHOUSE_UPDATED` exist but are not published: they have no payload type and nothing listens; add the payload (`organizationId`, `warehouseId`) to `DomainEventPayloads` and publish them in the same transaction when the organization screen needs live updates (**Step 4.7**); (b) `org:manage_warehouses` is held per store, and a member who holds it in one store may link or unlink the warehouses of the other stores of the organization and read their names (`TDD_16 §1`); accepted while the owner is the only user of the MVP, and a per-store restriction of the links is a hardening task (**Step 7.7**); (c) the warehouse list answers every store name of its links to any member who may view inventory, for the same reason (**Step 7.7**); (d) test debts of the review: `isolation-detects-leaks.spec.ts` has no case for the `scope: 'organization'` branches of `tests/helpers/isolation.ts`, and the `untouched` of `storeWarehouse` compares a count, not the (store, priority) pair (**Step 7.7**)
- [x] Extend what earlier steps built: the owner bootstrap also creates a default warehouse linked to the first store (idempotent), the dev fixtures create warehouses and opening stock, `seedTestContext` creates warehouses and links, and product creation takes `StoreSettings.defaultLowStockThreshold`. **Done in PR A1:** `ensureDefaultWarehouse` (bootstrap: it makes the default warehouse only when the organization has none and links a store only when it has no link, so it never undoes what the owner renamed, recoded, unlinked or reordered), two warehouses and 30 levels with their opening-stock movements and cost in the fixtures (run twice: no change), `seedTestContext` gives `warehouseA` (shared by stores A and A2), `warehouseASecond` (store A, priority 2) and `warehouseB`, `cleanUp` removes the stock a run wrote, and a product with no `lowStockThreshold` in its body starts with the default of its store (`null` in the body means no alert on purpose)
- [x] `StoreSettings.allowNegativeStock` and `allowSplitFulfillment` (both default off) and `defaultLowStockThreshold` (empty by default), honored by the rules of `TDD_16 §4.10`, `§4.1` and `§4.4`, with their Settings → Inventory controls **PR B built the switches and their routes:** `GET /settings/inventory` (`settings:view`) and `PATCH /settings/inventory` (`settings:edit`, partial, strict, the threshold a whole number from 0 to `ProductLimits.MAX_LOW_STOCK_THRESHOLD` or `null`), audited as `SETTINGS_CHANGED` with the before and after of each switch that changed (none for a request that changes nothing), serialized per store. **PR D1 built the controls** (`/s/[storeId]/settings/inventory`: the two switches with what each one does, a warning when negative stock is turned on, the default threshold as an optional whole number, read with `settings:view` and changed with `settings:edit`, only the changed keys sent, es/en, e2e in `apps/web-e2e/specs/inventory-settings.spec.ts`). **Still to do:** the rules that honor the switches (`InventoryService` in PR A2 and the orders of Step 4.3)
- [x] `RECONCILE_INVENTORY` job and metric. **Built in PR C** (`apps/worker/src/inventory`, `schedulers/inventory-reconcile.scheduler.ts`): `InventoryReconcileScheduler` runs daily at 04:00 UTC under the `LeaderLockService` lock and queues one `RECONCILE_INVENTORY` job for each store that has a level or a ledger (stores read in pages by id), with the job id `reconcileInventoryJobId(storeId, UTC day)` (`reconcile-inventory-<store>-<day>`, so a second run the same day is the same job; BullMQ keeps a completed job 24 hours); `HeavyProcessingProcessor` reads the job with a strict `exactly<ReconcileInventoryJob>()` schema (an `UnrecoverableError` on a bad shape) and hands the store to `InventoryReconciler`, which is **read only**: it pages the levels of the store by id (`InventoryLimits.RECONCILE_PAGE_SIZE`) and calls `InventoryService.checkLevel` for each, then pages the items that have a ledger and no level (a deleted level is the same disagreement), always with the `storeId` of the job. A mismatch of on hand, reserved or value increments `INVENTORY_RECONCILIATION_MISMATCHES{store_id}` and logs a Pino warning with both values (`actual`, `expected`, `mismatch: true`); a negative level in a store with `allowNegativeStock` off is reported the same way with its own field (`negativeNotAllowed: true`, per `TDD_16 §4.8`) and one level makes one count and one line whatever it is guilty of. It never writes a level or a movement. `InventoryModule` is registered in the worker (`InventoryReconcileModule`, imported by the module that holds the heavy processor). Covered by `tests/api/inventory-reconcile.spec.ts` (forged on hand, reserved and value reported and never fixed, deleted level, variant, pages, negative level in both settings, two stores, the schedule) and `tests/api/heavy-processing-processor.spec.ts`; mutation checked: without the `storeId` filter the tenancy cases fail

- [x] Isolation cases for `Warehouse`, `StoreWarehouse`, `InventoryLevel`, `InventoryMovement`, `OrderItemAllocation` (`TDD_16 §8`). **PR B added `Warehouse` and `StoreWarehouse`** (both with `scope: 'organization'` in the suite: another organization reaches neither, a sibling store of the same organization does; plus a block of cross-organization cases through the api for the links and the settings) and removed their exemptions. **PR A1 covers what has no route yet, at the database** (`tests/security/inventory-schema.spec.ts`: no link across organizations, no stock for a product of another store, uniques, CHECKs, the append-only ledger, the permission to see costs); the cases through the api need the routes: `InventoryLevel` and `InventoryMovement` with PR A2 (levels, movements, kardex), `Warehouse` and `StoreWarehouse` with PR B (cross-organization case, and the case that a user sees only the lines of their stores for the transfers and counts of 4.5 and 4.6), `OrderItemAllocation` with Step 4.3 (it is exempt until the orders exist); each of those PRs removes its exemption in `scripts/isolation-exemptions.ts` when it adds its case, and the check fails at the end of the step if one is left **`InventoryLevel` and `InventoryMovement` are done in PR A2** (their exemptions are gone: the cases of the matrix read stock through the product and the kardex, and `describe('stock adjustments')` checks that the one write route never takes the store from the body, names only a product and a warehouse of the store and its organization, and keeps the idempotency keys of a store to itself).
- [x] **For PR C (the reconciliation job and the `inventory.low` notification), decided in PR A2 and built in PR C:** (1) the low-stock state "already warned / re-armed" has **no column**: it is derived from the crossing inside the movement itself: after a movement that lowers availability, `InventoryService` compares the available stock of the product (all items, all warehouses of the store) before and after, and warns only when it goes from above the threshold to at or under it; going back above re-arms it because the next crossing finds the stock above again; (2) `Events.INVENTORY_LOW` is published after the commit of the transaction (so an order that rolls back warns nobody) with `InventoryLowPayload { storeId, productId, available, threshold }`, registered in `DomainEventPayloads` (it is per product: the threshold is a product's, so there is no warehouse or variant in it); `Events.INVENTORY_MOVED` carries `{ storeId, productId, variantId, warehouseId, type, quantity }`; the notification (members with `inventory:view`, `messageKey` texts in `es` and `en`, `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter` entry removed) is PR C's; (3) the reconciliation reuses `InventoryService.checkLevel({ storeId, productId, variantId?, warehouseId }, tx?)` (it reads the level and its ledger in the order of `seq` and answers `{ actual, expected, matches }` for `onHand`, `reserved` and `valueCents`) and the pure `replayLedger(rows)` of `libs/core/inventory/src/movement-math.ts`; a mismatch is reported, never fixed; the job lists the levels of a store itself (`inventory_levels` by `store_id`), and also reports a negative `onHand` in a store with `allowNegativeStock` off (a distinct log field, `TDD_16 §4.8`); (4) no SQL helper of the ledger is exported: the engine's raw SQL (creating and locking a level, the product, warehouse and link locks) is private to `InventoryService`, and the lint ban keeps everything else out; the worker must register `InventoryModule` from `@platform/core/inventory`
- [x] Debts of PR D3, the stock in the catalog (rule 12): (a) the stock column and tab read what the api has now; the stock screen of Step 4.7 (`/s/[storeId]/inventory`: all the levels with filters, the low-stock list and the bulk edit of the alert threshold) was built in Step 4.7 PR E (with the sort of the levels by product name); (b) the Stock tab of a new, unsaved product does not exist (the sheet asks to save first, as for pictures and variants); (c) the adjustment cannot move units between warehouses or reserve them: transfers are Step 4.5; (d) the kardex is one item in one warehouse at a time (the api's shape), with no filter by type or date; (e) `fetchWarehousesOfStore` reads the first 100 warehouses of the organization: a bigger list is for the organization pages of Step 4.7; (f) the stock summary asks the api again after every adjustment of any product of the store (one key for all the inventory of the store): fine at this size, narrow it if the list gets slow
- [x] Debts of PR A2 (rule 12): (a) an item with variants must name one when stock is added or promised (`INVENTORY_VARIANT_REQUIRED`), but stock already on the bare row can still leave it, and an inactive variant is accepted: the orders (Step 4.3) decide whether to refuse it; (b) the engine writes `RETURN_IN`, `TRANSFER_*` and `COUNT_*` movements already but no route reaches them until Steps 4.3, 4.5 and 4.6, which pass deterministic keys (`order:{orderId}:{lineId}:reserve`, `transfer:{id}:{lineId}:dispatch`, `count:{id}:{lineId}:apply`); (c) the levels list is newest change first, the order the stock screen of Step 4.7 will want is by product name: add the sort there; (d) the `lowStock` filter looks at the first 5 000 products at or under their threshold, by id (a subquery of the levels query would have no cap); the ledger is read by `InventoryMovement.seq` (migration `20261122100000_inventory_movement_seq`, a `BIGSERIAL` written under the lock of the level) because every movement of a transaction shares a `created_at`; (e) `InventoryModule` was registered in the api only: **PR C registers it in the worker** with the reconciliation job and turns `inventory.low` into the notification; (f) the engine counts a product's low-stock threshold over all its items and warehouses, as `TDD_16 §4.4` says; a threshold per item is not a feature; (g) **the value of a transfer is conserved to the cent only when the average cost of the source is whole cents**: the units that leave take `round(value * q / onHand)` and come in at the whole-cent unit cost of that movement, so the total value can drift by under one cent per unit moved; the exact fix is a `totalCostCents` on the engine, to be made with the costing of the finance module (`TDD_18`, after Step 4.13); (h) switching `trackInventory` off is refused (`conflict: in_transit`) while units of the product are in a transfer in transit; (i) review notes left out on purpose (hardening, no defect): the coverage threshold of `libs/core/inventory` is global to its two pure files, not per file; `transfer-rules.spec.ts` does not pin the order of the refusals of `planReceipt` when two mistakes meet; `tests/api/transfers.spec.ts` keeps a local `waitFor` like two other specs (a shared helper is for the next spec that needs it); each mutation re-reads the memberships of the caller once more for its answer; (j) `libs/core/inventory` has a coverage threshold on the pure rules (`movement-math.ts`) only; the service is covered by `tests/api/inventory-engine.spec.ts` against the real database (the `TDD_13` 90% of the whole lib is measured by the unit job, which has no database)
- [x] Debts of PR C (rule 12), each with its owner: (a) the text of `inventory.low` is still the generic one ("A product is running out of stock"): the notification carries `params { product, available, threshold }` and `resourceId` = the product, but the page, the push and the e-mail digest draw the body from `bodyVariantOf` (conversation-shaped variants), so a body with the product name needs a new variant read by those three renderers, with its words in `es` and `en` (done in Step 4.7 PR E: `bodyWithProduct`); (b) its `actionPath` was the products page: it is `AppPaths.store.inventoryLow` since Step 4.7 PR E; (c) a low-stock event raised by the **worker** would not reach the api listener: `Events.INVENTORY_LOW` is not in `BridgedEvents` (nothing raises it from the worker today, the orders of Step 4.3 run in the api); add it with its bridged payload schema if a job ever moves stock; (d) the mismatch counter has no alert rule and no dashboard: Step 7.4 (observability) must page on `platform_inventory_reconciliation_mismatches_total > 0` per store; a negative level that the store lived with before switching `allowNegativeStock` off is counted every day until a count or an adjustment lifts it (as `TDD_16 §4.8` asks); (e) the reconciler checks a level in its own transaction (`checkLevel` holds the level `FOR SHARE` while it reads its ledger, so a movement on that one level waits a few milliseconds) and reads the ledger of one level whole: fine for the stores of the MVP, a store with a very long ledger per level needs a checkpointed balance (**Step 7.3**); (f) the ledger-only pass pages with `skip` over a `GROUP BY` (quadratic on a store with tens of thousands of items): move to keyset paging by `(product, variant, warehouse)` if a store grows there (**Step 7.3**); (h) the reconciliation jobs share the `heavy-processing` queue (concurrency 2) with the scans and image variants, as `TDD_16 §4.8` places them: a store with a very long ledger could hold both slots for a while at 04:00; give the reconciliation its own limiter or queue if it is ever seen to delay a scan (**Step 7.3**); the processor of that queue lives in the media module, so the worker module that holds it imports the inventory one, a coupling to undo then; (i) an item that cannot be read (a lock timeout) is logged and counted in `report.failed`, and the job still ends successfully: the next day checks it again, and Step 7.4 should alert on the error line; (g) the daily job id is deduplicated by BullMQ itself and proven by the id the scheduler asks for, not by a run against a real queue.

**TDD:** `TDD_16 §1`–`§6`, `§8`, `§9`
**Done when:** two parallel reservations for the last unit leave exactly one success, a movement
retried with the same idempotency key applies once, stock never goes negative while
`allowNegativeStock` is off and orders are accepted beyond stock while it is on (a transfer
dispatch is still refused), a forged level mismatch is reported by the reconciliation job, and
the isolation suite covers the new resources.

### Step 4.3 — Orders

The web ignores an entry type it does not know (`customerTimelinePageSchema` drops it), so nothing draws them until Step 4.4, and until then a page made only of orders shows empty while "load more" still works: **Step 4.4 adds the `order` variant to `customerTimelineEntrySchema` (`libs/api-client`) and draws it in the same PR.** Neither the list nor the timeline returns `conversationId`: Step 4.4 adds a link to the conversation only after checking `visibleTo` for the viewer.

- [x] Scheduled delivery (decision D18): `Order.scheduledDeliveryDate` (`DATE`, nullable) in the migration with its rollback script, accepted by `createOrderSchema` and editable per `OrderEditableFields`; `GET /orders` filters `scheduledDeliveryFrom` and `scheduledDeliveryTo` (inclusive days) and the shortcuts `scheduledDeliveryOn=today|tomorrow`, resolved on the server with the store timezone, plus sorting by the date ascending; the filter names, the shortcut values and the audit action for a date change (old and new value) come from `libs/core/*`; an isolation case for the filter

Fill them from the orders of the customer in one grouped read per page, as the conversation dates are, and cover them in `tests/api/exports.spec.ts` Done: one `order.groupBy` per page of customers, the figures from `customerOrderFigures` (`libs/core/domain/order-thread-constants.ts`), `total_spent` and `average_order` in integer cents; covered in `tests/api/exports.spec.ts`.
- [x] System messages in the thread (`ORDER_SUMMARY`); the customer messages and their email fallback are Step 4.9 (`TDD_17 §4.7`), which replaces the order emails and the unconditional enqueue of `TDD_01 §6` **PR C built the writer, not the calls:** `writeOrderSummaryLine(tx, storeId, conversationId, line)` (`libs/core/messaging/src/orders/order-summary-line.ts`) writes a `MessageType.ORDER_SUMMARY` message by the system with `content: null` and a strict metadata (`event` created or status_changed, order id and number, status and previous status, total in cents, currency, item count, actor), never a note, address or phone; it does not move `lastMessageAt` or `unreadCount`, and writes nothing for a conversation of another store. **The calls are in PR B:** `OrdersService` writes the line in its own transaction when it creates an order and `OrdersStatusService` on every change of status, whenever `Order.conversationId` is set. `MessageView` does not expose the metadata yet: Step 4.4 adds `MessageView.orderSummary` (re-validated with `orderSummaryLineSchema`) and draws the card; until then the thread draws the neutral line "Order".
- [x] **Upload purpose `order` (left by Step 3.4; done in PR C):** add it to `ClientUploadPurposes` (`libs/core/domain/storage/upload-rules.ts` and `upload-schemas.ts`) together with the check that the `parentId` is an order of the store (`TDD_07 §6.3`); a purpose is never added before the code that checks who may attach it Done: `OrderFileUpload` (jpeg, png, webp or PDF, 10 MB), the parent is an order of the store (the same 404 for a foreign one and a missing one, checked again at the confirmation), and who attaches needs `ORDERS_VIEW` and `ORDERS_EDIT` or `ORDERS_CHANGE_STATUS` besides `MEDIA_UPLOAD` (without the view, the answer for an order id would tell more than reading the order does). Reading the signed link of such a file needs `ORDERS_VIEW` besides `MEDIA_VIEW` and is never given to the widget (`refuseUnlessMayRead`, a table over every `MediaPurpose` so a new purpose cannot be served by default). `Media.orderId` is set; linking the file as `proofOfDeliveryMediaId` / `paymentProofMediaId` is the order service's (PR B), which must check the media is a clean file of purpose `order` of the same order.
- [x] **Show the notification switches of this step (QA fix PR Q7):** this step raises `order.created`, `order.status_changed`, `order.price_approval_requested`, `order.price_overridden` (`NotificationService.notify`, with its `messageKey` texts in `es` and `en`); the preferences page hides the switch of a type nothing raises, so remove `NotificationTypes.ORDER_CREATED`, `NotificationTypes.ORDER_STATUS_CHANGED`, `NotificationTypes.ORDER_PRICE_APPROVAL_REQUESTED`, `NotificationTypes.ORDER_PRICE_OVERRIDDEN` from `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter` (`libs/core/domain/notifications/notification-constants.ts`) in the same PR, and add the case to the unit spec of that constant **Status after PR C:** PR C did NOT touch this (PR B edits the same two files, `notification-constants.ts` and `notifications-events.listener.ts`, and was not merged yet). **Owner: PR B**, which already publishes `Events.ORDER_CREATED`, `ORDER_STATUS_CHANGED`, `ORDER_PRICE_APPROVAL_REQUESTED` and `ORDER_PRICE_OVERRIDDEN`: it adds their listeners with the `es` and `en` texts, removes the four types from `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter` and adds them to the unit spec. If B closes without them, the last PR of the step does, and the step is not closed before. **Done in PR B:** the four listeners are in `notifications-events.listener.ts`: `order.created` goes to who holds `ORDERS_CONFIRM` (only while the order waits for its confirmation), `order.status_changed` to the member who made the order (unless they changed it), `order.price_approval_requested` and `order.price_overridden` to who holds `ORDERS_APPROVE_PRICE` (the second only while `orders.notifyOnPriceOverride` is on, read by `readOrderPreferences`), none of them to the actor; the four types left `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter`.
- [x] **"Replied to your story" card (2026-11-09, `feat/story-reply-card`):** the Instagram plugin writes `Message.metadata.storyReply = { url? }` (`MessageMetadataKeys.STORY_REPLY`) for any message whose `reply_to.story` is present, beside the text, the photo or the share it brings (before: a `story_reply` shared link, only when nothing else came). `MessageView.storyReply: { url: string | null } | null` (link re-checked on the way out, null once deleted) and the thread draws `StoryReplyCard` (label "Replied to your story", the thumbnail through `RemoteImage`, and a notice when the link did not come or no longer loads). The core and the web are channel agnostic: they read the view, not the channel. CSP `img-src` gains the exact host `https://lookaside.fbsbx.com` (`MetaStoryImageHosts` in `libs/core/domain/meta-constants.ts`, one list for the CSP and for the plugin; never `*.fbsbx.com`). The plugin keeps a story link only when it is https, with no credentials or port, on EXACTLY one of those hosts (`libs/channels/meta/src/story-reply.ts`); any other link is dropped and the card shows its notice. The web client also keeps only `https://` links. The thumbnail is drawn with `referrerPolicy="no-referrer"`, never as a navigation, and the link is not logged (the unsupported-shape log keeps field names only). A photo that answers a story is no longer merged into an image run or drawn without a bubble. Source (Meta docs read 2026-11-09): Instagram webhook `messages` documents `reply_to.story { url, id }` ("Included when a customer replies to a story"); the Messenger `messages` webhook documents `reply_to` with only `mid` (and `is_self_reply`), so Messenger is NOT built; the WhatsApp `messages` webhook documents `context` for a reply to a message, a "Message business" button, a product and a forward, and says nothing of a reply to a status, so WhatsApp is NOT built. Proved by `meta-messaging-events.spec.ts`, `messages.views.spec.ts` and `apps/web-e2e/specs/story-reply.spec.ts`.
- [ ] Debts of the story reply card (rule 12): (a) the thumbnail is hot-linked by the browser and dies with the link in about a day: keep a copy on the server (a media row with its own purpose, scanned, signed like the picture of an ad: `FETCH_ORIGIN_MEDIA` is the model) so the card survives the expiry (Step 7.3, with the Meta findings); (b) the host `lookaside.fbsbx.com` is the one the existing fixtures and Meta's examples use: confirm it, and the shape of `reply_to.story`, with a real story reply of the owner's Instagram account (Step 7.3); if the real host differs the card shows its notice and the CSP host must change; (c) Messenger and WhatsApp replies to a story/status: build them if a real payload shows one (Step 7.3); (d) a `story_mention` (the customer tags the business in their own story) stays the shared link it was; (e) the story replies stored before this PR (a `story_reply` shared link in `attachmentUrl`) are not migrated: they keep drawing the old link card (nobody uses the app yet, owner decision of 2026-11-08); (f) the CSP host is app-wide (`img-src` cannot be scoped to a component) and `lookaside.fbsbx.com` also serves content that users upload to Meta messaging: accepted for images only, and it goes away with (a) when the copy is served from our bucket.
- [x] **The gitleaks download retries (2026-11-09, `ci/gitleaks-download-retry`; owner authorized):** the release download of `security-scan (api)` answered 504 six times in a day and failed the leg each time. `curl` now retries 5 times, 5 seconds apart, on any error (`--retry 5 --retry-delay 5 --retry-all-errors`), and the SHA256 check, the pinned version and the scan itself are unchanged. Each attempt is capped (`--connect-timeout 15 --max-time 60 --retry-max-time 150`). A 404 (a wrong version) is retried too: `--retry-all-errors` was the owner's choice, and costs 25 s before it fails.
- [ ] Debt of the above (rule 12, Step 7.1): if the release host stays down longer than about 25 seconds the `security-scan (api)` leg still fails; the next step is a cached copy of the release verified by the same SHA256 (`actions/cache`), never a skipped scan.
- [x] **`order.payment_failed` and the payments of v1.2 (owner, 2026-11-10, closes the QA backlog line found in Q7):** v1.2 records the payment of an order **by hand**, with no online gateway. `PaymentMethod` (`CASH`, `TRANSFER`, `CARD`, `CASH_ON_DELIVERY`, `OTHER`), `PaymentStatus` and `PaymentStatusTransitions` exist; PR A adds `recordOrderPaymentSchema` (method, status, an optional reference, the receipt as a file of purpose `order`), the rule `checkPaymentChange` and the columns `Order.paymentReference` and `Order.paymentProofMediaId`. `CASH_ON_DELIVERY` is how the order was sold, not how the money came: the customer pays the carrier, who remits to the store (Step 4.13), so by hand a COD payment is `COMPLETED` only once the order is `DELIVERED`; a transfer has no such wait. The route that records a payment and its audit (`ORDER_PAYMENT_RECORDED`, `ORDER_PAYMENT_STATUS_CHANGED`) is PR B; its screen is Step 4.4; the remittance of Step 4.13 marks a COD payment `COMPLETED`. **The online gateway (the card charge, webhooks, refunds through the processor) is Phase 2**, next to the billing and card payments of the stores (not a step of the MVP), and it is the step that first detects a failed payment: `order.payment_failed` stays in `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter` until then and no MVP step owns it.
- [x] **From Step 4.1 (PR A), indexes of `order_items`:** `order_items` has no index on `product_id` or `variant_id`, so the history count of a product delete (and the foreign key check) scans it while the product row is locked: add `@@index([productId])` and `@@index([variantId])` to `OrderItem` in the migration of this step.

- [x] **PR A of Step 4.3 (schema and pure rules, no route; built):** migration `20261123100000_orders_phones_schedule_packing` with its rollback (`recipient_phones`, `scheduled_delivery_date`, `packing_notes`, `payment_reference`, `payment_proof_media_id`, the index `[store_id, scheduled_delivery_date]` and the indexes of `order_items.product_id` and `variant_id`); `libs/core/domain/order-rules.ts` (state machine, editable fields, totals in whole cents with the 32-bit ceiling, the discount limit in integers, free shipping, the fee range, price control and approval, confirmation, manual payments, the packing note notice), `order-schedule.ts` (today and tomorrow on the clock of the store), `order-schemas.ts` (`createOrderSchema`, `updateOrderSchema`, `changeOrderStatusSchema`, `recordOrderPaymentSchema`, the scheduled delivery filters), the constants of `order-constants.ts`, the audit actions, events, error codes and the notification type `order.packing_note_changed`, and `nationalPhoneNumber` in `libs/core/messaging`. PR B builds the service and the routes over them; PR C the rest. The case of `Order` and `OrderItemAllocation` in `tests/security/tenant-isolation.spec.ts` and the end of their exemptions are PR B's (no new model here).
- [x] **The three notes of an order (owner, 2026-11-10; `OrderEditableField` now has `customerNotes`, `internalNotes` and `packingNotes` where it had `notes`):** `customerNotes` is the customer's own words (also on the carrier label); `internalNotes` is for customer care (members with a permission on the order: never the customer, a channel, a plugin or the label; not the internal notes of the chat thread, which are `Message`s) and is read and written from this step on; `packingNotes` is for whoever packs the order, editable while the order is `PENDING_CONFIRMATION`, `CONFIRMED` or `PREPARING`. PR B: write and audit both internal notes with the old and new text (`ORDER_INTERNAL_NOTE_CHANGED`, `ORDER_PACKING_NOTE_CHANGED`), keep the three out of every event payload, the plugin API and the label, and raise `order.packing_note_changed` (to the members who may prepare or ship orders of the store) when the packing note changes on a `CONFIRMED` or `PREPARING` order, taking `NotificationTypes.ORDER_PACKING_NOTE_CHANGED` off `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter` in that PR. Cases required: another store reads and edits none of them, a member without the permission sees none, none shows in the label, the events or `/plugin-api/*`, and the notice goes only to a confirmed order. Screens: the fields in the drawer and the detail of Step 4.4, the amber "Packing note" band in Steps 4.10 and 4.14.
- [x] **From PR A, the store preferences the orders read (rule 12):** `storePreferencesSchema` has only `sendReadReceipts` and `assignOnReply`, so PR B cannot read `orders.discountLimitPercent` (0 by default) and `orders.freeShippingRule` (`all_items` by default). The rules take them as parameters; **PR B adds those two keys, read only, to `libs/core/platform/config/store-preferences.ts`** (the same defaults as `TDD_17 §4.14`) and Step 4.14 adds the write path and the rest of the block.
- [x] **From PR A, the national number of a customer for the forms (rule 12):** `nationalPhoneNumber` lives in `libs/core/messaging` (it needs `libphonenumber-js`, which the web bundle must not carry). The drawer of Step 4.4 pre-fills the recipient phones from `Customer.phone`: **PR B returns the national number with the customer data the drawer reads** (or Step 4.4 asks for it), so the web never parses a phone.
- [ ] **From PR A, the backfill of `recipient_phones` (rule 12):** it cannot be done in SQL (it needs `libphonenumber-js`), so rows that already exist keep `{}`; no code has created an order outside tests, so nothing real needs it. The migration does not refuse to run on a database that holds test orders (a guard would block every long-lived dev and test database). If real orders ever appear, backfill them with `nationalPhoneNumber` before their first guide. **Owner: nobody unless a database with real orders appears.**
- [x] **From PR A, for PR B (rule 12):** (a) `changeOrderStatusSchema` accepts any `deliveredAt`: the service refuses a moment in the future (a small clock allowance), because the schema has no clock; (b) recording a payment never changes `Order.paymentMethod` (the method of the record is how the money came); (c) an order with `recipientPhones = {}` (legacy or test row) is read, not refused; (d) a store timezone the runtime does not know makes `scheduledDeliveryShortcutRange` throw: the timezone is validated when the store saves it, and the list answers a 500 rather than a wrong day. (e) `Order.recipient_phones` is a nullable `TEXT[]` column (the Prisma way for a list with a default): the service reads a null as `[]`; the new indexes are built without `CONCURRENTLY`, which is fine while the tables hold no real orders; (f) `recipientPhonesSchema` strips a leading `+` as `TDD_02 §13` says but cannot know the country, so an international number reaches the server as if it were national unless the form reduced it with the national number of the customer (Step 4.4); (g) `evaluatePriceControl` takes the current approval status and honours `approved` only while it is `APPROVED`: the service passes the stored status; (i) `evaluatePriceControl` answers `withdrawsRequest` when a change makes a held request or approval unnecessary: the service clears `priceApprovalRequestedBy` and the approved columns, audits it and retracts the notice of the approvers; (j) `nationalPhoneNumber` gives the national number of `libphonenumber-js`, which for a few countries (the Argentine mobile `9`) is not what a local carrier dials; the carriers of the MVP are Salvadoran and the agent can edit the pre-filled number, so it is accepted; (k) `Order.packingNotes` and the notice raise `NotificationTypes.ORDER_PACKING_NOTE_CHANGED` (not the audit action of the same name), and PR B pins its recipients (members who may prepare or ship) with a test. (h) the address and the phones stay editable while a guide exists, as `OrderEditableFields` lists them: changing them after a guide is the cancel-and-recreate flow of Step 4.10.
- [x] **From PR A, payloads of the order events (rule 12):** `Events.ORDER_*` have no payload type yet (`event-payloads.ts` holds only the order id and number of the shipping ones). **Owner: PR B**, which is the first to publish them; the packing note and the other notes never travel in a payload.
- [ ] **From PR A, carrier rules of the fee (rule 12):** `isShippingFeeWithinRange` takes `ShippingFeeBounds`; until Step 4.8 builds the connections the service passes `unmanaged` (only the free-shipping rule limits the fee, as the task above says). **Owner: Step 4.8**, which passes the range of the planned carrier or `no_carrier`.
- [ ] **From PR C, the figures of a customer (rule 12):** `CustomerFigureOrderStatuses` (CONFIRMED, PREPARING, SHIPPED, DELIVERED) is what counts as an order of the customer in `figures` and in the CSV: a pending order is not a purchase yet and a cancelled or returned one is not one any more; the list and the timeline show every status. The best alternative, counting everything but cancelled, was rejected because it adds up orders nobody took on. Totals are cents of the order currency (v1.2 orders are USD only: a store with several currencies would need a figure per currency). **Owner: Step 4.4** draws them (a customer who has only a pending order shows 0 orders) and decides if the profile should show the pending ones apart; **Step 4.14** if returns should be netted instead of dropped.
- [ ] **From PR C, the order files (rule 12):** `Media` rows of purpose `order` are scanned like any other file but `PROCESS_IMAGE` re-encodes only images; a PDF proof is served as it came, after the scan. **Owner: Step 7.3** (the Meta and media findings) if PDFs of orders should be sanitized too. The files of an order are not shown in the gallery of the customer (they carry no `customerId`); Step 4.4 lists them on the order detail from the order's own route.

- [x] **PR B of Step 4.3 (`OrdersService` and the routes; built):** `apps/api/src/modules/orders/`: `POST /orders` (a customer taken `FOR SHARE`, the survivor of a merge; totals and the free-shipping flag computed from the catalog; the number of the order from `store_settings.next_order_number`, migration `20261124100000_order_number_counter` with its rollback), `GET /orders` (filters, the scheduled delivery days and the `today`/`tomorrow` shortcuts on the clock of the store, sort by delivery day, keyset pages), `GET` and `PATCH /orders/:id` (edit by field), `POST /orders/:id/status`, `/confirm`, `/cancel`, `/payment` (new route `orders.payment`) and `/price-approval/approve|reject`. Stock only through `InventoryService` (`OrderInventoryService`, plan in `libs/core/domain/order-allocation.ts`): allocate and reserve on create and on an items edit, release on cancel, issue on `SHIPPED`, optional restock on `RETURNED`, each per allocation with the key `order:{orderId}:{itemId}:{warehouseId}:{effect}`. Price control, approvals, the three notes (audited with the text before and after), the packing-note notice (`order.packing_note_changed`, taken off `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter`), the payload types of the `order.*` events (ids only), `readOrderPreferences` (`orders.discountLimitPercent`, `orders.freeShippingRule`), the cases of `Order` and `OrderItemAllocation` in `tests/security/tenant-isolation.spec.ts` and the end of their exemptions. `createOrderSchema` and `updateOrderSchema` lines may now carry `unitPriceCents` and `priceReason` (the level 1 override of `ORDERS_OVERRIDE_PRICE`; the server refuses them from anybody else) and `changeOrderStatusSchema` takes `confirmedVia`.
- [ ] **Debts of PR B (rule 12), each with its owner:** (a) the agent cannot yet choose the warehouses of a line (TDD_16 §4.1 rule 4: the create drawer sends allocations): the schema has no `allocations` field, the server always plans them; add it, with the check that every warehouse is linked to the store and the quantities add up (`INVENTORY_WAREHOUSE_NOT_LINKED`), when the drawer needs it (Step 4.4). (b) `hasActiveShipment` is always false and `ORDER_STATUS_DRIVEN_BY_SHIPMENT` is not raised: there is no `Shipment` yet (Step 4.8 passes the real value and refuses a manual `SHIPPED` or `DELIVERED` with an active shipment; Step 4.9 completes a cash-on-delivery payment when its shipment is delivered). (c) The lint rule of `TDD_17 §8` that forbids writing `order.status` and `order.priceApprovalStatus` outside `OrdersService` is not built: nothing else writes orders yet (Step 4.8, the first other writer, adds it next to `eslint-inventory-bans.cjs` with its spec). (d) A request that withdraws a held price approval is cleared and audited (`priceRequestWithdrawn` in `order.updated`), but the notice the approvers got is not retracted: the notice does not exist until the emitters of `order.price_approval_requested` land (Step 4.3 PR C, which removes the four types from `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter`; the retraction is Step 4.14 with the notification of the decision). (e) The `order.*` events are not forwarded to the browsers (no `RealtimeEvents` entry): the order screens of Step 4.4 poll or invalidate on their own actions until Step 4.4 adds the forwarding it needs. (f) A change of the planned carrier and the fee range of a carrier are Step 4.8 (`ShippingFeeBounds` is `unmanaged`). (g) Creating an order takes the row of `store_settings` (the counter) and the allocation lock of the store, so the orders of one store are made one after another until the commit: accepted for the volume of the MVP, to be measured in Step 7.3. (h) The order summary line of the thread, the confirmation message (`sendConfirmationMessage`, `orders.sendSummaryOnCreate`) and the customer messages are PR C and Step 4.9; `confirmOnCreate` here is the request flag only (the preference that initialises it is Step 4.14). (j) A return restocked into a warehouse that was switched off or unlinked after the order shipped, or for a product that stopped tracking stock, is refused by the engine (`INVENTORY_WAREHOUSE_INACTIVE`, `INVENTORY_WAREHOUSE_NOT_LINKED`, `INVENTORY_PRODUCT_NOT_TRACKED`): the agent returns it without restock and fixes the stock by an adjustment; letting the agent pick another warehouse is Step 4.12, which owns the returns inventory. (k) `sendConfirmationMessage` is accepted and has no effect until the summary message exists (PR C writes the thread line, Step 4.9 sends the customer message). (i) `PATCH /orders/:id` replaces all the lines when `items` is sent; a line that is already there and names no price keeps the price it was made at (an override included), a new line takes today's catalog price, and an approval never survives a change of lines (it goes back to pending). A discount or a fee the order already had is not put to the approval test again by an edit of something else, so a member without the override can edit the rest of an order whose discount an override holder set.

**TDD:** `TDD_02 §5`, `§13`, `TDD_09 §5`, `TDD_16 §4.1`
**Done when:** totals are never trusted from the client, illegal transitions are rejected, an
order for stock that the linked warehouses cannot cover (one warehouse, or all together when split is on) is refused with `INSUFFICIENT_INVENTORY` unless the store allows negative stock, and
every order lifecycle change produces the inventory effect of `TDD_16 §4.1`.

### Step 4.4 — Order UI
- [x] Create-order side drawer with the chat visible, variants, and per-line allocations and available stock per warehouse (the agent can change them), and an optional scheduled delivery date picker (empty by default, a past day accepted; the same field on the order detail page while `OrderEditableFields` allows it) **Done in PR B1 (create):** `OrderDrawerProvider` (`components/orders/order-drawer-context.tsx`, `useOrderDrawer().openCreate({ customerId?, conversationId? })`, mounted in the store shell and loaded on first open) opens `create-order-drawer.tsx`: customer (the conversation's, a search, or a new one), items from the product autocomplete (a variant is asked for), quantity, the price of the catalog (writable with `orders:override_price`, with its reason), per-warehouse stock and the agent's choice of warehouses, shipping fee, discount with its reason, scheduled day (empty, a past day accepted), recipient phones (pre-filled with the national phone of the customer, digits only on blur), a saved address, the payment method, the three notes (the packing note in an amber box) and confirm-on-create for `orders:confirm`. It never sends a total. The api grew **`allocations` per line** on create and on an items edit (`checkRequestedAllocation` in `libs/core/domain/order-allocation.ts`: linked and active warehouses only, units add up, a split only where the store allows it, stock unless negative stock is on; another organization's warehouse is `INVENTORY_WAREHOUSE_NOT_LINKED`, a product that does not track stock refuses a choice) and `GET /customers/:id` / `POST /customers` answer `nationalPhone`. The button "New order" is on the Orders page; `Shift+O` is in `conversation-shortcuts.tsx`. **The edit of an order in the same drawer, and the same field on the detail page, are PR B2.** **Done in PR B2 (edit):** `EditOrderDrawer` (`components/orders/edit-order-drawer.tsx`, `useOrderDrawer().openEdit(orderId)`, the "Edit order" button `order-edit-button.tsx` on the detail page, drawn only when the member holds `orders:edit` and the status still accepts items, shipping or discount): the items (quantity, add, remove, the price for who holds `orders:override_price`, the warehouses of a line), the shipping fee and the discount with its reason, each locked in the statuses `OrderEditableFields` locks; `lib/orders/order-edit-form.ts` sends only what changed and never a total; the lines, the price fields and the stock are the same components as the create drawer (`order-lines-section.tsx`, `order-price-fields.tsx`, `use-line-stock.ts`). The day, the address, the phones, the notes and the payment are edited on the detail page (PR A2), so the edit drawer does not repeat them.

Register them with `useShortcut`, add both names to `AvailableShortcuts` and their labels to `inbox.shortcuts.labels` in both message files
- [x] Recipient phones in the create and edit drawers (**the detail edits them in PR A2** with the same rules: digits only on blur, up to `RecipientPhones.MAX_COUNT`; the create and edit drawers are PR B's): a list of up to `RecipientPhones.MAX_COUNT` numbers pre-filled from `Customer.phone` reduced to its national number (no country code, the carriers do not take one), add and remove buttons, digits-only normalization on blur, editable where the address is (`OrderEditableFields`) **Done:** create drawer in PR B1; the edit is the detail's (PR A2), the same rules.
- [x] Status change flows with proof upload and the "restock" choice on returns (only for an order with no `OrderReturn`; the shipping, confirmation and returns UI is Step 4.14) **Done in PR A2** (`components/orders/order-flows.tsx`, rules in `lib/orders/order-detail-rules.ts`): the buttons are the transitions of `OrderStatusTransitions` the member's permission allows (confirm and cancel have their own permission, as in the api), a button that waits for a price approval is drawn disabled with its reason, the dialogs are confirm (how the customer confirmed), prepare, ship, deliver (optional proof of delivery, purpose `order`), return (the restock choice, only for a member with `inventory:adjust`) and cancel (a factory reason or free text), plus approve/reject of a price (`orders:approve_price`, no button to approve what the member asked for). The refusals of the api reach the person as their `errors.<code>` text. A file is handed to a form only once the scan said it is clean (`waitUntilClean`).
- [x] The three notes in the create-order drawer and the order detail (**the detail is done in PR A2**; the drawer is PR B's): `customerNotes`, `internalNotes` (customer care, never shown to the customer) and `packingNotes`, each editable where `OrderEditableFields` allows, with the Spanish and English labels; and the manual payment of the order: the method, the status, the reference and the receipt upload (purpose `order`) from the detail, against the route of Step 4.3 (decisions of 2026-11-10) **Done:** the drawer in PR B1.
- [x] Print documents (PDF) **Done in PR A2 (decision, rule 12):** the packing list and the order sheet are printed by the browser from the order the page already holds (`components/orders/order-print.tsx`, a sheet drawn in the body only while it prints, with the print rules of `globals.css`); "Save as PDF" of the print dialog makes the PDF. Chosen over a server-rendered PDF because the api has no renderer, no library enters the bundle and no byte of the order leaves the browser; the cost is that the layout is the browser's, not a fixed PDF. Both sheets carry the packing note in a bordered block and never the customer care note; the packing list has no prices. The invoice and the carrier's label are Steps 4.13 and 4.14.
- [x] **Orders page** (`/s/[storeId]/orders`): a full-page table with search by order number or customer, filters (status, payment status, date range, customer, scheduled delivery date range with the "today" and "tomorrow" shortcuts of Step 4.3), a scheduled delivery column, cursor pagination (`TDD_08 §2.4`), sorting and filters kept in the URL, and row selection that Step 4.14 turns into the bulk action bar **Done in PR A1:** `apps/web/app/s/orders-page.tsx` (route `s/[storeId]/orders`, entry "Orders" in the store navigation for `orders:view`, "no access" screen without it), the filters and the sort in the address (`lib/orders/order-filters.ts`, validated on the way in: what the api would refuse is left out), the ticked rows kept and counted (`lib/orders/order-selection.ts`: only ids still on the screen stay ticked), the number opens `/orders/[orderId]`, the customer opens the profile drawer. The api grew what the page needed (rule 12, `TDD_08 §5.4`): `GET /orders` takes `q` (the number by prefix, a bare "12" as `00012`, or a piece of the name, e-mail or phone of the customer), `createdFrom` and `createdTo` (inclusive days **on the clock of the store**, `startOfCalendarDayIn`) and each row names its customer (`customer: { id, name }`, the name only). One rule builds the `where` for the list and the export (`orderListWhere`, `libs/core/messaging`). Page budget of Lighthouse: `ScriptCeilingsKb.ORDERS` 300, set like the catalog's and **not measured locally** (CI measures it).
- [x] **Order detail page** (`/s/[storeId]/orders/[orderId]`) with lines, allocations, status flows and edits. **Done in PR A2:** `apps/web/app/s/order-detail-page.tsx` (the stub is replaced): customer, lines with the warehouse each leaves from (named for a member with `inventory:view`), totals in cents written by the formatter, the payment (method, state, reference, receipt link; "Record payment" with the receipt upload against `POST /orders/:id/payment`, only the states `checkPaymentChange` lets through), the delivery (scheduled day, address, recipient phones, tracking number, each edited in place where `OrderEditableFields` allows and the member holds `orders:edit`; the screen never offers what the status locks) and the three notes (the packing note in an amber band, the customer care note only when the api sent it) and a history of the moments the order has. The e2e is `apps/web-e2e/specs/order-detail.spec.ts` (CI is the first run). **PR A1 left a stub** (`apps/web/app/s/order-detail-page.tsx`, a title and the way back) so the links of the list never end in a 404: **PR A2 replaces it** (the file and the `pages.ts` re-export are A2's).
- [x] Orders export (found by the frontend alignment audit, owner, 2026-10-28; `ApiRoutes.orders.export` and `ORDERS_EXPORT` exist and no step builds them): `GET /orders/export` creates an `Export { type: ORDERS }` on the same queue, S3 key and download as the customers CSV of Step 3.4 (register the route before `orders/:id`), with the button on the Orders page and the result in the exports center; columns and rules to decide in this step **Done in PR A1 (decisions, rule 12):** `GET /orders/export` needs `orders:view` **and** `orders:export` (the rows hold the contact data of the customer) and takes the filters of the list without the page and the sort; the api resolves the days on the clock of the store **when the file is asked for** and freezes them in the row (`ordersExportFiltersSchema`), so the file holds what the screen showed. One row per order, newest first (`apps/worker/src/exports/orders-export.source.ts`): `order_number, created_at, status, payment_status, payment_method, customer_id, customer_name, customer_email, customer_phone, lines, units, subtotal_cents, shipping_cents, discount_cents, total_cents, currency, free_shipping, price_modified, price_approval_status, scheduled_delivery_date, confirmed_at, shipped_at, delivered_at, cancelled_at`; money in whole cents; **left out:** the three notes, the payment reference and the receipt, the address and the recipient phones, who made, confirmed or approved the order, the reasons of a cancellation and of a price change. `ExportTypePermissions` has the kind, the exports center lists it for who holds both permissions (`ORDERS_EXPORT` opens the center), the button is on the Orders page. Covered in `tests/api/exports.spec.ts`.
- [x] Orders in the customer panel (Step 3.3 left them out because they need orders): the orders tab of the profile drawer from `GET /customers/:id/orders`, the orders in the activity timeline, the order figures of `TDD_09 §6` (orders, total spent, average order, computed from the orders: the schema has no column for them) and the orders in the preview of the merge **Done in PR C1 (decisions, rule 12):** the profile drawer has an Activity / Orders switch (only for who holds `ORDERS_VIEW`): `components/customers/customer-orders.tsx` lists the orders from `GET /customers/:id/orders` (cursor, a tab per state, each number opens the order, "View orders" opens the list filtered by `?customerId=`); the figures (orders, total spent, average order) sit with the others of the profile and come with the first page of that same query (one read, shared with the tab), shown only with `ORDERS_VIEW`; `customerTimelineEntrySchema` has the `order` variant and the timeline draws it (number, state, total, link) with an "Orders" filter shown only with `ORDERS_VIEW`; the merge preview answers `counts.orders` (survivor, absorbed, merged), **null for a viewer without `ORDERS_VIEW`** (the count of orders is not theirs to read; covered in `tests/api/customer-timeline.spec.ts` with a viewer who holds only `CUSTOMERS_MERGE`), and the dialog leaves the row out when it is null. A pending order is not shown apart in the profile (rejected: a second figure the owner did not ask for; the tab lists it with its state). The figures also load in the customer panel of the inbox (one more request when the panel opens, no new dependency; **Lighthouse of the inbox not measured locally: CI measures it**). e2e: `apps/web-e2e/specs/customer-orders.spec.ts` (not run locally).
- [x] What Step 3.2 left for this step because it needs orders and products (`TDD_09 §4`): the `/product` and `/order` slash commands, the Product and Order buttons of the composer, `Shift+O`, the order summary line in the thread (`MessageType.ORDER_SUMMARY`) and the Create order action of the Quick actions widget **Done in PR C2:** `MessageView.orderSummary` (api and api-client, strict and bounded by `OrderLineLimits`, without who did it) and the line in the thread (`components/inbox/order-line.tsx`: number, state, total, link for who holds `ORDERS_VIEW`; the api leaves the line out of the thread for a viewer without `ORDERS_VIEW`, like it does with the internal notes: covered in `tests/api/messages.spec.ts`); `/product <text>` (and `/producto`) searches the catalog (`GET /products/search`, from 2 characters, for who holds `PRODUCTS_VIEW`) and puts the product, its variant if the search matched one, and its price in the box as plain text, and the Product button of the composer starts the command (only with an empty box: a command is the whole text of the box). Realtime: `order.created` and `order.updated` now reach the sockets that hold `ORDERS_VIEW` with ids only (the conversation is left out of the payload on purpose; `RealtimeEvents.ORDER_UPDATED` no longer carries the state) and the page reads the orders, the profile of the customer and every thread again (`lib/realtime/invalidation.ts`, covered in `tests/api/realtime.spec.ts`). Also `/order` (and `/pedido`), the Order button of the composer and the Create order action of the Quick actions widget, all through `useOrderDrawer().openCreate({ customerId, conversationId })` of PR B (offered with `ORDERS_CREATE`, not in a note; the widget makes the order for the open conversation, or asks for the customer when none is open; nothing opens while the conversation loads). **That finishes this task.**

(b) The address of the order is picked from the saved addresses of the customer; typing a new one is **PR B2** (the edit drawer shares it with `order-fields.ts` of A2). (c) The subtotal of the drawer is an estimate from the catalog prices (it ignores an overridden price, the discount and the fee): the server's totals are the only real ones. **Owner: Step 7.7** if the owner wants the server's quote before saving. (d) A drawer that is closed loses what was typed (no draft kept, unlike the composer). **Owner: Step 7.7**. (e) The phones are pre-filled only for a member with `customers:view` (the national phone comes with the customer); the others type them.
