(g) The drawer sends `sendConfirmationMessage: false`: the agent has no choice for it yet and no message may go to the customer unasked; the choice (and `orders.sendSummaryOnCreate`) is **Step 4.14**, and the customer message itself **Step 4.9**. (h) Escape and Back close the drawer and lose what was typed (a click outside does not); a confirmation for a dirty form is **Step 7.7**. (i) The edit of lines keeps the warehouses of a line whose product, variant and units did not change when they can still serve it, and plans again otherwise (the edit drawer of B2 sends `allocations` for a line the agent changes).
- [ ] **Debts of PR A2 (rule 12), each with its owner:** (a) an order that exists is edited in the detail only in the fields that carry no price (day, address, phones, tracking number, the three notes); the lines, the shipping fee, the discount and the way it was sold are edited in the edit drawer of **PR B**, which opens from the detail once it merges (the detail has no "Edit order" button until then). (b) The history shows the moments the order holds (made, confirmed with how, shipped, delivered with its proof, cancelled with its reason) and **not who did each one nor the edits**: the api has no per-order audit route (`organizations.auditLog` is Step 7.2), so **Step 7.2** adds the order audit entries to the history; the link to the conversation of the order (`conversationId`, only after checking `visibleTo` for the viewer) goes to **PR C**. (c) The detail does not refresh by itself when another agent changes the order (the `ORDER_*` realtime events): **PR C** (realtime) subscribes it; until then a refused change after a race shows the refusal and the page is refreshed by the person. (d) The return flow offers the restock choice for every order because `OrderReturn` does not exist until Step 4.12; **Step 4.14** hides it for an order that has one. (e) The cancellation reasons are the factory list (`OrderCancellationReasons`), not the per-store list of the preference `orders.cancellationReasons`: **Step 4.9** (preferences) makes the dialog read it. (f) "Mark as shipped" is the manual transition of `TDD_17 §3` with no guide; the guide, the carrier and `ORDER_STATUS_DRIVEN_BY_SHIPMENT` (the api refuses a hand change when a shipment decides) are **Step 4.14**, and the tracking number is editable while no shipment exists. (g) Delivering takes no "delivered at" moment (the api uses now); a past moment is **Step 4.14**. (h) The printed sheets are the browser's layout (see "Print documents"); a fixed-layout PDF, the invoice and the carrier's label are Steps 4.13 and 4.14. (i) The names of the warehouses need `inventory:view` (`GET /warehouses`): without it, and for a deactivated warehouse, a line says "Another warehouse". **Step 7.7** if a name should reach every member (the api would have to name it in the order). (j) The detail page is not measured by Lighthouse (the budgets measure the list): **Step 7.7** adds it with the other pages. (l) The hooks (`useOrderFileUpload`, `useOrderWrites`) and the dialogs have no component-level spec: the web unit tests run in node with no DOM, so the pure rules are unit-tested (`order-detail-rules.spec.ts`, `order-fields.spec.ts`) and the dialogs by the e2e; **Step 7.7** adds component tests if the project adopts a DOM test environment. (k) The e2e of the detail (`order-detail.spec.ts`) and the browser print were **not run locally** (memory of the machine, rule of the coordinator): CI is the first run.

(b) The figures and the list draw money with `formatCents` (US dollars, like the orders list): a store with another currency would need a figure per currency (the same limit as `CustomerFigureOrderStatuses`). **Owner: Step 7.3** if the owner ever sells in another currency. (c) The orders section reads pages of the default size and has no search; a customer with hundreds of orders uses the filtered list (`View orders`). **Owner: Step 7.7** if wanted. (d) The switch and its two new tabs are not covered by a unit spec (they are drawn components); the e2e case `customer-orders.spec.ts` covers them and CI is its first run. It has no negative case for a member with `CUSTOMERS_VIEW` but not `ORDERS_VIEW` (no figures, no Orders switch, no request): the support helper `addUserWithoutOrders` makes a role without `CUSTOMERS_VIEW` too, so a variant is needed. **Owner: this step's QA checkpoint** (check it by hand there). A review finding rejected on purpose: the merge preview always selects `_count.orders` even when the viewer may not read it (two index-backed counts on a route used a few times a day; a conditional select would cost more in typing than it saves).
- [ ] **Debts of PR B2 (rule 12), each with its owner:** (a) the edit drawer cannot type a new address, phones or notes (the detail edits them): a single place for each field. (b) The line rows of an order show the catalog price of a line that was overridden as "Catalog price" from `originalUnitPriceCents`; a line whose catalog price changed since the order was made keeps the price it was sold at (the server's rule), and the form cannot tell the agent today's catalog price: **Step 7.7** if the owner wants it. A price typed back to the catalog price the line had when it was sold is judged by the server against today's catalog: if the catalog moved, the server asks for a reason (`ORDER_PRICE_REASON_REQUIRED`) that the form did not ask for; the form cannot know today's catalog price (**Step 7.7**). The units the order holds are added back to the stock of its own edit from the order the form was made from, so if somebody else moved the order's allocations meanwhile the check on the screen can differ from the server's (the server decides; the banner says the order changed). (c) A save closed by Back while the request is on its way loses its toast (the detail still refreshes: the write is in `useOrderWrites`); the same for a confirmation of a dirty form: **Step 7.7**. (d) The e2e `order-edit-drawer.spec.ts` and the Lighthouse ceilings were **not run locally** (memory of the machine): CI is the first run.
- [ ] **Debts of PR C2 (rule 12), each with its owner:** (a) `/product` puts plain text in the box: a product card as a message of its own (`MessageType.PRODUCT_CARD`) has no channel-agnostic send path (the plugins declare no capability for it), so it is not built. **Owner: Step 4.9** (the messages to the customer), which decides what a channel may take. (b) `order.updated` and `order.created` carry no conversation, so every thread of the store is read again when an order changes (a refetch too many is better than a line that never shows); the open conversation of the order could be targeted if the payload gets the id after `visibleTo`. **Owner: Step 7.3** if the cost shows. (c) The line of an order is shown to who holds `ORDERS_VIEW` and the thread query leaves it out for the rest, but a message read by its own id (`GET /messages/:id`, if it ever returns system lines) is not filtered: today no such route returns an `ORDER_SUMMARY`. **Owner: Step 7.3** (recheck when a route that reads one message is added). (d) The inbox bundle grew by the order line, the product search in the composer and the figures of the profile: Lighthouse of the inbox **not measured locally** (CI measures it), and e2e `order-thread.spec.ts` not run locally. **Owner: this step's QA checkpoint.** (e) The product menu shows up to 20 active products and has no archived mode; a long list is narrowed by typing more. **Owner: Step 7.7** if wanted. (f) The realtime listener binds the events that mean "the order changed" (`order.updated`, `order.status_changed`, the two payment events, price approved and rejected): the other `order.*` events (confirmed, shipped, delivered, cancelled, price overridden, approval requested, packing note changed) are always published in the same transaction as one of those, so they add nothing; **a new `order.*` event that is not must be added to `onOrderUpdated`**. (h) Found by the e2e of this PR: the last-message lookup of the conversation list left out only system lines, so the line of an order became the preview and broke the list. It leaves out `ORDER_SUMMARY` too (`conversations.views.ts`, covered in `tests/api/conversations.spec.ts`), and an order line cannot be reacted to (`agent-reactions.service.ts`). Any new query that reads "the last message" must leave the lines of the platform out. (g) The line of an order and the lists draw money with `formatCents` (US dollars) and ignore the `currency` of the line: the same limit as debt (b) of C1. **Owner: Step 7.3.**

**Done when:** an agent creates an order without leaving the conversation, and the orders page lists, filters, searches and opens orders of the store.

**QA checkpoint: yes (after 4.1-4.4, money, stock and the life of an order):** totals, taxes and discounts to the cent; stock reserved, released and never oversold under two agents ordering the last unit; every status change of the order and its audit line; the same cases against the other store (`tenant-isolation`) and with a viewer who has no permission.

### Step 4.5 — Transfers between warehouses
- [x] `StockTransfer` and `StockTransferLine` with the draft, dispatch, receive (partial), close-with-shortage and cancel flows of `TDD_16 §4.5` (migration `20261125100000_stock_transfers` with its rollback; the stock only moves through `InventoryService`, under the lock of the transfer row, with the keys `transfer:{id}:{lineId}:{dispatch|receive:{receiptKey}|cancel_return|shortage_return}`)
- [x] Line visibility limited to the caller's stores; store-link validation on both warehouses (at the draft and again at dispatch)
- [x] Notifications and audit actions (`transfer.created`, `transfer.updated` besides the four of the TDD)
- [x] Isolation cases for `StockTransfer` and its lines (`tests/security/tenant-isolation.spec.ts`, and the rules of the tables in `tests/security/transfer-schema.spec.ts`)
- [x] **Show the notification switches of this step (QA fix PR Q7):** this step raises `transfer.dispatched` (`NotificationService.notify`, with its `messageKey` texts in `es` and `en`); the preferences page hides the switch of a type nothing raises, so remove `NotificationTypes.TRANSFER_DISPATCHED` from `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter` (`libs/core/domain/notifications/notification-constants.ts`) in the same PR, and add the case to the unit spec of that constant
- [x] Debts of the PR of Step 4.5 (rule 12): (a) **the permission of a transfer is the organization's** (`TDD_16 §1`) but what a caller sees and writes is the stores where they are an active member AND their role there holds `inventory:manage_transfers` or `inventory:receive_transfers`: the answers show only those lines (a transfer made only of other stores' lines is not shown at all, header included), **dispatching and cancelling in transit need sight of every line** (they move the stock of every store whose units travel: `INVENTORY_TRANSFER_LINES_OUT_OF_REACH` otherwise, so a transfer of two stores is dispatched by someone who belongs to both, such as the owner), changing the warehouses of a draft needs the same, and receiving is per line of the caller's stores; (b) **closing needs sight of every short line**: a caller who does not see a short line of another store gets `INVENTORY_TRANSFER_LINES_OUT_OF_REACH` and the transfer waits for someone who does (the owner belongs to every store); (c) **cancelling in transit returns the units that have not arrived**, not "the dispatched quantity" of `TDD_16 §4.5` when a part was already received (returning it too would make stock out of nothing); (d) a transfer in transit needs its destination switched on and the store of each line linked to it (`TRANSFER_IN` is refused otherwise): switching a warehouse off, or unlinking a store from it, is refused with `INVENTORY_WAREHOUSE_HAS_STOCK` (`reason: open_transfer`) while a transfer in transit leaves from or arrives at it; **Step 4.7** shows that refusal in words; (e) the web draws nothing yet: **Step 4.7** draws the routes (`scripts/frontend-coverage-map.ts` already points there), the counts of `transferLines` in the refusal to delete a product or a variant (`metadata.transferLines`, the catalog screen shows the generic refusal until then) and the transfer pages of `/org/transfers`; (f) the notification of `transfer.dispatched` goes to the members with `inventory:receive_transfers` of each store whose lines travel, and links to `/org/transfers` (the detail page is 4.7); (g) `libs/core/inventory` has a coverage threshold on the pure rules of movements and transfers (`transfer-rules.ts`); `TransfersService` is covered by `tests/api/transfers.spec.ts` against the real database

**TDD:** `TDD_16 §4.5`, `§6`, `§8`
**Done when:** a transfer goes draft to dispatched to partially received to closed with a
shortage and every step leaves the right movements; cancelling an in-transit transfer returns
the stock to the source; a line for a store the caller does not belong to is not visible.

- [x] Apply writes the variance, never "counted minus current"
- [x] Isolation cases for `StockCount` and its lines
- [x] **Show the notification switches of this step (QA fix PR Q7):** this step raises `count.awaiting_apply` (`NotificationService.notify`, with its `messageKey` texts in `es` and `en`); the preferences page hides the switch of a type nothing raises, so remove `NotificationTypes.COUNT_AWAITING_APPLY` from `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter` (`libs/core/domain/notifications/notification-constants.ts`) in the same PR, and add the case to the unit spec of that constant

**TDD:** `TDD_16 §4.6`, `§6`, `§8`
**Done when:** a count applied after sales happened during counting adjusts only by the
counted variance and leaves the sales intact.

### Step 4.7 — Inventory UI

  - [x] Settings → Inventory with the two toggles (Step 4.2, PR D1 #398); the stock of a product by warehouse with its low-stock banner, the adjustment dialog and the kardex, in the Stock tab of the product sheet and as a column of the catalog (Step 4.2, PR D3 #400); the actions of a level now sit under its warehouse, the first column, so on a phone they are not at the end of a table that scrolls sideways (PR D)

The review is the difference against the snapshot and says that sales and transfers made while counting are not touched; applying asks first (`APPLY_STOCK_COUNT`) and the refusals of the api (`short_stock`, `lines_refused`) are explained line by line; cancelling asks first (`CANCEL_STOCK_COUNT`, new tier HIGH). e2e `apps/web-e2e/specs/counts.spec.ts` (phone viewport, offline, reload, review, recount, apply with the stock of the product, discard a draft, list)

a harness is Step 7.7; (j) **`counts.spec.ts` needs a freshly seeded database** (it counts fixture products 11-13 at 20 units and applies, so a second run on the same database sees other snapshots); CI seeds each run; receive a transfer, run and apply a count.

### Step 4.8 — Carrier SDK, shipment engine and the `local` carrier
- [x] Refine the `CarrierPlugin` of Step 2.5 (`libs/plugins/sdk` and the `CarrierRegistry` beside the channel `PluginRegistry` already exist, with their fail-fast capability check): its params and results are the smallest shape the core needs and change here against the carrier documents; the lint rule (`TDD_14 §6`) forbidding writes to `shipment.status`, `shipment.codStatus`, `pickupRequest.status`, `orderReturn.status`, `codRemittance.status` and `order.priceApprovalStatus` outside their services; `tests/contracts/carrier-plugin.contract.ts` and a **fake carrier** with switches for reject, timeout before and after the carrier acted, idempotent create, no cancel, no webhook and pickup-bound mode **Built in PR A:** the params and results refined against `TDD_17 §4.4`, `§4.5`, `§4.8` and `§4.9` (`ShipmentRef` always carries the order number so "check with the carrier" can find a guide that never came back; the per-shipment outcome of a pickup batch; `refundStatus` of a void; `trackingUrl` and `cost` of a guide), `eslint-shipping-bans.cjs` for the six states, `tests/builders/carrier-plugin.ts` (the fake) and `tests/contracts/carrier-plugin.contract.ts`, which `pnpm test:contracts` runs in the `integration-tests` job
- [x] **Built in PR B1 (migration `20261127100000_shipping_schema`, its rollback, `tests/security/shipping-schema.spec.ts`):** the `TDD_17 §2` models and raw SQL as an additive migration (`CarrierConnection`, `CarrierService`, `GuideSequence`, `Shipment`, `ShipmentEvent`, `PickupRequest`, `BulkLabelJob`, `OrderMessageTemplate`, `OrderReturn`, `OrderReturnLine`), with the `Shipment` COD columns and the `CarrierConnection` fee columns that `TDD_17 §2` lists; the reception tables arrive with Step 4.12 and the remittance tables with Step 4.13, each as an additive migration; every new table joins the isolation suite and `check-isolation-coverage` in the step that creates it
- [x] `libs/core/shipping` `ShippingService`: `createShipments` for the three creation modes (standalone: one call per guide; internal: the core makes the guide, for `local`; pickup-bound, where guides exist only inside a pickup: one call per batch that carries the pickup and returns every guide, with partial success and an unknown outcome sending the whole batch to review) and its single-guide form (validate, write first with a deterministic idempotency key, call, store the label as `Media` `SHIPPING_LABEL`, advisory lock per order, unknown outcome to `REQUIRES_REVIEW`), the forward-only shipment state machine and `ShipmentEvent` history, manual events, "check with the carrier" **From PR B2 (debts (d), and the end of the `libs/carriers/local` item):** the rule for a guide whose connection was disconnected (cancel, check, reprint), written in `TDD_17 §4.8`; calling `takeLocalGuideNumber` and `renderLabelPdf` for a `local` guide, storing the file as `Media`, and the refusal of the guide when `LabelRenderError` is thrown **Built in PR C1** (`libs/core/shipping/src/shipping.service.ts` and its parts, `apps/api/src/modules/shipments`, `tests/api/shipments.spec.ts`): `createShipments` for the three modes (a `local` guide takes its number, draws the label and is written as `LABEL_CREATED` in one transaction, so a label that cannot be drawn gives the number back and the request answers `SHIPPING_LABEL_UNDRAWABLE`; a standalone guide is written `CREATING` and committed, then the carrier is called once, or up to three times with the same key when it declares `IDEMPOTENT_CREATE`; a pickup-bound connection is grouped by pickup day and hours and chunked by `maxBatchSize`, one `PickupRequest` and one call per chunk, per-shipment outcomes, an unknown outcome sends the whole chunk and its pickup to `REQUIRES_REVIEW`); an advisory lock per order, the origin rules and the amount to collect of `TDD_17 §4.4` (`codAmountFor`); the carrier's label is stored as `Media` `SHIPPING_LABEL` and scanned, a drawn one is clean; the only writer of a shipment or pickup status is `ShipmentWrites` (forward table, history row, events); manual status, cancel (synchronous), mark voided and "check with the carrier"; routes `POST /orders/:id/shipments`, `GET /shipments`, `GET /shipments/:id`, `GET /shipments/:id/label`, `POST /shipments/:id/{status,cancel,check,mark-voided}`; migration `20261129100000_shipment_engine` (see its rollback)
- [x] The foreign key `Order.carrierConnectionId` and the enforcement of the carrier fee rules of `TDD_17 §4.13` (fee range, a fee needs a carrier, the check at guide time) **Built in PR C1:** composite foreign key with the store (`orders_store_id_carrier_connection_id_fkey`, `RESTRICT`); `carrierConnectionId` in the create and edit bodies (null clears it, left out takes the default connection of the store on a new order, a connection of another store is a 404 and a disconnected one `SHIPPING_CARRIER_NOT_ACTIVE`); the range is judged by `planCarrier` (`apps/api/src/modules/orders/orders-pricing.ts`) and a change of carrier prices the order again; the guide refuses a fee out of the range of its connection (`SHIPPING_FEE_OUT_OF_RANGE`) unless the order is free-shipping with fee 0, the fee is the approved one or the caller holds `ORDERS_OVERRIDE_PRICE`, and an order with a price waiting or rejected (`ORDER_PRICE_APPROVAL_PENDING`)
- [x] Carriers are adapters of the `shipping` plugin of Step 2.7b (D19): the connection hangs from its installation, the catalog shows one entry, and uninstalling it warns about open shipments and pickups **Built in PR B2:** `PluginCatalogSync` writes the `shipping` row (type `carrier`, `ShippingPlugin`) from the carrier registry; `POST /plugins/install` accepts it; a connection is made only while that installation is active (checked again inside the transaction, under the store lock) and uninstalling it disconnects every connection of the store; the catalog entry carries `activeConnections`, `openShipments` and `openPickups`, which is the warning (a shipment not delivered, returned, cancelled or failed; a pickup pending, in review, requested or waiting for manual action), and so does the view of each connection. There is no `installation_id` column: the connection hangs from the installation of its store by the rule, not by a key (the pair store and plugin has one installation)
- [x] Connections: connect, test, edit and disconnect with encrypted credentials and webhook secret, the default shipping fee and its minimum and maximum per connection (also for `local`), `CarrierService` rows seeded from the plugin catalog, `GET /carriers/registry`, Settings → Carriers API (`SETTINGS_MANAGE_CARRIERS`) **Built in PR B2** (`apps/api/src/modules/carriers`, `tests/api/carriers.spec.ts`): `GET /carriers`, `GET /carriers/:id`, `GET /carriers/registry` (`SHIPPING_VIEW`), `POST /carriers`, `PATCH /carriers/:id`, `POST /carriers/:id/test`, `POST /carriers/:id/disconnect` (`SETTINGS_MANAGE_CARRIERS`); the credentials and the webhook secret are sealed with `CredentialCipher` and never come back (`hasCredentials`, `hasWebhookSecret`; the secret is given once, in the answer of the connect call); a failure of the carrier is a name of a closed list (`CarrierHealthReasons`), never its words; default connection of the store (`isDefault`, one at a time, cleared when it is disconnected); the services and the status mappings that the plugin's catalogs document are seeded when the connection is made; the fee range and the pickup rules are checked over what the row would hold (the CHECKs of the table say the same); disconnecting keeps the row, its shipments and its numbering, deletes the credentials at once and a connection that was let go accepts only a new name; `settings:manage_carriers` and `shipping:view` are inserted into the roles of the stores that exist (migration `20261128100000_carrier_permissions` with its rollback)

repeated delivery attempts (`FAILED_ATTEMPT` to itself, `Shipment.failedAttempts`, `CarrierConnection.maxDeliveryAttempts`, the `attemptNumber` and `maxAttempts` template variables and the `orders.failedAttemptMessages` preference with its Zod rule that refuses `autoSend` for `ORDER_FAILED_ATTEMPT`) **`statusCatalog()` in the contract and the empty one of `local` were built in PR A; the rest of this item is PR B1 and the status-flow PRs.** **Built in PR C2a:** the pure resolution (`resolveProviderStatus` in `libs/core/shipping`: the connection's row wins over the plugin's status, the code is normalized, the composite `status:reason` is tried before the plain status, a `null` row ignores on purpose, otherwise the code is unmapped under the composite code when there is a reason), `recordUnmappedStatus` (one row per connection and code, `occurrences` and `lastSeenAt` counted, a code that was mapped and taken back opens again as a first sighting, at most `CarrierStatusLimits.MAX_UNMAPPED_PER_CONNECTION` waiting per connection), the routes `GET /carriers/:id/status-mappings` and `/unmapped-statuses` (`SHIPPING_VIEW`) and `PUT`/`DELETE /carriers/:id/status-mappings/:code` (`SETTINGS_MANAGE_CARRIERS`, audited with `CARRIER_STATUS_MAPPING_CHANGED`, only a status in `CarrierReportableStatuses` or `null`, refused on a disconnected connection), with the isolation cases of `CarrierStatusMapping` and `CarrierUnmappedStatus`; the mappings are seeded when a connection is made since PR B2. **Left for PR C2c (after C2b):** the resolution inside `PROCESS_CARRIER_EVENT` against a shipment, the `REPROCESS_CARRIER_EVENTS` job (and `PUT` enqueuing it) and the single notification of the first sighting; **for PR C2b:** the repeated failed attempts.
- [x] Status flow: public `POST /webhooks/carriers/:connectionId` (raw body, signature, queue, `200` fast), `PROCESS_CARRIER_EVENT` with dedupe and out-of-order handling, `POLL_SHIPMENT_STATUS`, and the listener that applies the order effects of `TDD_17 §4.9` (`SHIPPED` with the inventory `ISSUE`, `DELIVERED`, COD payment completion, notifications) **From PR B2 (debts (a) and (d)):** the route that makes a new webhook secret for a connection (`POST /carriers/:id/webhook-secret`, `SETTINGS_MANAGE_CARRIERS`, audited, the secret shown once as at connect), and the rule for the events that arrive for a disconnected connection, written in `TDD_17 §4.9` **Built in PR C2a:** the public `POST /webhooks/carriers/:connectionId` (`CarrierWebhookService`: the raw body, at most `CarrierWebhookLimits.MAX_BODY_BYTES`, the signature checked by the plugin with the connection's secret, one answer `401 CARRIER_WEBHOOK_SIGNATURE_INVALID` for a malformed address, an unknown connection, a connection let go, a carrier with no webhook, an unreadable secret and a bad signature; the plugin normalizes, each event is checked against `carrierEventSchema`, every event becomes one `PROCESS_CARRIER_EVENT` job on the `webhooks` queue, with the store of the connection; `200` fast; `CarrierWebhookThrottle`), the route `POST /carriers/:id/webhook-secret` (audited with `CARRIER_WEBHOOK_SECRET_ROTATED`, the new secret shown once, the old one stops working at once), and a minimal `CarrierEventsProcessor` in the worker that validates the job and resolves the event for its connection (`CarrierEventResolver`) without touching a shipment. **Left for PR C2c:** `PROCESS_CARRIER_EVENT` against a shipment (find it by guide, provider id or reference; dedupe; out of order; `ShipmentEvent`; the transition table), the confirm-before-apply of `DELIVERED`/`RETURNED` for a carrier that signs nothing, and `REPROCESS_CARRIER_EVENTS`; **for PR C2b (session 50, `../omni-48f`):** `POLL_SHIPMENT_STATUS`, the listener of the order effects, the repeated failed attempts, the notification switches of `requires_review` and `failed_attempt`, and lifting the `409 ORDER_STATUS_DRIVEN_BY_SHIPMENT`.
- [x] **Left by PR B1 (rule 12), each with its owner:** (a) the isolation cases: the twelve new models (`CarrierConnection`, `CarrierService`, `GuideSequence`, `CarrierStatusMapping`, `CarrierUnmappedStatus`, `Shipment`, `ShipmentEvent`, `PickupRequest`, `BulkLabelJob`, `OrderMessageTemplate`, `OrderReturn`, `OrderReturnLine`) are in `scripts/isolation-exemptions.ts`, each naming its step, and until a route reaches them `tests/security/shipping-schema.spec.ts` is their isolation proof (the database refuses a link across stores, even of the same organization): PR B2 deletes the lines of `CarrierConnection`, `CarrierService` and `GuideSequence` and adds their cases to `tenant-isolation.spec.ts`, PR C1 those of `Shipment` and `ShipmentEvent`, PR C2 those of the two status tables; `OrderMessageTemplate` is Step 4.9, `BulkLabelJob` 4.10, `PickupRequest` 4.11, `OrderReturn` and `OrderReturnLine` 4.12. (b) The permissions of the stores that exist: `shipping:*` and `settings:manage_carriers` are in `RolePresets` (stores created from now on have them) but the system roles of the stores that already exist hold none; the PR that adds the first route guarded by each (B2 for `settings:manage_carriers`, C1 for `shipping:*`) inserts them into `permissions` in its own migration, as `20261125100000_stock_transfers` does, and takes the permission out of `PermissionsOfUnbuiltAreas` or `ReservedPermissions`. (c) `StoreSettings.defaultCarrierConnectionId` stays a plain column: PR B2 checks on every write that it is a connection of the same store, or adds the composite foreign key with the `ON DELETE` it chooses when it decides what disconnecting does to the default. (d) The links to `orders`, `order_items` and `warehouses` are plain foreign keys plus the triggers of the migration (an order has no `(store_id, id)` key and this PR leaves the table untouched); PR C1 may replace the order trigger if it adds that key with `Order.carrierConnectionId`. (e) `labelMediaId`, `mergedMediaId` and `codRemittanceId` are plain columns, like the other media ids; Step 4.13 adds the foreign key of `codRemittanceId` with its table. (f) The CHECKs of the status columns mirror the enums and `tests/security/shipping-schema.spec.ts` compares them: a status added to an enum needs a migration that widens its CHECK, in the same PR. (g) Rules the migration enforces that the services must respect: `cod_amount_cents` is null if and only if `cod_status` is `not_applicable`; `kind = 'return'` if and only if `return_id` is set; a `local` connection holds no credentials or webhook secret; a guide prefix is up to eight letters, digits or dashes; a `CarrierStatusMapping` targets only `CarrierReportableStatuses`; `BulkLabelJob.total` is at least 1 and equals the length of `orderIds`; a return shipment is the one made for its return. (h) The rollback drops the tables with their rows: guides already created at a carrier are not voided and the numbering of a `local` connection is lost. (i) `shipments_one_active_outbound` excludes only `cancelled` and `failed` (the text of `TDD_17 §2`), so an order whose package came back (`returned`) or was `delivered` cannot get a new outbound guide from the same warehouse: the re-ship of a returned package has no path yet; PR C1 decides it (exclude `returned` from the index in a migration, or create it through the return flow of Step 4.12) and writes the answer in `TDD_17 §4.8`. (j) `CarrierConnection.defaultServiceCode` is a free string (a `CarrierService.code` of the same connection by rule, not by key): PR B2 validates it on every write and when a service is deactivated or deleted. (k) `Shipment.idempotencyKey` is unique per store (`@@unique([storeId, idempotencyKey])`, not global as `TDD_17 §2` has it, so one store never learns that another uses a key); PR C1 builds it from the store, the order and the attempt. (l) The origin and restock warehouses are checked to belong to the organization of the store, not to be linked to the store through `store_warehouses` (the same weaker guarantee the inventory triggers give): PR C1 (shipments) and Step 4.12 (restock) check the link in the service, against the allocations of `TDD_16 §4.1`. (m) The open sets (`label_format`, `OrderMessageTemplate.kind`, `OrderReturn.reason`, `carrier_id`) have no CHECK on purpose (a private plugin or a new template kind needs no migration) and are validated by Zod in their services (B2, 4.9, 4.12). **Closed by PR B2:** (a) for `CarrierConnection`, `CarrierService` and `GuideSequence` (their lines are gone from `scripts/isolation-exemptions.ts` and `tests/security/tenant-isolation.spec.ts` has a case for each, reached by the route of the connection); (b) for `settings:manage_carriers`, and for `shipping:view`, which the read routes of B2 guard (PR C1 inserts the rest of `shipping:*`: `shipping:manage` and `shipping:manage_pickups`); (c) `StoreSettings.defaultCarrierConnectionId` stays a plain column, written only through `isDefault` of the carrier routes, which checks the connection against the store of the request in the same transaction, and cleared by the disconnect: no foreign key, because a disconnected connection is kept; (j) `defaultServiceCode` is checked on every write, and the services can be switched off only while the default one stays on; (m) the label format, the payer, the days and the times of a connection are validated by `connectCarrierSchema` and `updateCarrierSchema` (`libs/core/domain/carrier-connection-schemas.ts`).

- [x] **Debts of PR A (rule 12), each with its owner:** (a) the core draws the `LabelLayout` of an internal carrier with `@react-pdf/renderer` (not yet a dependency) and stores it as `Media` `SHIPPING_LABEL`, the localized `LabelTexts` need their `shipping.label.*` keys in `es` and `en`, and the renderer measures each line with the real font and scales down one wider than its `widthMm` (the layout wraps with a safe estimate that a run of W or M can still beat): PR B2, with `ShippingService`; (b) the plugin `name` and the one service label of `local` are English literals, like every carrier's own words, but they are the owner's default and rule 6 wants an i18n key: PR B2 seeds the `CarrierService` row from the store-locale key and the catalog shows the translated name; (c) the registry knows three `CarrierId`s, so the fake carrier borrows `forza` (standalone) or `c807` (pickup-bound) and an integration suite registers it instead of the real adapter, never beside it: PR B2 says so where it registers it, and Step 4.15 must keep the fake out of `PluginsModule`; (d) `scope:carriers` may import only the SDK and the domain, and nothing may import `scope:carriers` yet: the PR that registers `local` in `PluginsModule` (PR B2) adds `scope:carriers` to the tags `scope:plugins` may import and lists `localCarrierPlugin` with the official plugins; (e) the lint rule of the six states does not see a write whose data is built elsewhere (`data: row`, a spread) or sits inside a nested relation write, and its exemption block for `libs/core/shipping/**` could not be probed because the library does not exist: PR B2 proves with a probe file (as PR A did for the orders module) that a write there passes and one outside fails; (f) the contract suite covers what a plugin does at creation, cancel, webhook and pickups, not `fetchStatus` batching nor `lookupLocations`, and runs on fixtures made by hand: Step 4.15 adds the recorded provider fixtures of Forza and C807 and the cases for `fetchStatus` and `lookupLocations` with them; (g) the SDK changed shape with no consumer yet (`CancelShipmentResult.cancelled` became `refundStatus`, `ShipmentRef.reference` is required, a pickup batch answers per shipment): PR B2 is the first caller and keeps the shapes the SDK has, not the old ones. **Closed by PR B2:** (a) the drawing, the keys and the measuring (storing the file as `Media` is C1, see the `libs/carriers/local` item); (b) the services are seeded from `shipping.carriers.<id>.services.<code>` in the language of the store and `GET /carriers/registry` names the carrier from `shipping.carriers.<id>.name` (a plugin with no key keeps its own words); (c) `tests/api/carriers.spec.ts` says where it registers the fake (under `forza`, in the registry of that app only); (d) `scope:carriers` joins the tags `scope:plugins` may import and `localCarrierPlugin` is in `OFFICIAL_CARRIER_PLUGINS` (a plugin that is an object is provided by value); the new tag `scope:shipping` (`libs/core/shipping`) may import core, the domain, the SDK and the runtime, and the api and the worker may import it; (e) `scripts/lib/shipping-state-bans.spec.ts` lints the write of a shipment status as a file of the library (passes) and as a file of the api, the worker and the inventory engine (refused); (g) the connect, health check, disconnect and the two catalogs are called with the shapes the SDK has.
- [x] **Left by PR B2 (rule 12), each with its owner:** (a) the secret of the webhook is shown once, in the answer of the connect call, and nothing can show it again: a route that makes a new one (`POST /carriers/:id/webhook-secret`, audited, shown once) is PR C2, which builds the webhook that uses it. (b) The credentials an external carrier asks for are not declared by the plugin: `GET /carriers/registry` says only `hasCredentials`. Step 4.15 adds `connectFields` to `CarrierPlugin` (the declaration the channel plugins have) with Forza and C807, and Step 4.14 draws the form from it. (c) `GET /carriers/:id/locations` is Step 4.15 (no plugin implements `lookupLocations` yet, and its 24 hour cache is built with it); the status mapping routes are PR C2. (d) A disconnected connection keeps its shipments and its pickups, and the platform has not decided what its late events do (a webhook of a disabled connection, a poll, a cancel, a pickup that was due): PR C1 and PR C2 decide it and write the answer in `TDD_17 §4.9` and `§4.8`; until then nothing reads a disabled connection. (e) The label is drawn with the standard fonts, which cover the Latin alphabets: a name in another script (or an emoji) is refused with `LabelRenderError` (`isDrawable`) and so the guide is not made, rather than printed wrong. If the owner ships to such names, embed a font (and measure with it): Step 7.7. (e2) Uninstalling the shipping plugin disconnects every carrier even when shipments are still on their way: the catalog entry says how many, and the interface warns (Step 4.14); the api does not ask for a confirmation (the TDD says warn). (f) The plugins page of the web app lists `Shipping` but offers no install button (it installs only an entry with a `channel`), and the uninstall dialog does not use `openShipments` and `openPickups`: Step 4.14 draws both, with Settings → Carriers. (g) The list of connections has no `state` filter, so the connections that were let go stay in it: Step 4.14 adds it if the screen needs it (as `GET /channels/connections` has). (h) A connection of a plugin that is no longer registered (a private carrier removed) is listed with its id as the name and answers `409 SHIPPING_CARRIER_NOT_ACTIVE` to a test or an edit; nothing else reads it. (i) The pickup rules are stored and checked as the table says (days, times, the reminder) and nothing reads them until Step 4.11; `pollIntervalMinutes` is stored for the poller of PR C2. (k) The label is measured with `pdfkit` (`createLabelMeter`) and drawn with `@react-pdf/renderer`, two engines over the same standard Helvetica metrics; `fitLine` shrinks to the measure, so a difference between engines would show as a line a hair over its box and no test compares the two widths yet: Step 7.7 adds that check (or draws with `pdfkit` alone). (l) `CredentialCipher` has no associated data, so the credentials of a row are bound to it by the store filter of every read and not by the ciphertext; the same for the channels, and a change of cipher is Step 7.1. (m) Known limits of the connections, none fixed on purpose: replacing the credentials makes the carrier register a new account and the old one is not told (the platform no longer holds its credentials), Step 4.15 decides with each carrier whether `connect` is idempotent per account; a `test` that started before an edit can write its late failure over the new credentials' state (a version column on the connection, Step 7.3); a unique violation on the services, mappings or numbering rows is told as a name conflict (read the constraint, Step 7.3); `GET /carriers` loads the ciphertext of each row to say `hasCredentials` (select a boolean, Step 7.3); `disconnectAll` tells the carriers one after the other (concurrently, Step 7.3 if a store ever has many). (n) From the third security pass, not fixed on purpose: a `local` prefix that is changed frees the old one, and no table forbids the same guide number twice in a store (a unique `(store_id, guide_number)` index on shipments, Step 4.8 PR C1 with the first writer); `disconnect` tells the carrier before it takes the locks, so a replace of credentials racing it can leave a new account un-notified (Step 7.3, with (m)); a failed commit of `connect` whose row did commit would tell the carrier to forget the account (Step 7.3); a reinstall after a half failed uninstall disconnects outside the install transaction. Failed edits of credentials do not tell the carrier to forget the new ones (they may be a new key of the live account). (j) `GET /plugins/available` answers `openShipments` and `openPickups` for every entry (0 outside shipping), and the web schema of that answer ignores them until Step 4.14. **Closed by PR C2a:** (a) `POST /carriers/:id/webhook-secret`; (c) the status mapping routes; (d) for the webhook only: a connection that was let go answers like any unknown one (`401`, nothing queued, even if a secret were left on its row), decided in `TDD_17 §4.9`; what a late poll, cancel or pickup does for it is still C2b and C2c.
- [x] **Left by PR C1 (rule 12), each with its owner:** (a) the cancel of a guide is synchronous: the carrier is asked once inside the request, and a refusal or a carrier that does not answer leaves `CANCEL_FAILED` for the agent to retry or to mark voided; the `CANCEL_SHIPMENT` job with its five retries and exponential backoff, and the same for an idempotent create (today up to three calls with 250 ms and 500 ms pauses inside the request), are **PR C2** (the worker, with `PROCESS_CARRIER_EVENT`) and the bulk job of Step 4.10. (b) **The order does not follow its guide yet:** the listener of `TDD_17 §4.9` (`SHIPPED` with the inventory `ISSUE`, `DELIVERED`, the payment of a cash-on-delivery order, `codStatus`, the customer messages) is **PR C2**. Until it exists the status route of the order refuses `SHIPPED` and `DELIVERED` by hand while a guide is alive (`ORDER_STATUS_DRIVEN_BY_SHIPMENT`, `OrdersStatusService.changeStatus`); **C2 lifts that refusal for the system actor and keeps it for the people** (task of C2), and decides what to do with the manual events recorded before the listener existed (a shipment marked picked up in C1 never shipped its order). (c) An order whose guide is `LABEL_CREATED` is not cancelled until the guide is cancelled (`SHIPPING_ACTIVE_SHIPMENT_EXISTS`); `TDD_17 §4.8` wants the order cancelled at once and the guide voided by the job: **PR C2** with the job. `CREATING` and `REQUIRES_REVIEW` refuse the cancel (`SHIPPING_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN`) and a package that left refuses it (`SHIPPING_CANNOT_CANCEL_AFTER_PICKUP`), as the TDD says. (d) `SHIPMENT_REQUIRES_REVIEW` is published as an event and no notification is raised: the emitter and the removal of the three types from `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter` are **PR C2** (the item above). (e) "Check with the carrier" for a pickup-bound shipment is not built: the pickup of an unknown outcome has no `pickupRef` (the call never answered), so `fetchPickup` has nothing to ask with; the agent settles each shipment by hand (`status`) until **Step 4.15** decides with the real C807 how to find a pickup by its idempotency key. (f) The pickup rules are read only to choose the default day (`nextPickupDate`); the cutoff refusal `PICKUP_CUTOFF_PASSED` with its confirmation, taking a cancelled shipment out of its pickup (`modifyPickup`), and `EVALUATE_PICKUPS`/`ensurePickup` are **Step 4.11**. (g) A label from a carrier must be a PDF of at most 5 MB (`LABEL_MAX_BYTES`); an image wrapped into a PDF page, and asking the carrier again for a label that was not kept (`lastError: label_invalid`, or a guide with no label), are **Step 4.10** (labels and printing). (h) The sender phone has no source (`Warehouse` has none): a `local` guide prints the phone of its courier, an external carrier gets an empty one. **Step 4.15** (with `connectFields`) or **Step 7.7**. (i) `Order.carrierConnectionId` is written and judged but not in the order view or in `libs/api-client`: **Step 4.14** draws the planned carrier with the create dialog. (j) **Step 7.1:** the engine commits the `CREATING` row before it calls a carrier (rule of `TDD_17 §4.5`: a guide may cost money); the request interceptor that opens one tenant transaction for the whole request (`PrismaService.tenantTransaction`) must not wrap `ShippingService`, or the row would not exist when the carrier acts: run it outside that scope or from a job. (k) A fee is judged against the range only once the store has a connection (`planCarrier` answers `unmanaged` with none): a store that has not set carriers up (the screen is Step 4.14) is not stopped from charging a fee; with at least one active connection an order that charges a fee and names no carrier needs an approval or `ORDERS_OVERRIDE_PRICE` (`TDD_17 §4.13`). (l) `POST /plugin-api/shipments` and its events route (third-party carriers, `TDD_17 §4.1`) are **Step 5.6**. (m) A shipment stuck in `CREATING` is settled by an agent only after five minutes (`CREATING_STALE_MS`): before that a call may still be in flight. (n) Two `local` couriers cannot share a prefix, and a prefix printed on the guides of another courier cannot be taken (`assertPrefixFree` reads the shipments); no unique index on `(store_id, guide_number)` was added, on purpose: two carriers may print the same number and a guide the carrier already made must never be refused by our own table (`TDD_17 §2` defines the index per connection). (o) **From the fourth security pass over the connections (found and fixed in this PR):** the error of a plugin that fails in an unexpected way is no longer handed to the reporter (it could carry the request and its secret: only its name is logged and the answer is a plain 500); a connect that fails on a name that is taken no longer tells the carrier to forget the account (it may be the account of a live connection); an edit of a connection takes the installation lock and checks the installation again inside its transaction, as a connect does; and a courier that was let go and printed no guide frees its prefix (what it printed still reserves it). **Left on purpose:** (o2) disconnected connections are kept for good, so a loop of connect and disconnect grows the rows of a store (a cap would lock the store out, since nothing deletes them): purging a disconnected connection that has no shipment, pickup or planned order is **Step 7.3**; the same purge answers the `contact` (a courier's phone) that a disconnected row keeps (**Step 7.2**, retention); the connect and test routes that call a carrier have no throttle of their own (**Step 7.3**); `update` calls the carrier before it checks the settings of the body that only need the row (**Step 7.3**, with (m) of PR B2); a connect that fails on a name the plugin chose leaves the new account at the carrier (**Step 4.15**, an idempotent connect or an account the plugin can find again); (p) `plugin.connect` is not cancelled when the 15 s deadline passes, so a slow carrier may finish making an account the platform never hears of (a plugin must take an abort signal: **Step 4.15**, with the real carriers); (q) `GET /carriers/registry` lists every registered plugin to every member with `SHIPPING_VIEW`: harmless while only the official ones exist, a private plugin must be filtered by what its store may use (**Step 4.15**); (r) the credentials of an external carrier are an open map the plugin reads: a plugin must never fetch a URL it finds in them, and `connectFields` with `assertPublicHttpsUrl` is the platform's answer (**Step 4.15**); (s) `CredentialCipher` has no associated data, so a blob copied between rows opens (already (l) of PR B2, **Step 7.1**); (t) the races of `test` and `disconnect` against an edit of the credentials and the sequential `disconnectAll` are (m) and (n) of PR B2 (**Step 7.3**); (u) the webhook secret that is shown once and cannot be made again is (a) of PR B2 (`POST /carriers/:id/webhook-secret`, **PR C2**). (v) **From the review of the engine, left on purpose:** the `local` guide draws its PDF and puts it in the bucket inside the transaction that gives the number (so a failure gives the number back); a slow bucket holds the sequence row for up to 20 s and a Prisma timeout can leave the object in the bucket (the storage cleanup finds it by its prefix): **Step 7.3** (reserve the number, draw, then write); `prepareGuide` reads the connection without a lock and the carrier is called after the commit, so a disconnect that lands in between still sends one last call with the old credentials: **Step 7.3**; `createShipments` handles its requests one after another, so a batch of a hundred with a slow carrier is slow inside one request: the bulk job of **Step 4.10** runs it in the background with the per-connection parallelism of `TDD_17 §4.6`; the order cancel is allowed while a guide is `cancel_pending` or `cancel_failed` because `TDD_17 §4.8` says so (the guide is being voided), although a `cancel_failed` guide may still be alive: **Step 4.14** shows the warning on the order and may ask for a confirmation; the three copies that open the credentials of a connection for a call (`callAndRecord`, `callPickupBatch`, `cancel`) become one helper with the worker (**PR C2**). (w) **Third review pass of the engine (10 findings, the cap of the tool):** eight fixed in the PR (a cancelled guide named by an earlier event, the order number of an event, the amount collected when an order is allocated again after a guide, the pickup left `PENDING` after a fault, the shipment named in each error of a batch, the notes of the customer, the provider id of a found guide, the blank recipient name, the magic strings); **left:** "check with the carrier" can adopt for one shipment in review the guide of its sibling in review when the carrier has only that one under the shared order number (**Step 4.15**, with the recorded fixtures of the real carriers, which say what a lookup by reference returns), and one unique violation inside a pickup chunk rolls back the whole chunk (the order locks make it rare; **Step 7.3**). **Closed by PR C1:** (a) of PR B1 for `Shipment` and `ShipmentEvent` (their lines are gone from `scripts/isolation-exemptions.ts`, `tests/security/tenant-isolation.spec.ts` has a case for each); (b) of PR B1, `shipping:manage` and `shipping:manage_pickups` are inserted into the roles that exist (migration `20261129100000_shipment_engine`); (d) of PR B1, the order keeps its trigger and gets the composite key to the connection; (i) of PR B1, a `returned` package no longer holds its order (the partial index excludes it, `TDD_17 §4.8`); (k) of PR B1, the key is `shipment:{orderId}:{originWarehouseId}:{attempt}`; (l) of PR B1, the origin must be linked to the store (`prepareGuide`); (d) of PR B2, the rule for a disconnected connection is in `TDD_17 §4.8` and `§4.9`; (n) of PR B2, see (n) above.
- [x] **Left by PR C2a (rule 12), each with its owner:** (a) until PR C2c the worker resolves each event for its connection and records the codes nobody mapped, and then drops it: no shipment is touched (C1 is the first to make one), so nothing is lost today, but C2c must start from `CarrierEventResolver.resolve`, must not leave the window open, and must tie the first-sighting signal to the creation of the row (`resolve` is not idempotent: a retried job counts the code twice and loses the first sighting); a mapping `PUT` racing an event can also reopen a code just mapped (rare, harmless). (b) The notification `CARRIER_STATUS_UNMAPPED` on the first sighting hangs from `firstUnmappedSighting` of `resolve` and is C2c, with the two other switches of the item below. (c) `PUT` of a mapping marks the waiting code as resolved but does not enqueue `REPROCESS_CARRIER_EVENTS` (the job and the stored events do not exist yet) and the waiting list has no "shipments waiting" count: C2c. (d) The webhook and the worker read the connection by its id alone, with no tenant context (`TDD_17 §4.12`): the `SECURITY DEFINER` function and `TenantContext.run` are Step 7.1. (e) A shared-secret carrier (C807) proves less than a signature, and the confirm-before-apply of `DELIVERED` and `RETURNED` through `fetchStatus` is C2c; the signature scheme of each real carrier is its plugin's (Step 4.15 writes Forza and C807 with recorded fixtures), and a replayed request is harmless only once the dedupe of C2c exists. (f) The unknown-address answer runs a decoy comparison so it is not much faster than a bad signature; it is not constant-time against the plugin's own check, and the per-address limit (600 a minute, shared by every store behind one carrier address) is what bounds guessing connection ids; a limit per connection after the signature check is Step 7.3 (accepted). (g) The unmapped record keeps the carrier's status text and never its description, because a description can carry a name or an address, so the screen of Step 4.14 shows the status only. (h) The list of mappings holds the rows, so a catalog code with no default and no row is not in it: it appears in the waiting list when it first arrives; Step 4.14 merges the catalog into the table if the screen needs the full list. (i) A new webhook secret replaces the old one at once, with no overlap: the owner updates the carrier's panel right after; an overlap window is Step 4.15 if a carrier needs one. (k) The signature is checked over the body decoded as UTF-8 (the plugin's API takes a string; the carriers send JSON), and the endpoint has no timestamp window: a replay is harmless only once the dedupe of C2c exists. The room of waiting codes per connection is approximate under concurrency. (j) The job schema (`exactly<ProcessCarrierEventJob>()`) is in the worker because `plugins-runtime` may not import `core-queues`; the api checks the event with `carrierEventSchema` before queueing.
- [x] **Built in PR C2b (the order follows its shipment; rule 12 debts with their owners):** `OrderShipmentEffects` (`apps/api/src/modules/orders`) and its listener on `SHIPMENT_STATUS_CHANGED`: the first package that leaves ships the order (inventory `ISSUE` by the idempotent keys of `TDD_16 §4.1`, `shippedAt` from the first event of the history), the last one that arrives delivers it (`deliveredAt` from the event) and completes a cash-on-delivery payment (`ORDER_PAYMENT_STATUS_CHANGED`, actor of the event), all derived from the state the shipment and the order hold under the lock of the order (`planOrderFollow`, pure, in `libs/core/domain/shipment-effects.ts`), so an event heard twice, two at once or a lost one healed by the next one end in the same place; a manual status of the guide (`POST /shipments/:id/status`) takes the same path as a carrier event, `ShipmentWrites.move` moves a cash-on-delivery package to `PENDING_REMITTANCE` when it is delivered, a package that left for an order that is cancelled or not confirmed asks a person (`SHIPMENT_REQUIRES_REVIEW`, said once, when it leaves) and an effect that fails (no stock to issue) is logged and tells the team the same way while the carrier's word stays recorded. `ShipmentStatusEngine` (`libs/core/shipping`) is the one place a status from a carrier becomes a move: dedupe by `(shipmentId, providerEventId)` or by `(status, occurredAt)`, an older or not-allowed event kept in the history and moving nothing, `FAILED_ATTEMPT` to itself counted in `failedAttempts`, a disconnected connection dropped (`TDD_17 §4.9`); the poll (`ShipmentPollScheduler` every ten minutes on one replica, `POLL_SHIPMENT_STATUS` on the heavy queue, `ShipmentPoller` in the worker) asks each connection at its own interval and applies through the engine. `SHIPMENT_STATUS_CHANGED` and `SHIPMENT_REQUIRES_REVIEW` cross the bridge from the worker. `orders.failedAttemptMessages` (default `every_attempt`) is in `orderPreferencesSchema`, `orderMessageAutoSendSchema` refuses `autoSend` for `ORDER_SUMMARY` and `ORDER_FAILED_ATTEMPT`, `failedAttemptVariables` renders `attemptNumber` and `maxAttempts`; `shipment.requires_review` and `shipment.failed_attempt` have their emitter and left `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter`. **Left, each with its owner:** (a) the customer messages of the table of `TDD_17 §4.9` (`ORDER_SHIPPED`, `ORDER_OUT_FOR_DELIVERY`, `ORDER_DELIVERED`, `ORDER_FAILED_ATTEMPT` by the preference) are **Step 4.9**: they need the templates and `SEND_ORDER_MESSAGE`; the rule (`isFailedAttemptMessageDue`) and the variables are ready, and the events carry what that step needs. (b) `PROCESS_CARRIER_EVENT` (signature, the mapping of a carrier code, the confirm-before-apply of a shared secret for `DELIVERED` and `RETURNED`, the unmapped record and its notification), `REPROCESS_CARRIER_EVENTS`, the processor of the `webhooks` queue with `queue-coverage.spec` and the `CANCEL_SHIPMENT` job are **PR C2c**: its processor calls `ShipmentStatusEngine.apply`; a polled event whose status the plugin cannot read is counted (`unreadable`) and left for the mapping of C2c. `shipment.cancel_failed` and `carrier.status_unmapped` stay in `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter` until then. (c) With split fulfillment the first package that leaves issues the stock of every allocation of the order (`OrderInventoryService.issue` works per order, not per warehouse of the package): **Step 4.10**, with the creation of split shipments. (d) The effects run in the api after the commit and the worker reaches them through Redis (best effort, one instance): a lost event leaves the order behind its shipment until the next event of that shipment, and nothing sweeps for it: **Step 7.4** (a sweep of orders behind their shipment, with the dead-letter watch). (e) The shipments whose manual events were recorded before the listener existed are not backfilled (no store holds a real guide yet); the next event of each heals it. (f) The hand-made `SHIPPED` and `DELIVERED` of an order with a live guide keep their `409 ORDER_STATUS_DRIVEN_BY_SHIPMENT` for people: the order follows its guide through `OrderShipmentEffects`, which is not that route, and a person who has to move it moves the shipment. (g) `orders.failedAttemptMessages` has no write path yet: **Step 4.14** (the `orders` block of the preferences). (h) The packages older than two weeks are asked once a day in the run of 04:00 UTC; a carrier that needs another cadence for them is **Step 4.15**, with the real carriers. (i) The poll jobs share the `heavy-processing` queue with the scans and the stock reconciliation: a slow carrier holds a slot for its timeouts (the poll stops at the first failed call of a connection); a queue of its own or a limiter per connection is **Step 7.4**. (j) Each polled event is one transaction under the store lock (the twins are found before it); a poll that returns a long history for many packages still takes the lock many times: a batch per shipment is **Step 7.3**. (k) A polled event whose status the plugin cannot read, or that names a guide the connection does not hold, is counted and dropped, not recorded: its record belongs with the unmapped-status table of **PR C2c**. (l) A package that needs the carrier's word to be believed (a shared secret, `DELIVERED` and `RETURNED`) is confirmed by `fetchStatus` before the move, in the processor of **PR C2c**; a poll asks the carrier itself, so it needs no confirmation. (n) ShipmentStatusEngine is covered only by the integration suite (tests/api/shipment-status-engine.spec.ts), with no coverage threshold of its own: it touches the database, like the rest of the engine, so libs/core/shipping/jest.config.cts leaves it out of the unit coverage (the 90/85 threshold is unchanged); measuring the coverage of the integration suites is Step 7.7 (quality). (m) The order follows its packages and never waits for a price that was left pending: the goods are already with the carrier, so the approval does not hold the order back (it holds the guide, `TDD_17 §4.13`).
- [x] **Built in PR C2c (migration `20261201100000_carrier_event_lookups`, its rollback, `tests/api/carrier-event-processing.spec.ts`, `tests/api/carrier-status-unmapped-notification.spec.ts`):** the worker applies what a carrier says (`CarrierEventApplier`): the event is resolved for its connection (the mapping of the connection wins over the plugin, codes normalized, an ignored code kept with no effect), the shipment is found inside that connection by the carrier's id, then the guide number, then the order number (`findShipmentOfEvent`; an order number that names two live packages, or a guide nobody has, moves nothing), and a status goes through the engine of C2b (dedupe, order of arrival, the table). A code nobody mapped is kept in the history with no status, recorded in `CarrierUnmappedStatus` and announced (`Events.CARRIER_STATUS_UNMAPPED`, bridged, `NotificationTypes.CARRIER_STATUS_UNMAPPED` to `SETTINGS_MANAGE_CARRIERS`) **in one transaction with the history line**, so a job that is tried again finds the line and does nothing: the first sighting is neither lost nor told twice (it closes (a) and (b) of PR C2a). `REPROCESS_CARRIER_EVENTS` (`heavy-processing`) reads the lines kept under a code and its reasons, oldest first, re-resolves each through the mapping, and settles each in its own transaction (`ShipmentReplayer`: the line gets its status, or moves the shipment when the table allows it; the move announces `replay: true`); `PUT` of a mapping (not an ignore) and `DELETE` that restores a default queue it, and the list of waiting codes gives `shipmentsWaiting` (closes (c) of PR C2a). A carrier that declares `webhookTrust: shared_secret` (C807; the registry demands `FETCH_STATUS` of it) is asked through `fetchStatus` before DELIVERED or RETURNED is applied, from a webhook and from a replay; a mismatch is dropped and logged as suspicious (closes (e) and (k) of PR C2a with the dedupe). Two indexes find the shipment of a connection by guide number and by the carrier's id, and a partial index holds the lines with no status. `recordUnmappedStatus` inserts with `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` so it can run inside a transaction.
- [x] **Left by PR C2c (rule 12), each with its owner:** (a) the `replay` flag travels on `SHIPMENT_STATUS_CHANGED`; the listener that writes to the customer (Step 4.9) and any push of a team notification must honor it (an in-app notice is written, a customer message and a push are not). (b) A replay asks the carrier once for each DELIVERED or RETURNED line of a shared-secret carrier: batching those questions into one `fetchStatus` call is **Step 4.15**, with C807's recorded fixtures. (c) An event that names no shipment of its connection is dropped and logged, not kept (a history line needs its shipment): a guide the core never stored because the outcome was unknown is found by "check with the carrier" (**Step 4.15**), and the carrier may send the event again. (d) The lines of a code are found by the JSON path of their payload, under the partial index of the lines with no status; an index on the code itself, and a cap on the lines a shipment may keep, are **Step 7.3**. (e) The replay runs to the end of the lines it finds in one job: a very large backlog would hold the worker, so a page limit with a requeue is **Step 7.3**. (f) The notification of a first sighting is announced after the commit; if the api is down when it arrives the owner is not told again (the list of waiting codes in **Step 4.14** still shows it). (g) The history of a shipment keeps every line a carrier sends: nothing bounds a hostile or noisy carrier (**Step 7.3**, with the cap of (d)). (h) An event that cannot be settled yet (a closing status the carrier's own books do not back, or a guide that is not stored) is asked again by the queue's own retries, a window of seconds; a carrier whose books lag longer ends in the dead letter queue until the poll or a re-sent webhook settles it, and each retry of a forged claim costs one question to the carrier (the throttle of the webhook bounds it): a delayed requeue with a longer backoff is **Step 7.4** (queue operations). (i) The poller sends every event through the resolver and the twin checks one by one: loading the mappings once per batch is **Step 7.3**. (j) Only DELIVERED and RETURNED are confirmed for a carrier that shares a secret, as `TDD_17 §4.9` says; a forged FAILED_ATTEMPT, RETURNING or PICKED_UP from the holder of that secret is bounded by the transition table only (a carrier that signs nothing is accepted for now, and the real C807 is **Step 4.15**). (l) The replay job gets the heavy queue's two attempts: a carrier that stays down for longer leaves the lines kept (the code is already resolved), and a second `PUT` of the mapping queues the replay again; a longer backoff for it, and a `jobId` that does not block a later replay, are **Step 7.4**. A repeated `PUT` queues a repeated replay, which finds nothing left to apply (and asks a shared-secret carrier only about lines still kept): bounded by the per-IP limit, **Step 7.3** if it needs more. (k) A connection in `error` takes no event (the engine requires `active`, as `TDD_17 §4.9` says): what its carrier pushes meanwhile is dropped, and the poll covers the gap once it recovers.
- [x] **From the QA fix PRs (QA backlog, rule 12), the `webhooks` queue (left by Q4):** this step is the first to queue on `QueueNames.WEBHOOKS` (`PROCESS_CARRIER_EVENT`; Step 5.4 adds `TRIGGER_WEBHOOK` and Step 5.6 `TRIGGER_PLUGIN_WEBHOOK`; BullMQ hands a job to any worker of a queue, so those steps extend `CarrierEventsProcessor` with a dispatch by job name, or use a queue of their own, and never add a second `@Processor` on `webhooks`). Add its processor in `apps/worker` and remove the `QueueNames.WEBHOOKS` entry of `WITHOUT_PROCESSOR` in `apps/worker/src/processors/queue-coverage.spec.ts` in the same PR, so that spec keeps failing for a queue that has jobs and no consumer (the reason text there says "Milestone 4"; this step is the one) **Done in PR C2a:** `CarrierEventsProcessor` consumes `webhooks` and the entry is gone from `WITHOUT_PROCESSOR`; it is the minimal processor (see PR C2a above), and PR C2c completes it.

**TDD:** `TDD_17 §1`, `§2`, `§3`, `§4.1`, `§4.2`, `§4.4`, `§4.5`, `§4.9`, `§4.12`, `§4.13`, `§4.17`, `TDD_10 §3`, `§9`, `TDD_16 §4.1`
**Done when:** with the fake carrier, an order creates exactly one shipment even under two parallel requests,
an unknown outcome never creates a second guide, a duplicate or out-of-order webhook changes nothing,
every shipment status produces the order effect of `TDD_17 §4.9`, and a `local` shipment gets a unique
guide and a printable label; three failed attempts on three days count three and then a delivery completes the order; a code the plugin does not know is recorded once, and mapping it replays the stored events in order; the contract suite runs in `integration-tests`.

### Step 4.9 — Order confirmation and customer messages
- [x] `PENDING_CONFIRMATION` in the order state machine, with `confirmedAt`, `confirmedBy`, `confirmedVia`; "mark as confirmed" (single and bulk, `ORDERS_CONFIRM`); `ShippingService` refuses guides for unconfirmed orders **(built across three PRs: the state, the columns, the single confirmation and `confirmOnCreate` in Step 4.3 (PR B, no migration needed here: the columns of `TDD_17 §2` came with the first schema), the refusal `SHIPPING_ORDER_NOT_CONFIRMED` in Step 4.8, and in Step 4.9 PR B `POST /orders/confirm-bulk` (`OrdersStatusService.confirmBulk`: at most `OrderLimits.BULK_CONFIRM_MAX` ids, one way of confirming for all, each order a change of its own with its own audit entry, answers `{ succeeded, failed: [{ id, code }] }`, an order of another store is reported as `RESOURCE_NOT_FOUND`))**
- [x] `OrderMessagesService`: the generic `ORDER_MESSAGE` email template for the fallback (replacing the four order emails), the "Notify customer" checkbox of manual status changes initialised from the matching template's `autoSend`, and templates per kind and locale with the variable allowlist (including `scheduledDeliveryDate`: a send whose template uses it is refused as not sent, reason `MISSING_VARIABLE`, for an order without a date, `TDD_17 §4.7`; the template editor warns when an `autoSend` template uses it, because every order without a date would then go unsent), rendering by substitution, preview, `autoSend` (not for the order summary, which follows the store preference) and `attachSummary`, the order summary document, sending through the conversation's channel as an outbound `Message`
- [x] The messaging-window decision of `TDD_17 §4.7`: free text inside the window, the linked channel-approved template outside it (variables mapped in order), and free text always for a channel that declares no window, email fallback or the `ORDER_MESSAGE_NOT_SENT` notification otherwise; idempotent automatic sends per `(shipmentId, status, kind)`
- [x] Settings → Order messages API (`SETTINGS_MANAGE_ORDER_TEMPLATES`) with the link to an approved channel template; whether a message goes as free text or as a template comes only from the channel plugin's declared `messagingWindow` and `TEMPLATES` capability, never from its name
- [x] Store preferences (`TDD_17 §4.14`): `storePreferencesSchema` as the only place of factory defaults (including the editable `orders.cancellationReasons` list, seeded from `OrderCancellationReasons`), `StoreSettings.preferences`, `GET` and `PUT /settings/preferences` (`SETTINGS_MANAGE_PREFERENCES`), the ESLint rule that forbids a hardcoded initial state on checkboxes and switches in `apps/web`; the summary is sent on creation according to `orders.sendSummaryOnCreate` (default on) and `autoSend` is refused for `ORDER_SUMMARY` **Already started in Step 3.1 (owner, 2026-10-29):** `libs/core/platform/config/store-preferences.ts` exists with `storePreferencesSchema` and `readStorePreferences`, holding only `sendReadReceipts` (default `true`), which `ReadReceipts` reads; extend that file instead of creating a second source of defaults, keep `sendReadReceipts` as it is, and note that Step 3.6 draws its switch, so the `GET` and `PUT /settings/preferences` route must exist by then (build it in 3.6 with the keys that exist and let this task add the rest, or move this task before 3.6)
  - **Built in PR A of this step (preferences, rule 12; the box stays open for the creation send of PR C):** `storePreferencesSchema` now holds the `orders` block (`confirmOnCreate` false, `sendSummaryOnCreate` true, `cancellationReasons` seeded from `OrderCancellationReasons`, next to the keys of Steps 4.4 and 4.8), `GET` answers it and `PUT /settings/preferences` writes `orders.confirmOnCreate`, `orders.sendSummaryOnCreate` and `orders.cancellationReasons` merged key by key (audited as `orders.<key>`); the ESLint rule `web-controls/no-fixed-initial-checked` (`eslint-web-controls.cjs`, spec in `scripts/lib`) refuses `defaultChecked`, a literal `checked` and `checked={x}` from `useState(true)` on checkboxes and switches of `apps/web`; the refusal of `autoSend` for `ORDER_SUMMARY` was already `orderMessageAutoSendSchema` (`shipment-effects.ts`). **Left, each with its owner:** (a) the other keys of the block (`discountLimitPercent`, `notifyOnPriceOverride`, `freeShippingRule`, `failedAttemptMessages`) are read but have no write path: the Preferences page that draws them adds it, **Step 4.14**. (b) The cancel dialog still lists the factory reasons: it must read `orders.cancellationReasons`, but `GET /settings/preferences` needs `SETTINGS_VIEW` and a member who may cancel an order may not hold it, so the orders api has to hand the list to whoever cancels; **Step 4.14** with the Preferences page that edits the list. The api keeps the text of the reason as a snapshot and never validates it against the list. (c) The rule refuses a control that starts checked and leaves `useState(false)` alone (an unchecked filter is not a default of the store); the two create-order checkboxes read their initial value from the preferences in **PR B** (`confirmOnCreate`) and **PR C** (`sendSummaryOnCreate`), and the send of the summary on creation is **PR C**. (d) The rule follows the plain forms only: `checked={flag ?? true}`, `useReducer(r, true)`, a `useState` renamed on import and a string `checked="true"` are not caught (a hardening pass, **Step 7.7**). (e) `cancellationReasons` is written as a whole list (last write wins, under the store lock) and the audit keeps its ids, not its text.
- [x] Confirm at creation (`confirmOnCreate`, initial value from `orders.confirmOnCreate`, default false, `ORDERS_CONFIRM`) and `GET /orders/:id/copy` (a prefilled draft with warnings, current prices, `copiedFromOrderId`) **(Step 4.9 PR B, rule 12 decisions and debts: (a) `confirmOnCreate` itself was built in Step 4.3; the server never reads `orders.confirmOnCreate`, because the preference only gives the initial state of the checkbox, which the create-order drawer draws (Step 4.14, with `GET /settings/preferences` of PR A of this step). (b) `GET /orders/:id/copy` needs `ORDERS_VIEW` and `ORDERS_CREATE` (the draft carries the contact data and the notes), writes nothing, takes an order of any status, and answers `{ draft, lines, warnings }`: `draft` is shaped as the body of `POST /orders` (customer, lines with no price, fee, payment method, address, phones, the three notes, `copiedFromOrderId`) so the create path applies every rule again; the discount, the scheduled day, the thread and the planned carrier are not copied (the store's default carrier is planned again: a fee outside its range then asks for approval as any order's would); `lines` carries today's catalog price and the one the old line had; `warnings` (`OrderCopyWarnings`, pure rules in `libs/core/domain/order-copy.ts`) name the lines whose product or variant is off or gone, that need a variant now, that the reservation would refuse for stock (only a tracked product; it runs the same `planLineAllocation` as the create, so one warehouse must hold the line unless the store splits, and a store that allows negative stock is never warned) or whose catalog price changed (an override is compared with the catalog price the line had, not with the price the agent set); a line with a warning stays in the draft and the create refuses it if it is still wrong. A merged customer is replaced by the survivor, an erased one is refused (`CUSTOMER_ERASED`); the draft is a plain read with no transaction and no row lock, so it never waits for a merge. Naming `copiedFromOrderId` in a create needs `ORDERS_VIEW` too (an order that exists and one that does not answer the same 403 to a creator who cannot read orders). **Limits accepted on purpose (rule 12, review of this PR):** the draft copies the internal and packing notes of the old order as they are (the agent sees them in the drawer; a marker is a drawing decision for Step 4.14); it keeps the fee but not the carrier or the reason of an out-of-range fee, so the create may ask for a reason or an approval (the answer says which); an address stored in a shape the current schema no longer accepts is left out of the draft without a warning (orders are stored through that same schema, so only data of an older shape can hit it; Step 4.14 shows an empty address as empty); `POST /orders/confirm-bulk` runs its orders one after the other inside one request (at most 100: a retry after a timeout reports the confirmed ones as `ORDER_ALREADY_CONFIRMED`, never twice), a queue only if a real store needs more than that. **Backlog from the test review, not in this PR:** `orderCopySchema` of the api-client repeats the shape of `OrderCopyDraft` by hand (Step 4.14 may derive it from `createOrderSchema` when it draws the drawer), `confirmOrdersBulk` does not chunk or de-duplicate a selection (the list of Step 4.14 sends at most `OrderLimits.BULK_CONFIRM_MAX` distinct ids), and the isolation case of the bulk route does not assert that no audit row exists for the foreign order. The lines come in the order of their ids, the same order as the detail of an order. (c) `POST /orders` takes `copiedFromOrderId`, checks it is an order of the store (404 otherwise), stores it, and the detail shows it only to who may read orders. (d) The buttons ("Copy order", "Mark as confirmed" in bulk) and the drawer are Step 4.14 (`orders.confirmBulk` and `orders.copy` of `scripts/frontend-coverage-map.ts`).)**
