  entry; a customer who was erased or who withdrew their consent is recorded but raises no alert. `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter` no longer lists the type. The
  system's channel message is an outbound `Message` of `AuthorType.SYSTEM` (as the away reply), `TEXT` or `TEMPLATE` with the template metadata the worker already
  reads. **Decisions:** (1) no `SEND_ORDER_MESSAGE` job: sending needs the plugin registry and the credentials of the api (the approved template is checked against the
  provider) and the channel send itself already goes through the outbound queue, which is the durable part; the listeners of PR C2b call the service in the api
  with the same retries `ShipmentEffectsListener` has, and the job name stays declared in `QueueNames` for Step 4.15 if a worker ever needs it. (2) Consent: the
  platform has no flow that records a consent yet (the `ConsentRecord` of `TDD_05 §10` is **Step 7.2**), so the rule is the one that can be true today: no record is not
  a refusal (the messages about an order rest on the contract, `LegalBases.CONTRACT`), the latest record of purpose `order_messages` with `granted: false` or a
  `revokedAt` is, and a customer with `erasedAt` is never written to; **Step 7.2** builds the records and the screen that writes them. (3) A text longer than what
  the channel declares (`limits.textMaxLength`) is `not_sent` with `too_long` and does not fall back to email, as `MISSING_VARIABLE`. (4) The dispatch is a plain
  foreign key to the order with `ON DELETE CASCADE` (an order has no `(store_id, id)` key); the service always reads the order by store, so a foreign order is a 404.
  **Left, each with its owner:** (a) **C2b**: the listeners of the table of `TDD_17 §4.9` (`ORDER_SHIPPED`, `ORDER_OUT_FOR_DELIVERY`, `ORDER_DELIVERED`,
  `ORDER_FAILED_ATTEMPT` by `orders.failedAttemptMessages`, the `replay` rule), the summary on creation by `orders.sendSummaryOnCreate`, the "Notify customer" box of manual
  changes from `autoSend`, `attachSummary` and the document, and the kind of an attachment in `CHANNEL_CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED`. (b) The timeline of the order does not
  read the dispatches yet (no route lists them): **Step 4.14** with the order drawer, which draws "not sent, reason" and the "send" button; a `GET` of the dispatches of an order
  is built with it. (c) The api-client has no function for `POST /orders/:id/messages` (the drawer is **Step 4.14**). (d) A thread line that says a message is an order message
  (the metadata `orderMessage` is written, nothing draws it): **Step 4.14**. (e) The provider is asked for the template list at every send that needs it (no cache):
  fine for the volume of the MVP, a short cache per connection is **Step 7.3**. (f) A queued channel message or email that later fails for good (the provider refuses it, the address bounces) is a failed
  bubble in the thread or a failed job, and the dispatch still says `channel_*` or `email`, with no `order.message_not_sent`: closing that loop (the delivery status of the message
  or the email writes back to the dispatch, raises the alert and takes the email fallback) is **Step 7.4** (resilience of the outbound); the same step covers a window that
  closes between the decision and the write. (g) The email is queued first, under an id made from the key, and the dispatch is claimed after it, so a process that dies in
  between leaves an email that a retry sends again (once the first job has completed and been removed the id no longer dedupes); the other order would lose it silently, which
  is worse: an outbox for the email is **Step 7.4**. A provider that does not answer, or any error of ours that is not known to be permanent, throws before anything is claimed,
  so the listeners' retries (and the press of a person) send it later. (h) The manual route refuses `return_approved` and `exchange_shipped`: they are about a shipment that is
  not the outbound one of the order, and **Step 4.12** gives the send that shipment (and the route its kind). (i) The manual route has no cap per order: it needs
  `ORDERS_SEND_MESSAGES`, is audited and every press is a new message to a customer, so a cap per order and hour with the rate limits is **Step 7.1**. (j) The migration builds
  `orders_store_id_id_key` (the key the composite foreign key points at) without `CONCURRENTLY`, as every index of the migrations: fine for the volume of the MVP,
  **Step 7.3** re-measures it with the other indexes.

- [x] **Built in PR C2b (what sends the messages by itself; rule 12 decisions and debts):** `OrderMessageListener` (`apps/api/src/modules/order-messages`), on three events and after
  the commit that caused each: (1) `order.created` sends the summary (`orders.sendSummaryOnCreate`; `sendConfirmationMessage` of the create body is now optional: left out, the preference decides, said, the
  person decides, in both directions; the key is `creationDispatchKey(orderId)`); (2) `order.status_changed` made by a **person** sends the kind of the status (`OrderStatusMessageKinds`: shipped, delivered, cancelled)
  with the "Notify customer" box: `notifyCustomer` (optional on `POST /orders/:id/status` and `/cancel`) checked sends (a missing template is told to the team), unchecked sends nothing, not touched
  follows the `autoSend` of the template of the kind (the box of the interface starts from `GET /order-templates`, which already answers it); a status that the order reached by following its shipment is not told here
  (the shipment's message is), key `manualDispatchKey(orderId, kind, status)`; (3) `shipment.status_changed` sends what the table of `TDD_17 §4.9` says (`customerMessageOfShipment`: picked up or the first in transit is **one**
  shipped message, out for delivery, delivered, and a failed attempt by `orders.failedAttemptMessages` with its number in the key, so three attempts are three messages and a repeated event is none), key
  `automaticDispatchKey`, `shipmentId` and the connection's `maxDeliveryAttempts` fill the variables. Never for a `replay` (deuda (a) of PR C2c: the customer is not written to, and the team's failed-attempt notice is
  written in the center only: `SendNotification.inAppOnly` takes out push, sound and email), never for the package of an order that was cancelled, only for an outbound package. `OrderMessagesService.send` takes
  `onlyIfAutoSend`: a kind whose template is not set to go by itself, or that the store never made, sends nothing and records nothing (`SKIPPED`), so a store that never set its messages up is not alerted at every status;
  the summary and the failed attempt do not use it (the summary follows its preference; the failed attempt, its own). `attachSummary`: the template of the **summary** asks for the PDF (`renderOrderSummaryPdf`),
  the channel declares `DOCUMENT` and takes a PDF of that size (its declared file rule), and then the PDF is put in the bucket, a `Media` row (purpose `order`, clean: the platform drew it) is linked to a second
  `DOCUMENT` message of the thread right after the text, both queued; otherwise only the text goes (never the other way round). The listener asks again 3 times when a send fails for a cause that may pass and then, with nothing claimed (a later event
  of the shipment sends it), tells the team (`order.message_not_sent`, reason `send_failed`, which is only ever an event and never stored); a message that only goes if its template says so is
  logged, not alerted, because the store may never have wanted it, and an order that is gone is not asked again. The alert reaches the person whose action started a message that goes after
  the commit (a creation, a status): only a person who pressed "send" has the answer of their request. The change of status that the order makes by following its shipment carries
  `notifyCustomer: false`, so the shipment's message is the only one, and a package does not repeat what a person already told by marking the order by hand (the same key that change made). `CHANNEL_CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED` carries the kind (`metadata.kind`, `unsupportedKind` in `caption-rules.ts`) and the attachment tray names it, in `es` and `en`, with one case per kind in each spec.
  **Decisions:** (1) the summary on creation and the shipment messages use the same `send` as a person's press, so every rule of PR C2a (window, template, email, consent, idempotency) holds for them. (2) A manual
  change with a box left alone and no template does nothing, while the same with the box checked is told: the owner asked for the message only in the second case. (3) The messages of the shipment are told from the
  status the shipment has now (`failedAttempts` read at send time), and the cancelled-order rule reads the order at that moment. **Left, each with its owner:** (a) a store with no templates gets no automatic message and
  no alert except for the summary: seeding default templates for a new store is **Step 4.14**, with the templates screen. (b) The send of the failed-attempt message under `first_only` and `none` follows the
  preference at the moment of the event; changing the preference later does not send what was skipped. (c) The email fallback never carries the summary document (the text always goes, as `TDD_17 §4.7` says):
  attaching it to the email is **Step 7.4** with the email outbox. (d) The `OutboundQueue` job of the document is queued with the text; a channel that refuses the document fails that bubble only and the text stands:
  turning that failure into an alert is the delivery write-back of **Step 7.4** ((f) of PR C2a). (e) The failed-attempt number in the key and the policy decision read `failedAttempts` of the shipment when the listener
  runs, not at the moment of the event (the payload carries no count): two failed attempts handled within milliseconds can collapse into one message; the team's notice is written for each. Carrying
  the count in `SHIPMENT_STATUS_CHANGED` (and the bridge) is **Step 7.4**. (f) The email is queued before it is claimed, and the listener's retries make reachable the case where the first job already
  completed and was removed when the claim failed: a second email (the case of (g) of PR C2a). (g) The text and the PDF are two messages queued apart: the document can reach the customer first, or
  alone if the text fails; one job for both is **Step 7.4** with the outbound resilience. A summary drawn and stored before the claim costs a render and a bucket write when two events race for it:
  accepted, the loser removes its object. (h) The summary on creation and the failed attempt alert the team when the store has no template of the kind (they follow a preference, not `autoSend`):
  the seed of defaults is (a) above, **Step 4.14**. (i) The create-order form of the web still sends `sendConfirmationMessage: false` (Step 4.4 decided that no message goes to the customer that the agent did not ask for), so the preference `orders.sendSummaryOnCreate` decides only for orders made through the api: the drawer's box reads the preference and sends its value in **Step 4.14**. (j) The attachment tray of the interface names the kind from the file it holds, not from the error: a channel
  that refuses a kind only on the server answers the same code and the tray uses the same text (the error carries `kind` for the clients that have no file at hand): **Step 7.7** if a screen needs more.

**TDD:** `TDD_17 §4.3`, `§4.7`, `TDD_10 §2`, `§4`, `§5.1`, `TDD_09 §5`
**Done when:** a new order stays `PENDING_CONFIRMATION` unless created with the confirm checkbox, a guide for an unconfirmed order is refused, a copied order
starts from a draft with current prices, a channel plugin that declares no window gets free text at any time, the
summary goes as free text inside the window and as the approved template outside it, a missing template
produces the fallback or the notification and never a silent drop, and a repeated carrier event sends
nothing twice.

### Step 4.10 — Labels, bulk generation and cancellation
- [x] Single and bulk guide creation: `POST /shipments/bulk` with `BulkLabelJob`, per-connection concurrency cap for standalone carriers and one call per batch (with the pickup date and window) for pickup-bound ones, skipped orders reported with the reason, `BULK_LABELS_PROGRESS` and `BULK_LABELS_DONE`, merge of the stored PDFs with `pdf-lib` in selection order into one `BULK_LABELS` file, `CLEAN_BULK_LABELS`
- [x] "Print labels" for orders that already have a shipment: merge the stored files with no carrier call; voided labels are never merged
- [x] Cancellation: the table of `TDD_17 §4.8` (no guide, `LABEL_CREATED`, after pickup refused, unknown outcome refused), `CANCEL_SHIPMENT` with retries, `refundStatus`, "mark as voided", release of stock, leaving the pickup, cancel-and-recreate when the address, items or payment method change after a guide exists (the new guide waits until the old one is `CANCELLED`) **(Step 4.10 PR D2; see "Built in PR D2" below)**
- [x] **Built in PR D2 (cancellation; rule 12 debts with their owners):** `ShipmentCancellation` (`libs/core/shipping`, replaces `ShippingService.cancel` and `markVoided`) has three parts: `start` (inside a transaction: refuses `CREATING`/`REQUIRES_REVIEW` with `SHIPPING_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` and a package that left with `SHIPPING_CANNOT_CANCEL_AFTER_PICKUP`, moves the guide to `CANCEL_PENDING`, voids a `local` guide at once with `refundStatus not_applicable`, leaves one the carrier cannot void `CANCEL_PENDING` with `not_supported` and tells the team, and hands back a job for the rest), `settle` (the worker's call to the carrier) and `markVoided`. The carrier is no longer called inside a request: `POST /shipments/:id/cancel` answers the guide as `CANCEL_PENDING` and `ShipmentVoidQueue` queues `CANCEL_SHIPMENT` (heavy queue, `ShipmentCancelLimits.JOB_ATTEMPTS = 5`, exponential backoff from 30 s) once the transaction has committed; `ShipmentCanceller` (worker) runs `settle`: a carrier that does not answer (`mayRetryVoid`: unavailable, timeout, anything the platform cannot read) is asked again by the queue with the same `cancelShipmentKey`, the last attempt leaves `CANCEL_FAILED`; a refusal, rejected credentials or credentials that do not open end it at once as `CANCEL_FAILED`; a guide that is no longer `CANCEL_PENDING` is left alone, so the job runs twice safely; success is `CANCELLED` with the refund the carrier reports (`pending` unless `confirmed`). Cancelling the order (`OrdersStatusService`) no longer waits for its guides: `OrderGuides.voidLive` voids every outbound guide still only a label inside the transaction of the order (so the order, its release of stock through `OrderInventoryService.release` and the guides commit together, and the stock is released once: a job that runs twice never touches it) and queues the jobs after the commit; a guide already `CANCEL_PENDING` or `CANCEL_FAILED` starts no second void; one whose outcome is unknown or whose package left refuses the whole cancel. Editing an order after a guide exists (`OrdersService.update`) voids the guides when what they were made from changed (`guideAffectingChanges`, pure, in `libs/core/domain/shipment-cancel-rules.ts`: the address by value whatever the order of its keys, the phones, the payment method, the lines as a set, and the total only while the order is cash on delivery on either side); the order is not cancelled and its stock stays reserved, and the agent makes the new guide once the old one is `CANCELLED` (the index of one active outbound guide per order and origin answers `SHIPPING_ACTIVE_SHIPMENT_EXISTS` until then). A guide that is voided leaves its pickup: its `pickupId` is cleared, the count of the pickup and its `revision` are recomputed from the guides that still hold it, an emptied `manual_required` pickup is cancelled, and for a pickup the carrier holds (`requested`/`confirmed` with its id) the carrier is told after the commit (`modifyPickup` with the new count, or `cancelPickup` and the pickup `cancelled` when it is empty) when the plugin has `PICKUP_MODIFY`/`PICKUP_CANCEL`; otherwise, or when the call fails, the pickup keeps a note in `lastError` (`PickupLeaveNotes`) and the guide is voided anyway. `Events.SHIPMENT_CANCEL_FAILED` (bridged; raised when a guide moves to `CANCEL_FAILED` and when a guide the carrier cannot void is left for the agent) raises `shipment.cancel_failed` to the members who hold `shipping:manage` (once while the earlier line is unread), and `NotificationTypes.SHIPMENT_CANCEL_FAILED` left `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter` (the unit spec of the constant has its case). The audit entries of the order (`order.cancelled`, `order.updated`) name the guides voided with it (`shipmentsVoided`, and `guideChanges` for an edit). Decisions: **(a)** an edit voids the guide and does **not** make the new one: a guide may cost money, so it stays a person's click (rejected: an automatic recreate, which would spend without a click, `TDD_17 §4.10`); **(b)** one route and one path for every cancel (the job) instead of a synchronous try in the request and a job for the rest (rejected: two paths to keep equal, and a request that waits up to the 30 s deadline of a carrier). **Left, each with its owner:** **(c)** a queue that was down when a cancel committed leaves the guide `CANCEL_PENDING` with nobody asking: a second request asks again after fifteen minutes (`CANCEL_STALE_MS`, longer than the ten minutes of the job's whole schedule), and a sweep that does not wait for a person is **Step 7.4**, which also covers a job that ends on a fault of the database after the carrier answered (the guide stays `CANCEL_PENDING` with nobody told); **(d)** the void jobs share the `heavy-processing` queue with the scans and the poll, so a slow carrier holds a slot for up to five deadlines: a queue of its own or a limiter per connection is **Step 7.4**, with (i) of PR C2b; **(e)** the move the worker writes (`CANCELLED`, `CANCEL_FAILED`) carries the actor in the history of the shipment but no audit entry of its own (the request that started the void is audited); **(f)** the pickup the carrier holds is told after the commit with no retry: a failure leaves the note for the agent, and the screen that shows the note, the cutoff rules and the pickup reminders are **Step 4.11** and **Step 4.14**; **(g)** `ShipmentCancellation` touches the database and the carrier, so `libs/core/shipping/jest.config.cts` leaves it out of the unit coverage (the 90/85 threshold is unchanged) and `tests/api/shipment-cancellation.spec.ts` covers it; measuring the integration suites is **Step 7.7**; **(i)** the new count of a pickup is sent after each commit, so two guides of one pickup voided at the same moment may reach the carrier out of order: a full resync of the pickup against what its guides hold is **Step 4.11**; **(j)** the total of a cash-on-delivery order voids its guide even when a payment already covers it (the rule reads the method, not `codAmountFor`), and lines split or merged without a change of units count as a change: a void too many costs a click, a missed one costs money, so the rule stays conservative until **Step 4.14** (the edit form) refines it; the other things a plugin may send (a scheduled delivery date, a declared value) are not compared, which is safe only while no plugin sends them: **Step 4.15** checks it with the real carriers; **(k)** a void that fails on rejected credentials ends as a plain `CANCEL_FAILED` and does not flag the connection: **Step 4.15**; **(m)** left on purpose from the last review pass (one pass, not repeated): a connection that is not `active` when the job runs reads as a carrier that cannot void and leaves `not_supported` (`TDD_17 §4.8`, a disconnected connection), so a connection that comes back is settled by the agent; the move that leaves a guide `CANCEL_PENDING` with `not_supported` after a disconnect writes no line of history of its own; a pickup whose carrier call failed keeps its lowered count with the note and no retry (**Step 4.11**); a payload that does not fit its schema, or a job that dies of a database fault, leaves the guide `CANCEL_PENDING` until the sweep of **Step 7.4**; **(l)** two things the review of the engine found and the PR does not do on purpose: the duplicated 409 helper and the hand-written `Row` of `ShipmentCancellation` (a `GetPayload` type is not exported by the database lib yet): **Step 7.1**; **(h)** the order view and the create dialog that say "this order's guide was voided, make another" and show the warning of a guide that is `cancel_failed` are **Step 4.14**. Closes (a) and (c) of PR C1, the `CANCEL_SHIPMENT` job that (b) of PR C2b left, and (d) of PR C1 for `shipment.cancel_failed`. **PR D3** (coordinator's decision) closed this step: the labels of every active shipment of a split order in a bulk run (debt (e) of PR D1); asking the carrier again for a label that was not kept (debt (i) of PR D1, (g) of PR C1) moves to **Step 4.15**, which needs a real carrier adapter.
- [x] The amber "Packing note" band (`Order.packingNotes`, decision of 2026-11-10) on the preparation and packing screens and on the packing list printed for the warehouse: it never goes on the carrier label or to the customer. **Done in Step 4.3 PR A2** for what exists: the amber band in the order detail (`components/orders/order-notes.tsx`) and the note on the packing list the browser prints (`components/orders/order-print.tsx`). **PR D2 adds** `tests/api/shipment-cancellation.spec.ts`, which makes a guide for an order that has a packing note and checks that the note reaches neither the carrier nor the shipment row (the label drawn for a `local` guide takes the fields of `labelTextsFor`, which has none for notes). The preparation and packing screens do not exist yet: the band on them is the packing-note item of **Step 4.14** (its preparation panel)
- [x] **Show the notification switches of this step (QA fix PR Q7):** this step raises `bulk_labels.ready`, `shipment.cancel_failed` (`NotificationService.notify`, with its `messageKey` texts in `es` and `en`); the preferences page hides the switch of a type nothing raises, so remove `NotificationTypes.BULK_LABELS_READY`, `NotificationTypes.SHIPMENT_CANCEL_FAILED` from `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter` (`libs/core/domain/notifications/notification-constants.ts`) in the same PR, and add the case to the unit spec of that constant **(Done: `bulk_labels.ready` in PR D1 and `shipment.cancel_failed` in PR D2.)**
- [x] **Built in PR D1 (bulk labels and "print labels"; rule 12 debts with their owners):** `POST /shipments/bulk` (202; `orderIds` unique, at most 100, optional `connectionId` and `pickup` for every order of the run), `GET /shipments/bulk/:jobId` (state, per-order `results`, a link to the file) and `POST /shipments/labels/merge`, all `shipping:manage`; the worker's `BulkLabelsRunner` (job `BULK_LABELS_MERGE` on the heavy queue, two attempts) makes the missing guides through `ShippingService.createShipments` at the pace of each connection (`ShippingService.routesOf`: a standalone or internal carrier at most `maxConcurrency` at a time, a new optional field of the plugin, three by default and ten at most; a pickup-bound one in one call per connection, which the engine splits by `maxBatchSize` and by pickup), then merges the stored labels with `pdf-lib` in the order of the selection (`MergedLabels`, the same piece "print labels" uses) into one `Media` of purpose `bulk_labels`; `BULK_LABELS_PROGRESS` and `BULK_LABELS_DONE` are domain events that cross the bridge and reach only the requester's tabs; the notification `bulk_labels.ready` (texts in `es` and `en` already existed) goes to the requester while they still hold `shipping:manage`, and `NotificationTypes.BULK_LABELS_READY` left `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter` (the cancel that failed, `shipment.cancel_failed`, stays there for the cancellation PR); `CLEAN_BULK_LABELS` (`BulkLabelsCleaner`, daily) deletes the merged files past `StoreSettings.bulkLabelRetentionDays` of their own store and closes as failed a run that has been queued or running for two hours; `BulkLabelJob` left the isolation exemptions and has its case in `tests/security/tenant-isolation.spec.ts`; the media route asks for `shipping:manage` to read `bulk_labels` and now also `shipping_label` (a label holds the name, address and phone of its recipient, and the route that serves it already asked for it); a store may have at most five runs queued or running (`429 RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` after that) and `GET /shipments/bulk/:jobId` answers only the person who asked for the run (404 for another member), because the file holds the customers of their orders; the cleanup never keeps a file less than a day whatever the days of the store say. Decisions: **(a)** an order that already has a guide whose label can be printed is `reused` (its stored label goes in the file, nobody is called) and counts as a success, one whose guide cannot be printed yet (`creating`, `requires_review`, `cancel_pending`, `cancel_failed`) is skipped with `SHIPPING_ACTIVE_SHIPMENT_EXISTS`, which reads the two sentences of `TDD_17 §4.6` ("already have an active shipment: skipped" and "an order that already has a shipment reuses its stored label") as one rule; **(b)** `succeeded` is the orders whose label is a page of the file and `failed` every other one (skipped, refused, or left out of the file with its `reason`), because the table demands `succeeded + failed = total` when a run ends; a run that ends by itself with any order out of the file is `completed_with_errors` even when none made it, and `failed` is only for a run that stopped on a fault on its last attempt; **(c)** the options of the request (carrier, pickup) and whether the person could override the price travel in the queue job because `BulkLabelJob` has no column for them, and the run re-checks that the person is still an active member with `shipping:manage` before it makes a guide that costs money; **(d)** a label a carrier made waits for the scan of the worker: the run asks for it every two seconds for up to a minute and leaves out, with `reason: not_ready`, a label that is still not clean (the guide exists and prints later with "print labels"), and a merge keeps at most 64 MB of labels in memory (`reason: too_large` for the rest). Left out, each with its owner: **(e)** an order with several active shipments (split fulfillment) reuses only its newest printable one; the creation of split shipments, and with it the reuse of all their labels, is **Step 4.10 PR D3** (done: see "Built in PR D3"); **(f)** a run that waits for a scan holds one of the two slots of the heavy queue, so two runs at once delay the scans they wait for (they give up after a minute and report `not_ready`): accepted until the load is measured in **Step 7.7**, which also decides whether the run gets a queue of its own or the merge becomes a second job that does not wait; the merge of `POST /shipments/labels/merge` also runs inside the api request (at most 64 MB of labels, read one after another): **Step 7.7** moves it to a job if the load asks for it; **(g)** `bulkLabelRetentionDays` has no route and no control: it keeps its default of seven days until the store preferences of `TDD_17 §4.14` (**Step 4.14**, with its API route); **(h2)** `pdf-lib` (pinned at 1.17.1, its last release) parses a label that a carrier sent before the page limit of the merge is checked: a label is at most 5 MB, a PDF and scanned, so the cost is bounded, but a parse timeout or an isolated process for it is **Step 7.1**; a queue job whose payload does not fit its schema is refused for good without closing its run (the daily cleanup does, after two hours): **Step 7.1**; **(h1)** a merged file can still be asked for through `GET /media/:id` by any member who holds `shipping:manage` and knows its id (a random UUID that only the requester is shown): reading a file only as the person who made it is **Step 7.1**; the claim and the read of a run are outside the guard that closes a failed attempt, so a run can stay queued up to the two hours after which it no longer counts against the cap of five and the daily cleanup closes it; **(h0)** the cleanup retries at the front of the next run a file that cannot be deleted, so two hundred such files would hold back newer expired ones: it logs each one, and a back-off is **Step 7.4** (monitoring); **(h)** the screens (the bar of progress, the report of skipped orders, the pickup dialog, the warning that page sizes differ, the download) are **Step 4.14**; **(i)** the rest of the labels debt of PR C1 (g), a carrier's image wrapped into a PDF and asking the carrier again for a label that was not kept, is **Step 4.15** (it needs a real carrier adapter)
- [x] **Built in PR D3 (split orders in a bulk run; closes (e) of PR D1; rule 12 debts with their owners):** the bulk run (`BulkLabelsRunner`) no longer treats an order as one guide. An order is made of **parts**, one per warehouse its lines are allocated to (`OrderItemAllocation`, read at the start of the run and ordered by the priority of the warehouse in the store, then by id, so the order of the guides never changes between runs) and one for every live guide whose warehouse the allocation no longer names; an order with no allocation and no guide is one part with no origin and the engine picks the warehouse of the store, as before (`partsOfOrder`, pure, in `bulk-labels.rules.ts`). Each part is judged on its own: a part whose guide can be printed is reused (nobody is called), one whose guide cannot yet (`creating`, `requires_review`, `cancel_pending`, `cancel_failed`) is skipped with `SHIPPING_ACTIVE_SHIPMENT_EXISTS`, and a part with no live guide gets a request for **that warehouse** (`originWarehouseId` of `ShipmentRequest`, always the warehouse the run judged, so the engine refuses it if the order no longer ships from there), so a guide that was voided (`CANCELLED`) is made again and its label is never merged. The requests of one order go together: one after another in a standalone or internal lane (the amount to collect of each guide is read after the guides before it, so the parts add up to the total exactly as `TDD_17 §4.4` says, and a lane's concurrency counts orders, not parts) and in the same call of a pickup-bound lane (the engine writes them in that order inside one transaction). The account still has **one entry per order**, so `succeeded + failed = total` holds: `PartsLedger` keeps what each part came to and gives the order once, when the last part has settled (`entryOfParts`): a split order is `created` (some guide made by the run) or `reused` (none) only when every part has its label, and carries `shipmentIds`, the guides whose labels are pages of the file in the order of the parts, with `shipmentId` the first of them; otherwise it says what its first part without a label says (`skipped` or `failed`, with its `errorCode` and the `shipmentId` of that part) and **still lists the labels the other parts have in `shipmentIds`, which are printed** (a guide that exists is never left out of the file because its sibling failed). The file puts the labels of a split order together, in the order of the selection and, inside the order, of the parts; a label that cannot go in the file (`not_ready`, `too_large`, ...) turns its order into `failed` with that `reason`. An order that is not split has no `shipmentIds`: the shape of PR D1 is unchanged for it. "Print labels" (`POST /shipments/labels/merge`) takes shipment ids, so it already prints every label of a split order when the screen sends them all; `tests/api/shipments-bulk.spec.ts` has a case for it. Decisions: **(a)** one entry per order with a list of guides, not one entry per guide (rejected: it would break `succeeded + failed = total`, which the table demands, and a run of a hundred orders would report two hundred lines the agent did not select); **(b)** a split order whose part failed is a failure of the order even though a label is printed (rejected: counting it as a success, which would hide the guide that is missing: the agent must see that a part is still to be made); **(c)** the parts of an order are made one after another and not in parallel, even for a carrier that takes three at a time (rejected: parallel parts, whose amounts to collect would depend on which one the database sees first). **Left, each with its owner:** **(d)** a split order whose part made its guide and whose sibling failed keeps no mark that the run paid for the first one: a later attempt of the same run reports it as `reused`, not `created` (nobody is called twice either way; the report of what the run spent is exact only for orders that ended whole, and a split order that lost a part reads `skipped` or `failed` even when the run made another of its guides: the guide is in `shipmentIds`, printed and on the account): **Step 4.14** if the screen needs the figure per guide, and a fault that stops the run keeps the guides its finished parts made (`PartsLedger.unfinished`) as a `failed` entry with `INTERNAL_ERROR`; **(e)** the entry names only the first part that failed, so an order with two failed parts shows one error: the agent reads the rest in the shipments of the order, and the screen that lists them per guide is **Step 4.14**; **(f)** the parts are read from the allocation when the run starts, so an allocation that changes while the run is under way (a line moved to another warehouse) is judged at the next run: accepted, the engine re-checks the origin inside its own transaction and refuses a warehouse the order no longer ships from (`SHIPPING_ORIGIN_MISSING`); **(h)** a live guide whose warehouse the allocation does not name (the lines moved after the guide was made; the database never lets a guide change its origin) stands for the first warehouse of the order that has no guide, one by one, so the run never pays a second guide for what the order already shipped; the cost is that a stale guide may cover a warehouse that is not really the one it left from, and the agent sees it in the shipments of the order: **Step 4.14**; **(i)** the origins of an order are computed twice, in `prepareGuide` (the engine) and in `BulkLabelsRunner.originsOf`, from the same rows: one shared helper is **Step 7.1** with the other duplicated helpers; a request list longer than the hundred the engine takes (a split order sends more than one request) goes in runs of a hundred; a part that makes the engine throw stops the parts after it, as a single guide did before; **(g)** the `shipments` list of an order that the bulk dialog shows per guide, and the "print all labels of this order" button that sends every shipment id of the order, are **Step 4.14**; asking the carrier again for a label that was not kept (debt (i) of PR D1, (g) of PR C1) stays in **Step 4.15**.

**TDD:** `TDD_17 §4.6`, `§4.8`, `TDD_09 §11`, `TDD_16 §4.1`
**Done when:** with the fake carrier, a bulk run over a mixed selection produces one PDF with the successes in the
selected order and a per-order report for the rest, reprinting calls the carrier zero times, and cancelling
an order with a guide voids it (or leaves `CANCEL_PENDING` with a notification when the carrier cannot),
releases the stock once and is refused after pickup.

**QA checkpoint: yes (after 4.8-4.10 and 4.14, labels and messages to customers):** a bulk run over a mixed selection (successes, failures, already labeled), cancel after a label, the confirmation message sent once and only inside its window or template, no message to a customer who has no consent; run with the fake carrier only until the owner's real carrier test.

### Step 4.11 — Pickups
- [x] (PR A; the auto-request time and the reminder are read by PR B) Per-connection pickup rules (days, cutoff, auto-request time, reminder) and `PickupsService.ensurePickup` (the core service of `libs/core/shipping`, not a method of `ShippingService`) with the advisory lock and the one-open-pickup-per-day rule for standalone carriers (a service rule, not a database index, because a pickup-bound carrier may need several per day)
- [x] (PR A) Joining and modifying a pickup's package count where the carrier has `PICKUP_MODIFY`, waiting for the next day where it does not, `MANUAL_REQUIRED` with the printable manifest where there is no pickup API
- [ ] (PR B) `EVALUATE_PICKUPS` every minute (one job for all connections), the cutoff reminder, retries and `FAILED`; for pickup-bound carriers `autoPickup` generates guides and pickup together (off by default)
- [ ] (`pickup.manual_required` done in PR A; `pickup.cutoff_approaching` is PR B, which removes `PICKUP_CUTOFF_APPROACHING` from the list) **Show the notification switches of this step (QA fix PR Q7):** this step raises `pickup.cutoff_approaching`, `pickup.manual_required` (`NotificationService.notify`, with its `messageKey` texts in `es` and `en`); the preferences page hides the switch of a type nothing raises, so remove `NotificationTypes.PICKUP_CUTOFF_APPROACHING`, `NotificationTypes.PICKUP_MANUAL_REQUIRED` from `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter` (`libs/core/domain/notifications/notification-constants.ts`) in the same PR, and add the case to the unit spec of that constant
- [ ] (PR A reads `pickupEnabled`, `pickupDays` and `pickupCutoffTime` in `ensurePickup`; PR B reads `autoPickup`, `autoPickupTime` and `pickupReminderMinutes`) **From Step 4.8 PR B2 (debt (i), rule 12):** the pickup rules of a connection (`pickupEnabled`, `pickupDays`, `pickupCutoffTime`, `autoPickup`, `autoPickupTime`, `pickupReminderMinutes`) are stored and checked by `PATCH /carriers/:id` and nothing reads them: `EVALUATE_PICKUPS` and `ensurePickup` are the first readers
- [x] **Built in PR A (pickups: service, routes, job; rule 12 debts with their owners).** `PickupsService` (`libs/core/shipping`) is the only place a pickup is made or joined: `ensurePickup` takes the store lock and `pg_advisory_xact_lock` of the connection (`PickupLockScopes.CONNECTION`), reads the rules of the connection (`pickupEnabled`, days, cutoff; the store timezone), resolves the day (`resolvePickupDate`, pure), finds the eligible guides (`LABEL_CREATED`, outbound, no pickup, a guide number) and either makes the pickup (`PENDING`, or `MANUAL_REQUIRED` at once when the plugin has no `PICKUP_REQUEST`, which raises `pickup.manual_required`) or joins the open pickup of the day by `decideJoin` (pure): a pickup not yet asked, or arranged by hand, takes the guide; one the carrier holds takes it only with `PICKUP_MODIFY` (revision + 1, the carrier is told), and not after the cutoff unless the person confirmed (`PICKUP_CUTOFF_PASSED`; the scheduler's guides wait instead); otherwise the guide waits for the next pickup day and the answer names the reason (`PickupWaitReasons`). A pickup-bound connection has no pickup of its own to ask for (`PICKUP_NOT_ENABLED`). The carrier is never called inside a request: the api queues `SYNC_PICKUP` (heavy queue, five attempts, backoff from 30 s) after the commit and `PickupSync` (worker) asks for the pickup or tells the carrier the new count; `PickupRequest.carrierPackageCount` (migration `20261204100000` with its rollback) is what the carrier last acknowledged, so a count left behind (two voids that landed out of order, the debt (i) of Step 4.10 PR D2) is told again by the same job, and `pickupNeedsSync` (pure) says when. A carrier that does not answer is asked again with the same key (`pickupRequestKey`); the last attempt ends the pickup `FAILED`, gives its guides back and tells the team; a refusal or credentials that do not open end it at once; an answer the platform cannot read leaves it `REQUIRES_REVIEW`. The package count is the sum of the `packageCount` of the guides that hold the pickup (`ShipmentCancellation.leavePickup` now sums instead of counting, and records what the carrier was told). Routes (`ApiRoutes.pickups`): list and manifest ask `shipping:view`; request, add, remove, confirm and cancel ask `shipping:manage_pickups`; cancel asks the carrier first when the pickup is held (a pickup still being asked, and a held one whose carrier has no `PICKUP_CANCEL`, answer `PICKUP_NOT_EDITABLE`); removing the last guide of a held pickup is refused too (cancel it). Tests: `tests/api/pickups.spec.ts`, the `pickupRequest` case of `tenant-isolation.spec.ts`, `pickup-rules.spec.ts`; the lock was mutated (without it the race case fails). Decisions: **(a)** a request that ends in an unreadable answer is `REQUIRES_REVIEW`, not `FAILED` (rejected: `FAILED` for everything, which would let the agent ask twice and make a second pickup at a carrier that already has one); **(b)** a manual pickup is made `MANUAL_REQUIRED` at once, not `PENDING` first (rejected: a job that only moves it); **(c)** `pickup.manual_required` also tells the team of a `FAILED` or `REQUIRES_REVIEW` pickup (rejected: a third notification type for one more switch). Debts: **(i)** the scheduler, the cutoff reminder, `autoPickup` and the sweep (a pickup left `PENDING` because the queue was down, a count not yet acknowledged) are **PR B** of this step; **(ii)** settling a `REQUIRES_REVIEW` pickup (found at the carrier, or not) is a screen of **Step 4.14**: today cancelling it ends it `FAILED` and frees its guides; **(iii)** a held pickup whose carrier has no `PICKUP_CANCEL` cannot be cancelled here, the agent cancels it with the carrier and the screen of **Step 4.14** marks it; **(iv)** the window of a pickup is the default one (`PickupDefaults`), a setting per connection is **Step 4.14**; **(v)** the guides that wait are not stored: the screen of **Step 4.14** lists them as the eligible guides of the connection; **(vi)** a later guide for a pickup-bound pickup (joining rules of `TDD_17 §4.10`) waits for the real carrier, **Step 4.15**; **(vii)** `PickupsService` and `PickupSync` are covered by the integration suite only (`jest.config.cts` leaves them out of the unit coverage, the pure rules are at 100%), measured in **Step 7.7**; `PickupSync` is also a provider of the api module so the suite can run it. **(viii)** a count the carrier keeps refusing is left with its note and is not told again by the job (a refusal does not change by asking); surfacing it to the team and asking again after the owner fixes the connection are the sweep of **PR B**, which also gives its own job id (the id of a sync job is `(pickup, revision)` and BullMQ keeps a finished one for a day, so the same revision is never asked twice by the request side); **(ix)** three places tell a carrier a count or a cancel (`PickupSync.modify`, `ShipmentCancellation.tellCarrierOfPickup`, `PickupsService.cancelAtCarrier`) with different retry rules: one shared helper is **PR B**, which touches all three for the sweep; **(x)** `pickup.manual_required` is one line per pickup while unread, not per store. **plugin-api (owner request, 2026-11-12):** none of the routes is a plugin route in this PR; proposed for **Step 5.6**: every `pickups.*` route is mapped in `scripts/plugin-api-coverage-map.ts` to the proposed scopes `pickups:read` (list, manifest) and `pickups:write` (request, add, remove, confirm, cancel); the carrier capabilities stay declared in the SDK (`CarrierCapability.PICKUP_*`), no new one is added by this step.

**TDD:** `TDD_17 §4.10`, `TDD_07 §4`
**Done when:** the scheduler creates one pickup per due connection, a later shipment joins it or waits as the
capability dictates, the reminder fires once, a manual request racing the scheduler ends with one pickup,
and a cancelled shipment lowers the count.

### Step 4.12 — Returns, exchanges and reception
- [ ] `ReturnsService` with the state machine of `TDD_17 §3`, quantity limits per order item, the return leg (return guide when the carrier has `RETURN_SHIPMENT`, manual otherwise), `StoreSettings.returnShippingPayer`
- [ ] Exchange: the replacement order (`exchangeOfOrderId`, already `CONFIRMED`, the returned items' value applied as a system credit so the total is the price difference) through `OrdersService`
- [ ] Return reception by scanner (`ReturnReceptionService`, `TDD_17 §4.16`): a persistent batch, lookup by guide number, per-item reason, condition, warehouse, note and caution toggle, `ReturnsService.receiveFromScan` per item in its own transaction, retry of failed items
- [x] Customer caution flag, API and api-client (PR 4.12-R3): `PUT` and `DELETE /customers/:id/caution` behind `CUSTOMERS_FLAG_CAUTION` (audited `customer.caution_set` / `customer.caution_cleared`, the reason never copied to the audit entry), `isCaution` in the order list and the conversation list and detail, `isCaution` in the order detail (never the reason, which `customers:view` reads), `setCustomerCaution` / `clearCustomerCaution` in the api-client. The columns were in the initial schema, so there is no migration. **Merge:** keeps the flag of either side (Step 2.2, with its reason, setter and date; an unmerge restores them). **Deletion:** a customer with conversations or orders is not deleted, so the flag goes only with a customer that has no history. **Erasure:** the flag stays and the reason, the setter and the date are cleared, which belongs to the erasure job of Step 7.2 (task there). The screens are Step 4.14 (task there); the realtime event `CUSTOMER_CAUTION_CHANGED` has no emitter on purpose (the flag informs and never blocks, and the screens read it again with the customer).
  - Q6 (done): `CUSTOMERS_FLAG_CAUTION` left `ReservedPermissions` because `CustomersController` reads it.
- [ ] Inventory on receipt through `InventoryService`: `RETURN_IN` for resellable items, `RETURN_IN` then `ADJUSTMENT_OUT` (`DAMAGED`) for defective ones; the order becomes `RETURNED` only when received returns cover every unit; refund recorded by hand
- [x] **Show the notification switches of this step (QA fix PR Q7):** this step raises `return.awaiting_receipt` (`NotificationService.notify`, with its `messageKey` texts in `es` and `en`); the preferences page hides the switch of a type nothing raises, so remove `NotificationTypes.RETURN_AWAITING_RECEIPT` from `NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter` (`libs/core/domain/notifications/notification-constants.ts`) in the same PR, and add the case to the unit spec of that constant

- **PR R1 (returns core, done in the PR that checks this note):** `ReturnsService` (create, list, get, approve, reject, cancel, in-transit, receive, complete, `receiveFromScan`), the quantity limits per order item, the partial/full return and the order status (`OrdersStatusService.markReturnedByReturns`), the inventory on receipt (`RETURN_IN`; `RETURN_IN` then `ADJUSTMENT_OUT` `DAMAGED` for defective or damaged units, keys `return:{returnId}:{orderItemId}:{movement}`, once), the refund by hand (never above the order total, `markRefundedByReturns` when it reaches it), the `return.awaiting_receipt` notice (on `return.in_transit`, to `RETURNS_MANAGE`), routes `/returns` (list, byId, create, approve, reject, cancel, in-transit, receive, complete), the api-client and the isolation cases. A hand-made `RETURNED` is refused while a return holds units (`ORDER_HAS_ACTIVE_RETURNS`). Debts, each with the PR that closes it:
  - (a) **R1b**: the exchange (`exchangeOfOrderId`, the replacement order already `CONFIRMED` through `OrdersService`, the system credit) and the `exchange_shipped` message; until then `POST /returns` refuses `type: exchange` with a 400.
  - (b) **R1b**: the return leg (`POST /returns/:id/return-shipment`, a `kind = RETURN` guide for a connection whose plugin declares `RETURN_SHIPMENT`) and the manual send of `return_approved` with the guide it refers to (`KINDS_OF_ANOTHER_SHIPMENT` in `order-messages.schemas.ts` stays until then). `RETURN_SHIPMENT` is declared in the SDK (`libs/plugins/sdk`); no official carrier declares it yet, so the leg is only reachable with the fake carrier. A shipment of kind `RETURN` must not drive the order status through `OrderShipmentEffects`: R1b adds the test.
  - (b2) **R1b**: `receiveFromScan` refuses a shipment of kind `RETURN` (its package is received through the return that made it, which R1b links by `Shipment.returnId`, instead of making a second one), and a scan without `lines` of an order sent in parts is a 400 (the package does not say which units it holds; the reception screen of R2 must send the lines of such a package).
  - (b3) **Step 4.14**: the manual event of a scan has the description `shipping.event.receivedAtWarehouse` (`ShipmentEventNotes`); the timeline translates it in `es` and `en` with the other event notes.
  - (c) **R1b**: `StoreSettings.returnShippingPayer` has no route yet (the default is the column default `sender`); the setting is read by `create` already. The Settings → Returns screen is Step 4.14.
  - (d) **Step 4.13**: `receiveFromScan` does not touch `Shipment.codStatus` (the field is not built yet); 4.13 voids it on a return.
  - (e) **PR R2 / R3**: the customer caution flag after a scan (`receiveFromScan` leaves it to the reception service) and the batch tables.
  - (f) **Step 4.14**: every screen of the returns (list, detail, reception).
  - (g) **Step 5.6, plugin-api** (a plugin reaches what the owner reaches): `GET /returns`, `GET /returns/:id` (scope proposed: `returns:read`), `POST /returns`, `…/approve`, `…/reject`, `…/cancel`, `…/in-transit`, `…/receive`, `…/complete` (scope proposed: `returns:write`, as in `scripts/plugin-api-coverage-map.ts`; Step 5.6 decides whether `…/receive` and `…/complete`, which move stock and money, need a scope of their own).
  - (h) Accepted limit: a refund is capped by the total of the order (and only a payment that is `COMPLETED` has anything to refund), not by the amount the customer really paid, because a payment is recorded by status only (no partial amounts in the MVP); the cap of several refunds of one order is the total of them.
  - (h3) **Step 4.14** (the returns list screen): `GET /returns` pages by `(created_at, id)` of the store and has no index for that sort (only `(store_id, status)` and `(store_id, order_id)`); fine for the volume of the MVP, the screen adds the index with its migration and rollback.
  - (k) **Step 7.1** (not this PR): make `tests/api/carriers.spec.ts` ("answers a carrier that refuses with a reason of a closed list, never with its words") deterministic: it asserts the answer does not contain the trigram `abc`, which a random id in the answer can contain; it should look for the carrier's own word. Seen red once in the CI of #440 (`security-shard (3/4)`, run 43145715659); the rerun passes.
  - (h2) Accepted duplication: `markReturnedByReturns` repeats the summary line and the `order.status_changed` event of `changeStatus`; a shared helper is a refactor of `OrdersStatusService` left to the next PR that edits it (Step 4.14 touches it for the returns panel).
  - (i) Accepted limit: the notice is raised on `in_transit` only; a return that goes from `approved` straight to `received` (goods brought by hand) raises none, because the person who receives it is already at the counter.
  - (j) Accepted limit: a partial package scanned from a `SHIPPED` order leaves the order `SHIPPED`; the shipment is `RETURNED` and the order follows only when every unit is back.
**TDD:** `TDD_17 §4.11`, `§4.16`, `TDD_16 §4.1`
**Done when:** scanning a batch of guides receives each package with its own reason, condition and note, a failing item does not roll back the others, a flagged customer is shown at the next order, an exchange creates the replacement with the right stock and total, a defective item leaves net stock
unchanged and a resellable one adds it back, a partial return keeps the order `DELIVERED`, and a return
above the delivered quantity is refused.

### Step 4.15 — Forza and C807 adapters
**Starts when the owner has the API documentation and credentials of each carrier** (`TDD_17 §14` lists what to
ask for); it is the only step of the plan that depends on an external input, so everything before it builds and
ships with the `local` carrier and the fake carrier.
- [ ] `libs/carriers/forza` and `libs/carriers/c807`: authentication and signing, guide creation with the service codes and COD and payer fields, the label, the `statusCatalog()` with every tracking status the documentation lists and its default `ShipmentStatus`, and exactly the capabilities the documentation confirms (cancel, pickup and its modification, return guide, webhook, polling, location lookup)
- [ ] C807 specifics (`TDD_17 §14`): the 24-hour token cached per connection in Redis (a `CacheKeys` entry added here) and renewed once on a `401`; `CCE` chosen for a COD order and `SEC` for a recipient-paid one; `tipo_entrega` and `sede` as connection settings; no cancel, no pickup change, no quote (the connection's fixed `defaultShippingFeeCents`); the webhook secret as a custom header with confirmation by `fetchStatus` before `DELIVERED` and `RETURNED` apply; code `16` with its reason as composite mapping codes
- [ ] Recipient phones (`TDD_17 §14`): the C807 adapter sends `Order.recipientPhones` as one `phone` string with `formatPhoneList` (`n1 / n2 / n3`); the Forza adapter sends the first one unless its documentation shows a second field; both carriers take national digits with no country code (owner, 2026-10-27), which is what `Order.recipientPhones` already holds, so the adapters strip nothing; both covered by a fixture in the contract suite
- [ ] Recorded provider fixtures for the contract suite; the carrier's webhook registration documented in Settings → Carriers
- [ ] A capability that cannot be confirmed is left out and the manual path covers it; a status the documentation does not explain is left out of the catalog on purpose, and the owner maps it from the unmapped list when it first appears

Make the search of customers (and the inbox search that shares the service) accent-insensitive, with a test for names with accents next to the `%` and cross-tenant cases
- [ ] **From the QA fix PRs (QA backlog, rule 12), the search event of the `analytics` queue (left by Q4, (c)):** `apps/worker/src/processors/analytics.processor.ts` writes only `TRACK_EVENT` and refuses a job it does not know. The "Search analytics" task of this step queues `TRACK_SEARCH`, so it also teaches the processor that job, with its spec (the unique key on events is Step 5.2's)
- [ ] **From Step 4.1 (PR B), a fuzzier product search:** the autocomplete of `GET /products/search` is not ranked by similarity or tolerant to typos (`pg_trgm` `similarity`, `SearchConfig.SIMILARITY_THRESHOLD` of `TDD_08 §5.2`) and does not search the description (the `search_vector` column is there for it): add both here, with the global search that also reads `search.products`, if the owner asks for a fuzzier result.

**TDD:** `TDD_08 §5`
**Done when:** global search returns results under 300ms and leaks nothing across tenants.

### Step 5.2 — Analytics
- [ ] Rollup workers, hourly and daily (2-period recompute)
- [ ] Human and automated response (owner, 2026-10-29): the first response time reads `firstResponseAt` only (a person); `firstAutomatedResponseAt` is reported apart. A conversation is in one of three states for the reports: **waiting for a person** (no human reply and not resolved, even if a plugin answered), **answered by automation** (a plugin answered and no person did) and **resolved by automation** (a plugin resolved it with no human reply). The first two can both be true of one conversation. A response SLA or the pending queue changes only by an explicit resolve, never because a plugin answered
- [ ] Metrics query service
- [ ] Dashboard UI, and the My performance widget of the inbox sidebar (`TDD_09 §8`: resolved today, open, average response time), read from the metrics service and added to the widgets that Step 3.2 builds
- [ ] The View analytics action of the Quick actions widget (left by PR B6a of Step 3.2; Find customer in Step 3.3 and Create order in Step 4.4 join the same widget): a link to `AppPaths.store.analytics`, shown with the permission that opens the dashboard
- [ ] Scheduled reports (PDF/CSV/Excel), and `GET /analytics/export` (`analytics.export`) as an export of the exports center
- [ ] **From the QA fix PRs (QA backlog, rule 12), the rollups in the `analytics` processor (left by Q4, (c)):** the processor writes only `TRACK_EVENT`; the "Rollup workers" task above adds `ROLLUP_HOURLY` and `ROLLUP_DAILY` to it (it refuses any job it does not know). Also add a unique key on the event, so that a job redelivered after a stalled worker cannot write twice (the producer uses the Svix event id as job id and the queue allows one attempt, so a webhook delivered twice already queues one job). `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` does not defer the rollups: they are built here

**TDD:** `TDD_11 §7`, `TDD_08 §6`
**Done when:** the dashboard loads under 1s reading only rollup tables.

### Step 5.6 — Integration plugins (runtime)

In the v1.2 MVP the platform runs third-party and private integration plugins (for example
the owner's own POS or inventory app, or a Shopify connector later). The marketplace, its
review queue and revenue sharing stay deferred (`TDD_10 §8`). Builds on Step 5.4 (webhook
delivery, SSRF protection, key hashing) and Step 1.0 (queues).

A person starting a conversation from the inbox is not planned, and needs the template and window design first.
- [ ] Channel plugins by manifest (`type: "channel"`), the whole of `TDD_10 §6.7`, so that a channel can be added without touching this code: the migration (`commandSecret`, `commandSecretPrevious`, `commandSecretRotatedAt`, `Plugin.manifest`, `Plugin.iconKey`, with rollback), the manifest `channel` block and its validator (ids against `ChannelIds` and the table, the capabilities, the window bounds), the icon fetched once and stored as a 128 px PNG, the registry refresh and `list(storeId)`, `RemoteChannelPlugin` (all its methods, §6.7.4), signed commands with the secret rotation, `PUT/DELETE /api/v1/plugin-api/channel/connections/:connectionId` and `POST /api/v1/plugin-api/channel/events` (scopes `channel:connections`, `channel:events`, the caps and the rate ceilings), the hooks that send `typing.send` (from the agent typing events of Step 2.4) and `message.markRead` (from the read state), inbound media by URL with pinned addresses, the health job per connection with its counter, and `tests/contracts/remote-channel.contract.ts` with the reference plugin
- [x] ~~Remote (manifest) channel plugins cannot declare `media.album`~~ — discarded with the native album (owner, 2026-11-09; item "Native album discarded in the MVP" below); nothing to add to §6.7.2. Was: (native album, TDD_10 §5.1.1; rule 12, PR `feat/native-album-meta`): the manifest `channel` block of §6.7.2 has no `album` field and no command to send several files in one message. Add `album: { kinds, maxFiles }` to the validator (same bounds as `snapshotChannelMedia`: 2..30, kinds the channel declares), a `send-album` command with the files in order, one id for the message and optionally one for each file, document it in §6.7.2, and test that a manifest without it never inherits an album. Until then only an in-process plugin sends an album and a remote one gets one message per file. Closes with the `caption_per_file` item below.
- [ ] Remote (manifest) channel plugins cannot declare `caption_per_file`: add it to the capabilities the manifest validator accepts (TDD_10 §6.7.2, which lists the allowed set), check that the send-file command to a remote plugin carries the text of each file, document it in §6.7.2, and test that a manifest that declares it is accepted and one that does not never inherits it. Until then a third-party channel is offered only the one text of the message (safe default). Found in the caption-per-file cut (#311).
- [ ] Audit that the plugin SDK (`libs/plugins/sdk`) is aligned with the core: every capability, event and contract the core relies on is declared in the SDK and documented in TDD_10, in-process and manifest plugins alike (no official-plugin-only behavior hidden in the core), and that the core is agnostic in every sense: no channel id, channel name or per-channel branch in `libs/core` or in the apps outside the plugins (a channel's differences live in its plugin and its declared capabilities), with a check or lint rule that keeps it so. Owner decision 2026-11-09.
- [ ] Manifest, scopes and scope-to-permission mapping (`PluginScopes`, `PluginScopePermissions`, including the inventory scopes of `TDD_16 §3`)
- [ ] Install flow: consent screen, webhook-endpoint challenge with `assertPublicHttpsUrl`, `PluginInstallation` in `PENDING_SETUP`, scoped API key stored as prefix and sha256 hash only, one-time setup code (5 minutes, Redis) exchanged for the key, then `ACTIVE`
- [ ] `PluginApiKeyGuard` on `/api/v1/plugin-api/*`: scope check, per-installation rate limit, bound to exactly one store; thin controllers over the same services for conversations, messages, customers, orders, products and inventory
- [ ] **Messages from a plugin, with the author and the quiet mode chosen by the plugin (owner, 2026-10-29; Crisp's plugin API lets the plugin choose how a message looks):** `POST /plugin-api/messages` (`messages:write`) sends a reply or an internal note to a conversation. It takes: `type` (`text`, `note` or a file type with the media of Step 3.4: uploaded through the API, scanned, and sent with its text as the caption; a note carries no files in the MVP); `authorType`, `system` (the default) or `agent`; `userId`, required with `agent` (an active member of the store; the message is theirs, with their own name and avatar); `quiet` (default false); an optional `nickname` (cut to a length, control characters removed; the plugin's name by default) and an optional avatar (never a remote url: the icon of the plugin, and the plugin's own image once Step 3.4 stores it), both only for `system`. **The author and `quiet` are separate choices** (`authorType` is its own column), so a plugin can send as `system` quietly, as `system` and raise the chat, or as an agent in any of the two ways. **Author:** `system` never counts as a human answer: `recordReply` does not set `firstResponseAt` for it (that date feeds the first response time of `TDD_11 §7.2`, and an automatic answer would make it look instant) and it sets `Conversation.firstAutomatedResponseAt` instead (new nullable column, once; migration with its rollback script), so that automation is measured on its own and never hides how long the team takes. `agent` counts as that person's answer, as if they had written it. Answering does not take a conversation out of the pending queue or stop a response SLA: only an explicit resolve does, and a plugin with `conversations:write` can resolve its own. **`quiet`** (core: `CreateMessageBody.quiet`, `MessagesService.create` and `ConversationsService.recordReply`): the customer gets the message and the open thread and the preview of the list show it in real time, but the conversation does not change at all: `lastMessageAt` stays (the order of the list stays), it is not reopened, and `lastOutboundAt`, `firstResponseAt` and `firstAutomatedResponseAt` are not touched. Without `quiet` a reply moves and reopens the conversation like an agent's. A note changes nothing in the conversation, as today. `quiet` is for plugins only in the MVP (no checkbox for agents) and the send is subject to the messaging window of the channel like any other. **Trace:** two new columns of `Message`, in one migration with its rollback script: `authorPluginId` (the plugin that wrote it, always, whichever the author) and `authorNickname`; `MessageView` returns both, and the "automatic" mark and "sent through <plugin>" of Step 3.2 are drawn here, once a message can carry them (decided 2026-10-30). Every plugin message is audited with the plugin as the actor. There is no `from: user`: a plugin cannot write as the customer. Tests: a quiet reply leaves `lastMessageAt`, status, `lastOutboundAt`, `firstResponseAt` and `firstAutomatedResponseAt` as they were; a `system` reply never sets `firstResponseAt`; an `agent` reply does; `agent` with a user of another store or with no `userId` is refused; a plugin without `messages:write` gets 403; isolation case
- [ ] Outbound plugin webhooks (`TRIGGER_PLUGIN_WEBHOOK`): signed, payload redacted to the granted scopes, same delivery log and auto-disable as tenant webhooks
- [ ] Revocation with typed confirmation, final `plugin.revoked` webhook, audit
- [ ] **Expose to plugins everything the product does (owner, 2026-11-12: all of it must be reachable from the SDK and `plugin-api` by third-party plugins).** The map is `scripts/plugin-api-coverage-map.ts`, checked by `pnpm validate:plugin-api` (CI and pre-push); the routes still to expose are frozen in `scripts/plugin-api-baseline.json` (162 at the start of this task) and the list can only shrink. For each route the controller goes under `PluginApiKeyGuard` over the same service as the UI, the entry of the map gets `exposed: '/plugin-api/...'`, and `pnpm validate:plugin-api --update-down` lowers the baseline. Add the new scopes to `PluginScopes` and to `PluginScopePermissions` (a scope grants a fixed subset of the human permissions) in the same PR as their routes; a scope that no route uses is not added. Groups, by scope:
  - **Exist, routes pending:** `conversations:read|write` (list, by id, origins, messaging window, assign, status, snooze, tags, read/unread, bulk), `messages:read|write` (send, edit, resend, discard, resend caption, refetch, reaction, revisions, deleted content; the item above defines `POST /plugin-api/messages`), `customers:read|write` (the whole customer profile: identities, notes, addresses, tags, merge/unmerge, timeline, caution), `orders:read|write` (list, by id, create, cancel, status, items, payment, confirm, bulk confirm, copy, price approvals), `products:read` (search), `inventory:read|write` (levels, summary, movements, kardex, adjustments, the inventory preferences), `shipments:read|write` (the 4.8 to 4.10 routes: by order, bulk label runs and merge, label, cancel, status, check, mark voided), `analytics:read`, and the search routes of messages, customers, orders and products.
  - **Proposed, new:** `products:write` (create, variants, images), `warehouses:read|write`, `transfers:read|write`, `counts:read|write`, `carriers:read` (list, registry, locations, unmapped statuses; the credentials, the secret and the status mappings stay human), `pickups:read|write`, `returns:read|write` (including the reception batches, Step 4.12), `remittances:read|write` (cash on delivery, Step 4.13), `order-messages:read|write` (the messages and templates of an order, Step 4.9), `media:read|write`, `exports:read|write` (the export of conversations, customers, orders, analytics and the download-all of a conversation), `tags:read|write`, `channels:read` (directory and templates), `settings:read` (store preferences).
  - **Never, with the reason in the map:** the session, the account, notifications and push, presence and the team directory, the organization, team, roles, invitations, the security settings (safety, webhooks, API keys, retention), plugin administration, compliance, the public routes (webhooks, widget, OAuth callback), the connection of a channel account and of a carrier, and the settings of routing, hours, snippets and inbox behaviour.
  - Open points: (a) a route whose methods need different scopes lists all of them (`scope: [read, write]`) and the controller of this step states, for each method, which it takes: every human permission of that method must be inside the scopes it takes (the code review of the map found ORDERS_CANCEL, ORDERS_CONFIRM, ORDERS_APPROVE_PRICE, ORDERS_EDIT, CUSTOMERS_MERGE, MESSAGES_VIEW_DELETED, MEDIA_VIEW, SETTINGS_EDIT and SETTINGS_MANAGE_ORDER_TEMPLATES outside today's scopes: they have scopes of their own in the map), and a route that names only a read scope exposes its GET only. The validator cannot read the permissions of the controllers: write the check that every plugin-api route's `@RequirePermissions` is inside the union of the permissions of its scopes (`PluginScopePermissions`) in this step's tests; (b) when `exposed` exists the validator checks only its shape: add the plugin-api route constants to `ApiRoutes` here and make the validator read them; (c) a session that adds a route before this step writes "plugin-api: Step 5.6 (scope ...)" in its own step and maps the route in its PR.

- [ ] Settings → Plugins UI: register, list installations with their scopes, revoke (`SETTINGS_MANAGE_PLUGINS`). `plugins.list`, `byId`, `enable`, `disable` and `config` have no handler and no task: build them here with their screens, or remove the constants if the install and uninstall of Step 2.7b made them redundant

## Milestone 7 — Production readiness

### Step 7.1 — Security hardening
- [x] **Lock timeout of the api (QA, 2026-11-07; the base blocked for 25 s left every request waiting):** the role `platform_app` gives up after `ApiLockTimeout` (5 s, under the 15 s of a tenant transaction) through `ALTER ROLE ... IN DATABASE ... SET lock_timeout` (migration `20261113120000_api_lock_timeout`, rollback beside it; a role setting and not `SET`, which `TDD_01 §3.2` forbids through PgBouncer). The api answers `503 DATABASE_BUSY` with `retryAfterSeconds` (`isLockTimeoutError`, `GlobalExceptionFilter`; a warning, not a Sentry event). **Rollout:** a connection already open keeps the old setting, so restart the api (and PgBouncer's server connections) after the migration. **Left on purpose, owner Step 7.1:** (1) `statement_timeout` for the api (a runaway query still holds a connection; needs the slowest real query measured first, then a value above it); (2) the worker has no lock timeout: its jobs are long and retried, decide per job with the measures of Step 7.3; (3) `idle_in_transaction_session_timeout`; (4) the web draws `DATABASE_BUSY` with a generic message and no retry button; (5) no metric counts these timeouts (the log line is all there is); (6) **the web retries a 503**: `shouldRetry` in `apps/web/lib/query-client.ts` retries every 5xx twice, so a blocked table makes each page view hold up to three connections for 5 s each: skip `DATABASE_BUSY` there and honor `retryAfterSeconds` (a web task; the inbox bundle has little room, measure it) **Done in PR `fix/web-no-retry-database-busy`:** a busy database is tried once more (not twice) after the wait the api asks for (`retryDelayOf`, up to 30 s), through `ClientErrorCodes.DATABASE_BUSY`; only reads are tried again (a mutation never is: a write sent again blind can repeat its effects), and a query that sets its own `retry` does not get the rule, and none does today; (7) the 503 is mapped only by the HTTP filter: a lock timeout in the websocket gateway, a bridged event handler or a cron that runs as `platform_app` surfaces as an error log; (8) **the migration needs the privilege to alter `platform_app`** (the one that created the role has it); on a server where infrastructure provisioned the role, grant it or run the `ALTER ROLE` once by hand, and the deploy docs of Step 7.3 must say so.
- [ ] Helmet on the API, CSP in Next with per-store plugin origins
- [ ] Rate limiting: application (Redis), edge (Cloudflare rules)
- [ ] **Row-Level Security** (`TDD_01 §3.2` layer 3; owner decision D16, `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §7):
  - The roles `platform_app` (subject to RLS, used by `apps/api`) and `platform_worker` (`BYPASSRLS`, used by `apps/worker`, which also processes every inbound channel event) already exist since Step 0.4, and the services already connect with them; this step only turns the policies on
  - `scripts/rls.sql`, generated from the schema, with `ENABLE` + `FORCE ROW LEVEL SECURITY` and one policy per table: tables with `store_id` use `app.store_ids`; the organization-scoped inventory tables (`warehouses`, `stock_transfers`, `stock_counts`) use `app.organization_ids`; `store_warehouses` uses its `store_id`; child tables (messages, order items, transfer and count lines, allocations, movements) follow their parent. The `audit_logs` grants already exist since Step 0.4
  - Identity and bootstrap tables are NOT under RLS, because the guards read them before any tenant context exists: `users`, `sessions`, `verification_tokens`, `invitations`, `organizations`, `stores`, `memberships` and `roles`. They stay protected by code (user id or token) and by the isolation suite
  - **The mechanism is already chosen and was verified by a spike** (2026-10-24, `TDD_01 §3.2`): one interactive transaction per unit of work with `set_config(..., true)` as its first statement, kept in an `AsyncLocalStorage`, with `PrismaService.client` returning that transaction and `PrismaService.transaction(fn)` joining it. The per-operation extension was rejected because it breaks atomicity. This step implements it and re-runs the same checks as tests: atomic rollback, no cross-tenant leak under concurrency through PgBouncer, raw SQL (`SearchService`), parallel queries on one transaction, and row locks (`TDD_16 §4.2`)
  - **Tenant interceptor:** after `TenantGuard`, a `TenantContextInterceptor` runs the whole handler inside `PrismaService.tenantTransaction(ctx, fn)`, which sets `app.store_ids` and `app.organization_ids` with `set_config(..., true)` (never the session-level form; a lint rule forbids `false`) and applies explicit `maxWait` and `timeout` limits. The `await` of every query stays inside the scope, and **no external I/O runs inside it** (carrier, channel, S3 and e-mail calls are queued and run in the worker). `TenantGuard` fills the context from the caller's memberships; plugin API requests set only the installation's store; widget requests set only the store of the site key's connection; requests with no context see no rows. Streaming endpoints and the WebSocket gateway use an explicit `TenantContext` instead of the interceptor
  - **API code that runs outside a tenant request fails loudly instead of returning empty results:** the WebSocket gateway, domain-event listeners fed by the events bridge (notifications, plugin webhooks), and the public endpoints must either run inside an explicit `TenantContext.run({ storeIds, organizationIds }, fn)` taken from the event payload or the verified credential, or hand the work to the worker. In the `api` process a query on a store-scoped table with no context throws in dev, test and CI
  - **Pre-context lookups** (widget site key to store, plugin API key hash to installation, tenant API key hash to store, a carrier webhook's connection id to its store) use small `SECURITY DEFINER` SQL functions that `platform_app` may call and that return only the store id. A globally unique value that belongs to another store (a `ChannelConnection` `(pluginId, connectionId)`) is detected from the unique violation, never by reading the other store's row
  - Add the policies for the Step 4.2 to 4.12 tables here; the isolation suite and the integration, security and e2e CI jobs run as `platform_app` from this step on, and a test proves that a query without `storeId` still returns nothing from another store
- [ ] **Outbox for the invitation email (review backlog of PR A1, Step 3.6):** inviting and resending revoke or replace the old invitation before the email is queued, so a queue that is down leaves the invitee with no live link until the cooldown passes. Write the email in the same transaction as the invitation (an outbox row the worker sends) on both routes, and test that a failed queue leaves the old invitation alive
