The sweep of all suites found no other wide cleanup (queues and Redis keys carry the prefix of their run). Nothing is left unscoped.
- [ ] **One helper for the sweeps of the orphan cleaner (review backlog of PR A1, Step 3.6):** `deleteStrayAvatarUploads` copies the paging loop of `deleteStrayObjects`; extract one helper (root, cursor key, classifier) when a third sweep is added, or here if none was
- [x] **Dependency audit gate (dependencies lane, PR A, 2026-11-07):** `lint-and-types` runs `pnpm audit --prod --audit-level=high`. It audits the `dependencies` of every workspace package (api, worker, web and widget alike, not only what an image carries), never the `devDependencies`. A new high or critical advisory fails every pull request until it is fixed: on purpose, and the same severity bar as Trivy. Unlike Trivy it does not skip advisories without a fix (`pnpm audit` has no such option, and a registry outage also turns the step red: rerun it; the step has a 3 minute limit); one that has no fix goes to `auditConfig.ignoreGhsas` in `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, one entry per GHSA with the reason written beside it, and is listed here. `zod` of `@serwist/build` and `@serwist/turbopack` is pinned to 4.6.7 by override (GHSA-mrhx-c7xc-m3w5) and `fflate` is 0.8.3 (GHSA-px8p-9vwx-vf98; a root devDependency, so the gate does not cover it, bumped because it is a patch). The lockfile also re-resolved `ts-jest` to the root `esbuild` 0.28.2 (it kept 0.25.12 before); unit, integration and security suites pass with it.
- [ ] **Production advisories left open on purpose (dependencies lane, PR A):** `deepmerge` 4.3.1 under `react-email > html-to-text` (GHSA-gcqx-w48m-w2h4, low, no patched version exists; its input is our own email templates, not user data) and `glob` / `minimatch` under `@sentry/nestjs > @sentry/node > @sentry/bundler-plugins` (GHSA-3g3m-2fjr-6pw4, GHSA-g3hv-75wg-m249, moderate; a build-time plugin that does not run in the api, and forcing glob 14 over a major would break it). None blocks the gate (it stops at high). Closes in Step 7.3, before the api is exposed: re-run `pnpm audit --prod`, bump `@sentry/*` and `react-email` if they have published a release, and write the reason for whatever stays. The development-only advisories (axios, follow-redirects, brace-expansion, smol-toml under `nx`) closed with the Nx 24 upgrade (see the next task).
- [x] **Nx 24 upgrade (dependencies lane, 2026-11-07):** `nx`, `@nx/js`, `@nx/eslint`, `@nx/eslint-plugin` and `@nx/jest` 23.2.0 to 24.0.4 together with `nx migrate` (24.0.4, not 24.0.5: the supply-chain release age policy is not relaxed). `pnpm audit` went from 13 high, 10 moderate, 1 low to 0 high, 2 moderate, 1 low. Dependabot #93 to #95 are closed with a comment pointing at this PR once it is merged. All 18 targets are `nx:run-commands` and no plugin is inferred, so the removed `@nx/jest` and `@nx/eslint` executors touch nothing. `"affected": {"legacy": true}` in `nx.json` keeps the whole-project `nx affected` of Nx 23: the affected project list of four commit ranges was identical under Nx 23 and Nx 24.
- [ ] **Move `nx affected` to the task-based mode of Nx 24 (closes in this step, 7.1, and before the Nx 25 upgrade at the latest, since the legacy flag may go):** Nx 24 selects individual affected tasks by default instead of whole projects; the upgrade keeps the old behavior with `"affected": {"legacy": true}` in `nx.json`. Remove that setting in its own PR. It changes which tests and lints run on each pull request (CI `nx affected`, `pnpm validate:affected`, the `pre-push` hook), so that PR must show that no coverage is lost: the same change run under both modes, with the list of tasks each one ran.
- [ ] **Metadata of files that are not static pictures (PR strip-image-metadata, rule 12):** the picture cleaning (`TDD_07 §6.5`) leaves a gif, an animated WebP and an animated PNG as they came, and does not touch PDF, docx or xlsx, so the author, the software and, for an animation, any location or comment they carry still reach the customer. Close it by cleaning documents on upload (PDF info and XMP, the Office `docProps`) and by re-encoding an animation frame by frame within the caps of `AnimatedImage` while keeping the loop and the delays; owner: this step.
- [ ] **Video and audio metadata (PR strip-image-metadata, rule 12):** an mp4 or mov from a phone carries its GPS in the container (`©xyz`); not cleaned. Needs a re-mux without the metadata atoms (ffmpeg in the worker image); owner: this step.
- [ ] **Memory of the picture cleaning (PR strip-image-metadata, rule 12):** a picture of up to `ImageSanitizing.MAX_INPUT_PIXELS` (50 million) is decoded whole: about 200 MB of raster, plus the encoder, per job. The heavy queue runs 2 jobs at once (`@Processor(..., { concurrency: 2 })`), so the worker needs room for about 1 GB of it at the peak; an out-of-memory error is retried, not refused. Size the worker container for it (at least 1 GB) and keep that concurrency (or lower the cap) if it ever grows; owner: Step 7.3.
- [ ] **Existing pictures (PR strip-image-metadata, rule 12):** files stored before this change keep their metadata. A one-off job over the `clean` image rows of messages would run `sanitizeImage` on each stored original (safe to repeat; there is no marker of which were cleaned); decide with the owner whether it is worth it for the MVP, which holds one store's own history; owner: this step.
- [x] **The spec of the api that lost avatars to the cleaner (found in PR strip-image-metadata, closed in PR test/api-integration-flakes, rule 12):** `AccountController › the profile › replaces the picture and removes the old row and file, then takes it away with null` and `... serves a picture once it is clean and re-encoded, and says nothing of one still being prepared` failed now and then (once in CI as `media not found`). One cause, not two: the `worker` and `api` integration targets run in parallel on one database and one bucket (the default of `nx run-many`), and the spec of the orphan cleaner ran `cleaner.run(later())` with a clock 25 hours ahead, which makes every unattached avatar and every stray object of the last hour look a day old, so its sweep, which looks at all the table and all the bucket on purpose, took the avatars that the api spec had just made (the case then failed on a 404 that it never checks, or found a row gone). A first reading of this note said the second case failed alone, from a deletion that happens after the answer: it did not (the deletion is in the transaction, and the spec alone passed 32 times in 32); the "alone" runs had two cleaner specs beside them. Closed by `OrphanMediaCleaner.runScoped(scope, now)`, a method of its own with a required `CleanupScope` (so that forgetting it is a compile error and never a sweep of everything; the schedule calls `run()`), that limits rows and objects to the stores and users it names (the media of a store, the avatars of a user, the prefix of each), validates the ids as uuids, uses no cursor of the schedule, and is used by every case of the cleaner spec that moves the clock; measured: the avatar spec failed 3 times in 10 with two unscoped cleaner specs beside it and 0 in 10 (twice) with the scoped ones. The cases that check the cursor of the schedule, the listing of the roots and the sweep of everything run without a scope, with the real clock and with the passes they do not test replaced by nothing, so they reach nothing of the suites beside them. Left as it was, by intent: a scoped run counts its batch per prefix and lists each prefix from the top (it is for the few objects of a spec).

Decide with the pentest whether to freeze it: a conversation reassigned in the seconds between the request and the file could be included for a member who no longer sees it. If frozen, store the visible ids at request time for filter exports as the bulk export already does, and add the case to `tests/api/exports.spec.ts`.

Give `GET /settings/business-hours` the zone of the store when the hours are empty and read it from there
- [ ] **From the QA fix PRs (QA backlog, rule 12), the hourly avatar limit has no code of its own (Q7a, (b)):** the avatar refusal for too many pictures cannot tell "five held" from "twenty signed urls in the last hour" (both are `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`), so its text says "several pictures in progress" for both. Give the hourly limit its own error code and text with the upload quota work of this step
- [ ] **From the QA fix PRs (QA backlog, rule 12), currency validation:** the currency `ZZZ` is accepted and the list holds obsolete currencies. Validate against the current list in the input validation pass and remove the obsolete ones

The bell and the e-mail digest read it, so it is kept. The retention job and the deletion of a customer's data (DSR) must sweep it together with the rest of that customer's data.

Accepted risk: two pull requests that are green apart may break each other once together, and that shows only in the CI of `main`, which is watched after every merge.
- [ ] **From the final QA pass (api lane, session 8e; rule 12; owner Step 7.1):** (a) the audit log says `conversation.resolve_undone` for any status change with `undoesResolution`, also hours after the resolution (the window of the Undo is held by the client; the server only decides whether the thread keeps its lines, `retractResolvedLine`): P3, record a late reopening as a reopening (`conversations.controller.ts`, where the action is chosen); (b) `assign` answers 409 `the conversation is assigned to someone else` with `assignedTo` to a caller who may not see the conversation (same store, by design so that who loses a race learns it is taken): P3 of tenancy, leave `assignedTo` out of the 409 when the conversation is not visible to the caller; run `/code-review` at `high` when it is done.

tax-retention legal hold on orders, and the day-25 alert fires in a test.

### Step 7.3 — Deployment
- [ ] **MVP platform: Railway** (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §7, D9). The Dockerfiles for api and worker (`docker/node-service.Dockerfile`, Step 0.5) and web (Step 3.1) already exist; this step adds PgBouncer and ClamAV as Docker services in the same Railway project (`admin` image and service DEFERRED). Google Cloud (Cloud Run, Cloud Build, Cloud SQL, Memorystore, Secret Manager) is the Phase 2 target
- [ ] **The websocket is public with the api** (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §10 item 34): set `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS` to the proxies in front on Railway (the limit of handshakes by address reads the client from it), and add an edge rule that caps connections and handshakes per address (Cloudflare in front, `TDD_05 §3.3`, or the platform's own)
- [ ] **`/metrics` and the probes share the port of the service**, and a Railway public domain exposes every path of it: decide here how `/metrics` stays private (scrape over private networking and keep the public domain off the api, or a `METRICS_TOKEN` checked by the metrics controller) before the first deploy; `TDD_04 §2.2` assumes a network rule that Railway does not offer
- [ ] Railway project with staging and production environments; Postgres and Redis as Railway services; the worker always running (one instance minimum); secrets as Railway variables; private networking between services
- [ ] Release flow (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §7, D13): merges to `main` deploy to staging automatically; production deploys only from a version tag with manual approval, as `TDD_03 §4` says. Migrations run before each deploy. Verify with Context7 how Railway deploys from a tag, how to require approval and how to run migrations before a deploy, and write that into `TDD_03 §4`
- [ ] Postgres on Railway's official image, pinned by **major** tag (`:18`; Railway supports 14 to 18 and does not support minor tags such as 18.6; PITR also requires the official image), so production runs Postgres 18 at whatever minor Railway publishes while local development keeps `postgres:18.6-alpine`; record this in `TDD_01 §2.4`. Backups: volume backups and PITR enabled from day one (`railway postgres pitr`), plus periodic `pg_dump` copies stored outside Railway
- [ ] Media stays on AWS S3 (versioning and replication per `TDD_03 §7.3`; the IAM role of the api and the worker holds `s3:ListBucket` besides get, put, copy and delete: without it S3 answers 403 for a missing key and `confirm-upload` fails with a 500 instead of `FILE_UPLOAD_FAILED`, and `HeadBucket` in `/health/ready` needs it too; only `S3_*` and `AWS_*` variables change). Railway buckets lack versioning and lifecycle rules
- [ ] Backup restore drill executed and documented (PITR restore into a new sibling service)
- [x] **`pnpm dev`, `dev:api`, `dev:web`, `dev:worker` and `dev:all` (debt of PR D2 of Step 3.3, owner 2026-11-01):** `TDD_14 §2` promises them and they do not exist. The api, web and worker have no `serve` target (only lint, type-check, test and build), so a laptop runs the `nx build` bundles by hand with no hot reload. Add the targets and the root scripts, and document them in the README **Done in QA fix PR Q8:** `pnpm dev` (api, worker and web), `dev:api`, `dev:worker` and `dev:web` exist as `serve` targets (`nest build --watch` plus `scripts/dev-service.ts` for the two Nest apps, `next dev` for the web); there is no `dev:all` or `dev:admin` because the MVP has no admin app. Documented in `TDD_14 §2` and `§3`. `pnpm infra:reset` is not part of it: see "Left by QA fix PR Q8" in the QA backlog.
- [ ] **Media memory and cleanup at scale (debt of PR B2 of Step 3.4):** (1) `InboundMediaFetcher` holds the whole file in memory (up to 25 MB, and about twice that at the peak while the chunks are joined, two jobs at a time): store it by streaming (multipart upload) if the worker memory budget measured here needs it; (2) `OrphanMediaCleaner` lists 30 pages of 1000 objects per run and keeps its cursor in Redis, so a large bucket is covered over several hours: measure how long a full turn takes and move to `DeleteObjects` batches or a per-store prefix if it is too slow
- [ ] **Several pictures in a row: reaction or quote that does not show (MULTI-IN, owner 2026-11-08, open):** the owner reports that with 2 or 3 pictures received in a row (WhatsApp, Messenger, Instagram) only the first one shows a reaction or a quote. QA and the lane could NOT reproduce it: the core stores each wamid/mid and resolves reactions and quotes for the 2nd and 3rd picture (`tests/api/inbound-multi-image.spec.ts`), and the simulator draws them. The api now logs, with the channel only and no id, when a quote points at a message the thread does not have (info `an inbound message quotes a message that is not here`) and when a reaction found nothing to attach to (warn `reaction not applied: no message, customer or connection to attach it to`; it also covers an unknown connection or customer). The quote line and the reaction line now carry `target`, the SHAPE of the id that matched nothing (`length`, a 3-character `prefix` when plain, `sameShortPrefix` and `sameLongPrefix` = messages of the thread whose id starts the same, `recentCustomerMessages` in the last 2 minutes), never the id (read after the transaction of the ingestion, so a failing count cannot abort it; Prisma does NOT escape `_` or `%` in `startsWith`, so `escapeLike` does it: any new `startsWith` on a provider id needs the same, a Messenger id starts with `m_`): the owner's log on 2026-11-08 showed that the 2nd picture's reaction and quote find no message though each picture has its own id, so the shape tells whether Meta sends another format or prefix, a truncated id, or a message that never arrived. Close it: the owner repeats it on the three channels with that deployed; a line means the target id Meta sends is not the one of the stored message (map it); no line means the screen does not draw it (web fix).
- [ ] **Instagram location (IG-LOC, owner 2026-11-08):** the owner confirms Instagram lets a customer share a location, and the thread shows "this channel does not allow showing it". Do not assume a Meta limit. The worker now logs, permanently and with names only (never a value or a link), the type and the field names of every message of a type it cannot show (`inbound message of a type the channel cannot show`, `unsupportedShape` in the message metadata). Close it: read that log after the owner resends a location in dev, then map the real shape to the location card (`MessageType.LOCATION`, #228/#238) in `meta-messaging-events.ts`, with a test of that payload; if Instagram sends no location field at all, keep the honest text and record the limit here. **Update 2026-11-08 (owner's log):** Instagram sends a place as a `template` attachment whose payload holds `generic.elements[]` (title, subtitle, image, a link). It is now drawn as the location card when a link of the element (its `default_action` or a button) is a map of an EXACT host (Google Maps, Apple Maps, OpenStreetMap; https, no credentials; the `l.instagram.com` redirect followed once) that holds coordinates (`map-link.ts`); the title and subtitle become the name and address. A template with no such link stays the marker of an unsupported type, and the log now also names the fields of the elements read (`elementFields`, e.g. `default_action.url`). Open: (1) the owner resends a location in dev and the log says whether the link holds coordinates (a short link such as `maps.app.goo.gl` holds none and is not followed: if that is what Instagram sends, the place needs a lookup that this step does not make); (2) the hosts are exact (`MapLinks`: Google `.com`, `.com.sv`, `.com.mx`, Apple, OpenStreetMap): another regional Google domain stays the marker until it is added there; (3) only what names the place counts (Apple `coordinate`, Google place pages and `query`/`q`/`destination`, OpenStreetMap `mlat`/`mlon`); the `ll` of Apple and the view of OpenStreetMap are map centres and stay the marker: if the owner's log shows that is what Instagram sends, decide there; (4) any template with a map button is drawn as a place, whatever its title: no place-only signal is known, and the log of `elementFields` is where one would show; (5) the redirect hosts (`l.instagram.com`, `l.facebook.com`) are followed whatever their path, once and only to a map: nothing is fetched, so this only widens what is read; pin the real redirect path when the log shows it; (6) a link with 7+ decimals is cut to 7; (7) a template that is a place but has no usable link is not shown with its title: a text with a key `Shared location` in the thread (es/en) is the next step if the log shows it is needed.
- [ ] **The pre-lookup check of a media link is opt-in (debt of the MS-19 fix, QA round v3):** `ChannelPlugin.acceptsMediaLink` runs before the worker looks up the host of a link, but only the Meta plugins define it. A plugin with `fetchMedia` and no such list (WhatsApp and the web widget take ids today, not links) still gets its customer's link resolved by `assertPublicHttpsUrl`. Make the hook required for any plugin whose media refs can be links, or refuse links for a plugin that does not declare it. Also: a host the plugin accepts is resolved by the worker and again by the plugin's own `fetch` (no pinning across the two).
- [ ] **Messenger like sizes are verified only by Meta's documentation (MSG-LIKE, QA 2026-11-08):** `MetaMessaging.LIKE_STICKER_IDS` holds the small, medium and large like ids from the docs; the owner's test saw the medium and the large arrive as stickers with an id, but no raw webhook has confirmed the ids. When a raw payload is captured (worker log of PR IG-STK 1 or the simulator), check them against it; an id that is not in the list stays a plain sticker, never guessed. Owner: Step 7.3 QA repeat.
- [ ] **A Messenger sticker whose image has a different link is not marked as a sticker (MSG-STK limit, QA 2026-11-08):** `isStickerOnly` marks a message only when every file shares the link of an attachment with a sticker id (the pair Meta sends today). If Meta sends a sticker beside a second image of another link, the message stays a photo. Not guessed: confirm with a raw webhook (same capture as the like ids) and then decide which file is the sticker. Owner: Step 7.3 QA repeat.
- [ ] **Instagram stickers, GIFs and likes (IG-STK, QA 2026-11-08):** real logs of the owner's test: the GIF is a link to `media1.giphy.com` / `media4.giphy.com`, which this PR accepts (closed list `MetaGraph.MEDIA_GIF_HOSTS`, exact hosts of Giphy and Tenor, only for a customer's link); a sticker or a like reaches us as a message marked `is_unsupported` with no attachment, so there is nothing to fetch. The GIF host list is closed on purpose: another Giphy/Tenor host (e.g. `media.giphy.com`) is added only when a real log shows it. Left: the thread says so in a clear sentence instead of the marker (PR IG-STK 1c, web). Known limit, not a defect of ours: Messenger delivers a sticker as one still frame (a small jpeg, measured in the owner's test of 2026-11-08), so the animation never reaches us; GIFs (image/gif) are kept animated. Reopen only if a sticker arrives as an animated gif or webp and is drawn still. Remove the diagnostic `host` field of `unavailableFiles` when no longer needed.
- [ ] **Backfill of ad pictures (debt of PR E4 of Step 3.4; owner agreed 2026-11-01):** origins recorded before PR E4 have no `Media` row for their picture. A one-off script queues `FETCH_ORIGIN_MEDIA` (job id `originMediaJobId`, deterministic, so it is safe to repeat) for each origin that has a link and no `Media` row; skip it when the database holds only test data at go-live
- [ ] **Old export rows (debt of PR D1 of Step 3.4):** `ExpiredExportsCleaner` deletes the file of an export and marks the row `expired`, but the row of a `failed` or `expired` export stays for ever. Delete them after a period (they hold no personal data, only the filters), in the same cleanup, with `ExportLimits` constants
- [ ] **A stalled bucket read hangs a ZIP job (debt of PR D2 of Step 3.4; closed in this step, 7.3):** `ZipExportWriter` waits for each file with no idle timeout; a connection that goes silent holds the job until its lock expires and the retry may write the same key. Destroy the file stream after an idle period so the stall is an ordinary retryable failure
- [ ] **Export generation at scale and scope (debts of PR D1 of Step 3.4, from its review):** (1) the conversations source runs one message query per conversation; read the messages of a page of conversations in one keyset query ordered by `(conversationId, createdAt, id)`. (2) `ExpiredExportsCleaner` reads the expired rows oldest first and a row whose file keeps failing to delete is read first on every run, so a hundred of them would starve the rest: move failed rows behind (a retry-after column or a cursor in Redis). (3) The conversations export applies the assignment of each conversation when the file is made, not when it was asked for, and the worker does not check that the requester is still a member with the permissions; freeze the set of conversation ids at the request (or check the membership and permissions in `ExportGenerator` before reading), and cover a reassignment and a removed member in `tests/api/exports.spec.ts`
- [ ] **A throttled customer-profile read waits as long as any failure (debt of the CAN-31 fix, QA round v3):** a send that Meta throttles now fails with `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` and waits from 15 s (`outboundBackoff`), but the `CUSTOMER_PROFILES` queue still retries 3 times from `CustomerProfileFetch.BACKOFF_MS`, so a throttle can spend them before it lifts. Give that queue the same throttle-aware strategy.
- [ ] Cursor pagination, with a hard page size, on the lists of members, roles, pending invitations and stores (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §10 item 20)
- [ ] **The default rate limit counts per IP, not per person (QA FA-6, decided in QA fix PR Q4, rule 12):** `ThrottleProfiles.DEFAULT` (100 per minute) keys on the client address, so several agents behind one office address share it. It stays per IP in v1.2 on purpose: the throttler guard runs before `AuthGuard`, so the person is not known yet, and keying on the token without verifying it would let a caller choose a fresh key per request and never be counted. The fix is a second throttler, registered after the auth guard, keyed on `request.user.id` for authenticated routes, with the IP one kept for public routes; measure first with the real office address once the demo has users (`rate_limit_exceeded` per route in Grafana), then set the per-person number. Closes in this step with `TRUST_PROXY_HOPS`, because the IP key is only right once the proxies are counted.
- [ ] **One config for the public API URL (debt (d) of PR A2 of Step 3.6, see the note of that step):** `PublicApiConfig` (`libs/core/http/src/public-api-platform.module.ts`) and `WidgetEmbedConfig.apiUrl` (`libs/channels/web-widget/src/widget-config.ts`, provided in `web-widget-platform.module.ts`) both read `API_URL`. The widget lib takes `PublicApiConfig` and `WidgetEmbedConfig` keeps only `scriptUrl`, so the public URLs of the deployment have one source. The only consumer of `apiUrl` is `apps/api/src/modules/widget/widget-settings.service.ts` (the connect-src origin and the embed snippet): `widget-settings.service.spec.ts` must still show the same snippet and origin.

- [ ] **A second Meta app for Instagram (PR `feat/instagram-own-app-secret`, owner, 2026-11-05):** Meta's Facebook Login for Business needs the portfolio that owns the app to differ from the one that owns the Instagram account, and `omnichannel-dev` and `@smartgadgetsv_` are both in the portfolio "Erick Martinez". The owner chose a new app owned by "Erick Ads", for Instagram only (WhatsApp and Messenger stay on `omnichannel-dev`, the account does not move). Built: `META_INSTAGRAM_APP_SECRET` (optional; the same length and not-the-verify-token rules as `META_APP_SECRET` in a deployed environment; an empty value is unset); `MetaAppConfig.instagramAppSecret`; `MetaGraphClient.isValidSignature(rawBody, headers, app)` with `MetaWebhookApps`: Instagram's webhooks are signed by the Instagram app's secret ALONE when it is set (the main secret no longer passes there) and by the main secret when it is not; WhatsApp and Messenger always answer to the main secret (the contract suite proves both). The verify token stays one, shared: the owner sets the same value in both apps. Owner steps: `~/qa-reportes-2026-11-04/instagram-app-nueva.md`. **Debt, consolidation later (one app for the three):** if Meta changes the rule or the app becomes a Tech Provider, delete `META_INSTAGRAM_APP_SECRET`, restart the api and the worker, generate the token with the main app and connect Instagram again: no code change. A connection made with the first app keeps working only while the variable matches the app that sends its webhooks; changing one without the other makes the webhooks of Instagram fail the signature check (HTTP 401 in the api log, no message reaches the inbox). The same happens to any Instagram webhook the MAIN app still delivers once the variable is set (the Instagram endpoint then accepts only the second app's key). Cutover order: the second app's Instagram webhook must be live before the variable is set (the main app's deliveries to the Instagram route are refused from then on). Accepted trade-offs (security review): the new variable is checked only in a deployed environment, like `META_APP_SECRET`, and neither secret is trimmed (a trailing space in a secrets store makes a different key); the verify token is shared by both apps (an administrator of the second portfolio learns it; it only answers the GET challenge and does not sign anything), and nothing at boot reports which key protects Instagram (the log of a refused webhook is the signal). A per-app verify token and a boot log of the mode wait for the next PR that touches the Meta webhook controller.
- [x] **Connecting a channel says why it failed (PR `feat/meta-connect-reasons`, owner, 2026-11-05; design in `~/qa-reportes-2026-11-04/meta-conexion-ux-diseno.md`):** every failure of `ChannelConnectionsService.connect` was `CHANNEL_CREDENTIALS_INVALID` ("the channel credentials are not valid") whatever the cause, and the real reason was only in the api log (hours of diagnosis for the owner). Now the code stays one and `metadata.reason` carries a name of the closed list `ChannelConnectFailureReasons` (`libs/core/domain/errors/channel-connect-failures.ts`: `token_malformed`, `token_rejected`, `permission_missing`, `request_rejected`, `account_not_linked`, `other_account`, `provider_answer_unexpected`, `subscription_not_confirmed`, `account_unreadable`, `subscription_failed`, `subscription_unreadable`, `provider_busy`, `provider_unavailable`). The plugin says it: `PluginError.reason` (optional, so third-party plugins still compile), derived by `connectReasonOfCheck` (an exhaustive `switch` over `PluginChecks`: a check added without a reason does not compile, and a test walks the list) or set by `graph-failure.ts` from Meta's numeric code (190 or 401 = token refused; 10, 200 to 299 or 403 = permission; 100 = request refused; throttles = busy; 5xx and outages = unavailable). The api sends only a name of the list (`connectFailureReasonOf` checks it again), never Meta's words, a value or an id; a page older than the server shows the generic message. The web reads it in `connectFailureReasonOf` (`error-message.ts`) and `useErrorText` shows `settings.channels.connectFailure.<reason>` (es and en); the connect form goes back to the token field (`aria-invalid`, the `role="alert"` of `MutationError`) when the reason is about the token. The health check keeps what the owner can fix (`token_rejected`, `permission_missing`, `other_account`, `account_not_linked`) in `lastError` (they are in `ChannelHealthErrors`) and the card offers "Connect again"; a busy or unavailable Meta stays `check_failed`.
- [x] **Update the token of a connection in its own card, and the connect dialog fits its box (PR `feat/channel-connection-ux`, PR A, owner, 2026-11-05):** a connection whose token expired had to be deleted and made again. `POST /channels/connections/:id/token` (`SETTINGS_EDIT`, audit `channel.token_updated`) takes only the secret fields; what the connection holds that is not secret stays, so the account cannot be changed through it. Order: (1) WHO the token belongs to is READ with the plugin's own `healthCheck` run on the credentials the row would have (the account id, and for Instagram the Page id, are compared there), so a token of another account or Page answers `other_account` with no subscription, no write and nothing of the other account in the body or the log; (2) only for the same account `connect` runs (it subscribes the app again, merging fields) and the sealed credentials of THAT row are replaced under `lockStoreForWrite`, conditional on the row still being active or in error (a disconnect that wins stays disconnected). The row, its name, its account id and its conversations are the ones it had; the old token is gone from the database. Another store's connection is the same 404 as one that does not exist. Web: "Update token" in the card (main button when the state says a new token fixes it), `UpdateTokenDialog` reuses `ConnectForm` with the secret fields only; it replaces "Connect again", which opened a blank form that created a second connection for another account. The dialog overflow of the owner's screenshot was measured on the real CSS (scrollWidth 545 over 510 at 1280 px): a grid column is `auto`, and the truncated address in a flex row sets its minimum width, so every `grid` wrapper in a dialog is now `grid-cols-1` (dialog and the settings and org components); an e2e checks 390 and 1280 px. Polish of #190: "expiró" and no "caduca", "venc", "webhook", "enlazada" in the failure texts (spec), a 403 with no Meta code gives no reason (the generic text shows), one `isPluginCheck` for the log and the error, the throttle codes 80007 and 80010-80013, an invented `FAKE_APP_ID`, and the CI trace of a failed e2e explained in `playwright.config.ts`.

- [ ] **Response headers of the bucket (QA fix PR Q6, SEC-3414):** the files the api signs are served by the bucket, not by the api, and S3 lets a signed URL set the type and the disposition only (`ResponseContentType` and `ResponseContentDisposition`, which the api fixes from the row: an uploaded file is always an attachment and the type is the one the bytes were checked against). It cannot set `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` or a `Content-Security-Policy: sandbox`. Put a response-headers policy (CloudFront, or the bucket's own) in front of the production bucket that adds `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; sandbox` to every object, and check it with `curl -I` on a signed URL. The development bucket (SeaweedFS) does not add them; the check of the first bytes at the confirmation (`matchesDeclaredType`) is what keeps a page out of the bucket there.
- [ ] **A wrong credentials key flips every connection to error (left by QA fix PR Q13):** a connection whose saved credentials cannot be opened now gets the health verdict `credentials_unreadable` (status `error`, a `channel.disconnected` event and the notification of each store), and the next health round restores it once the key opens it again. A deploy with the wrong `CREDENTIALS_ENCRYPTION_KEY` therefore flips and notifies every store at once. Decide here whether the api and the worker check at start-up that a sample connection opens and refuse to boot otherwise, and put it in the deployment checklist next to the key rotation. Also: an outbound send with unreadable credentials is retried like any failure and its last attempt fails the message with `CHANNEL_CREDENTIALS_INVALID` (a worker with another key for a while loses nothing), and it does not touch the connection; the health round is what marks it, so the state follows within one round
- [x] **Messenger and Instagram sent files by a public https link (left by the WhatsApp media upload PR; closed by the Meta media upload PR, 2026-11-07):** the cause of "El canal no aceptó el mensaje" on every picture was local, not Meta: the development signed link is `http://localhost:8333`, and `meta-channel.plugin.ts` refused anything that was not a public https url before calling Meta (so the log has no `providerCode`). `sendMedia` now uploads the stored file and sends it in one call (`POST /<owner>/messages`, multipart, `recipient`, `message` and the file in `filedata`, no `attachment_id` and no `is_reusable`, as Meta says for upload-and-send), reading the bytes with `openFile` (checked again to be clean when opened) and never giving Meta a link. The content type is the one the platform recorded when it checked the file, never the client's; the type and the size are checked against a table per channel before the file is opened (`MessengerMediaRules`, `InstagramMediaRules` in `libs/core/domain/meta-media-rules.ts`: Messenger 8 MB for an image and 25 MB for the rest, no list of formats because Meta names none; Instagram png/jpeg/gif up to 8 MB, aac/m4a/wav/mp4 audio, mp4/ogg/avi/mov/webm video and **pdf only** as a file, 25 MB each), with `CHANNEL_FILE_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED` and the new `CHANNEL_FILE_TOO_LARGE`. The send is not repeated after a failure that may have reached Meta (`MetaGraphClient.uploadMedia` takes `fileField` and `safeToRepeat`). Instagram now declares `AUDIO` and `DOCUMENT`, which Meta's page supports. **Left, none blocking:** (1) not proved against a real Messenger or Instagram account (the `filedata` form against `/me/messages` with a Page token is as Meta's page shows it; the Graph fake of the specs is strict about the form): the owner's next real test of a picture, a PDF and a voice note decides it, and the documented formats may differ from what the account accepts; (2) **done in the capability map PR (2026-11-07):** the formats and sizes are the `media` declaration of each plugin (`ChannelPlugin.media`, `libs/core/domain/channel-media-rules.ts`), copied and frozen by the registry (`PluginRegistry.mediaOf`) and required for exactly the kinds a channel declares as capabilities; Messenger, Instagram, WhatsApp and the web widget declare it and the Meta plugins hold a file to it, but nothing in the API reads it yet (the PR of several files per message makes the API refuse a file before queueing it, and the composer offer only what the channel takes); until then the composer offers files by capability, so an agent can attach a docx or an mp3 on Instagram and gets a failed bubble with `CHANNEL_FILE_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED` after the message was queued: the API must refuse it before queueing once the map exists; (3) **done in the same PR:** `SendMediaParams.mediaUrl` is gone and the worker signs no link (no plugin read it; the SDK is in-process only until remote plugins exist in Phase 2, so no third-party plugin breaks, and that Phase must version this contract); (4) Messenger documents list no formats, so a type Meta refuses (100/2018047) arrives as a generic refusal; (5) the worker budget for a media send is now 205 s (upload 180 s, the caption post 15 s, a margin); a document is named by the plain-character name of its file, and a name with other characters is shown with underscores (a name with accents is not kept); (6) stickers and GIFs (WhatsApp) are not sent yet either

the text "[Adjuntar foto]" comes in `message.text` of the webhook and is not ours; an Instagram voice note arrives as mp4/aac/m4a, which `AllowedContentTypes.AUDIO` refuses or which is stored as `video/mp4` (the thread draws by the type of the file, the list by the type of the message); a location needs `payload.coordinates` or, for `is_unsupported`, a text of its own; a WhatsApp `contacts` message needs `MessageType.CONTACTS` with caps. **(a) is built (PR-3 of the series, 2026-11-07):** the like is a single `👍` text with no file; a sticker and its image of the same link are one file; the placeholder text is dropped only when it is exactly one of `MetaMessaging.PLACEHOLDER_TEXTS` (today `[Adjuntar foto]`) **and** a file came with it (an exact list and not a bracket pattern, because `[ok]` beside a photo can be the customer's words; a new placeholder is added when a real payload shows it); a Messenger place with `payload.coordinates` is a `LOCATION` (a pin with no usable coordinates, or one Meta marks `is_unsupported`, stays the `[type]` marker the thread translates); `audio/mp4` and `audio/aac` are allowed uploads (m4a is an mp4 container: same signature as a video, then the scan), an audio of the webhook carries the hint `audio/mp4` so the worker keeps a clip the server calls `video/mp4` or `audio/x-m4a` as `audio/mp4`; `MessageType.CONTACTS` and `ChannelCapability.CONTACTS` are channel agnostic (the cards are in `metadata.contacts`, at most `ContactCardLimits` cards, phones and emails and under `InboundLimits.METADATA_MAX_BYTES` together; addresses, birthday, company and links of a card are dropped on purpose), WhatsApp maps `contacts`. Debts of (a): an `audio/mp4` upload is held to an `ftyp` box whose brand is not QuickTime's or a picture's (HEIC, AVIF), but an mp4 video has the same container as an mp4 audio and the head cannot tell them apart, so a video of brand `mp42` declared as audio is stored as audio (it is scanned and size-limited; telling them apart needs reading the `moov` tracks, left to Step 7.3); control characters in any text a customer shares as a place or a contact become spaces (`placeTextOf`); until (b) lands the thread shows a CONTACTS message as the neutral line "a message this version cannot show" (the list shows "Contact"); the Messenger location shape is Meta's reference and has not been seen in a real payload; the agents' uploads (web and widget) now accept m4a and aac too, so the browser `accept` lists change by two types (no UI change); nothing sends a contact card or a location from the platform (`LOCATION` and `CONTACTS` are inbound only in the MVP). (b) *web:* **the contact card is built (W6, 2026-11-07):** `components/inbox/contact-card.tsx` draws `MessageView.contacts` (names, phones and emails as plain text, never links; each value with a button that copies it and says so in a toast; the kind of a phone or email is translated when it is one of WhatsApp's `CELL|MAIN|IPHONE|HOME|WORK`, and drawn as it came otherwise); the server re-reads the cards from the metadata with the same cleaning and caps as the channel (`sanitizeContactCards`, one card at a time), the client schema bounds them again and falls to the line of a type the page cannot show when they do not fit; `placeTextOf` keeps at most three combining marks on a glyph. A Messenger or Instagram place needs nothing: the Meta plugin stores the same `latitude`, `longitude`, `name` as WhatsApp and the existing card draws it (`messageView location from Messenger and Instagram`). Debts of (b): the card has no component test (the web has no component test setup; it is covered by an e2e that runs in CI only and by unit tests of the view, the kind and the actions); nothing offers to start a conversation or save the contact. Still open: the type of an AUDIO message deciding how the thread draws it. (c) *calls (WhatsApp Calling API: webhook field `calls`, `connect` and `terminate` with duration; no API found for Messenger or Instagram):* researched, **no work until a real test with Calling enabled on the number**; what can be shown is a line "call, duration", never audio, which needs WebRTC. (d) *several files in one message:* design in the investigation of 2026-11-07 (capability map per channel in the SDK, state per attachment with a migration and its rollback, the worker sends N in order and retries only the failed ones); built after the capability map PR. **(d) server side is built (PR-4 of the series, 2026-11-07), switched OFF** (`OUTBOUND_MULTI_FILE_ENABLED=false`; with it off a message carries one file, as before): migration `20261112130000_media_send_state` (+ rollback) adds to `media` the nullable `position`, `delivery_status`, `channel_message_id`, `send_error_code`, `sent_at` and an index `(store_id, channel_message_id)` (no new resource, so no new case in `tenant-isolation.spec`: a file of another store inside a multi-file request is a 404 that links none, tested in `outbound.spec`); `POST /messages` takes up to `MediaMessageLimits.MAX_FILES` (30) ids and refuses, before queuing, more than `maxFilesFor(channel declaration, flag)` (`CHANNEL_TOO_MANY_FILES`, 409, with `max`), a type the channel does not list (`CHANNEL_FILE_TYPE_UNSUPPORTED`) or a file over its size (`CHANNEL_FILE_TOO_LARGE`); all files of a message are of the type of the message (an album of images, or of documents); the worker sends them in order, awaiting each answer, with `idempotencyKey` `<messageId>-<position>` (`-<resentAt>` after an agent sends again; a one-file message keeps the key it always had), the text as the caption of the first, marks each file, continues after a failed one, stops at a retryable failure while attempts remain and skips the files already sent on the next; the message is `sent` when all are, `failed` (with the first failure's code and the first file's provider id kept) when one is not; "send again" re-queues only the failed files; the echo and the receipts of a later file find its message by the id on the file. **API contract for the web lane (2f)**, per file in `MessageView.media[]`: `position` (0-based, null for a customer's file), `delivery` (`queued|sent|delivered|read|failed`, null for a customer's file or one sent before this change: then the message's state is the file's) and `errorCode` (ours, only when failed); the message's own `deliveryStatus` is the aggregate; the files come in the order the agent gave. The composer reads the number of files a channel takes from the channel directory once the web lane adds it (not in this PR: nothing offers more than one file while the switch is off). Debts of (d): (1) the switch is global, not per store or per channel; turning it on needs the web lane (composer, album bubble, "send the failed again") and a real test with each provider; (2) a message of N files takes up to N x `MEDIA_SEND_TIMEOUT_MS` in the worst case and `GIVE_UP_AFTER_MS` is one hour (fine for the 10 files WhatsApp takes, to be re-measured for a channel that declares more); (3) Messenger and Instagram send one provider message per file (no array of images); (4) a receipt moves its own file; the message becomes `delivered` or `read` only when every file has (a `failed` receipt of any file fails the message, and "send again" re-queues only the failed ones); (4b) a file that failed with the outcome unknown (a timeout) is put back in the queue by "send again" like any other failed file, and may reach the customer twice: the doubt is kept on the message (`failureDefinitive` false) and not on the file, so the thread cannot warn per file (a per-file flag is the fix, with the web lane); a file that failed on an attempt is never sent again by the retries of the same job; the refusal of too many files says "one file per message" while the switch is off; (5) a message of mixed kinds (an image and a PDF) is not allowed yet.

WhatsApp, Messenger and Instagram now declare `MetaMediaFilesPerMessage` = 10, a number the platform chose (Meta publishes none); it counts only with the switch on, and the real test with each provider decides whether it stays. The composer (`components/inbox/attachment-tray.tsx`, `lib/media/use-attachments.ts`, `attachment-batch.ts`) takes several files from the picker, shows one chip per file in the order they will go (a picture for an image), uploads at most three at a time, refuses before any byte leaves what the channel does not take (kind, type outside its list, size) and tells the agent when the message is full or a file is of another kind (a message carries images, or videos, or audio, or documents, as the api requires), sends the ready files with `mediaIds` in order and one idempotency key per set of files, and the text goes as the caption of the first. The tray is downloaded when the first file is chosen (dynamic import), so the inbox script grows by about 0.6 KB (353.387 to 353.975 KB with the local recipe). Debts: (1) there is no drag and drop in the composer today, so none was added; (2) the tray has no component test (the web has no component test setup): the rules are unit tested (`attachment-batch.spec.ts`, `upload-gate.spec.ts`) and the flow is covered by `multi-file.spec.ts`, which runs with the switch on (`playwright.shared.ts`); (3) a caption under several audios or one audio is refused by WhatsApp (`checkMedia`, left by the thread PR) and the composer does not warn; (4) the refusal of a file by the channel is judged on the type the browser declares, as the api reads it; (5) a file that was uploaded and then removed stays in the bucket until the orphan cleaner takes it, as before; (6) the picker accepts the types of all kinds at once, so the per-kind number (images 10, documents 10) is checked as the files arrive, not by the picker; the paperclip is off at the largest number of any kind, so on a channel whose kinds differ (one video where images take 10) it stays on until the message tells the agent it is full; (7) the tray keeps its files while the conversation stays open and reports its state to the composer in an effect, one render late: sending itself refuses while any file is still uploading or being checked, so nothing is lost in that render; (9) the `e2e-tests` job of CI now runs a ClamAV service (the same image as the integration job): without a scanner every uploaded file ends `unscannable`, so a case that attaches files could never send; the job waits for the scanner's signature database, which adds up to a few minutes to its start; (8) abandoning the page cancels the uploads one tick after the unmount (so that React in development, which unmounts once right after mounting, does not cancel the files the composer hands over). Owner of the rest: W5 PR 2.

(b) An animation over the caps (`AnimatedImage`: 100 frames, 16 million pixels in all, sized for the two jobs the heavy queue runs at once), one whose bytes are not a gif or a webp, and one with an EXIF orientation (the encoder cannot rotate the frames of an animation by a quarter turn) is drawn still from its first frame and the original downloads as it came; a transcode that thins the frames or turns them is not built (no customer has asked, and decoding more is the risk the caps exist for). (c) A picture the worker never settles (the job died in the dead-letter queue) reads as a skeleton until its links were read eight more times (`VariantWait`: the poll of 15 seconds, so about two minutes, or sooner if events read them) and then as a download; the count lives in the query, so drawing the picture again does not start the wait over, and an event that does bring the variants later still repairs it. The same is true of the pictures the decoder refused before this PR, which have no `processedAt`. `orphan-media-cleaner.service.ts` (`settleStuckScans`) requeues only scans that are still `pending`: make it also queue the variants (`JobNames.PROCESS_IMAGE`, same job id) of a clean image with no `processedAt` older than an hour, so those rows settle by themselves; a test in `orphan-media-cleaner.integration.spec.ts`. (d) Stickers are not sent to a customer yet (WhatsApp accepts them by media id), nor does the composer offer them. Owner: Step 7.3.
- [ ] **Hardening of the media pipeline found by the review of the media PR, in code that PR did not change (rule 12):** (a) `HEAVY_PROCESSING` has `attempts: 2` with a 5 s backoff (`queue-job-options.ts`), so a scanner outage of about ten seconds makes the last attempt of `scan` run `giveUp`, which marks the file `UNSCANNABLE` and deletes it from the bucket; a customer file the provider will not hand over again is lost. Give the scan more attempts with a backoff of minutes, or give up by age of the row. (b) A store image is decoded three times (once per variant) with the 100 million pixel ceiling, against 25 million for an avatar, and the heavy queue runs two jobs at once: decode once to the big variant and derive the other two from it, lower `MAX_IMAGE_PIXELS`, and set sharp's processing timeout. (c) Every sharp error is final (`UnrecoverableError` for a message image; for an avatar `UNSCANNABLE` and the file deleted), including a resource error such as running out of memory, which the queue should retry; and a message image is decoded with sharp's default `failOn` while the avatar uses `failOn: 'error'`, so a photo with a flaw in its tail is refused. (d) `processAvatar`, unlike `processImage` (fixed in the media PR: guarded `updateMany` and the variant keys deleted when the row is gone), still writes with no guard on a row that may have been removed. (e) `processAvatar` deletes its deterministic final key when its update matches no row, even if a second run already finished and the row points at it: delete only when the row is gone. (f) In `media.service.ts` the confirmation drops the staging object and the requester key for any error of the signature check, including a transient bucket failure, and `dropStaging` logs the raw error and the key: clean up only for a refusal, and log `errorName`. (g) Tests: the pixel limit is tested only for avatars (add a store image), the give-up case does not assert that `MEDIA_UNSCANNABLE` is announced, `signVariant` has no case for a key outside the store prefix, and `tests/api/media.spec.ts` writes `3_600_000` and status codes as literals (`QUOTA_WINDOW_MS`, `HttpStatusCodes`). Owner: Step 7.3.

It contradicts that explicit sentence of the TDD, so the TDD changes with it.
