- [x] **Closed by the QA round 1 fix PR (2026-11-04), P2 and the small P3:** (a) the `conversation.unassigned_queue` notification carries the customer in its params (`customer`, the name or `····` plus the end of the handle) and the email of the digest shows, for every block, the store, the date and the time of the newest notification on the clock of the person (`UserNotificationSettings.timezone`), words the link by where it goes (conversation, channels, team, exports, app) and says when the newest of a repeated block happened; (b) the body of every email takes the language of the email (`<body lang>` and its cell; react-email defaulted both to `en`); (c) `GET /members` says `canBeAssigned` (the rule of `AssigneePermissions`) and the pickers of `/asignar`, of the bulk assignment and of the routing rules offer only those, and a refusal of the assignment reads true; (d) the web reads the missing permission from `metadata.permissions` as well as `metadata.required`, so a refusal of a change of status names it; (e) `/no-access` no longer says the account is in no store when it is in one, and offers the way back; (f) SEC-R1-04: the log redaction covers `accessToken`, `password`, `client_secret`, `clientSecret`, `appSecret`, `verifyToken`, `webhookSecret` and the other spellings of a secret at the top level and up to four levels down, and `credentials` and `headers.Authorization` up to four levels (tested with the real pino).
- [ ] **Left by the QA round 1 fix PR (rule 12), the redaction of the log is bounded:** pino's `redact` names a depth with `*`, so the secret keys are covered to four wrapping objects (`REDACTION_DEPTH` in `logger-options.ts`) and not further, and each added level makes pino look at more of the logged object (the cost per log line was not measured with a large payload). Measure it with a realistic large context, and decide between a deeper list and a serializer that walks the object once, matching the names with the same suffix rule as `SENSITIVE_QUERY_SUFFIXES` (`*token`, `*secret`, `*_key`), which also covers the spellings the list does not know. Owner: Step 7.3.
- [ ] **Left by the QA round 1 fix PR (rule 12), the directory of members (review findings):** (a) the rule "a role can be given a conversation" (all of `AssigneePermissions`) is written in four places (`requireAssignee` in `conversations.service.ts`, the SQL of `routing.service.ts`, `routing-rules.service.ts` and `MembersService.directory`): give it one helper; (b) `canBeAssigned` is a yes or no about a teammate's role that any reader of conversations can see (the role name already is public to them): accepted; (c) the routing rule dialog keeps listing, unmarked, a person the rule already holds whose role can no longer answer (so the rule can be fixed; saving it is refused by the api), and the bulk dialog says "no members" when the only others are read-only roles: mark the first with the reason and word the second; (d) the directory cache of the other open sessions is not refreshed when a role changes (only the editing browser invalidates it), so an agent may be offered someone just made unassignable until the query goes stale, and the refusal reads as the generic text above: invalidate it on a realtime event of role change. Owner: Step 7.3.
- [ ] **Left by the QA round 1 fix PR (rule 12), 403 refusals that name no permission:** the refusal of a change of conversation status now names its permission, but the refusals without `metadata` stay generic by design or by absence: deleting a note that is not yours (`notes.service.ts`, it needs `customers:delete` or being the author), suspending yourself, and a message change by someone who may not (`message-changes.service.ts`, `denied()`). Give the first and the last their `metadata.required` so the interface can name the permission. `metadata.permissions` means "all of these are missing" for the services of the team and "any of these" for `requireAnyPermission` (today always one name); keep it so, or split the key, when a route needs two alternatives. Also unify the two keys (`required` from the guard, `permissions` from the services) into `required` on the api, and drop the second reader in `missingPermissionsOf`. Owner: Step 7.3.
- [ ] **Left by the QA round 1 fix PR (rule 12), assigning to someone who cannot answer reads the same as a missing conversation:** the api answers 404 `assignee not found` for a person who left the store, one with a role that cannot answer and a random id, so as not to say which (security). The composer now words that toast for both causes ("the conversation is no longer available, or that person can no longer be given it"); the bulk result still shows the generic "No existe o no puedes abrirlo" per conversation when the role changes between the dialog and the send. Decide with the owner whether a dedicated code (`ASSIGNEE_CANNOT_REPLY`) is worth telling a teammate's role, which every member of the store already sees in the directory. Owner: Step 7.3.
- [ ] **From the QA round 1 (2026-11-04), security, P3, not fixed in its PR (rule 12):** (SEC-R1-01) a truncated PNG of 30 bytes is accepted as an image: confirm answers 200, the scan is clean, `processed` is true and the three variants are null, so the interface may draw a broken image; the check of the magic bytes looks at the header only. Make the processing fail visibly (`failed` state with its text) when `sharp` cannot decode the file, with a case for a truncated and a polyglot PNG. (SEC-R1-02) a customer text with a bidi override (U+202E) is stored and drawn as is, while place names are cleaned (`placeTextOf`): decide with the owner whether free text keeps legitimate bidi, and in any case isolate the name of a file or a sender (`<bdi>` or `unicode-bidi: isolate`) in the thread. (SEC-R1-03, a suspicion not run) an incoming text or place name from a Meta channel with a NUL (`libs/channels/whatsapp/src/whatsapp-events.ts`, `placeTextOf`) is not rejected by `channelEventSchema`, and Postgres refuses NUL in `text` and `jsonb`, so the job would retry until the dead letter and the message would be lost: confirm with an integration test that feeds a signed event, and then use `safeText` (`libs/core/domain/text.ts`) at the boundary. Also from that round: `httpUrl` accepts `http://169.254.169.254.nip.io/` (a name that resolves to an internal address); today the link is only shown, so whoever fetches a customer URL must resolve and refuse private addresses (see the media fetch task); the Resend webhook with a valid signature into the analytics queue (WORKER-03) was not run for lack of the secret: cover it with an integration test that signs with a test secret. Owner: Step 7.3 (messaging and media).
- [ ] **From the QA round 1 (2026-11-04), `CHANNEL_ACCOUNT_IN_USE` cannot be reached in practice:** `channel-connections.service.ts` calls `plugin.connect` (the Graph of Meta, `connect()` around line 346) before the check of an account held by another store (`accountInUse`, around line 376, inside the transaction), so a number that belongs to another store is verified against Meta first and only then refused, and a test cannot show the refusal without a real Graph. Move the check before the provider call when the connection id is known without it, or give the plugin a `describe(credentials)` that returns the account id without side effects; keep the transaction check as the final word. Owner: Step 7.3.
- [ ] **From the QA round 1 (2026-11-04), limits that cannot be exercised, not tasks (rule 12):** the web widget declares no `REPLY_TO` nor `REACTIONS`, so the reply bar does not draw in a widget conversation (tested with a seeded WhatsApp conversation); the ClamAV outage (the scan deletes the file when ClamAV is down) needs a stopped container and is the debt (a) of the hardening of the media pipeline above; a row with `author_type` in capitals breaks `/conversations` of a whole store with a 500 because `enumValueOf` refuses a value outside the enum, which the api cannot create but a hand-written row can: accepted, since a broken row should be loud.
- [ ] **From the QA round 1 (2026-11-04), notifications that do not say who or which, P3:** `invitation.accepted` ("Alguien aceptó tu invitación al equipo") and `member.joined` carry `params: {}`, so two acceptances are indistinguishable in the bell and in the email; add `name` and the role to the params of both emitters (`notifications-inbox.listener.ts`), a `bodyWithName` text in es and en, and a case in `tests/api/notifications-emitters.spec.ts`; the same for `media.infected` and `media.unscannable` (TEXTOS-R1-21: the file name and the customer). The body of the digest already picks a text by params (`bodyVariantOf`). Owner: Step 7.3.
- [ ] **From the QA round 1 (2026-11-04), the inbox, P3 (FUNC-R1-07):** the customer panel of an archived conversation says "Conversaciones 0" (it counts only live ones; count the archived or say "activas"), and the list takes as its preview the text of the automatic away reply instead of the last message of the customer (use the last customer message, or mark the automatic one "Respuesta automática"). Both may be design: decide with the owner. Owner: Step 7.3 (messaging).
- [ ] **From the QA round 1 (2026-11-04), rate-limit and file errors that do not say how much, P3 (TEXTOS-R1-13, R1-20):** `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` says "Espera un momento" while `Retry-After` is 60 s and an accepted-invitation block lasted 3572 s; show the wait from `Retry-After` and do not say "intentos" for the general limit. `FILE_TOO_LARGE` carries `metadata.maxSizeBytes` and the text ignores it; `PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE` does not say what to shorten; `FILE_TYPE_NOT_ALLOWED` does not say which types are. Owner: Step 7.3 (api answers) and Step 7.7 (the words).
- [ ] **From the QA round 2, functional and security roles (2026-11-05, rule 12; the fix PR `fix/customer-name-single-line` closed TRX-04: a customer's name is one visible line, and U+2028/U+2029 are refused everywhere `singleLineText` is used):** still open. (a) NOT-06, **done (PR `fix/server-robustness`)**: the notification `message.failed` carries `params.reason` (the error code of the event) and the page draws `notifications.message.failed.reason.<CODE>` for the seven reasons that have a sentence (window expired, credentials, capability, file type, template, not connected, rate limit); any other code keeps the plain text. (b) MED-11, **done (same PR)**: `export.failed` carries `params.reason` from the `error` of the export row, with a sentence for `EXPORT_TOO_LARGE` and `EXPORT_EMPTY`; the generic `EXPORT_FAILED` keeps the plain text. The bell, the email digest and the push read the same helper (`reasonNameOf`), and the reasons with a sentence are listed in `NotificationFailureReasons` (generated keys, so `validate:translations` fails if one lacks its text). Old notifications saved without a reason keep the plain text. Left: most failures of a send still arrive as the generic `CHANNEL_MESSAGE_FAILED` (the outbound sender, the reaction sender and Meta's own codes map to it), so in practice the seven sentences show only where a channel names the cause (window expired, credentials, capability); giving Meta's error subcodes their own `ErrorCodes` is the work that makes the rest reachable (Step 7.3, channels). (c) AJU-13, **done (PR `fix/settings-robustness`)**: `POST /settings/routing` answers a retry (same person, same name, enabled flag, conditions and action inside `RoutingLimits.RETRY_WINDOW_MS`, 60 s) with the rule it already made, without a second audit entry; no `idempotencyKey` column was added (no migration). Accepted limit: an identical rule asked on purpose inside the window (a rule deleted and made again within a minute) returns the old one, and after the window the same request makes a second rule, as it always did; a client `Idempotency-Key` with a unique index would be exact, and needs a migration with its rollback (Step 7.1). (d) the store `PATCH` and `DELETE` (`/organizations/current/stores/:id`) **done (same PR)**: a cache that cannot be dropped is logged and the answer stays 200/204; other call sites of `invalidateStore` were not changed. The tenant cache expires on its own (`CacheTTL.VERY_SHORT`), so a deleted or renamed store can still be resolved by the guards for that time when Redis is down; nothing retries the invalidation. (e) The original of an image keeps its EXIF and GPS data (only the variants are re-encoded): decide with the owner whether the original is served at all or stripped on upload, a privacy decision; until then the download of an image is the original. (f) Names already stored with a line break or an invisible character (before this fix) are shown as stored; a one-off cleanup of `customers.name` and `customer_identities.display_name` with the `cleanText` rule, and the search by name that ignores accents ("nandu" finds "Ñandú"). (g) The limit of a name counts UTF-16 units when a person types it (`singleLineText`) and characters when a channel reports it (`cleanText`), so a name of 70 emoji can arrive from a channel and be refused on the next edit; count both by characters. (h) Other names that are drawn in lists still take any text: the organization name, the invitation's name, the address label, line and city, the display name of a connection and the name and address of a location a channel sends; give them `singleLineText` or `cleanName`. Owner: Step 7.3 for (c) to (h); Step 4.x notifications for (a), (b).
- [ ] **From the QA round 2, channels (2026-11-05, P3, rule 12; the fix PR `fix/unsupported-message-markers` closed the English `[type]` marker in the thread and the list, and the download name):** still open. (a) IG-07, **link done (PR `fix/channels-p3`)**: the story an Instagram customer replied to (`reply_to.story.url`) is kept in `metadata.attachmentUrl` (type `story_reply`) and drawn by `SharedLink`, as a shared post is; what the customer shared wins over it. **Still open:** the picture of the story is not fetched (the link expires within a day, and Meta's reference does not say the CDN allows a server to read it), and the story is not drawn as a quote (`replyTo`), because a story is not a message of the thread. (b) MS-04, **done (same PR)**: the cause of the silent `[image]` was the webhook parser, which dropped any attachment whose link was not a public web name (`httpUrl` refuses a literal IP); it now hands the raw link to the worker, which refuses it with `unsafe_link` and the notice on the message, like `http://` or a port; a link to a host that is not a Meta CDN (`downloadMediaUrl`) raises `UnsafeMediaLinkError` (plugin sdk) and the worker marks it `unsafe_link` instead of `fetch_failed`. The raw link of the customer (bounded, not blank, never shaped like a plain id of the provider) travels in the job and is refused by the worker before anything is fetched; it is not stored on the message. A link that is only a bare id is still dropped, as before. Left: `UnsafeMediaLinkError` is an `Error`, not a `PluginError`, so only the inbound fetcher classifies it (the other callers of `fetchMedia` see an unknown error and retry); a story reply that carries a file keeps the file and not the story link. (c) WW-23: under a CSP that blocks the widget's API (`connect-src 'self'`) the launcher never appears and the page says nothing; the bootstrap could log one `console.warn` naming `connect-src` and the API origin when its first request is blocked. (d) The `[type]` marker still reaches the quote of a reply, the notifications and the digest as English text (they read `content`, not the message view), and a message stored before the mark `metadata.unsupportedType` existed still shows its marker; give those readers the same rule as the view (the mark plus `content` equal to `[type]`) or store the marker as `null` content with the type in metadata, which the core refuses today (a text with no text). The download name (`downloadFilename`) is applied where a person downloads from the inbox (`media.service`), not in `StorageService`, because the links the worker hands to the providers carry their own filename; the outbound document names a customer receives (`a.pdf.exe` sent by an agent) are not renamed. Owner: Step 7.3 for (a) and (b), Step 7.7 for (c) and (d).
- [ ] **From the QA round 2, the composer and channels (2026-11-06, rule 12; HIL-04, CAN-13, ORG-03, WA-22; reports in the owner's QA folder `qa-reportes-2026-11-05/`):** (a) **HIL-04, DECISION OF THE OWNER PENDING:** the composer accepts only images, PDF, plain text, docx and xlsx (`AllowedContentTypes`) and one file per message (`MediaMessageLimits.MAX_FILES`, already listed among the limits of PR E2a in Step 3.4), while the case matrix asks for audio and video to be sendable. Decide whether the agent can send audio and video (the channels already receive them) or the matrix is corrected; if yes, it needs the upload types, the per-channel size limits and the streaming of the body that the memory task of this step asks for. (b) CAN-13, **decided, nothing to change (PR `fix/channels-p3`)**: a store may hold several connections of the same channel (one per Page), so connecting another Page does not invalidate the old one and it stays `active`; the matrix expected `error`, which is the discrepancy of the matrix and not a failure. The only case that replaces a Page is the token update of a connection, which refuses another Page (`other_account`). (c) ORG-03, **done (PR `fix/settings-robustness`)**: `DELETE /organizations/current/stores/:id` takes `{ confirmation }` and answers 400 unless it equals the name of the store (the rule of the screen, `phraseMatches` in core, now shared); a store the caller cannot see is still a 404 before the phrase is read. The web and the e2e helper send it. The confirmation travels as a JSON body of the `DELETE`: a gateway that drops bodies of a `DELETE`, or a web bundle cached from before the deploy, gets a 400 (a deploy-time risk for Step 7.3); it only guards against an accidental delete, since a script can read the name and send it back. The permission, the last-store rule and the data check stay the real guards. (d) WA-22: the 100 MB limit of a WhatsApp video is proved only by the spec of the limits, not with a real file (needs the streaming of the body above).
- [ ] **A member who leaves with many conversations (from the review of PR `fix/channels-notices`, rule 12; owner Step 7.1/7.3):** suspending or removing a member publishes one `CONVERSATION_ASSIGNED` event per worked conversation it frees, inside the transaction (`MembersService.backToTheQueue`), so a person with hundreds of open conversations makes the removal longer and fans out as many events after the commit. Put a cap or a batch event (a payload with a list of ids) on it, with a test that measures the number of events for a large set.
- [ ] **From the QA round 2, security role (2026-11-05, SEG-3, P3, rule 12 accepted trade-off):** `POST /auth/magic-link/request` answers an existing and an unknown email with the same status, body and 429, but the median differs by ~7 ms on loopback (17 against 10 ms, ranges overlap), because creating the link and queueing the email run inline for an existing account. Real network noise covers it almost entirely, so nothing changed. If it ever matters, make the unknown-account branch do the same work (a dummy link and a no-op enqueue) or move both to the queue and answer first. Owner: Step 7.3 (hardening before the api is exposed). SEG-1 (`X-Powered-By` of the web) and SEG-2 (`Content-Type` of the Meta challenge) are fixed in the same PR as this note (`poweredByHeader: false`; `text/plain` only on the challenge).
- [x] **Closed by the Meta connection fixes PR (2026-11-05, found live with Meta):** `InstagramApi.SUBSCRIBED_FIELDS` used `messaging_referrals` and `messaging_reactions`, which Meta refuses (error 100); the names are `messaging_referral` and `message_reactions`, then `messages,message_reactions,messaging_referral,message_edit`, and `messages,message_reactions,messaging_referral` after the unified-login PR. The plugin specs now reject, as Meta does, a name outside `MetaSubscribableFields` (Instagram and Messenger), so a misspelt field fails in CI. `ChannelConnectionsService.connect` logs `providerCode`, `providerSubcode` and, for a local refusal, `failedCheck` (`PluginChecks`) through `pluginFailureLog` (libs/plugins/sdk), never a message, a value or the token. The access token is trimmed. Messenger reads the Page id alone first and falls back to the name `Messenger <id>` when `pages_read_engagement` is missing.
- [ ] **Left by the Meta connection fixes PR (rule 12), owner Step 7.3:** (a) the worker's `providerCodesOf` (`apps/worker/src/outbound/provider-codes.ts`) and the other warn sites of the api and the worker (health checks, message changes) can adopt `pluginFailureLog` and drop their own copy; (b) `messaging_seen` is not subscribed: the plugin declares `READ_RECEIPTS` but no parser reads the `read` event (`meta-messaging-events.ts`); build the parser and add the field then; (c) `message_edit` is NOT subscribed on Instagram any more (unified-login PR, item (g) of its entry above); its Instagram payload has not been seen (same box as the Messenger `message_edits` payload above); (d) `MetaSubscribableFields.INSTAGRAM` comes from the webhooks reference and the error text of Meta, which truncates the list at `live...`: complete it when read in full; (f) `channel-webhook.controller.ts` (~line 114) logs `err: error` for a plugin that fails on a webhook, which writes the message and stack of the error (a parser error can echo a piece of the customer's payload): log `pluginFailureLog(error)` there too; (g) a Messenger reconnect with a token that cannot read the Page name overwrites a stored good `displayName` with the `Messenger <id>` fallback: keep the existing name on reconnect; the fallback is a brand plus an id, not a sentence, so it needs no i18n key; (h) accepted trade-off: Messenger treats Graph error 100 and the permission codes on the name read as "name unreadable" (Meta answers 100 for a missing `pages_read_engagement`, generic as it is); a token that really cannot read its Page still fails at the subscribe call, so nothing is accepted silently; record the exact Meta answer when a connection is next tried live; (e) the Instagram webhook signature secret: the Instagram app may have its own secret (`META_INSTAGRAM_APP_SECRET`, with `env-schema.ts` and `.env.example`) different from the Meta app's; not built here, it depends on the Instagram variant the owner chooses (session 6e); until a real Instagram webhook arrives it is not known which secret signs.
- [ ] **Left by the Meta connection fixes PR (rule 12), Messenger connection findings (2026-11-05), owner Step 7.3:** (a) a clearer error text when the credentials of a Meta channel are refused: today the owner sees the generic "the channel credentials are not valid" for a revoked token, a token with a stray character and a failed subscription alike; the log now says which, the answer to the client could say "check the token" vs. "Meta did not confirm the subscription" with a new error code in `libs/core/domain/errors/error-codes.ts` and i18n en/es; (b) the dashboard step "Generar tokens de acceso" gives a Page token without `pages_read_engagement`: add it to the setup guide of the channel (`ChannelSetupStepKeys.MESSENGER_PROFILE_ACCESS` is the neighbour) so the owner knows the name is optional and the permission is needed for the customers' profiles.
- [ ] **From the QA of 2026-11-08 (owner Step 7.3):** CAN-09, "Test connection" of a channel gives no detail for each check (which one failed); DUE-07, the cron that warns before a token expires has no manual trigger to try it.
- [ ] **Debt, WhatsApp: several images sent at once by the customer (owner, 2026-11-10; found in the real Meta test; owner Step 7.3):** when the customer selects MORE THAN ONE image in the WhatsApp picker, besides the images the thread shows the grouped notice "El cliente envió contenido que este canal no permite mostrar…" (an event that falls in the unsupported branch); with ONE image everything is normal. Investigate which event or message type WhatsApp sends alongside the album (capture the raw webhook payload of a send of 2 or more images and look at the types the plugin maps to unsupported), and decide: ignore it if it is only a grouping marker, map it if it carries content, and keep the notice only for what really cannot be shown. The grouped notice itself is the one of "One notice for content the channel does not deliver" (PR `feat/unsupported-content-notice`, #321, Step 2.8 notes): the fix is in what the WhatsApp plugin maps to unsupported, not in the grouping of the notices. Verify with a real send of 2 or more images.

**TDD:** `TDD_03 §1`, `§3`, `§7` (rewritten for Railway per `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §3)
**Done when:** a restore drill completes within the 4-hour RTO target and a job enqueued
in production is processed by the worker within 10 seconds.
- [ ] **Tie the job schemas of the other queues to their job types (debt of the fix `fix/outbound-job-schema-drift`, 2026-11-09):** zod drops what a schema does not name, and two jobs lost fields that way (a reaction under one file of an album lost `mediaId`; the inbound media fetch lost the `mediaId` the web widget chose and the `correlationId`). Fixed and tied with `exactly<Job>()` (`libs/core/platform/queues/exact-schema.ts`, a compile error on any key that differs): the whole outbound queue (`outbound-job-schemas.ts`) and the inbound media fetch of `heavy-processing`. Also tied: the other schemas of `heavy-processing`. Still inline and not tied: `customer-profile`, `exports`, `notifications` and `analytics` (no schema). Their keys were compared with their job types by hand on 2026-11-09 and match except for `correlationId`, which each of them drops (it only labels logs). Owner: Step 7.3, one small PR that moves each one under `exactly` and decides on `correlationId`, and adds a lint rule or a test that fails on any `*.processor.ts` schema not built through `exactly` (the strip is the default, so tying one at a time closes instances, not the class).
- [ ] **Say why a job was refused (found in the review of the same fix):** the processors throw `UnrecoverableError('the ... job does not fit its schema')` with no detail. Log the paths of the zod issues (never the values) so that the next drift is a field name in the log and not a silent loss. Owner: Step 7.4 (monitoring).

### Step 7.4 — Monitoring
- [ ] Sentry for errors, an external uptime check on `/health/ready` and Railway's own service metrics, plus **Grafana Cloud** (free plan, `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §7, D12): a Grafana agent runs as a Docker service in the Railway project, scrapes `/metrics`, and the alert rules of `TDD_04 §6` (queue depth, dead letter, `TenantViolation`, error rate, latency) load there. Verify the free plan's limits with its documentation when building this step **Web image (left by PR E1):** `NEXT_PUBLIC_SENTRY_DSN` is likewise not a build argument of `apps/web/Dockerfile` (nothing reads it yet): when the browser reports to Sentry, add it as an `ARG`/`ENV` there, because the CSP of `proxy.ts` only allows the endpoint the image was built with
- [ ] Alerts go to one Slack channel with push notifications on the owner's phone (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §7, D6); no PagerDuty; no AI alert or dashboard panels
- [ ] **Partial index for the profile errors (debt (c) of PR A2 of Step 3.6, see the note of that step):** the count of identities with a profile error filters `customer_identities` by `store_id` and `profile_fetch_error IS NOT NULL` with no partial index (fine for the single owner of the MVP). When that count appears among the slow queries, or a store passes a few hundred thousand identities, add `(store_id) WHERE profile_fetch_error IS NOT NULL` as raw SQL in a migration with its rollback (rule 4; out of `schema.prisma` like the other partial indexes).
- [ ] Status page on separate infrastructure
- [ ] Runbooks written in `docs/runbooks/` (no `ai-kill-switch.md`)
- [ ] **From the QA fix PRs (QA backlog, rule 12), sweeps can starve behind rows that always fail (review of Q1):** `SnoozeWakeScheduler` and `AutoArchiveScheduler` take the oldest due rows first and only log a failed one, so 200 or more rows that fail for good would hide the healthy ones behind them. Nothing makes a row fail for good today. Add an alert on the warn line of these two jobs and, if it ever fires, a retry counter or a skip list
- [ ] **From the QA fix PRs (QA backlog, rule 12), `media.processed` arrives twice and the dead-letter queue holds jobs with no consumer:** the QA found `media.processed` delivered twice and 37 jobs in the dead-letter queue. Find why the event is emitted twice, and look into the 37 dead-lettered jobs with the dead-letter alert of this step (the dead-letter queue has no processor by design: `apps/worker/src/processors/queue-coverage.spec.ts` names Step 7.4 as what watches it)
- [ ] **From the final QA pass (api lane, session 8e; rule 12; owner Step 7.3):** (a) an event of a plugin dropped because its text is over `InboundLimits.CONTENT_MAX_LENGTH` (65 536) is dropped on purpose, answering 200 so that Meta does not send it again for ever, and only a `warn` with the plugin and the paths of the issues is left: it carries neither the id of the message nor a metric. Optional improvement: log the provider's message id and count it in a metric (a real provider cannot reach the limit, Meta cuts texts at 1000-4096; only a forged signed body does); (b) `resend-caption` (by message) has no kill-switch: it repeats a text already stored, so that a text that failed is never left with no way out. By design; pending decision of the owner on whether to have one for resends (the switch `OUTBOUND_CAPTION_PER_FILE_ENABLED` is gone: the capability `CAPTION_PER_FILE` decides what a channel is offered).

**TDD:** `TDD_04 §5`, `§6`, `TDD_11 §9.5`
**Done when:** a simulated incident (for example stopping the worker or failing `/health/ready`)
sends a Slack message that reaches the owner's phone with a runbook link.

### Step 7.7 — Visual polish

- [ ] **Index for the duplicate hint (PR feat/customer-duplicate-hint, rule 12):** the e-mail of the hint is compared as `lower(email) = lower(...)`, which cannot use the index `(store_id, email)`: each creation reads the customers of its store. Fine for the customers of one store at the MVP; before a store holds hundreds of thousands, add an expression index on `(store_id, lower(email))` (a migration with its rollback) in this step, 7.1.
- [x] Draw the possible duplicates (PR feat/customer-duplicate-hint, CLI-02): **moved to Step 4.4 (owner of the task: the create-order drawer, where the M4 flow creates a customer).** The web has no screen that creates a customer before Milestone 4 (only `POST /customers` exists), so a dialog built now would be a new screen no step asks for. The contract stays: `POST /customers` answers `possibleDuplicates` (`id`, `name`, `email`, `phone`, `matchedOn`), empty when the caller may not view customers or the lookup fails.
- [x] Draw the expiry of a channel token (PR feat/meta-token-expiry, plan 7.3 (c)): the view of a connection answers `tokenExpiry { state: 'never' | 'expires' | 'expired' | 'invalid' | 'unknown', expiresAt: ISO | null, checkedAt: ISO | null, kind: 'page' | 'user' | 'unknown' }`. The card of the connection says "Expires on <date>" for `expires` (a warning tone from 7 days), "Does not expire" for `never`, "Expired on <date>" for `expired`, "Meta says the access is no longer valid" for `invalid` (with the Update token button already there), nothing for `unknown` (never say "expired" from `unknown`), and for `kind: 'user'` the help "this is a token of a person; use the one of the Page" (the plan 7.3 (c) text). The notice `channel.token_expiring` already has its title and body (en, es) and is in the preferences (System). **Done in #235.**
- [ ] **To confirm with real Meta (PR feat/meta-token-expiry):** `debug_token` documents `GET` only; the client tries `POST` (the token asked about in the body) first and falls back to `GET` when Meta answers that the method is wrong. The owner runs the real test (a pasted Page token with `META_APP_ID`/`META_APP_SECRET` set): the card must show a date or "does not expire". If Meta accepts neither method, every connection reads `unknown` and nobody is warned: the test is what proves the feature works.
- [ ] Known limits of the token expiry (PR feat/meta-token-expiry, rule 12): WhatsApp is not asked (its tokens are of a system user); the daily round reads the id, store, plugin and metadata of the active connections and filters in memory (fine for the MVP, to be a query when a store holds thousands); a token that is not valid but whose connection is still up is only shown and logged, the health check is what takes it down.
- [x] Draw the discard of a failed reply — **done (PR `feat/discard-failed-reply`, web; the api is #232)**: `failureStateOf` (`lib/inbox/failure-state.ts`, tested) reads `failureDefinitive`: `true` is a definitive failure (red, "Send again" and "Discard" for who may send, or send templates for a template), anything else, `undefined` included, is "could not confirm" (amber triangle and note, only "Send again", which first asks and warns that the customer may receive it twice); "Discard" asks in one step (`DiscardMessageDialog`, lazy) and calls `POST /messages/:id/discard`; success or a 409 (`MESSAGE_DISCARD_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` and any other: the screen was out of date) read the thread again, and the realtime `message.deleted` already reads it for the other agents; the discarded reply is not in the list, so it is never drawn as a deleted message. The reason the channel gave (`deliveryError`) is said in both states (`failureNotesOf`, tested): the amber state adds "could not confirm" to it, it does not replace it. A failed reply that the api does not mark definitive (`false`, `null`, or an older api that sends nothing) is shown as "could not confirm" with the duplicate warning and cannot be discarded, even if the channel plainly refused it long ago: the contract says so, and a failure from before `failureDefinitive` stays that way. The two confirmations share one dialog (`ConfirmFailedReplyDialog`), which closes itself if the reply is no longer in the state it was opened for. An e2e (`discard.spec.ts`) covers both states through the screen and the api: a definitive failure is red, "Discard" confirms and the reply leaves the thread with no trace of a deleted message; a failure that may have gone out is amber, has no "Discard", and "Send again" asks first with the duplicate warning. It writes the states in the database (`failureDefinitive`); the failure itself (a refusal of Meta, a timeout) is covered by the integration suite of the api (`message-discard.spec.ts`), and the manual script of the QA (`guion-descarte.md`) is the check against a fake Graph. Left: no component test of the dialogs (no DOM environment; the rules are tested as functions). Original text of the task: (PR feat/discard-failed-message): the view of a message answers `failureDefinitive: true | false | null` (set only for a reply that failed). For a failed reply of the team: `failureDefinitive === true` shows "Discard" beside "Send again" (for who holds `MESSAGES_SEND`, or `MESSAGES_SEND_TEMPLATE` for a template; confirm it in one step, nothing is lost: the text is kept 30 days); otherwise (`false`, `null`) only "Send again", with the warning that the customer may already have the message and may get it twice. `POST /messages/:id/discard` answers 204 (also when it was discarded already); 409 `MESSAGE_DISCARD_OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` ("this reply may have reached the customer, so it cannot be discarded; check with them or send it again", already in the error catalog, en and es) means the screen was out of date: show it and read the thread again; any other 409 is a message that is not a failed reply (read the thread again too). The realtime event `message.deleted` of the thread already arrives for the other agents; after it, read the thread again: a discarded reply is not in the list (do not draw it as the tombstone of a deleted message). Words in en and es; no emojis.
- [ ] Known limits of discarding (PR feat/discard-failed-message, rule 12): the failures of before this change read `null` and cannot be discarded; whether a failure is definitive depends on each plugin affirming a refusal (`refused`) (the Meta plugins do; a third party's must also affirm a refusal with `refused`); the files of a discarded reply leave the bucket with the hourly sweep of stray files (within a day), not at once, and their rows (scan state) go with the reply; the text stays 30 days like a deleted one; a discarded reply still counts for `firstResponseAt`, `lastOutboundAt` and `lastMessageAt` of the conversation, as a reply that failed and was not discarded already does (they are written when the reply is queued); the audit entry is written after the commit, as every audit entry of the api, so a process that dies between the two loses it (the row keeps who and when in `deletedById` and `deletedAt`). Two jobs that work on one message at the same time (a stalled job that is run again while the first is alive) could still write the mark over each other; they would also send the message twice, which is the hazard the idempotency key of the provider already answers, and it is not made worse here. Whoever may send a reply may throw away a failed one of another agent, as they may send it again (the decision of the owner: the same permission as to resend); the row keeps who did it.
- [ ] Refresh grace follow-ups (PR fix/refresh-grace-window): `/login` explains "your session was closed, sign in again" when the cause was a revoked login (today the person arrives with no explanation), and `refreshSession` stops clearing the cookie on a 401 that is not a revocation.
- [ ] **Media scan that never got a verdict (PR fix/media-scan-early-return, rule 12; owner's dev, 2026-11-09):** the cause was two stacks on one Redis with two databases, both on the BullMQ prefix `bull`: a worker with no row for the media took the `scan-file` job, found none and ended it as a success (50 ms, `returnvalue` null), and the file stayed `pending`. Done: the prefix of the queues is derived from the name of the database (`queuePrefixFor`, `QUEUE_PREFIX` overrides; name only, not host or port, because the api and the worker may reach the database in different ways and a prefix that differed would leave every job unread; two servers with one database name on one Redis share the queues and must set `QUEUE_PREFIX`; the suites that start a worker or an api against Redis must use a database name or a `QUEUE_PREFIX` of their own per session), the api and the worker log the prefix at start, a job with no row logs a warning, and a failed file that is stored shows "check again". Left on purpose: (a) jobs that were waiting under the old prefix `bull` when this lands are not seen by the new workers (dev only; the hourly cleanup queues the pending files again) and any tool that reads the queues (Bull Board, Step 7.x) must use the same prefix; (b) focus goes to the page body when the `Check again` button disappears as the file goes back to `checking` (move it to the chip), and a test of the button needs the DOM environment (there is no component test of the tray yet: `canRecheck` is covered, the button is not); (c) a job with no row still ends as a success, because a row dropped while the job waits is a valid end and the worker cannot tell it from a foreign database; the prefix is what prevents the second case. Closes with: Step 7.7 (b), Step 7.3 (a).
- [ ] **From Step 4.8 PR B2 (debt (e), rule 12):** the label of a `local` guide is drawn with the standard fonts (Latin alphabets; any other character refuses the guide); if the owner ships to names in another script, embed a font and measure with it (`libs/core/shipping/src/label-pdf.ts`, `createLabelMeter`)
- [ ] **From Step 4.9 PR C1 (debt (b), rule 12):** the order summary PDF that travels with a message (`renderOrderSummaryPdf`, `libs/core/shipping/src/order-messages`) is drawn with the standard Helvetica face, so a customer name in a script it has no glyphs for (Arabic, CJK) prints blank in the file while the text message is right; embed a font that covers the scripts the stores serve, with a test that draws one of them.
Owner decision (2026-10-30): the interface of Steps 3.1 to 5.x stays functional and plain, and one design pass happens here, when every screen exists, so that nothing is restyled twice. Do it before the live demo of Step 7.3 is announced.
- [ ] **Strict first task, before reading or reviewing this plan (owner, 2026-10-30):** open the running app in a browser with the Claude in Chrome extension and go through every page, one by one, and every endpoint the pages call (watch the network requests and the console), and check that each page, each endpoint and the plan match: every route of `scripts/frontend-coverage-map.ts` is drawn by a page or has its stated reason, every page calls only endpoints that exist, and every state the plan describes is on screen. Nothing is skipped and nothing is assumed from the code or the tests. Each mismatch is fixed or recorded as a decision of the owner before any visual change starts; the plan is read after this walk, not before
- [ ] **Settings leftovers of Step 3.6 (rule 12, from PRs B2, C, E1, E2 and F1):** a test of the drag and drop reorder of routing rules (a component test with the DOM environment above, or a Playwright drag that is reliable on native HTML5 events; today only the buttons are in the e2e, PR E1 limit (1)); component tests, once the DOM environment above exists, for `AvatarPicker` (processing, refused and polling states), the uninstall dialog (it counts the accounts it read when it opened) and the widget card (the sites text is lost if the section is collapsed); cancel an avatar upload when the person leaves the page, and free the row of an upload whose attach failed; the picture of a teammate in the team list and the inbox (a signed link per teammate or a copy to a public-read key); keep the previous profile picture until the new one is ready (the pointer of the profile would hold two ids; today the old one is deleted when the new one is attached, so for the seconds the worker takes the person has initials); the unsaved-changes warning on the browser's back button, on the store navigation, on the brand link of the top bar (it leaves a dirty form with no warning, as the store switcher does) and on the editors that are dialogs (rule, tag, snippet, role); Team: warn before a person removes themselves or changes their own role, refresh the permissions of the page when the person's own role changes (today they stay those of the session until it reloads), and search and paging for the member list; Profile: stop the two-minute limit of the picture polling from leaving "Getting your picture ready" for good (a retry button) and use the density that is saved and read by nothing. The e2e specs sign in as the same seeded addresses (`admin@test.com`, `agent@test.com`), and the magic link is limited to `MagicLinkThrottle` (5 per minute per address), so on a laptop a fast batch or a quick rerun leaves a test with "no email for admin@test.com in Mailpit" (measured in PR G: seven reruns of one spec in 70 s fail from the third; CI is slower and retries once); a `signIn` helper that gives each spec its own address, or waits out the window, closes it. The e2e of the Done when leaves one store per run on a laptop database, because the product will not delete a store that holds a conversation (a delete that removes what it holds, or a reset of the e2e data, closes it). Suspending and reactivating members is built in PRs H1 and H2 of this step, not here (see Team above) (From PR H2) A request of a suspended person that meets 403 `TENANT_MEMBER_SUSPENDED` with no socket to move them (the api client) should send them to "/" like the socket does.
- [x] **Realtime event when a member's conversations are unassigned (debt (a) of PR H1 of Step 3.6, see the note of that step) — done (PR `fix/channels-notices`, QA EQ-14/EQ-15): `MembersService.suspend` and `remove` now publish `CONVERSATION_ASSIGNED` (`assignedTo: null`, `previousAssignee`) for each conversation they free, inside the transaction, so the other agents' sockets learn it and the queue notification is raised for who can take it (not for the person who left, nor for the one who did it); tests in `team.spec.ts` for both paths. Only the rows that update really changed are announced, and only the worked ones (open, pending, snoozed); the finished ones also leave with the member but without an event. The tests listen to the events on the in-process emitter and read the notifications; the existing realtime listener tests cover the fan-out to sockets, so there is no new test with a real socket. Left: the unassignment leaves no system line in the thread (a manual unassign writes one), and the departing member's still-open socket may receive the event in the instant before it is closed (the payload carries ids, not text); the notification is raised only for OPEN conversations, as the queue rule always was. Original text:** suspending a member (and removing one) leaves their open, pending and snoozed conversations unassigned and back in the queue in the database, but the inbox lists of the other agents do not hear it and show the old assignee until their next read. Emit the reassignment event (`CONVERSATION_ASSIGNED`, `libs/core/domain/realtime/realtime-events.ts`) for each conversation the suspend and remove paths unassign, with a test that a second agent's socket receives it. The same event also closes the second half of this debt (QA round 2, EQ-14 and EQ-15): the notification `conversation.unassigned_queue` ("a conversation went back to the queue", `notifications-inbox.listener.ts`, which listens to `CONVERSATION_ASSIGNED` with `assignedTo: null`) is not raised either when a member is suspended (`members.service.ts`, `updateMany` of the suspend path) or removed (the same call in the remove path), so the team is not told that those conversations are waiting. The test must also assert that notification for the other members who can take them, and that the person who is suspended or removed does not get it.
- [ ] **From the QA round 2, focus and widget sites (CAN-17, CAN-R2-01; rule 12 limits left on purpose):** (a) `useFocusReturn` (`apps/web/lib/use-focus-return.ts`, used by `Dialog`, `Sheet` and `AlertDialog`) remembers the focused element when a dialog opens and gives the focus back to it on close; a dialog opened from a menu item or after an async call finds `<body>` focused (the menu closed first), so it keeps the old behaviour there: return the focus to a stable anchor (the menu's own button). The hook and the wiring of the three wrappers have no component test because the web project has no DOM environment yet (the item "DOM test environment for the web project" of this step), and only the connect dialog has an e2e; add a component test of the hook and an Escape case for a `Sheet` and an `AlertDialog` there. (b) `originOf` now refuses a `*` in the host, but a connection saved before with `https://*.shop.test` keeps it in `metadata` (it never matched anything, so it is harmless): the form shows the generic "not an origin" error on the next save, so add a wildcard-specific message to `validation` and drop the old rows when the owner saves. Other hosts that parse but never match (an underscore, for example) are the same class: a hostname allow-list would close it.
- [ ] **From the QA round 2, the text pass of phase 2 (2026-11-06, rule 12; the fix PR `fix/text-pass-p3` closed F2-T13 the full-page refusals name the permission, F2-T14 the invite dialog checks the email in the app's language, F2-T16 "Requested" and "Email" in English, F2-T17 the 404 goes to `/`, F2-T3 "paste it again" in the token dialog, the approved sentence of Preferences, WW-17 the widget reads the history when the agent read a message it does not know yet):** still open. (a) NOT-21, **done (2026-11-06)**: the "Sonido" column of the notification preferences now plays a short Web Audio tone (`apps/web/lib/notifications/notification-tone.ts`, loaded by a dynamic import the first time a `notification` event with `sound: true` arrives, once per event, a burst sounds once (400 ms between tones), skipped and never queued while the browser has not allowed the page to make sound: `resume()` is asked without waiting for it, so the first click makes the following notifications audible). Measured with the recipe (sum of the gzip of the chunks the inbox loads, same base, `next build`): 230.841 KB before, 230.938 KB after (+0.10 KB: the import call and the payload check; the tone itself is its own chunk the inbox does not load). The ceiling (`ScriptCeilingsKb`, 340 KB) was not touched. Left on purpose: the choice of tone and a mute per device (a setting stored per browser) are not built; a tone while the tab is in the background depends on the browser's autoplay rule, and the tone has no component test (no DOM environment), it is covered through an injected context. (b) The 19 px overflow of Business hours at 389 px: the buttons of a day now wrap (`flex-wrap` in `hours-editor.tsx`), not measured in a browser; confirm at 389 px in the visual pass. (c) F2-T15, **done (PR `fix/settings-robustness`)**: `senderOf` (`libs/core/email`) gives a bare `EMAIL_FROM` the name of `EMAIL_FROM_NAME` (new, optional, plain text the schema checks) or, without it, "Omnichannel Platform"; a `EMAIL_FROM` that already has a name is kept. The example env stays a bare address. **Still open of this item:** The invitation to an address with no account is written in the language of the store, not of the inviter; the decision of 2026-11-05 says it must be documented: say it in `TDD_09` and in the invitation email test. (d) The error text of the api for an invalid email stays in English for a client that reads it directly; the app translates it by field. Owner: Step 7.7.
- [ ] **From the QA round 2, accessibility (2026-11-06, rule 12; ATA-10):** in a conversation thread the keyboard needs four `Tab` presses to reach "Resolve" (header links and menus come first). Reorder the focus (skip link to the thread actions, or move the primary actions before the secondary ones) and keep a keyboard test for it in the accessibility pass of this step. Owner: this step.
- [ ] **From the QA round 2, one send per form (TRX-01 of the functional role; `useSubmitGuard`, `apps/web/lib/submit-guard.ts`, closed it for the forms that create or merge):** the guard is on the tag, snippet, routing rule, role, invitation, rename, connect (and update token) dialogs, the customer note, the identity link, the merge and the store creation, because a second send there makes a second thing or a refusal that hides the first result. The forms that save a whole record with a `PATCH` (store general, hours, inbox behavior, preferences, organization profile, customer profile and address, widget card, the rename of a store) are idempotent and still rely on `isPending`; give them the guard when the interface is redrawn in this step. The template dialog sends an `idempotencyKey` and needs none. Owner: Step 7.7.

Then the pass at 390 px (`TDD_09 §16.4`) that the QA could not do because its tools do not resize the window: the top bar cuts "Clientes", "Exportaciones" and "Ajustes", and four filters stack on a phone. Done by hand in DevTools device mode, page by page
- [ ] **The e2e suite and the rate limit of the api (2026-11-07, rule 12; measured for the coordinator; PR `test/e2e-spread-inbox-addresses`):** the api limits each ROUTE to 100 requests a minute for each address (a control the e2e exercises on purpose, #168, and that stays as it is). The 16 cases of `inbox.spec.ts` keep one session and so one address: measured with a logger of the requests of the api, `GET /conversations` was called 97 times on `main` (5ef53d8) and 94 on #212 (d56d822), 311 and 310 requests in all: the count did not grow, the suite sat at 94-97% of the limit of one route, and a slower run, a retry or one more list request tipped it into "Too many attempts" in cases that had nothing to do with it (the phone list, and the specs that ran in the same minute). Done: `reachServicesFromOwnAddress` returns a function that changes the address and `inbox.spec.ts` calls it before each case (the same session; the most any address then asks of one route in a minute is 15). **Left:** only `inbox.spec.ts` was measured; `customers.spec.ts`, `media-states.spec.ts`, `thread.spec.ts`, `templates.spec.ts` and `login.spec.ts` also keep a context for several cases and were not (the logger was a local patch and is not in the repository); `channels.spec:333` and `content-settings:131` failed with "Test timeout of 30000ms exceeded" and no "Too many attempts" in their snapshots, so their cause is still open. Known limits of the change: the address changes for the requests sent after the change (one still in flight from the case before counts against the new address), the realtime connection keeps its first address, the session of the inbox now moves between addresses from case to case (nothing in the product binds a session to an address today; the grace path of the refresh only logs `ipChanged`: if that changes, these cases will say so), and nothing asserts the count of a case stays small.
- [ ] **The chat UX list of the owner, W2: the page that scrolls (2026-11-07, rule 12; evidence `chat-ux-repro.md` and a capture of the owner; PR `fix/thread-layout-scroll`):** with no conversation open the page was still; opening one made it scroll as a whole (the header went out of sight and a white gap stayed under the thread, with a picture of a thumb with no height limit). Cause found (measured by the QA in a real Chrome, which replaced the first guess): the hidden texts of the sent replies (`sr-only`, which is `position: absolute`) had a container OUTSIDE the scroller of the thread (the scroller was not `relative`), so they escaped its clip and made the height of the whole page grow, and the wheel at the end of the thread went on to scroll the page; `relative` and `overscroll-contain` on the scrollers of the thread, the list, the sidebar, the notification panel, the customer drawer and the gallery fix it. Other causes found on the way: the inbox was `-m-4 h-[calc(100dvh-3.5rem)]` inside a frame with `min-h-dvh`, a height computed from the size of the top bar that was a few pixels off (1 to 22 measured), and `scrollIntoView` (jump to a quoted message) moves every scrollable ancestor, the page included. Done: on the routes of the inbox (`isInboxPath`, read from the address so it is right in the first draw and in every browser) the frame of the app is exactly the window, with `overflow-clip` (a browser cannot scroll it to reveal a focused field) and no padding; the top bar takes what it needs and the inbox the rest, so the panes scroll on their own; the top bar (brand, store, links, bell, account) no longer overflows at 485 or 390 px, which the clipped inbox would have made unreachable and which also fixes the page of customers: the brand and the store name shorten, the links scroll on their own, and the bell and the account menu never leave the window (the e2e measures the boxes of the store and account buttons at 1280, 485 and 390 px, and that /customers has no sideways scroll at 485); the jump to a message moves only the thread (centred, or by its top when the message is taller than the thread); an e2e (`inbox.spec.ts`, with 14 sent replies and the wheel to the end and past it) checks at 1536, 1280, 485 and 390 px that the page is as tall as the window, stays at the top, and that the top bar and the box to write in are inside the window. On a phone the filters and the list of conversations are one column that scrolls as a whole and the list has a height of its own (`70dvh`): with the frame fixed, the filters alone are taller than a phone window and left the list with no height (found by the e2e of the phone in CI). The e2e opens the conversation once and resizes the window under it (the api limits a person to 100 requests a minute per address, and a first version with five page loads made the following specs of the run fail with "Too many attempts": `channels.spec:333`, `content-settings:131`, `invitations:74`, the phone list, which looked like flaky tests and were this). **Left:** not run in a browser by the author (the e2e of CI is the check); the thumb with no height limit of the capture was not reproduced (the pictures of the thread already have fixed boxes), so nothing was added for it; the jump to a quoted message has no e2e; the mobile keyboard (`dvh` does not shrink with it) was not tested.
- [ ] **The chat UX list of the owner, W3a: edited and then deleted (2026-11-07, rule 12; PR `fix/edited-then-deleted`):** done: a message that was edited and then deleted is a deleted message in every view of the api (`messageView`): no `editedAt`, editor, edit waiting or edit state, so the bubble shows only that it was deleted and by whom, never "edited"; its text stays hidden as before (the 30 days of `DeletedMessageRetention` are the owner's/coordinator's, not changed here). The route of the revisions of a deleted message gives the list (without text) only to who holds `MESSAGES_VIEW_DELETED` and an empty list to anyone else, so the edits do not leak who edited and when. **Left:** the row keeps its edit fields (`pendingContent`, `editStatus`) for the worker: a customer message that arrives deleted by a channel tombstone while an edit is in flight is not covered by the lock that blocks deleting a queued edit, and the api view reports no edit then; the list preview of a deleted message already carried no text.
- [ ] **The chat UX list of the owner, W3c: new messages and the day that floats (2026-11-07, rule 12; PR `feat/new-messages-pill-sticky-date`):** done: when messages arrive while the reader is above the end of the thread, a button "N new messages" (`unseenAfter`, tested) takes them to the end; the conversation is marked read only when the reader is at the end (`useMarkRead(…, isReading)`), where before it was marked as soon as a message arrived whatever the reader was looking at; while the reader scrolls, the day of the top of the view floats over the thread (`stickyDayOf`, tested, found once for each frame) and goes away 1.5 s after the last scroll (it is `aria-hidden`: the separators of the days are in the list already); an e2e (`thread-reading.spec.ts`) covers the floating day. An e2e (`widget.spec.ts`) covers the button: a visitor writes through the widget while the agent reads above the end, the button "1 new message" appears, the conversation stays unread until the button is pressed and is read then, and a reply of the agent shows no button. **Left:** only the messages of the customer count as new (what the agent wrote, from this tab or another, and the system lines do not); nothing floats while the reader is at the end (so not on opening or when a message makes the thread follow itself); the end is worked out again when the thread or its window changes size, not only on a scroll; a mark that ends well looks again for a message that arrived while it was in flight; the button respects reduced motion, is said to a screen reader through a polite region and hands the focus to the last message; there is no test of `useMarkRead` (no DOM environment), the manual QA covers that nothing is marked read while the reader is above the end.
- [x] **The e2e cases that timed out at 30 s, `channels.spec:333` and `content-settings:131` (2026-11-07, rule 12; measured from the traces of the failed runs of #219 and #222; PR `test/e2e-signin-waits-outside-timeout`):** the traces of both cases show NO action of the browser between the creation of the page and the timeout: the case was inside `waitForLinkBudget`, the wait that keeps the specs under `MagicLinkThrottle` (5 links a minute for each address, and the specs share `admin@test.com`). When several cases sign in as the same person within a minute, the next one waits for the window to free up (up to 62 s) and that wait was counted in the 30 s of the case, so the case failed before it began. Done: when a call has to wait, the timeout of the case grows by exactly the wait the stamps say (once for each wait; the time for its own steps and the timeout of every assertion stay as they were; a case with no timeout keeps having none), and the line `[link-budget] <address> waits N ms` goes to the log of the run; `link-budget.spec.ts` proves it with a case whose timeout (3 s) is shorter than its wait and with another that must not grow the timeout when there is room. The limit of the api is untouched.
- [x] **Fewer sign-ins per address in the e2e suite (done in #245, 2026-11-07):** six more people are seeded in the main store (`admin2`, `admin3`, `admin4`, `agent2`, `agent3`, `viewer2` @test.com, the same roles as the first three) and the specs that do not care who the person is sign in as one of them, so that no address spends the budget of five links a minute. Measured on a clean database, whole suite: waits for the link budget 287 s (`admin@` 227 s) on `main`, 131 to 188 s with the change; the run goes from 7.6 to about 5 to 6 minutes. **Left on purpose:** `agent@` still waits about 56 s (`notifications`, `realtime` and `login` depend on who receives the events, so they keep the original people); a storage state was not used because refresh tokens rotate.
- [ ] **Width of the dialogs, follow-up of MODAL-390 (PR of the template dialog):** `expectDialogFitsThePhoneWidth` measures every dialog the specs open at 390 and 360 px, but at the moment the spec opens it (the fields of a few are still empty and no error text is shown), and it does not run on the pages that hold a `fieldset` (`store-inbox-page`, `notification-preferences-page`). Measure the widest state (long values, error text) and those pages, and count `label`, `span` and `legend` in the `stray` list of `support/dialog-fit.ts` once its false positives (the `sr-only` spans) are ruled out. Owner: Step 7.7.
- [ ] **The e2e specs that create channels pile them up in a database that is not reset between runs** (second run on the same database: `channels:48` and `channel-token-expiry:32` fail because the connected list is paginated). Clean up what each spec creates, or key them by a run suffix. Owner: Step 7.7.
- [x] **The chat UX list of the owner, W3d: one state machine for the files of the thread (2026-11-07, rule 12; PR `feat/media-single-skeleton`):** the worker stores a customer's message first and fetches its file after, so the thread drew the word of the kind ("Image", with an icon) for that moment and then the skeleton: two different looks before the picture. Now a customer's message that is a file and has none yet is the skeleton of the box its file will fill, from the first frame (`PendingMedia`, `awaitsFiles` and `pendingShapeOf` in `lib/inbox/media-state.ts`), and the states of a file that exists come from one function (`fileStateOf`: scanning, loading, ready, infected, unscannable, unreadable), used by `MediaBody` in place of its chain of conditions. The box is the one of the kind (4:3 picture, square sticker, the pill of the others, the voice note with its name line), so nothing moves when the file arrives. Tests: `media-state.spec.ts` and two cases in `media-states.spec.ts` (the box of a file not stored yet, and the box kept while the links are slow). **Decisions and limits:** the skeleton waits at most `MediaArrival.WINDOW_MS` (10 minutes) after the message; after it a message with no file reads as the word again, so a file that is gone does not pulse for ever (the clock is read once, when the message is first drawn, so a thread left open does not flip it). The list of conversations keeps saying the kind of the last message ("Photo"): a text row has no box to fill and cannot know the state of a file, so there is no second state to keep in step with the thread.
- [ ] **The chat UX list of the owner, W3b: the send states (2026-11-07, rule 12; PR `fix/send-status-states`):** done: the warning flash of a reaction on a healthy network is closed; the chip of an emoji drew a triangle whenever one of its reactions had any state, and a reaction that is only waiting for the channel (`queued`) has one, so every reaction flashed a warning until the channel answered; now `reactionStateOf` and `groupStateOf` (`lib/inbox/reaction-state.ts`, tested) give a clock to waiting and the triangle only to a reaction the channel refused (red stays for definitive failures). `Button` takes `busy` (off, `aria-busy`, and a spinner that appears after 400 ms by CSS, `busy-spinner`, with no room taken before and no flash for an answer that comes sooner), applied to the 32 buttons that were off while a request waited. **Left (own PR, needs the api and the worker):** the third state "could not confirm" (amber) for a reply whose outcome is unknown (a timeout or a 5xx of the provider: `PluginError.outcomeUnknown` exists but the message keeps only `failed`, so the thread cannot tell a refusal from a send that may have gone out) with a Retry that warns of a possible duplicate; the message ticks (clock, sent, delivered, read) already follow the delivery states; "Discard" for a failed reply is W4; the other buttons that wait with a condition that is not a plain `pending` do not show the spinner yet.
- [ ] **Debt, reaction count in the thread (owner, 2026-11-10; found in the real Meta test; UI improvements of this step):** on a message of an AGENT, two people reacting with the same emoji show one chip with the sum and their names (`😮 2`, the names listed when the chip is opened); on the image of a CUSTOMER the same emoji from two people shows as two separate chips (`❤️ 1` `❤️ 1`). It happens on every channel and the agent's message is the correct one. `ReactionChips` (`apps/web/components/inbox/reaction-chips.tsx`) already groups by emoji inside ONE list ("Reactions in the thread", Step 3.2; per-image reactions, #326 and #331); the probable cause, to verify first, is that a customer's image is drawn with two lists, the reactions under the file (`reactionsUnderFile`) and those of the message (`reactionsOfMessage`, `apps/web/lib/inbox/file-targets.ts`), so the same emoji lands once in each. Merge the reactions that belong to the same place before grouping, so that one emoji is one chip whatever the direction, with a test of both directions.

(2) A cell has no accessible name of its own (the button that opens it has); give it one with its hour. (3) When a picture of the block is rescanned and stops being clean, the block splits and the open viewer closes. Owner: Step 7.7.
- [ ] **View "With draft", follow-ups of the PR that drew it:** (1) it looks for the drafts it knows of in the pages of the list (up to 20 pages); a draft of a conversation that the active filters leave out, or that was deleted, is never found, and past the 20 pages the view is partial with no word of it. The count on the button is of every draft of the tab, so it can say more than the view shows. A call to the api by id (the conversation detail) for the drafts not found would close both. (2) The index is rebuilt whole at each draft change (`[...previews].filter().sort()`); fine for the drafts of one tab, to measure if a person ever has hundreds. Owner: Step 7.7.
- [x] **The cases that seed a window of time no longer depend on the hour (QA 69, 2026-11-08):** `thread-reading`, `inbox` (the replies of the thread), `widget` (the earlier messages) and `customers` (the files of the gallery) seeded "the last hour" with `Date.now()`, so a run across midnight UTC drew one day separator more. `support/day-anchor.ts` reads the clock once (`seedAnchor`, which moves a window that would cross midnight to noon of the day before; `noonUtcDaysAgo` for one message a day) and `pinAccountToUtc` fixes the zone the page writes in. Checked with the zone of the machine in Pacific/Auckland and every minute of a day: no window crosses midnight.
- [ ] **Draft in the list, follow-up of the PR that drew it:** `resetDraftIndex` runs when the person signs out and when the login page opens, but not when the session ends by itself (a refresh that expired, a 401) while the inbox is still mounted: the rows keep the text of the last draft in memory until the login page opens, and the sessionStorage copy until the same moment (`clearComposerDrafts` has the same two call sites). Call both from the place that handles the end of the session. Owner: Step 7.7.
- [ ] **Waking of a snooze in the web, follow-ups of the PR that drew the badge:** (1) the bulk actions "mark read" and "mark unread" (`lib/inbox/bulk.ts`) still decide by `unreadCount` alone, so a row that only carries the mark of attention counts as read; it should count `attention` too. (2) A screen-reader announcement when a row gets the mark (the realtime announcer reads new messages only). (3) The note field shows no counter; `CharCounter` would add bytes to the inbox bundle, so it waits for the room that Step 7.7 frees. Owner: Step 7.7.

Make the hint say so for a video, or move the focus rule. Owner: Step 7.7.
