Add component tests of `RealtimeProvider` (left by the fix of the bell on the account pages): a null `storeId` opens no socket, and a refused handshake with `leaveWhenRefused={false}` reloads the profile and neither redirects nor shows the suspension toast
- [ ] Bring the initial script transfer down to the 200 KB of `TDD_08 §4.4` (the ceilings `ScriptCeilingsKb` of Step 3.2 are 260 KB on `/login` and 340 KB on the inbox, over measured sizes of 244 and 322 KB) by splitting the bundle, and lower `ScriptCeilingsKb` in `apps/web-e2e/lighthouse/budgets.ts` to it **Measured in PR D1 (inbox, gzip, `productionBrowserSourceMaps` temporarily on):** Next and React about 138 KB, `zod` 31 KB (a move of the client schemas to `zod/mini` would save most of it), the app itself about 34 KB plus 13 KB of `@platform/*`, `@tanstack/query-core` 13 KB, `sonner` 9 KB, `tailwind-merge` 9 KB, `@tanstack/virtual-core` 9 KB, socket.io and engine.io about 12 KB
- [ ] **Room under the script ceiling of the inbox (from the QA round 2 PRs `fix/text-pass-p3` and `perf/inbox-bundle-room`; owner: this step, linked to the item above):** lighthouse runs on pull requests and not on `main`, so the inbox crept up to 340.00 KB of its 340 KB ceiling (`ScriptCeilingsKb`) across five small PRs and every web change after that fought for tenths. The first real saving is done: the browser no longer ships the full `ErrorCodes` table (about 120 codes, one binding for the bundler) but `ClientErrorCodes` (22 codes, drift-checked by a spec in `libs/core/domain`), and the page asks the messages which codes it knows (`useCodeText`); a spec fails if browser code imports the full table again. Measured as the sum of the gzip of the chunks the inbox loads, same base: 196.185 KB before, 195.175 KB after (-1.01 KB; the error table is gone from those chunks). The CI number of the inbox (the one against the ceiling) goes with it: 340.00 before; the first run of this change gives the new one, which this item records. **Permissions, done (PR `perf/permissions-client-table`):** the browser reads `ClientPermissions` (the 30 permissions the web checks, drift-checked against `Permissions` by a spec in `libs/core/domain/auth`), a spec in `apps/web/lib` fails if browser code reads the full table anywhere but `permission-groups.ts`, and the role dialog, the only reader of the full table (the matrix of a role), is a lazy chunk (`next/dynamic` in `team-roles-panel.tsx`). Measured by the lighthouse job (the line it now prints on every run): the inbox was at 339.84 KB of 340 on `main` before the change and is at 339.14 KB with it (room 0.86 KB; login 252.60 of 260). Accepted and left: the guard spec looks for the name `Permissions` only (a namespace import or another library that re-exports the table would pass it), the lazy role dialog has no loading fallback and no error boundary for a chunk that fails to load, and the drift between the tables is checked in one direction (a key missing from the client table is a compile error). **sonner, done (PR `perf/inbox-margin`):** the toasts go through `lib/toast.ts`; `sonner` (about 10 KB of the inbox) is its own chunk, fetched when the first toast is asked for, with a queue so that none is lost, and the same Toaster as before (position, look, aria-live); a spec fails if a page imports `sonner` directly. Limits accepted: the first toast of a session shows after the chunk arrives and a moment (100 ms) for the live region to exist, so a screen reader announces it; the wrapper offers `toast`, `success`, `error`, `info` and `warning` with their options and no id, `dismiss`, `loading` or `promise` (nothing uses them; widen the bridge when something does); the host and the Toaster have no component test because the web project has no DOM environment (the queue is tested, and the e2e specs that read toasts cover the whole path); a load of sonner that fails is retried by the next toast and never takes the page down; the queue keeps the newest 20. Measured by the lighthouse job: the inbox went from 339.14 KB to 329.52 KB (room 10.48 KB, saved 9.62 KB). **zod/mini** (`api-client`, the biggest single saving left, about 8 to 12 KB) is deferred by the coordinator: an option if the room falls under 3 KB; it touches every schema that validates the api, so it is its own PR, and the hostile cases to keep are: each schema must reject what it rejected before and keep the same visible error messages. **Measured with the real list of scripts (the job prints them since PR `ci/lighthouse-script-breakdown`):** the runtime of Next and React is about 115 KB of the 339, the rest is what the pages bring; the code already loads about 25 dialogs and panels on demand; what is still in the first load and is not on demand: zod (the chunk of 36.9 KB; `zod/mini` in `api-client`, its own PR with hostile cases), `next-intl` (20.7 + 11.8 KB), Radix (about 60 KB over five chunks), `socket.io` (16 KB, static on purpose: the gateway validates token, store and permissions and no event may be lost between the load and the join of the rooms), react-query (9 KB), lucide (15 KB, to audit for barrels). Preloading what is lazy in idle does not lower the figure (lighthouse waits for the network to settle): only weight that goes away does. **Still to do:** code-splitting what is not on the critical path of the inbox (the settings and customers code that rides in shared chunks), a single source for `phraseMatches` (the web keeps a local copy of the rule of `libs/core/domain/text.ts` because the domain barrel cost bytes of the inbox: bring them back to one when there is room, with a test that runs both with the same cases), and a lighthouse run on `main` (or a nightly one) so a creep is seen when it happens. **NOT-21** (the notification tone) is built, +0.10 KB measured (see the text pass item above). The lighthouse job used to print the number of the inbox only when it failed, so the CI number of #201 was not recorded (it passed, under 340). **Done (PR `ci/lighthouse-print-numbers`):** every run prints one line per page (`[lighthouse] inbox: scripts X KB of 340 KB (room Y KB), FCP, LCP, TTI, TBT, CLS`) to the log of the job (the `line` reporter is on in CI; whether the `github` reporter alone would show it was not checked) and to the summary of the run, so the room is seen on every pull request. It also lists the 25 heaviest scripts of the load, one line each (`[lighthouse] inbox script 36.20 KB chunks/...js`), and how much of the figure the listing explains, so the ceiling can be read by file (PR `ci/lighthouse-script-breakdown`). Limits: log only (not in the run summary), taken from the run that is the median of the scripts, silent (an empty listing) when the audit is missing from the report, and the format of the lines was not checked against a real run when it was written. Still open: that number is on pull requests only; the lighthouse run on `main` or nightly (below) is what shows a creep between them. **Radix on demand, done (PR `perf/web-lazy-radix-first-load`, 2026-11-09):** the menus of the account, of the store switcher and of the arrow of each row, the sheet of the notifications, the options dialog of resolving and the confirmation of a destructive action are no longer in the first load. Each button is a plain one, with the attributes Radix gives a closed trigger, until the person points at it, focuses it or presses it; then its code is downloaded (`OnDemandMenu`, `components/shared/on-demand-menu.tsx`: a `lazy` module and a `Suspense` whose fallback is the same button) and the press that asked for it opens it as soon as it is here (`defaultOpen`), so the first press is not lost and Radix moves the focus in and gives it back. The sheet and the two dialogs are `next/dynamic` mounted on the first open (`useMountedOnce`). Measured by the lighthouse job on a private stack: inbox 333.92 KB to 301.32 KB (room 38.68 KB of 340), login 251.36 to 241.61 KB; by source maps, gzip of the chunks of `/s/[storeId]/inbox`: 327.0 to 294.8 KB, with Radix and floating-ui from 33.7 KB to 2.6 KB (`Slot`, used by `Button`). Not done on purpose: a preload on idle (the job waits for the network, so it would not lower the figure and would hide the cost). Limits accepted (rule 12): the first press of a menu on a slow network shows the plain button until the code arrives, then the menu opens (a case of the e2e slows the chunks by 1.5 s to prove the press is not lost); the menus open on the press (`pointerdown`, as Radix does) and on the click of the keyboard; the sheet, the options dialog of resolving and the confirmation of a destructive action show nothing until their chunk arrives, and a chunk that fails to load leaves them closed with no message (the same as the other lazy dialogs of the inbox; for the confirmation, `openRef` then stays true until a reload, so a later confirmation is ignored): an error boundary with a retry for the lazy dialogs is open work of this step; the plain button and the real trigger are different elements, so the focus is the one Radix gives when the menu closes; e2e `lazy-radix-menus.spec.ts` covers the pointer, the keyboard (Enter, arrow down, Escape and the return of the focus), the trap of the sheet and Shift+E. **Left, by weight (measured with the same maps):** zod 33 KB in the first load.
- [ ] **zod/mini for the browser (from the measure of PR `perf/web-lazy-radix-first-load`, rule 12; owner: this step, its own PR):** about 18 to 22 KB gzip of the inbox (zod classic 33 KB: `core/schemas` 10, `classic` 4, `util` 4, and 5 KB of `to-json-schema` and `json-schema-processors` that nothing here uses). It rewrites `libs/api-client/src/api-schemas.ts` and the shared schemas of `libs/core` to the functional API of `zod/mini`; risk high: each schema must reject what it rejected and keep the same visible messages (defaults, coercion, `.optional().default()`); do it when `api-client` is quiet.
- [ ] **Split `ApiRoutes` by area (from the same measure; owner: this step):** `libs/core/domain/api/api-routes.ts` is 2.5 KB gzip in the first load and the inbox uses a part of it; splitting it per area would save about 1.5 to 2 KB. Low risk, small gain: only when room is needed.
- [ ] **The chat UX list of the owner, W1 (2026-11-07, rule 12; evidence `chat-ux-repro.md` of the QA; PR `fix/composer-focus-after-send`):** done: the reply and note box keeps the focus after Enter and after a click on Send (it was released on purpose by Q5 A2-3 so that the next key was a shortcut; the owner reversed that, because the next letters of a person who keeps writing fired shortcuts: one assigned a conversation and opened the tag dialog), covered by the e2e of `inbox.spec.ts`; a shared counter (`CharCounter`, `charCounterOf`) tells what is left from 80% of a limit, announced to a screen reader at 80, 90, 95 and 100% and not at every key, on the reply and note box, the customer notes and the resolution reason; the time of a message has two digits on a 24 hour clock (`00:35`, `messageTimeOptions`). The file-send path takes the focus back only if the agent has not moved it elsewhere meanwhile; the counter counts UTF-16 units, as `maxLength` and the api do. **Left:** the counter is not yet on the other fields with a limit (names, titles, snippets, the address and profile forms: `maxLength` in about 15 components); the system lines and the other places that write a time (`system-line.tsx`, the cards of the settings) still use the short style; the one-key shortcuts still fire when the focus is on `<body>` for any other reason (a dialog closed, a toast), which is their design.

- [ ] **QA round v3 (2026-11-07), the Back of the browser with a dialog open (TRX-02, BAN-20; PR of branch `fix/web-dialog-back-stays`):** a dialog takes one step in the history when it opens (`useCloseOnBack`, used by `Dialog`, `AlertDialog` and `Sheet`), so Back closes it and stays on the page; one Back closes one dialog (the one on top). A step that a dialog leaves when it closes any other way is skipped by the next Back and used by the next dialog; it is not taken back at once, because a save that navigates would be undone by it. **Known limits, for this step:** a Back that lands on a left step after the app navigated and another dialog opened on the new page can need one extra press; Forward onto a left step stays on it; Next patches `history.pushState` and the step is a copy of its state, so a change of the shape of that state in a new Next version has to be checked against `dialog-back.spec.ts`.
- [ ] **QA round v3 (2026-11-07), the touch targets left (BAN-21, TRX-14; PR of branch `fix/web-touch-rest`):** the snooze button of a row reaches 40 px and the toggle of the message templates 40 px on a coarse pointer. The checkbox of a row is a 16 px glyph inside a 40 px label (the label is the control a finger hits and the e2e measures it); a QA that measures the glyph alone will read 16 px. **Left for this step (visual pass):** the 40 px snooze button over the lower right of a row may cover a tag chip or the preview on a narrow list, and the links of the top bar (28 px, already here).
- [ ] **QA round v3 (2026-11-07), the resolve dialog and a long tag name (ATA-12, BAN-04, TRX-04; PR of branch `fix/web-resolve-double-submit`):** the form of the resolve dialog sends once however fast the keys come (a synchronous guard), offers the same Undo as the way with no dialog (the closing message waits for it on the server), and a tag name without spaces wraps at 390 px. The success toast of the dialog is now the plain toast of the other way (it had the success style). **Left for this step:** the two Undo toasts are copies of one block (`resolve-flow.tsx`, `resolve-form.tsx`): one helper for both; and an e2e case of the Undo with a closing message chosen in the dialog.
- [ ] **QA round v3 (2026-11-07), small web fixes (PER-19, ORG-08, CLI-03; PR of branch `fix/web-lote4-small`):** the refusal of the exports center names the three permissions it opens with; `/s/<id>` goes to the inbox of the store (it answered not found); the tags a customer can still take get a search box from 13 on and show 30 at most. **Left for this step, as design decisions that were not taken here:** TRX-02 and BAN-20 (the browser Back with a dialog open closes the dialog and also leaves the page; closing it and staying needs one history entry per dialog, which has to be tried with the router of Next and with a dialog that opens right after another closed, the case that `inbox.spec.ts` guards), and a landing by permission for `/s/<id>` (today always the inbox, whose own refusal says what is missing).

The `e` of the open conversation waits up to 5 s for the settings of the store and is dropped after that; the `e` of a row (`RowResolve`) already waited. Left for this step: a unit test of the order of the layout effect of `useResolveFlow` and of the effect of its callers (only the e2e case covers it).
- [ ] **QA round v3 (2026-11-07), time zone of the hours (TRX-03, PR of branch `fix/web-time-zone-and-notification-text`):** the hours follow the zone of the account, else the one of the store, else the one of the browser (`TimeZoneProvider`; the store reports its zone through `StoreTimeZone`, owner finding TZ-MSG 2026-11-08, where only the snooze followed the store). The end-to-end cases of the account zone and of the store zone are in `time-zone.spec.ts`. Decision (changed by the owner finding SNOOZE-ZONE, 2026-11-08): the snooze dialog counts its presets and its date field in the same zone as every hour (account, else store, else browser), so what is picked is what is read; the person without the inbox permission has no store zone and gets the browser one, and the account and organization pages (outside a store) show the account zone or the browser one; both limits close in Step 7.7 (read the store zone from the session, which also removes the one render of the snooze dialog in the zone of the browser when it opens before the store zone reaches the provider). Left for this step: one e2e for a date inside a toast (the toaster sits outside `AppProviders` and has no zone of its own), and a browser zone that changes while the tab is open (it is read once).

- [x] **Flaky `widget.spec.ts` W3c (2026-11-08):** the case failed in CI (first try and retry) at the lookup of the conversation of the visitor, not at the fixed wait: the widget draws the message as soon as it sends it and the conversation is stored later by the api and the worker, so the lookup (`findFirstOrThrow`) came first. It now waits for the conversation (`conversationIdWithMessage` with `toPass`), writes the 40 older messages in one write (`addMessagesAt`) and replaces the 1.5 s wait with the chain the page goes through (the api answers with the unread count, the button is drawn, two frames) plus a record of the requests: no "mark read" may have been asked. Checked by mutation: with `useMarkRead` ignoring `isReading` the case fails at that record. Known limit: a negative check cannot prove that nothing will ever be asked. It catches a mark that is asked as soon as the page has the count and the button; a regression that asks for it a few frames later, or only after the button is drawn, would pass.

The api side is #297. Left for this step: the e2e does not reply as an agent to a conversation nobody has (the api suite covers the assignment); do it with the inbox cases of the visual pass.
- [x] **Owner decision CAPTION PER FILE (2026-11-08), screen side:** where the directory says `captionsPerFile` (WhatsApp, the web widget; the e2e api turns the switch on), each file but the first in the tray has a field for its text (limit of the channel with a counter, label and Escape that leaves the field without closing the conversation); the first takes the text of the message box, and the hint says so; without the switch the hint stays "sent with the first file". `captions[]` goes in the request only when a file but the first has a text (the key of idempotency counts them). The thread draws the text under each file but the first, with the state when the channel sent it apart. Decisions: the field of the first file is the message box (one place for the text of the message, as the api defines `content`); the draft does not keep the texts under files because it keeps no files either. **Resend (removed, PR `refactor/web-drop-per-file-caption-state`):** the screen no longer reads the state of a text under a file nor calls `POST /messages/:id/media/:mediaId/resend-caption`, because a text under each file is offered only where the channel carries it itself (`CAPTION_PER_FILE`, WhatsApp), which has no state to resend; the thread draws the text under each file and nothing else, and "Send the text again" stays only for the text of the message (`CaptionNotice`, Meta channels, an `unconfirmed` one asks first). The switch `OUTBOUND_CAPTION_PER_FILE_ENABLED` stays off by default; only the api of the e2e turns it on.

- [x] **A text under each file only where the channel carries it (owner decision, 2026-11-08; PR `feat/web-caption-per-file-by-capability`, rule 12):** new `ChannelCapability.CAPTION_PER_FILE` (`caption_per_file`, `libs/core/domain/channel-capability.ts`), declared by the WhatsApp plugin and by no other (Messenger, Instagram and the web widget send the text of a file as a message of their own or ignore it); the directory of channels already carries `capabilities` to the web, so the api needed no change. `takesCaptionPerFile` (`lib/media/attachment-batch.ts`) decides in the tray and in the send: no capability, no field under files 2 and later, no `captions` in the request, and the one text of the composer keeps working. Closed in PR `fix/caption-per-file-capability` (session 8e): the api refuses (409 `CHANNEL_CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED`) a text under a file other than the first on a channel without the capability, and `channelFileLimits` reads the capability, not a switch. The switch `OUTBOUND_CAPTION_PER_FILE_ENABLED` is removed (env schema, `.env.example`, the provider, the e2e api): a channel that declares `CAPTION_PER_FILE` (WhatsApp) takes a text under each file from this PR on, with no flag. A text under a file that is not the first never goes apart (an audio on WhatsApp): it is refused; the text of the message under a first audio still goes after it, and the directory says `captionsPerFile: false` for that kind so that the composer offers no field for it. **Decisions (rule 12):** (1) no kill-switch: the owner replaced the switch with the capability, so turning the feature off for a channel is removing `CAPTION_PER_FILE` from its plugin (a redeploy); rejected, an AND of the switch with the capability, because the owner asked for one rule; the real test with Meta of the text under each file and of an audio text sent apart is still open (Step 7.3, line of the Meta findings); (2) the lines 500-501 and 1187 above describe the switch as it was. **Left (owner Step 5.6):** a plugin that declares `CAPTION_PER_FILE` but no media rule for a kind gets no per-file text for it (the registry ties rules to capabilities), and nothing checks that a plugin that declares it carries `caption` in `sendMedia`: a contract test for the capability goes with the manifest line above.

What stays: the text itself (`media.caption`, `captions[]`, native per-file text of WhatsApp) and, for the text of a message (under its first file), `messages.caption_state`, the echo, `resend-caption` by message and the text of a WhatsApp audio sent after it. **Open debts and their Step:** the web spec cast of `captionState` (web lane, Step 7.7); no kill-switch for `resend-caption` and for the per-file text of WhatsApp (owner decision, Step 7.3); the real test with Meta of the per-file text and of an audio text sent apart (Step 7.3, line of the Meta findings); the contract test of `CAPTION_PER_FILE` and the manifest capability for remote plugins (Step 5.6, two lines added); the second echo-by-text hole `reconcileUnconfirmedCaption` (an owner who types the same text, Step 7.3); delete or edit of a message looks only at `Message.captionState` (Step 7.3); the audit of the 409 `assignedTo` and of the late `resolve_undone` (Step 7.1).
- [x] **The text of the message apart, a field for every file (owner, 2026-11-09, real test; PR `feat/caption-box-per-file-general-last`, session 8d, rule 12):** where the channel declares `CAPTION_PER_FILE` (WhatsApp) the composer no longer glues the text of the box to the first file: every file, the first included, has its own field (`takesCaptionPerFile`, `captionsForSend` sends one text per file), and the text of the box is the text of the message, sent as a message of its own AFTER the last file. New column `messages.text_apart` (migration `20261117100000_message_text_apart` + rollback; default false, so every message sent before keeps reading `content` as the text under its first file) set by the api from `ChannelFileLimits.textAfterFile`; `MessageView.textApart`; `captionsOf(body, textApart)` / `requireCaptionsPlaced` / `requireTextFits` (the text of the message has the limit of a plain text, a text under a file the limit of its caption and none under an audio); the worker sends the text after the files only when none failed, only when `captionState` is null (so a retry, a sweep or a resend of a failed file never sends it twice), writes `unconfirmed` BEFORE the call, looks at the messaging window first (`failed` when closed) and records no caption state for the first file. The thread draws the own text of every file (the first too) and the text of the message after the files; the hints "sent with the first file" / "under the first file" are gone (`captionAfter` for the channels without the capability, `captionEach` for the others). Messenger, Instagram and the widget are unchanged (one text, under the first file; the Meta plugin sends it as a second message). **Decisions:** a column over an inference from the channel (the view in core cannot know the plugin, and an old message must keep reading as before); the text goes last only when every file went (a text that overtakes a failed file would reach the customer first) and it is not retried by itself (Meta has no key for it: `unconfirmed`, the agent resends it). **Debts:** (a) the real test with Meta of the field of the first file and of the text last (Step 7.3, line of the Meta findings); (b) a message queued BEFORE this deploy and still unsent is sent as before (text under its first file), by design; (c) editing the `content` of a message with `textApart` would target the id of the first file: WhatsApp takes no edits, so nothing offers it, and a channel that takes edits and declares `CAPTION_PER_FILE` must bind `editMessage` to `captionChannelMessageId` (Step 5.6, contract of `CAPTION_PER_FILE`); (d) `resend-caption` of a text apart does not check that the files after the first were delivered (it checks the message did not fail), same as before (Step 7.3). (e) Found by `/code-review`, same gap as the text of a WhatsApp audio sent apart: deleting the message deletes only the id of the first file, not `captionChannelMessageId`, and the receipts (delivered, read, failed) match `channelMessageId` and `media.channelMessageId` only, so a late failure of the text apart is dropped (Step 7.3: delete the second id too, and match receipts on `captionChannelMessageId`); (f) a text held back because a file failed has no state of its own (`captionState` stays null until the files are resent), and a failed write of the `unconfirmed` mark leaves the text unsent with null state: the agent sees the failed file or, in the second case, only the log (Step 7.3: a `pending` caption state). **Fixed in this PR:** the retention purge also clears `media.caption`, and the conversation export writes the text under each file after the text of the message.
- [x] **One notice for content the channel does not deliver, grouped (owner, 2026-11-09; PR `feat/unsupported-content-notice`, session 8d, rule 12):** every plugin (WhatsApp and Meta) marks such a message with `metadata.unsupportedType` and the views carry it to the web, so there is one route; the thread now says ONE sentence for all of them (`unsupportedKind.notice`, es/en, no name of a channel or of a form of one provider: `unsupported-kind.ts` no longer knows `story_mention`, `ig_post` or `location`; the old per-kind sentences are gone) and, for two or more consecutive notices of one customer, ONE bubble with the count (`unsupportedGroup`; `lib/inbox/notice-runs.ts`, drawn by `MessageBubble` for the last notice, its hour and its menu, with an anchor per covered message so a quote still finds its place; the unread count, the order and the marks read the messages, not the entries). Only a file that could not be fetched (`media`) keeps its own sentence: it is a failure, not content the channel hides. A notice with a reaction, a quote, a link to open or a file does not join (its menu and reactions are its own). Worker: the log "inbound message of a type the channel cannot show" is one line per event with `pluginId`, the type and the shape (no id of message or connection), and it is now written for channels that send the type alone (WhatsApp). **Debts:** (a) the menu of the group is the last notice's: replying to or reacting to an earlier notice of a group is not offered until it is drawn apart (a reaction that arrives later splits the run, by design; Step 7.7); (b) the conversation list preview and the notification text of such a message are not touched (Step 7.7). (c) a run has no time window (owner: consecutive notices of one customer, nothing between), so the group shows the hour of the last one only; (d) the entry of a run is keyed by its first message, as the run of pictures is, so loading earlier messages that join a run remounts that entry (Step 7.7, with the same case of pictures); (e) `InboundProcessor` has no spec: the fields of the line are tested through `unsupportedLogFields`, not the call (Step 7.3).
- [x] **The viewer walks all the pictures of an album (owner, 2026-11-09; PR `feat/image-viewer-arrows`, session 8d, rule 12):** opening a picture of a message with several (a reply, or an album of the customer) now opens the same modal with the arrows (and the keys) to walk every picture of that message, coming round at the ends, as the block of consecutive pictures already did (the two share one viewer, `image-set-viewer.tsx`, downloaded the first time a picture is opened; the links of the two neighbours are read ahead). A screen reader hears "Image 2 of 4" (`inbox.thread.media.position`, a polite status) each time it changes, and the text under the picture is drawn under it with its number. Focus trap, Escape and the return of the focus are Radix's (unchanged). A picture alone opens in it with no arrows and no count; a video keeps the lightbox it had (its arrows seek). `lib/inbox/viewer-items.ts` decides what is walked: clean pictures of the message, in its order. Each picture wears the number of its thumbnail (its place in the message or the block), the arrows ignore Alt/Cmd/Ctrl/Shift, the viewer closes by itself if the open picture leaves the list, and the block of customer pictures leaves out files that are not clean. **Debts:** (a) a message that mixes pictures and a video walks the pictures only; the video opens apart (rare: a message carries one kind, Step 7.7); (b) a clean picture the decoder refused (no variants) is walked and shows its original, while the grid draws it as a download (Step 7.7); (c) no zoom or swipe (Step 7.7); (d) while the viewer chunk loads the click shows nothing (no fallback; the chunk is small, Step 7.7).

Contract: `media.album` + `sendAlbum` (TDD_10 §5.1.1), checked by the registry; the grouping is `apps/worker/src/outbound/album-groups.ts` (pure, with its own spec) and the send is `OutboundSender.sendAlbumGroup`. Decisions: (1) each image is stored on `message_attachments` (repeatable, sends nothing) and one non-repeatable POST sends them; (2) a timeout, a 5xx or a nonsense answer is an UNKNOWN outcome: every file of the group fails as one that may have gone (`failureDefinitive` stays false) and the images are NEVER sent again one by one; only a refusal the provider gave in so many words, with nothing delivered, falls back to one message per image (rejected: falling back on any failure, which would duplicate images to the customer); (3) the id of the message is written on every file of the album (`Media.channelMessageId` is not unique), or one id per file when the plugin returns `fileExternalIds`; receipts move the whole album, and a reaction or quote to any image finds the one message; (4) an image the channel's rules refuse is left out of the album with its own error and the rest go; (5) the text of the message goes after the album and only when the album went out whole. Rejected: one POST with `url` payloads (the storage is private and a development link points at localhost).

If Meta echoes one id per image and answers only one, the echoes of the other images are stored as messages of the owner (`inbound-message.service.ts`, `messageOfFile`): confirm with the first real album and, if so, reconcile them by the recipient, the time and the number of attachments. If Meta refuses the multi-attachment message the fallback sends the images one by one by itself (no data loss) and the log shows `sending an album failed` on each send; to switch the album off, remove `album` from `InstagramMedia` or `MessengerMedia` (`meta-media-rules.ts`). (b) Video, audio and documents in an album are not declared (Meta documents images only); revisit if Meta extends it. (c) `ALBUM_FILE_TIMEOUT_MS` is the upload budget of the plugin per image: a 30-image album can take 90 minutes in the worst case within one job; measure it in the real test and cap the group if the job lock is shorter. (d) The composer limits a message to `maxFilesPerMessage` (10) files, and the registry refuses an `album.maxFiles` above it, so Messenger declares 10 although Meta takes 30 in an album; raise both together only with the owner's decision. (e) The images of an album are stored one after another (up to 180 s each); bounded concurrency would shorten a long album but was left out because the order of the stores is what the provider shows. (g) Review findings of the tests lens left for Step 7.3 (hardening, not defects): no test drives the `withTimeout` budget of an album itself, nor a retryable error with an unknown outcome on a non-last attempt (the worker retries it as it does for one file: only the plugin decides `retryable`, and the Meta post is never retryable after a doubt); Messenger lists no image types (`types: null`), so a format Meta refuses reaches the album post and falls back to one message per image. (f) Review finding left on purpose: with the shared id, a reaction or quote the AGENT sends to a given image of the album points at the album's message (same as the R-2 debt above).
- [x] **An agent's reaction must be ONE emoji (QA albums, P3; PR `fix/reaction-single-emoji`, rule 12):** `PUT /messages/:id/reaction` took any text as `emoji` (`abc`, `<b>`, `x😀`, `😀😀`) and sent it to the customer. `isSingleEmoji` (`libs/core/domain/text.ts`) now accepts exactly one grapheme: a pictograph with optional selector and skin tone, a keycap, a flag (two regional indicators or a tag sequence) or those joined with ZWJ, within `EMOJI_MAX_LENGTH`; text, spaces, controls, invisible characters, a dangling ZWJ and a second emoji are refused. `reactToMessageSchema` uses it and answers 400 `VALIDATION_FAILED` with the issue message `ValidationMessages.EMOJI_NOT_SINGLE` (a name, not a sentence); `null` still takes the reaction back and `''` stays refused, as before. The inbound side keeps the lax `isWholeEmoji` (a customer's reaction is never refused for its shape). The reaction dialog of the web disables its button with the same rule. Known limit, accepted: a text-default symbol without selector (`©`, `❤`) counts as an emoji, since keyboards send `❤` bare; a dedicated error code and a translated per-field message are not added (the dialog never sends a value the api refuses), so a hostile client sees the generic `VALIDATION_FAILED` text.

The album already repeats only a `PluginError` that is `retryable` and NOT `outcomeUnknown` (`sendAlbumGroup`); do the same for a file and for the text: any other doubt fails the file as one that may have gone (`failureDefinitive` false) and the agent decides. Tested for the text and the file with the three kinds of doubt. Left out on purpose (not customer-visible sends): reactions, edits/deletions and read receipts keep `refusalOf`, which retries any non-plugin error; a repeated reaction is the same reaction.

A person has one reaction for each target: the two unique indexes are raw SQL on `coalesce(media_id, '')` (`uq_message_reactions_customer`, `uq_message_reactions_user`, like `uq_roles_store_name`). `PUT /messages/:id/reaction` takes `mediaId` (a file of that message and store, with a provider id; else 404/409); the worker sends or takes back the reaction on the id of THAT file (`SendOutboundReactionJob.mediaId`, in the job id and in the status writer). A file is a target of its own only when the provider can tell it apart (`isTargetOfItsOwn`): the album has several files and the file has an id that is not the message's (the first file IS the message: one id) and that no other file shares (Instagram and Messenger send an album as ONE message and every file carries that one id). Otherwise the reaction or quote is the message's (`mediaId` null), so one provider target is never two rows; a reaction being taken back is found under the file that was named whatever the file is now. `MessageView.reactions[].mediaId` and `MessageView.replyToMediaId` (null: the message) and `reactToMessage(..., mediaId)` / `replyToMediaId` in the api-client. STORY_REPLY cannot be written by an agent (the body is strict, no `metadata`) nor a visitor (the widget plugin reads none): tests in `messages.spec.ts` and `web-widget.plugin.spec.ts`. Proved by `agent-reactions.spec.ts`, `outbound-targets.spec.ts`, `messages.spec.ts`, `outbound.spec.ts`.
- [x] *(`albumNative` / `isNativeAlbum` superseded by the item "Native album discarded in the MVP" above)* **What the message view says of the files of an album (R-2 view, 2026-11-09, `feat/view-targets-per-file`; the owner's real test of #322/#324/#326):** the web had to guess which files can be reacted to and where a reaction goes. `MessageView.media[].targetable` (the provider has an id for the file that no other file shares: true for each file of one message for each picture (WhatsApp), the first included; false in a native album, whose files share one id, for a file with no id yet and for a message of one file), `MessageView.albumNative` (at least two files have an id and every id is the same one: derived from the data, no channel name in the core), `reactions[].fileId` (the file to draw the reaction under, already resolved: `mediaId`, or the first file when the reaction is the message's and the message IS the first file; null in a native album and for a message of one file) and `replyTo.file = { mediaId, position }` (the file the reply quotes; `position` is the one the agent gave or, for the files of a customer that have none, their order from 0). A reply of an agent stores `replyToMediaId` for any file the provider tells apart (the first too: the sender uses that file's id, which for the first is the message's), and the quote of a CUSTOMER of a file of our album stores it too (`insertInboundMessage`, by the id it quoted); a file that shares its id is the message. The provider's ids never leave: only these derived values. Rules in `provider-message-match.ts` (`isDistinctFile`, `isNativeAlbum`; `isTargetOfItsOwn` is the stricter one for reactions: the first file is the message's). Proved by `messages.views.spec.ts`, `outbound-targets.spec.ts` and `messages.spec.ts`.

The reaction to the first picture is stored on the message by the api and drawn under the first by `reactions[].fileId`; "my reaction" is told by the same rule (`isReactionOfFile`). A quote of the first picture already carried `replyTo.file {mediaId, position: 0}` (confirmed by session 3c): following it marks that thumbnail. The count of the grouped notice of content the channel does not show is highlighted (`<num>` in the message, a `strong` with a surface). e2e `album-per-image.spec.ts` reproduces the owner's captures (three pictures, one picture, quote of the first). ### Step 7.8 — Connect with Facebook (OAuth) for Messenger and Instagram
Owner decision (2026-11-05, nine questions answered yes): the provider's own login in place of a token pasted by hand, as the next feature after the three channels were connected. The design is in the owner's QA folder (`meta-oauth-diseno.md`); what it settled, and what was measured on 2026-11-06 in the Graph API Explorer with the owner's account (app `omnichannel-dev`, no secret or token left in any chat): classic Facebook Login with `response_type=code` (the token never passes through the browser), no `config_id` (Facebook Login for Business with a system-user token is what gave a token with no assets); `me/accounts` answers nothing for a Page that belongs to a portfolio, `me/businesses` (two portfolios) and `owned_pages` find it and carry its `access_token`, and the token of that Page reads the linked Instagram account with no error 100/33; reading a Page by its id needs `pages_read_engagement`, and the dialog lets the person clear it. The credential does not change: the login ends in the same `{ accessToken }` of the Page and in the same `connect`, so the account-in-use check, reconnection, audit, subscription and encryption are reused. The core owns the `state`, the exchange and the session; a plugin only answers about its provider (`ChannelPlugin.oauth`). WhatsApp stays out (Embedded Signup is Phase 2); a pasted token always stays as a link ("Use a manual token") and the two kinds of connection live together; disconnecting does not revoke at Meta; business verification and App Review wait until the platform opens to other people (Phase 2).
- [x] **PR 1, foundations (2026-11-06):** `libs/core/domain/channel-oauth-constants.ts` (`ChannelAuthMethods`, `ChannelOAuthKinds`, `ChannelOAuthLimits` with the 256-bit `state`, the 10-minute state and 15-minute session lifetimes and 20 callbacks a minute per address, `ChannelOAuthQuery`, `MetaOAuth` and `MetaOAuthScopes`) and `meta-oauth-scopes.ts` (the scopes of each channel, `metaOAuthScopeParam` that refuses a name that would add a parameter to the address, `grantedMetaScopes` over `me/permissions`, `missingOAuthScopes` that names what the person cleared); the scopes are `pages_show_list`, `pages_messaging`, `pages_manage_metadata`, `pages_read_engagement` for Messenger (and `business_management` until 2026-11-07, see Measure 1), plus `instagram_basic` and `instagram_manage_messages` for Instagram, and nothing that publishes, reads ads or touches money; `CacheKeys.channel.oauthState` and `oauthSession` (keys are the SHA-256 of the value, never the value, and outside the `store:*` pattern); `ApiRoutes.channels.oauthStart`, `oauthCallback` (a fixed path, because it is registered in the Meta app), `oauthAssets`, `oauthComplete`, `oauthSession` and their lines in `scripts/frontend-coverage-map.ts`; `META_OAUTH_REDIRECT_URI` (optional; set, it must be the callback of this api with no query, fragment or credentials, a public https address when deployed, and it needs a numeric `META_APP_ID` and `META_APP_SECRET`; unset, the login is off and `.env.example` ships it commented); the optional `ChannelPlugin.oauth` block of the SDK (`kind`, `scopes`, `grantedScopes`, `listAssets`, `credentialsFor`), checked and copied by the registry at boot (`snapshotChannelOAuth`, `PluginRegistry.oauthOf`) and held by the contract suite; the callback joins the public routes of rule 5 of `CLAUDE.md` (owner, 2026-11-05, confirmed 2026-11-06). **No endpoint, no screen and no plugin declares a login yet**: nothing here changes what a user can do
- [x] **PR 2, the server (2026-11-06; the security lens ran twice over the server code, the second run returned 8 findings; mutants of the state, the owner, the store, the scopes, the redirect and the sealed token each fail a test, and the store check got a case of its own after one survived).** Built: `ChannelOAuthStore` (state and session in Redis under the SHA-256 of the value, `GETDEL` for the state), `ChannelOAuthService`, the four authenticated routes (`SETTINGS_EDIT`; the store from the guard; `assets` and `complete` limited to 30 a minute; `start` and `assets` `no-store`) and the public callback (20 a minute per address, a bare 302 with `no-store`; the api already sends `no-referrer`), `MetaLogin` for Messenger and Instagram with the discovery A, B (every portfolio, five at a time) and the numeric Page id, `MetaGraphClient.exchangeToken` (secret in the body of a POST, a refusal of 4xx never read as an outage) and `loginDialogUrl`, `ChannelOAuthConfig` in the connections lib, `authMethod` `facebook_login` in the metadata and the audit of a connection made through the login (a connection made another way says nothing), `res.headers.location` and `oauth` added to the redaction of logs, the four new reasons with their texts, `HttpStatusCodes.FOUND`, a case in `tenant-isolation.spec.ts`, and `tests/api/channel-oauth.spec.ts`. Decisions: the core checks that the address a plugin makes keeps its state and callback; `complete` checks the scopes too, so a call that skips the list cannot connect with a permission the person cleared; the session is consumed only after the connection is made; `cancel` needs only to be the owner; the callback never throws and answers a reason of the closed list. Original scope, as planned: the `state` in Redis (a digest as the key, `GETDEL`, user, store and plugin in the value, 10 minutes), `POST .../oauth/:pluginId/start` (`AuthGuard` + `TenantGuard` + `SETTINGS_EDIT`, the store from the context and never from the body), the public callback with its own limit and a `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` redirect to the web app that carries only the opaque session id, the exchange with `META_APP_SECRET`, the encrypted session (15 minutes; the person's token is never kept after `complete`), `assets` and `complete` over the existing `connect` (the session belongs to the user and the store that started it; `metadata.authMethod`), `code` and `state` added to the redaction of logs, an integration test that the five routes are reached by their own handlers and not captured by a `:id` route of the connections (PR 1 proves it only on the path patterns), a case in `tests/security/tenant-isolation.spec.ts` (a state and a session of another store), and the implementation of `oauth` in `MetaChannelPlugin` for Messenger and Instagram: `grantedScopes` from `me/permissions` (a missing scope ends in the existing reason `permission_missing` carrying the list of names, which are not secret; no second near-duplicate reason), `listAssets` trying `me/accounts`, then `me/businesses` with `owned_pages` of EVERY portfolio, then asking for the numeric id of the Page, `credentialsFor` reading the Page by its id. Written against a strict fake Graph that rejects any call it does not declare and answers with the shapes measured on 2026-11-06. New reasons for the failure list (`no_assets`, `page_without_instagram`, `oauth_cancelled`, `oauth_expired`) arrive here with their texts in both catalogs, because nothing uses them before this PR
- [ ] **Left by PR 2, each with the PR that closes it (rule 12):** (1) a callback that cannot name the store (a state that expired, was used or never was) sends the browser to the root of the web app with `oauth_error`, and nothing reads it yet: PR 3 makes the root carry the reason to the channels settings of the last store. (2) The callback is limited to 20 a minute per address, and the throttler answers with its own JSON before the handler runs, so several people behind one address finishing the dialog in the same minute would see a raw 429: an exception filter that turns it into a redirect with `provider_busy` belongs to PR 3 (or Step 7.1, hardening). (3) The calls of a login (the exchange, the listing of the Pages) are bounded by `withTimeout`, which does not cancel the call it gives up on: an `AbortSignal` in `ChannelContext` and `MetaGraphClient` is already the debt (2) of the profile fetch, and closes both. (4) `ChannelOAuthConfig` carries the one redirect address of Meta's login (`META_OAUTH_REDIRECT_URI`), which the core service shares with every plugin: a second provider of login derives its address from the public base of the api and the same callback route, in Phase 2. (5) The long-lived token of the person is not revoked at Meta after `complete` or `cancel`, on purpose: revoking the grant (`DELETE /me/permissions`) also takes away the permissions that the Page tokens depend on; it is never stored and expires at Meta in about 60 days.

The bundle of the inbox did not change (361.28 KB gzip on this branch and on `main`, measured by summing the chunks of the inbox). - [ ] **Left by PR 3, each with the PR that closes it (rule 12):** (1) *Closed by PR 4:* "Reconnect with Facebook" now carries the connection in the state and `complete` takes only the Page that connection already is. (2) A tab closed or a page reloaded with the Pages dialog open leaves the session to expire on the server in 15 minutes: the address no longer holds it, so it cannot be resumed, and a keep-alive request cannot carry the token of the person; Step 7.1. (3) A login that cannot name its store lands on the channels of the last store, not always the one it was started from, and shows nothing to a role without `SETTINGS_VIEW` there; a short-lived record of the consumed state that holds its store, or a cookie, would fix the first: Step 7.1. (4) A Page dialog or a notice that closes leaves the focus on the heading of the page, not on the button that started the login (the dialog opens from a return, not from a control). (5) The records of the state and the session are strict objects: during a rolling deploy an instance that does not know a new field reads a record that has it as missing and the person sees `oauth_expired` once; fields are added to them only with the deploy of Step 7.3 in mind. (6) The e2e of the login chains its six scenarios in one test, because each sign in spends the magic link budget of the address and the login is the same store state throughout: a failure in an early scenario hides the later ones, and `retries` replays all of them; splitting them into serial tests needs a way to share the session of the page between tests (Step 7.7, with the rest of the e2e debts). (7) The inbox has no DOM test environment, so the dialogs and the button are covered by the e2e and by the pure helpers (`oauth-return`, `start-path`, the requests) and not by component tests: the debt of Step 7.7 stays.
