That is the "Done when" of this step, so Step 7.8 is `done` (2026-11-06). The rest of the guion (a permission cleared, a no, reconnecting a connection that broke) was not reported and stays covered by the suites; and the two measures of the guion were taken afterwards, the same day (below):
- [x] **Measure 1, closed (owner, 2026-11-06), and REDONE on 2026-11-07, which supersedes it: `business_management` is no longer asked for.** *First measure (2026-11-06):* `me/businesses` does NOT answer without `business_management`. A user token with only `pages_show_list`, `pages_read_engagement` and `instagram_basic` got error 100, "(#100) Missing Permission". So `business_management` is necessary for the discovery of a Page that lives in a portfolio and STAYS in `metaOAuthScopesFor`; no PR to remove it. (For people other than the owner it will need Advanced Access, Phase 2, as `Left by PR 1` already says.) **Redone (owner, 2026-11-07), a token WITHOUT `business_management`:** `me/businesses` lists the two portfolios and `me/businesses/<id>/owned_pages` of the one that holds the Page lists it; the other portfolio holds none. The first result (error 100) was therefore not caused by the missing permission alone; what differed is not known. **Done in the scopes PR:** `business_management` leaves `MetaOAuthScopes` and `metaOAuthScopesFor` (the dialog no longer asks it, and a plugin cannot declare it either), tests and the e2e fake Graph follow, and the guion drops it. **Limit, written on purpose:** both measures were taken with an administrator of the app in development mode; for other accounts (Advanced Access, Phase 2) Meta may still refuse the portfolios, so `listAssets` now answers an EMPTY list when every way that failed was a refusal that will not change (errors 100, 10 and 200 to 299) and nothing was found, and the screen then asks for the number of the Page (the way in that needs nothing from the lists); an outage, a throttle or a dead token still fails as before. Only the refusals of the PORTFOLIO lists count (a refusal of `me/accounts` still fails), and a failure that may be tried again or whose outcome is unknown is never a refusal, whatever code the Graph copied into it. **Left, none blocking:** a refusal swallowed this way is not logged (the login code has no logger; an unexplained error 100 on a bad `fields` value of the portfolio lists would show as an empty picker), and the e2e fake Graph cannot refuse the portfolios, so the screen's typed-number path after a refusal has only unit coverage: Step 7.7 adds both.
- [x] **Measure 2, closed for Messenger (owner, 2026-11-06): `messaging_referrals` was missing from the `subscribed_fields` of the app on the Page, and the cause was the panel, not the code.** What each plugin posts at connect (`POST <owner>/subscribed_apps`, the union of what the app already has and its own list, #183, #188, #189): Messenger `messages,message_echoes,message_reactions,message_edits,messaging_referrals` on `<pageId>`, Instagram `messages,message_reactions,messaging_referrals` on `me`, both with the Page token. Before the change Meta listed `message_deliveries`, `message_echoes`, `message_edits`, `message_reactions`, `message_reads` and `messages`: four of the five names Messenger posts, and two that no plugin posts. The hypothesis was that the field was not enabled at the level of the app, so the Page call did not keep it. **The owner enabled `messaging_referrals` in Webhooks, object `Page`, of the app `omnichannel-dev`, and `me/subscribed_apps` with the Page token now lists `message_deliveries`, `message_echoes`, `message_edits`, `message_reactions`, `message_reads`, `messages` and `messaging_referrals`.** The hypothesis is confirmed and no code changes: a field must be enabled in the app before the Page keeps it. **Still to verify, if the owner enables it:** Instagram, the field `messaging_referral` (singular) of the object `instagram` in the same Webhooks panel, and then the same read. Until then the `referral` of a click-to-Direct ad does not arrive for Instagram; for Messenger it does. What a referral gives today: the plugin reads `ad_id`, the title and the creative of an ad and the link of `source` into the origin card of the conversation, and keeps the raw referral (with the `ref` of an `m.me/<page>?ref=...` link) in the database only; the `ref` itself is not shown in the inbox, and showing it would be a task of its own.
- [ ] **Pages ticked in the Facebook dialog (OAUTH-PAGES, owner 2026-11-08):** after the app was removed from Facebook and connected again, the login listed no Page: `me/accounts` and the portfolios answer only to a person with a role or the `business_management` grant of an earlier consent, and the code never read what the person ticked. `MetaLogin.listAssets` now also reads `granular_scopes` of `debug_token` (targets of `pages_show_list`, `pages_messaging`, `pages_manage_metadata`, at most 25, plain ids) and reads each missing Page by id; a failure there loses nothing. The text of the empty case tells the person to tick the Page in the dialog. Open: (1) the real test of Step 7.8 (the owner removes the app, connects again and ticks the Page: it must be listed; this closes the Step); (2) the e2e fake Graph (`apps/web-e2e/support/fake-graph.ts`) does not serve `debug_token`, so the path is covered by `meta-login.spec.ts` against a strict Graph, not by a browser test: add the route and a case that ticks a Page when `me/accounts` and the portfolios are empty (Step 7.7).
- [ ] **Debts of PR 1, each with the PR that closes it (rule 12):** (1) *Closed on 2026-11-07:* `business_management` is not asked for (see Measure 1). (2) The Explorer does not exercise our `redirect_uri` or the real dialog (it has its own), so the screen where the person picks the Pages and the portfolios was never seen: the owner's real test. (3) *Closed on 2026-11-08 by the OAUTH-PAGES PR (see the entry above):* `debug_token` (`granular_scopes.target_ids`) was not measured and was left out of discovery; it is Phase 2, if a person other than the owner turns out to need it. (4) The `subscribed_apps` of the page, read with the Page token on 2026-11-06, lists `message_deliveries`, `message_echoes`, `message_edits`, `message_reactions`, `message_reads` and `messages` and NOT `messaging_referrals`, which `MessengerApi.SUBSCRIBED_FIELDS` and `InstagramApi.SUBSCRIBED_FIELDS` ask for: either the subscription did not take it or Meta does not list it, and the click-to-message ad `referral` (Step 2.8) would not arrive; the owner's real test reads the fields after a real connection through the login (step E2 of the guion) (it also belongs to Step 7.3, where the other Meta unknowns are kept). (5) The login scopes are Standard Access, which serves the owner and the people with a role on the app; accounts of other people need Advanced Access (App Review) and business verification: Phase 2, with the opening of the platform. (6) The check that refuses `META_OAUTH_REDIRECT_URI` in a deployed environment knows `localhost`, loopback and `0.0.0.0` (the list `FRONTEND_URL` uses) and not other private hosts (`10.x`, `*.local`): the address must be registered in the Meta app, which only takes a public one, so a wrong one fails at the first login and not at boot; a check of private ranges would serve every address variable at once and is left for Step 7.3 (deployment). (7) `metaOAuthScopesFor` and the `scopes` that `MetaChannelPlugin` declares are two lists that must agree: PR 2 builds the plugin's from the function and tests it. (8) The shared contract suite checks a declared login only for its `kind` and `scopes` (a plugin without one has nothing to check): PR 2 adds the fixtures of Messenger and Instagram for `grantedScopes`, `listAssets` and `credentialsFor` against the fake Graph, and the suite then runs them for every plugin that declares a login. (9) A plugin of a third party cannot declare `oauth` (it is code, a remote plugin is a manifest): by design, until a third-party flow exists

**TDD:** `TDD_10 §2`, `TDD_05` (pentest checklist)
**Done when:** the owner connects Messenger and Instagram from "Connect with Facebook" with no pasted token, in both orders, with the permission he clears in the dialog named instead of an error of Meta; a state that is reused, expired or of another user or store is refused; the pasted-token path still works; and the bundle of the inbox did not grow.

### Step 7.9 — Channel-agnostic core (owner's mandate, 2026-11-09; before Milestone 4)

Core, SDK, web and api-client must not know a channel by name: everything is decided by data the plugin declares
(its descriptor), so that a third party adds a channel without editing core or UI. Only `libs/channels/*` knows
its provider. The audit and the PR order are in the owner's reports (`auditoria-agnostico.md`, 28 findings); the
database has no CHECK or enum that blocks a new plugin id (verified), so no migration is planned.
- [x] A1 (#336) — descriptor in the SDK: `ChannelPlugin.limits` (`ChannelLimits`: text and caption limits) and `identityKind` (`phone | opaque`), checked and copied by the registry at boot (`limits-snapshot.ts`, `limitsOf`, `identityKindOf`), declared by the four official plugins and published in the channel directory (`limits`). The toy third-party plugin suite (`libs/plugins/runtime/src/toy-plugin.spec.ts`, id `acme_chat`) is the lock and grows with each PR below.
- [x] A2 — read the limits from the registry: `ChannelTextLimits` and `ChannelCaptionRules` are gone; `caption-rules` take the plugin's `ChannelLimitsView`, the outbound sender and `channel-file-limits` use `limitsOf`, and the composer reads `limits` from the directory. A test with a plugin nobody named (limit 20) holds the worker to what the plugin declared.
- [x] A3 — customer identity by `identityKind`: `parseChannelIdentity`/`normalizeChannelIdentity` take the kind, not the channel id; the plugins module provides `CHANNEL_IDENTITY_KINDS` (from the registry; a channel it does not know is opaque) to the services of core; linking a handle (`IdentitiesService`) normalizes in the service, no longer in the zod transform. A channel nobody named that declares `phone` has its handles read as digits (integration test).
- [x] A5a — the rest of the descriptor, declared by the plugin and checked at boot (`descriptor-snapshot.ts`, `connect-manifest.ts`): `displayName {es,en}`, `iconKey` (closed set), `anonymousCustomers`, `managementSurface`, `connectHelp` steps, `imageHosts {avatar, media}` (https origins, one wildcard only as the first label, no port/path/credentials; bounded) and a connect field label as a text of the plugin (`label {es,en}`, text only: no markup, link, placeholder or control character) as well as a message key. Published without the hosts: `descriptor` in the channel directory and in the plugin catalog (`channel.descriptor`); schema `channelDescriptorSchema` in `libs/api-client/src/api-schemas.ts`, which checks again in the browser and falls back to something safe. A contract spec pins the declared hosts to the CSP of today (`tests/contracts/channel-image-hosts.spec.ts`).
- [x] A5b — the OAuth part of the descriptor, checked at boot (`oauth-snapshot.ts`): `dialogOrigin` (https origin exactly: no path, query, port, credentials, wildcard, address or private name), `providerName`, `linkedAccount {required, label with `{username}`, missingLabel}` and `assetIdPattern` (one bounded run of one class of character, anchored, which cannot backtrack: a ReDoS guard by grammar, not by luck); published as `oauth` in the plugin catalog when the login is available, and re-checked by `channelOAuthDescriptorSchema` in the browser. The toy channel `acme_chat` (`libs/channels/toy`) registers as a plugin of a third party only with `E2E_TOY_PLUGIN=1` in `NODE_ENV=test` (any other environment refuses to boot with it); the catalog writes a row for every plugin the code of the platform registers (`listFromCode`). Integration `tests/api/toy-plugin.spec.ts`. The Meta login declares its descriptor; the pattern of its ids (`MetaOAuth.ASSET_ID_PATTERN`) moves with A4. The api holds `start` to the declared origin and `complete` and the asset list to the declared id pattern. **Debts, each with its owner:** (1) the web still draws the login from `facebookLogin` and from the Facebook origin and id pattern; drawing it from `oauth` is B1b (fe). (2) The only kind of login the core runs is `meta-facebook-login` with Meta's permission names: a provider with another sequence or other permission names needs its own `ChannelOAuthKind` and the scopes it may ask for declared with it, which is Step 5.6 (remote channel plugins).
- [x] A5c — `LINKED_ACCOUNT_MISSING` replaces `PAGE_WITHOUT_INSTAGRAM` (the Meta login names it; the web has a generic text in both languages); the old name stays as a deprecated alias for one release (still believed, same text, same health verdict) and is removed in the next release, which closes this task; the checks `PAGE_ID_NOT_NUMERIC`/`PAGE_ID_MISMATCH` became `PARENT_ID_*` (log names only, no alias).
- [x] B1a — web: the composer holds a reply to the text limit of its channel (`textMaxOf`, from `limits` of the directory): box, counter, snippet insertion, edit-back and the send button; a note, and a reply with a file ready (its text is a caption, held by the tray and the api), keep the general limit. Debt (owner: Step 7.7): an e2e that types over the limit of a channel with a smaller text limit, and a visible reason (not only the counter) when an over-limit draft cannot be sent. The toy plugin e2e (`apps/web-e2e/specs/toy-plugin.spec.ts`, with `E2E_TOY_PLUGIN=1` in the api env of `playwright.shared.ts`) is written red and waits for A5 (the hook and the descriptor): it ships with B1b.
- [x] B1b-1 (done in its PR) — web: every channel is named from its descriptor in the language of the reader (`pluginNameOf`, `usePluginLabel(descriptor, serverName)`): inbox, settings and routing rules; `settings.routing.channelNames` and the official-ids fallback list of the rule dialog are gone (a person without `CONVERSATIONS_VIEW` sees only the channels a rule already names; accepted, they cannot read the store's channels; the same person sees the ids in the rule summary: Step 7.7 gives the routing page its own channel names read). The catalog parses `channel.descriptor`.
- [x] B1b-2a — web: the connect form draws the label the plugin wrote (`label{es,en}`, `fieldLabelOf`) when it names no message key; the embed panel of a connection follows `descriptor.managementSurface`, not the id of a channel; the toy plugin e2e (`acme_chat`, `E2E_TOY_PLUGIN=1`) is green; rule 13 (no channel known outside `libs/channels/*`) is in `CLAUDE.md`. Known limit (owner: Step 7.7): when the catalog cannot be read the embed panel of a connection is not drawn, because the surface now comes from the catalog entry (the page already shows its catalog error then).
- [x] B1b-2b — web, split in two PRs. **Done in the first (login):** the login button (`OAuthLoginButton`), its dialog origin, the account picker (linked-account label and missing label, typed-id pattern with a bound) and the texts follow `channel.oauth`; `startOAuthLogin`/`useStartOAuthLogin`; `isOAuthDialogUrl(url, dialogOrigin)` refuses everything without a declared origin; the web no longer reads `facebookLogin`, `MetaOAuth` or the id of a channel in these files; `descriptor.iconKey` draws the channel icon from a closed Lucide map (`ChannelIcon`, in the channels settings; the inbox keeps its text only, so its bundle does not grow). **Second PR (visitors, done):** "visitor" naming follows `anonymousCustomers` of the conversation and of each identity (`customer-name.ts`, `use-customer-name.ts` and the inbox rows take a boolean instead of a channel id); the flag is read from the cached view because the realtime events do not carry it. **Debts of the first PR:** the failure reasons of the connect form that A5 did not neutralise (`request_rejected`, `account_not_linked`, `account_unreadable`, `subscription_*`, `provider_*`, `other_account`…) still name Meta, Instagram and Page in `settings.channels.connectFailure`; they need texts that travel with the plugin (owner: the step that adds `PluginError.userMessage` to the contract); the legacy flag `facebookLogin` stays in the api catalog until the alias is retired; the e2e `channels-oauth-login.spec.ts` (renamed from `channels-facebook-login.spec.ts`) names the provider because it drives the Meta plugin on purpose. The security rules of the descriptor are tested in the web.
  - [ ] Anonymous customers: tests (owner: Step 7.7). An e2e that a widget visitor with no name reads "Visitante" in the list, the header and a toast, and a customer of another channel reads "Cliente"; the integration cases of the flag on the routes of update, status, snooze, assign, read, unread, link, create and merge (see A6a). The customer's profile still decides "visitor" from the first identity only, while the inbox uses the flag of the conversation; a merged customer with identities of several channels can read differently in the two (unchanged limit, now visible).
- [x] A4a — the leaf constants and media rules move to their plugin libraries with no new logic: `MetaUpload`, `MetaWebhookApps`, `MetaTransientStatuses`, `MetaErrorCodes`, `MetaMessaging`, `MetaSubscription`, `MetaSubscribableFields`, `InstagramApi`, `MessengerApi`, `MapLinks`, `WhatsAppErrorCodes` and the media rules of the three channels (`MetaMediaFilesPerMessage` too) go to `libs/channels/meta`; the `WhatsApp*` constants and `WhatsAppMedia` go to `libs/channels/whatsapp` (alias `@platform/channels/whatsapp-constants` for the e2e fake, which must not pull in the plugin). **Still in core, each with the PR that moves it (the Nx boundaries forbid core, api, worker and web to import `libs/channels/*`, so each one first needs its consumer to read data):** `MetaOAuth`/`MetaOAuthScopes` (A4c: the id pattern and the scopes come from the descriptor; the rest of the A4b list moved), `MetaStoryImageHosts` (read by the web's CSP spec: B2), `ChannelConnectFieldKeys`/`ChannelSetupStepKeys` of the official channels (their texts become plugin texts in B1b).
- [x] A4b — `WhatsAppLimits` moves to `libs/channels/whatsapp`; `MetaWebhook`, `MetaGraph` and `MetaLimits` move to `libs/channels/meta` (core keeps only `MetaStoryImageHosts`, which the CSP reads: B2); `metaOAuthScopesFor`, `metaOAuthScopeParam` and `grantedMetaScopes` move to `libs/channels/meta/src/meta-oauth-scopes.ts` and `missingOAuthScopes`, which only compares names, stays in core (`oauth-scopes.ts`). **Redaction:** the core lists no header or query name of Meta any more: `x-hub-signature(-256)` is read as secret by the name rule `isSecretHeader` and `hub.verify_token` by the `token` suffix (pinned by `redaction-constants.spec.ts` and the old logger specs). What the core cannot guess, a plugin class declares as `static redactedKeys` (names of secret fields, checked by `declaredRedactedKeys`: plain names, at most 32, never a path); `officialRedactedKeys()` (plugins-runtime) joins them and the apps pass them to `HttpPlatformModule.forRoot({ redactedKeys })`, which hands them to the logger (`redactedPathsFor`). The Meta channels declare `instagramAppSecret` and `instagram_app_secret`. **Decisions:** (1) the audio hint of the worker is not a plugin answer: m4a aliases and the mp4 container of an audio message are MIME normalization with no provider in it, so the worker reads a generic `AUDIO_MP4_CONTENT_TYPE` (core storage) and the Meta plugin keeps its own `MetaGraph.AUDIO_HINT_CONTENT_TYPE` for what it puts on a webhook media; moving the logic into `fetchMedia` would have given the SDK the webhook type for no gain; (2) the keys are read from the plugin CLASSES, not from the registry, because the logger is built before the registry (the registry needs the logger). **Debts:** (a) only the logger uses `redactedKeys`; `scrubUrl` and the error report (`sentry.ts`) stay on the name rules, which cover query and header names; a plugin whose query parameter or header secret is not caught by a name rule needs a name the rules read (owner: Step 5.6, with the dynamic descriptor of remote plugins, which cannot reach the logger built at boot); (b) a third-party plugin's `redactedKeys` are not read until Step 5.6 for the same reason.
- [x] A4c — `MetaOAuth`, `MetaOAuthScopes` and `MetaGrantedPageScopes` move to `libs/channels/meta/src/meta-oauth-constants.ts` (alias `@platform/channels/meta-oauth-constants` for the e2e, like the WhatsApp one); core keeps the generic `ChannelOAuthLimits`/`ChannelOAuthQuery`. The kind of login is named by its sequence (`ChannelOAuthKinds.AUTHORIZATION_CODE_WITH_ASSETS`, value `authorization-code-with-assets`; the kind is not sent to the web) and the stored auth method is `ChannelAuthMethods.PROVIDER_LOGIN` (value `facebook_login` kept: it is in existing rows). **Security decision:** the allowlist of scopes by kind that named Meta's permissions in core is replaced by a data-driven ceiling: an official plugin is held to the shape of a permission name (`ChannelOAuthLimits.SCOPE_PATTERN`, at most 20, no repeats), and any other plugin to the permissions that the official plugins with the same kind of login already ask for (`PluginRegistry.oauthCeilingFor`; none official with that kind = nothing allowed). Rejected: dropping the ceiling (a third party could make the platform's app ask for ads or publishing) and a ceiling the plugin declares itself (vacuous). `MetaOAuth.ASSET_ID_PATTERN` was already read from `oauth.assetIdPattern` by the core; what remains is the plugin's own use. **Debt, owner A4d:** the redirect address of the login (`META_OAUTH_REDIRECT_URI`, debt (a) of A7) is still read by `ChannelConnectionsLibModule` through `parseMetaEnv` into `ChannelOAuthConfig`; it moves to the plugin's `oauth` declaration (`redirectUri`), with the test seams of `channel-oauth.spec.ts` and `tenant-isolation.spec.ts` (they override `ChannelOAuthConfig`) and the second parse of the Meta block going away. The ceiling is keyed by the kind of login, which names a sequence and not a provider: a third party for another provider that uses the same sequence is held to the permission names of the official plugins of that kind (it fails closed; a provider with other permission names needs its own kind or an official plugin, owner: Step 5.6). The official plugins are registered first by `PluginsModule` whatever the order of the list, and only a plugin that owns a reserved id counts as official for the ceiling.
- [x] A4d — the redirect address of the login moved from `ChannelOAuthConfig` (which read the Meta block of the environment) to the plugin's `oauth` declaration: `redirectUri: string | null` (null: the login is off). The registry is given the callback of this platform (the origin of `API_URL`, the api prefix and `ApiRoutes.channels.oauthCallback`, composed in `@platform/channels/oauth-callback`) and holds a login to that string exactly: another site, a look-alike or sibling host, another port or scheme, a login in the address, another path, a query, a fragment, a trailing slash or a different case turn THAT plugin's login off and the registry logs an error (without the declared address); a declaration that is not text is a contract violation like any other. A mismatch is a mistake of configuration and must not stop the webhooks and every other channel from booting. `channels/connections` no longer imports the Meta library. Known requirement: `API_URL` must be the public address the provider is told (for a real test through a tunnel, API_URL is the tunnel's address); if it is not, the login of Meta is off and the log says why. Debt: none.
- [x] A6a — the views carry `anonymousCustomers` (from the registry, via the `CHANNEL_ANONYMITY` token of `PluginsModule`): on each conversation (list, detail, the page of a customer, the answers of the status, snooze, assign, read and unread routes) and on each identity of a customer (detail, create, update, link, merge, unmerge). No permission of its own: it holds nothing of the customer. The web (B1b-2b) reads it instead of comparing a channel id; `api-client` reads it with a default of false. Debt: the realtime events and the export do not carry it (the web reads it from the cached view); tracked in B1b-2b. Also open: the routes of update, status, snooze, assign, read, unread, link, create and merge have no integration case of their own for the flag (the detail, the inbox list and the customer's page do); owned by the open task "Anonymous customers: tests" of B1b-2b.
- [x] L1 (QA P2) — a plain text longer than its channel holds was created (201) and failed later in the worker with a generic definitive error. `MessagesService.create` and `MessageChangesService.edit` now hold a TEXT (and the new text of an edit) to `plugin.limits.textMaxLength` from the registry before the message is stored or anything is queued (400, characters not UTF-16 units; a note is not held to it). Not a regression of A2 (#339): the check never existed for a plain text (`requireTextFits` served only the text that goes apart); #339 moved the tables the worker reads. Tests: `tests/api/text-limit.spec.ts` (limit passes, limit+1 gives 400 with nothing stored or queued, on the three official channels, the toy `acme_chat` 500/501 and an editable test channel); two cases of `messages.spec.ts` moved to a channel whose text holds the general limit. **Debts:** (a) the 400 carries the limit in its text only, not as structured metadata the web could draw (owner: Step 7.7, with the visible reason for an over-limit draft); (b) a template and the text under a file keep their own checks.
- [x] A6 — the three special cases of the widget are gone from core and the apps: the generic webhook door refuses a channel whose manifest says `receivesProviderWebhooks: false` (it has doors of its own); the typing relay tells a channel the api reaches itself through the `DIRECT_TYPING_DELIVERIES` list that the module of that channel provides (any other goes through the queue by `TYPING_INDICATOR`); the media fetcher stores a file under the id its customer was told only for a plugin that declares `NAMES_INBOUND_FILES`. No `deliveryMode` field was needed: what the plugin already declares (a manifest flag, a capability) and what its module provides say it.
- [ ] A6c — a plugin that declares `NAMES_INBOUND_FILES` must give DIFFERENT ids to the files of one message (the worker takes a repeated id for a retry and drops the second file silently); the widget cannot repeat one (one file per message). Enforce it (reject or derive) before a plugin of a third party can declare the capability: Step 5.6. Also: `DIRECT_TYPING_DELIVERIES` is one provider, so a second channel the api reaches itself needs a collector (see `direct-typing.ts`); closes with A6b.
- [x] A6b-1 — the doors of the widget are pinned (`widget-surface.spec.ts`: path, method, status, public flag, rate limit of each public door, permission of each settings route; it fails whichever file declares the controller) and the parts of the module that need no module of the api moved into `libs/channels/web-widget`: the schemas of the public doors and of the settings, the message view (`widgetMessageView`, `WIDGET_FILES_SELECT`, `visitorMessagesWhere`) and the typing throttle. Behavior unchanged; the api imports them from the plugin library.
- [ ] A6b-2 — move the rest of `apps/api/src/modules/widget/*` (controllers, gateway, services, listener, CORS middleware) into the plugin module. It is blocked by what they use from the api: `TenancyModule` (decorators `CurrentStore`, `RequirePermissions`, `actorOf`, the guards), `MediaModule`, `ChannelsModule` (`InboundEventQueue`), `PluginRegistry` (a `scope:plugins` lib that a `scope:channels` lib may not import) and `WidgetDelivery`, which `RealtimeModule` and `ConversationsModule` reach. First step: make the tenancy decorators and the inbound queue libraries a channel may depend on. Also pin, before moving them, what `widget-surface.spec.ts` does not reach: the CORS middleware (preflight methods and origins), the visitors' gateway (namespace, origin check) and the binding of each handler to its `ZodValidationPipe` schema (the schemas themselves are pinned in `widget.schemas.spec.ts`). Before the controllers leave `apps/api`, add `libs/channels` to `ROOTS` in `route-protection.spec.ts`, or the scan that proves every route has a protection stops seeing them.
- [x] A7 — the Meta block of `env-schema.ts` moves to the Meta plugin (session 3c, PR `refactor/meta-env-schema-in-plugin`). `libs/channels/meta/src/meta-env.ts` holds `metaEnvShape`, its checks (Graph host only in test, login redirect shape, secrets of a deployed environment, the Instagram secret) and `parseMetaEnv`; the core keeps only the rules every block shares (`libs/core/domain/env-rules.ts`, alias `@platform/core/env-rules` and out of the domain barrel so no zod constant travels to the web bundle: `nodeEnvSchema`, `isDeployedEnvironment`, `emptyAsUnset`, `urlOrNull`, `LOCAL_HOSTNAMES`, `pickEnv`) and `envSource(keys)` in `env.ts` (the only reader of `process.env`), which hands a plugin a FROZEN copy of only the keys it asks for (`META_ENV_KEYS`), never the secrets of the core. **Composition point (decided with evidence):** the plugin parses its own block from `envSource()` when its module builds (`MetaPlatformModule`), not in `parseEnv`: `libs/core` cannot import a plugin (Nx tags), and the only readers of `META_*` were `MetaPlatformModule` and `ChannelConnectionsLibModule` (both lazy), so no app needed to compose a schema. `.env.example` is unchanged and `pnpm validate:env` checks it against core + `metaEnvShape`. Behaviour: a deployed environment without `META_APP_SECRET` / `META_WEBHOOK_VERIFY_TOKEN` still cannot boot the api or the worker (the error now comes from the Meta module's factory, the text is the same); a tool that loads the core env alone (migrations, seed) no longer judges `META_*`. **Debts:** (a) `ChannelConnectionsLibModule` reads `META_OAUTH_REDIRECT_URI` through `parseMetaEnv` for the generic `ChannelOAuthConfig.redirectUri`: the address of the login belongs in the plugin's `oauth` descriptor (owner: the PR of A4b/oauth that moves `MetaOAuth`); (a2) the Meta block is parsed twice per process (the module of the plugin and the login config of `channels-connections`): cheap and idempotent, goes away with (a); `NODE_ENV` is judged in both by the same `nodeEnvSchema`, so they cannot drift; (b) `MetaOAuth.ASSET_ID_PATTERN` is still imported from `libs/core/domain` until A4b moves it; (c) `.github/workflows/ci.yml` and `apps/web-e2e/playwright.shared.ts` keep setting `META_*` (legitimate while Meta is an official plugin).
- [x] A8a — the `ChannelType` alias and the unused `officialChannelSchema` are gone; the builders, fixtures and specs that used the alias name `ChannelIds` (allowed there: tests and development fixtures). `isOfficialChannelId` has no consumer in the UI: it is only the policy of the registry (`PluginRegistry`, `RegisteredChannels`, the catalog sync), which is its legitimate place.
- [x] A8b — decided NOT to move the Meta contract suite under `libs/channels/meta` (owner's coordinator, 2026-11-10). The Instagram and Messenger contract specs and `meta-subscription-union.spec.ts` import both sibling plugins, which `scope:channels-shared` (Meta) may not depend on, and the shared harness `channel-plugin.contract.ts` uses `PluginRegistry` (`scope:plugins`), which no `scope:channels` lib may import. Moving it would need a new testing library, new `depConstraints` and a split of the CI job "Plugin contract suites" (risk for the names of the checks of the ruleset), with no gain in agnosticism: `tests/` is outside the ratchet and is the platform's place for contracts that span plugins. Revisit only if Step 5.6 (remote plugins) brings its own contract runner.

## Explicitly deferred (Phase 2 / MATURE — not in this plan)

- v1.2 MVP deferrals: Milestone 6, Steps 5.5, 7.5, 7.6, 8.1, 8.2, 8.4, 8.5 and the "MVP trim" items (see `INDEX.md` → "MVP scope")
- Versioned plan editor (`TDD_15 §5`)
- Third-party plugin marketplace UI, moderation queue, revenue-share ledger (`TDD_10 §8`, `TDD_15 §7`)
- Cross-tenant BI / data warehouse (`TDD_15 §10`, `TDD_11 §7.5`)
- A plugin identifier with an owner, in the style of Crisp's `urn:<developer>:<name>:<instance>` (owner, 2026-10-29): with a marketplace two developers can register the same name, and the platform needs to say who published a plugin and to tell instances of one plugin apart. The MVP keeps the current id (`CHANNEL_ID_PATTERN`, official ids reserved by the registry). To keep the change cheap, no code reads meaning from an id (`Conversation.channelType` and `ChannelConnection.pluginId` stay opaque strings): Phase 2 adds a publisher column to `Plugin` and makes the key publisher plus name, without rewriting existing ids; the official plugins stay unprefixed
- CSAT (the rating prompt in the closing message and the parsing of the reply; `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §3 and §10 item 39): until then `PATCH /settings/inbox` accepts only `requestCsat: false` (QA backlog, Q3, S.10); Phase 2 widens the schema to a boolean when the closing message carries the prompt
- Elasticsearch (`TDD_08 §5.7`), Kubernetes (`TDD_03 §2`), React Native (`TDD_09 §16.6`)
- 2FA for platform users, OpenAI adapter, prompt canary rollouts

## Dependency graph

```
M0 Foundation
 └─> M1 Auth and tenancy
      ├─> M2 Core messaging
      │    └─> M3 Inbox interface
      │         └─> M4 Commerce
      │              └─> M5 Operations
      │                   ├─> M6 Monetization (DEFERRED, Phase 2)
      │                   └─> M7 Production readiness
      │                        └─> M8 Enhancements (only 8.3 in MVP)
      └─> (inside M1, Step 1.0 Queues and email runs first: the magic link of 1.1 needs it)
```

**Critical path:** 0.3 constants → 0.4 schema → 1.2 tenancy → 2.6 inbound
pipeline → 3.2 inbox. Everything else can be parallelized around it.

---

## Rules for the executor

1. **Never skip Step 0.3.** Constants are a blocking dependency.
2. **Every new tenant-scoped resource** gets added to the tenant isolation
   suite in the same PR that introduces it (`check-isolation-coverage` enforces it).
3. **Every migration ships with a rollback script** (`check-rollbacks` enforces it).
4. **Every endpoint** has `AuthGuard`, `TenantGuard` and a permission decorator unless it is
   explicitly public (`ApiRoutes.webhooks.*`, `ApiRoutes.widget.*`, invitation preview and
   accept, auth magic-link endpoints, DSR verify, health). `AccessLevelGuard` joins this list in Phase 2.
5. **`storeId` comes from `X-Store-Id` through `TenantGuard`** — never from a body or query.
6. **No user-facing string is hardcoded.** i18n keys only — including persisted notifications.
7. **The pre-push hook (`pnpm validate:affected`) must pass** before any push, `pnpm validate:ci` before any PR, and `pnpm validate:all` is the full local run; the hooks never replace CI.
8. **When the TDD is ambiguous, stop and ask.** Do not invent a design.
9. **Anything marked MATURE or Phase 2 is out of scope** unless this plan
   explicitly includes it.
10. **A step is done only when its PR is green on GitHub** with every CI job that exists at
    that point. When a step creates a CI job, it also says which check to add to the `main`
    ruleset (see "CI rollout"); add it after the job has run once.

---

## Progress tracking

One row per step. The checkboxes inside each step track its tasks; this table tracks the step itself, with the
pull request that delivered it. **The step's own PR updates its row**, so the progress lives in the repository and
moves with the code.

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `todo` | not started |
| `in progress` | a branch for the step exists and is not merged |
| `done` | merged to `main` with every task checked and the "Done when" criterion verified |
| `deferred (Phase 2)` | out of the v1.2 MVP, kept for later |

How to keep it:
1. When the work on a step starts, the first commit of its branch sets the row to `in progress`.
2. When the step is complete, the last commit of its PR sets `done`, checks the step's task boxes, and fills in the PR
   number and the date (open the PR first to know its number, then add it as the last commit).
3. One step, one branch, one independent PR from an up-to-date `main`; never stack PRs.
   Never open a PR that only changes documentation: the row, the boxes and any doc fix travel inside the step's own PR,
   or inside the next PR that carries a real change.
4. Step 2.1 does not exist: it moved to Step 1.0. Step 8.3 is optional and is not needed to complete the MVP.

The MVP is complete when every row that is not `deferred` and not optional is `done`: **49 steps**.

## QA backlog

Findings of the QA of 2026-11-03 (Steps 0.1 to 3.6, two runs, 326 cases; no P0). The report is not in the repository. Every finding is closed by a QA fix PR (Q1 to Q8) or sits here with the step that closes it (rule 12). A line leaves this list when its PR or step merges.

| QA fix PR | Scope | Status | PR | Date |
|---|---|---|---|---|

| Q3 | Assignment to a read-only user (B-07), business hours (B-06), the away message (FA-5), the default role of an invitation (FUNC-4), `requestCsat` (S.10) | done | #166 | 2026-11-04 |
| Q4 | Channels pagination (FUNC-1, SEC-4), saving offline (FUNC-18), 413 for big bodies (SEC-1), Resend webhook with a bad body (SEC-6), the `analytics` and `webhooks` queues (WORKER-03), the default rate limit (FA-6) | done | #167 | 2026-11-04 |

Findings that no QA fix PR takes, with the step that closes each:

- [ ] **SEC-2 (conditional P2):** accepting an invitation spends the quota and blocks the IP for an hour (3 per hour) on any attempt. Rises to P2 if the proxy is badly configured. Step 7.3 (deployment: trusted proxy, then decide the limit) (task in Step 7.3)
- [ ] **`/metrics` is public** (declared in the report as known). Step 7.3 (task already in Step 7.3: "`/metrics` and the probes share the port of the service")
- [ ] **Sweeps can starve behind rows that always fail (review of Q1):** `SnoozeWakeScheduler` and `AutoArchiveScheduler` take the oldest due rows first and only log a failed one, so 200 or more rows that fail for good would hide the healthy ones behind them. Nothing makes a row fail for good today (a refused change is a person who got there first, and the next query no longer finds it); Step 7.4 adds an alert on the warn line of these two jobs and, if it ever fires, a retry counter or a skip list (task in Step 7.4)
- [ ] **The `security-tests` job runs out of heap as specs are added (found in Q2, PR #165):** `pnpm test:security` ran every spec of `tests/` in one Jest process (`--runInBand`, heap 4096 MB) and the heap grows with each spec file (every spec builds its own Nest app); at 48 passing files the 50th, the new notifications spec, crashed it twice in CI with `JavaScript heap out of memory` (no assertion failed; `main` passed with 49 files). Q2 splits the run into two shards (`--shard=1/2` and `2/2`, each its own process; since PR 375 each in its own CI job, and `security-tests` fails if either does): that is margin, not a cure, because the heap per spec file still grows. Step 7.3 (CI of the deployment pass) finds why a spec keeps its app in memory after `afterAll` (a leak in a shared helper or a service that is never closed) and, if it cannot be fixed, adds a shard when a shard passes 40 files (task in Step 7.3)
- [ ] **`order.payment_failed` has no step that raises it (found in Q7):** the v1.2 orders carry no online payment (billing and card payments are Phase 2), so nothing detects a payment that failed. Its switch stays hidden (`NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter`); the step that first handles an order payment raises it and removes it from the list. No step of the plan charges an order: Steps 6.2 (Stripe) and 6.4 (Dunning) bill the platform's own subscriptions, not a store's orders, and Steps 4.3 and 4.9 do not. Step 4.3 decides it when it closes: the owner confirms that orders are cash on delivery only in v1.2 (then this line moves to Phase 2 with no step) or adds a step for card payments of orders next to 6.2 (task in Step 4.3)
- [ ] **`plugin.error` has no emitter (found in Q2):** no plugin runs code inside the platform in v1.2 (the official channels are the only ones, and when one fails the platform already says so with `channel.disconnected` and `message.failed`); an external plugin only calls the plugin api and reports nothing back. The type, its text and its default stay in the matrix for the plugin runtime of Phase 2; QA fix PR Q7 decided: the preferences page hides the switch of a type nothing raises (`NotificationTypesWithoutEmitter`), `plugin.error` among them, until the plugin runtime of Phase 2 (Step 5.6 for the integration plugins that run) raises it and removes it from that list; the type, its text and its default stay (task already in Step 5.6: "Show the notification switch of this step")
- [ ] **Business hours start in the browser's time zone for a role without `CONVERSATIONS_VIEW` (Q3, B-06):** the empty state of Settings → Business hours starts the schedule in the time zone of the store, read from `GET /settings/inbox/behavior` (`CONVERSATIONS_VIEW`); a custom role that edits settings but cannot see conversations has no way to read that zone and starts in the browser's. Nothing is saved until the person saves, and the zone is a select they can change. Step 7.1 (give `GET /settings/business-hours` the zone of the store when the hours are empty) (task in Step 7.1)
- [ ] **The away reply reads the closed stretch every 5 minutes (Q3, FA-5):** `lastOpenBefore` samples the schedule at `AwayReply.STEP_MS`, so a range shorter than that (only possible through the api) can go unseen and the customer is not answered again after it; a stretch closed for more than `AwayReply.LOOKBACK_MS` (8 days) is answered again. The reply is the saved reply as stored (no variables are filled in), it is not translated, and a store whose channel window is closed at that moment sends nothing. Accepted for v1.2; Step 7.7 reviews the wording of the saved reply with the owner (task in Step 7.7)
- [ ] **Away reply, review limits (Q3):** (a) the worker module repeats the `MESSAGING_WINDOW_POLICIES` factory of the api (`apps/worker/src/inbound/inbound.module.ts`, as `worker.module.ts` already does); one shared provider in `@platform/core/messaging` is the fix. (b) A message written just before opening and processed just after is not answered (the store must be closed when it was written and when it is processed, so that a late webhook retry never says "closed" at noon). (c) The "already answered" lookup orders by `createdAt`, and the echo of the owner's own message carries the provider's date, so a skewed provider clock can hide the real latest answer and send the reply twice in one stretch. Step 7.3 (hardening of the inbound pipeline) (tasks in Step 7.3, one for each of (a), (b) and (c))
- [ ] **`requestCsat` accepts only `false` (Q3, S.10):** `PATCH /settings/inbox` refuses `true` because nothing in the MVP sends the prompt or reads the rating (`MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` §3). Phase 2 (CSAT) widens the schema to a boolean when the closing message carries the prompt (no step: listed under "Explicitly deferred")
- [ ] **Left by QA fix PR Q4 (rule 12):** (a) `GET /channels/directory` (what the inbox names its channels from) still answers every connection of the store in one list; each row is small (id, plugin, name, status), so 941 rows are tens of KB, but it is the same unbounded shape: Step 7.3 pages it together with the lists above, or the inbox reads the names of the channels it shows. (b) The `webhooks` queue has no producer and no consumer until Step 4.8 (the carrier events; the outbound webhooks follow in Steps 5.4 and 5.6); `apps/worker/src/processors/queue-coverage.spec.ts` names it as the one queue allowed to have no processor, and fails when a new queue is added without one, so Step 4.8 removes that line when it adds the processor. (c) The `analytics` processor writes only `track-event` (the one job that is queued); the search event (Step 5.1, "Search analytics") and the hourly and daily rollups (Step 5.2, "Rollup workers"; `MVP_TRIM_CHECKLIST.md` does not defer them to Phase 2) are not built yet, and the processor refuses a job it does not know. It does not deduplicate: the producer uses the Svix event id as job id, so a webhook delivered twice queues one job, and the queue allows one attempt, so only a job redelivered after a stalled worker could write twice; Step 5.2, which builds the rollups, adds a unique key on the event. (d) Every mutation of the web app now runs with `networkMode: 'always'` (`apps/web/lib/query-client.ts`): a save with no network fails at once with `NETWORK_UNAVAILABLE` and the form keeps what was typed; before, the request waited for the network and ran later behind a closed dialog. Sending a message with no network is the same: it fails and the person sends it again. Reads keep the default (a query started with no network waits for it and the page shows its loading state; Step 7.7 decides if they should fail at once too). Offline queueing (send when the network returns) is not in the MVP; Step 7.7 decides with the owner if the inbox wants it. (f) The connections list orders by `(createdAt, id)` and filters by `(storeId, status)`, the only index; Postgres sorts the rows of the store for each page, which is cheap at the hundreds of rows the QA store had (941) and grows with the store. Step 7.3 adds the index `(store_id, created_at, id)` (migration with rollback) when it reviews the query plans with real data. (e) A JSON body over 100 KB is refused with 413 by the default limit of Express; it was not raised, because no route of the MVP sends more (the largest is a webhook of a few KB). (Tasks: (a) and (f) in Step 7.3, (b) in Step 4.8, (c) in Steps 5.1 and 5.2, (d) in Step 7.7; (e) is an accepted limit with no task.)
- [ ] **Left by QA fix PR Q5 (rule 12):** (a) compact density (the `compact:` variant on `<html data-density>`, set by `DensitySync`) tightens the page padding, the thread and the composer; the rows of the conversation list keep their fixed height because the virtualizer measures with `CONVERSATION_ROW_HEIGHT_PX`, so a compact list needs that height to follow the density: the visual pass of Step 7.7. (b) The composer keeps text and kind (reply or note) per person, store and conversation in `sessionStorage` and drops it on send and on sign out; a file waiting to be sent is not kept (it lives in the bucket and the scan, not in the browser), and a draft of a closed tab is gone: acceptable, a longer life would keep customer text longer. (c) Escape in an empty reply box leaves the thread and goes back to the list; it does not resolve (`conversation-shortcuts.tsx` pushes the inbox path), and since Q7a (#170) the help sheet names it "Cerrar el panel" (`shortcuts.conversation.CLOSE`), the owner's decision #32. Closed. (d) The old `platform_sidebar_widgets` key (one layout for the whole browser) is no longer read and stays in browsers that had it: it holds only widget names, so it is not cleaned. (f) `DensitySync` sets the density after hydration, so a person with compact density sees one frame of comfortable spacing: the server cannot read the saved density until it is in a cookie like the language (visual pass, Step 7.7). (g) One Back closes every open dialog at once (a dialog inside a sheet too), and also leaves the page: dialogs do not push a history entry. (e) The browser's Back closes any open `Dialog`, `AlertDialog` or `Sheet` (`useCloseOnBack`); a dialog that must survive a route change would need its state in the route (none today). (Tasks: (a) and (f) in Step 7.7; (b), (d), (e) and (g) are accepted limits and (c) is closed, none needs a task.)
- [ ] **Left by QA fix PR Q6 (rule 12):** (a) the bytes of an upload are checked against the declared type by their first 8 KB (`matchesDeclaredType`: signatures of the allowed types, and no NUL byte for `text/plain`); the check does not decode the file, so a file with the right signature and a broken body still reaches the scan and the decoder (the worker already refuses what `sharp` cannot decode). A refused upload leaves its staging object in the bucket until the orphan cleanup removes it (24 hours). (b) `httpUrl` refuses addresses, `localhost`, names with no dot and the `.local`, `.internal`, `.intranet` and `.lan` suffixes, but a public name can still resolve to a private address: the server that fetches a link resolves it itself and refuses private answers (`resolvePublicHttpsUrl`, Step 3.4 PR B1; checked again in this PR with the forms `2130706433`, `0x7f.1`, `[::ffff:7f00:1]` and a redirect, which is not followed). A web link that is only shown (an ad's, a customer's) still opens in the browser of the agent, who sees its host before clicking. (c) The redactor of provider errors (`providerErrorText`) keeps the words around a credential, so a message such as `Invalid signature: mismatch` loses `mismatch` (a name followed by a separator is treated as a value), and a hex string of 40 or more characters is replaced even when it is only a hash. Both lose a little detail on purpose. (d) `conversations:create` and `conversations:delete` stay in the catalog and in the roles of every store, listed in `ReservedPermissions` with the step that reads each (5.6 and 5.4): taking them out needs a migration that deletes rows of `permissions` of custom roles that cannot be put back, and `TDD_02 §4` and `TDD_10` name them. `permission-usage.spec.ts` fails for a permission of a built resource that nobody reads and nobody reserved. (Task for (d) already in Step 5.4, `DELETE /conversations/:id`, and Step 5.6, `POST /plugin-api/conversations`; (a), (b) and (c) are accepted limits with no task.)
- [ ] **Left by QA fix PR Q7a (rule 12):** (a) the customers list has no identity per row, so a visitor with no name shows as "Visitante" there and as "Visitante ····ab98" in the inbox and in the profile; the list needs `displayIdentity` per item to match. Step 7.7; (b) the avatar refusal for too many pictures cannot tell "five held" from "twenty signed urls in the last hour" (both are `RATE_LIMIT_EXCEEDED`), so its text says "several pictures in progress" for both; give the hourly limit its own code with the upload quota work of Step 7.1; (c) the text for an attachment the channel does not accept names audio and video, the only kinds the channels refuse today (`CHANNEL_CAPABILITY_UNSUPPORTED` in `caption-rules.ts`); when a channel refuses another kind, the code needs the kind. Step 4.9; (d) a field the validation refusal names is translated only when it is in `validation.fields` (12 fields of today's forms), any other shows the generic text: a form that adds a field adds its entry (the spec of the messages does not catch a missing one). Step 7.7 sweeps the forms for a field with no entry; a test that fails when a form field has no `validation.fields` entry is code and goes in its own PR, not in a docs PR; (e) only the official channel plugins are named from the message files (`settings.routing.channelNames`), any other plugin keeps the name its author gave it. Step 5.6 (private registration is what creates a plugin that is not official): the manifest it validates carries the name per language, or the interface says plainly that the name is the author's. (Tasks: (a) and (d) in Step 7.7, (b) in Step 7.1, (c) in Step 4.9, (e) in Step 5.6.)
- [ ] **Left by QA fix PR Q7b (rule 12):** (a) other refusals that are rules still travel as `RESOURCE_CONFLICT` (connecting an account that another store holds no longer does: `CHANNEL_ACCOUNT_IN_USE`, Q13), which the interface reads as "someone changed it at the same time": a customer with conversations or orders that is deleted (`customers.service.ts`), a widget or a connection that is disconnected, a limit of settings (`settings.support.ts`), the messages of `messages.service.ts` and `message-changes.service.ts`. Each needs its own code and text (the web already handles the role, tag and snippet ones by hand). Step 7.7; (b) the digest names who and whose only for the mention; the other types keep their plain text, and a block of mentions is one per author and conversation (two authors in one conversation are two blocks). Step 7.7; (c) a mention saved before Q7b carries no names and keeps the plain text in the center and in the email; nothing rewrites those rows (they are read and cleaned within days). No step; (d) a customer with no name is told apart by the end of the handle ("····ab98") in the text of a mention, in no language: the word "Visitante" would be wrong for a customer of another channel. Step 7.7; (e) `EMAIL_FROM` now defaults to "Omnichannel Platform <noreply@platform.local>": a deployed environment sets its own sender with its own name (Step 7.3, deployment checklist). (Tasks: (a), (b) and (d) in Step 7.7, (e) in Step 7.3; (c) has no step on purpose: those rows are read and cleaned within days.)
- [ ] **Left by QA fix PR Q8 (rule 12):** (a) the suites now write to `platform_test`, but Redis (queues, cache, rate-limit counters), the bucket and Mailpit are still the ones of development: the suites prefix what they create and delete their own cache keys. Step 7.7 gives the test run its own Redis database number (`REDIS_URL` of the three `test-env.ts` files and the `webServer` of the e2e) together with the "one store per run" item; (b) `pnpm infra:reset` (promised by `TDD_14 §2`) is not built: it deletes the Docker volumes, so it needs `--yes`, a refusal outside a local server and the owner's say-so on what to keep (Mi Tienda, Tienda Escalon). Step 7.3, or a follow-up PR if the owner asks; (c) `pnpm dev` restarts the api and the worker on every file that the build rewrites, so a save that changes many chunks can restart them twice; there is no `--inspect` port (Step 7.7 if the owner wants one); a hard kill (`SIGKILL`) of the wrapper leaves the `node --watch` child listening on 8080 or 8081 (the wrapper forwards `SIGINT`, `SIGTERM` and `SIGHUP`), and the next start then fails to bind: stop the leftover by hand; a launcher that makes the child die with its parent is for Step 7.7; (d) the e2e no longer reuses a server that is already listening (set `E2E_REUSE_SERVERS=1` to do it), so running `pnpm test:e2e` needs ports 8080, 8081 and 3000 free: stop `pnpm dev` first; (e) the web only checks that `NEXT_PUBLIC_VAPID_PUBLIC_KEY` is a string: a public key of the wrong shape fails in the browser, not at build (the api and the worker do validate the pair); Step 7.3 validates it in the web env schema when the lighthouse budget of the inbox (340 KB, 339 KB today) has room for the helper; (g) the cache of `workspace:test` hashes `scripts/lib`, the jest config and every non-spec `.ts` under `libs/core` (two helpers import their constants), so a change in the core invalidates it even when no script spec reads that file: correct, and still a hit while one works in the apps; (h) a database named `platform_dev` is refused by the suites (`DEVELOPMENT_DATABASE_NAME`), but a differently named copy of it is not: `TEST_DATABASE_URL` is trusted when it names another database. Step 7.7, with the item of one store and one Redis per run, makes the suites refuse a database that holds data that is not theirs (for example one that has stores without the run prefix). (f) the QA found `/sw.js` answering 404 and took it for a bug: the worker is served at `/serwist/sw.js` (the CI proves it with the image) and `/sw.js` never existed. What was missing is the key pair, which is the owner's to make (`TDD_14 §15`). (Tasks: (a), (c) and (h) in Step 7.7, (b) and (e) in Step 7.3; (d) and (g) are documented behavior with no task, and (f) is closed.)
- [ ] **Pendiente del dueño (Q8), data of the QA that stays until the owner runs it in a terminal of his own (rule: no session deletes or updates the development database).** The repository's `platform_dev` still holds the rows below. Each block is read-only first; run the `SELECT`, compare with the number written, then the change inside `BEGIN`, and `COMMIT` only if the counts match. Connect with `psql "$DIRECT_DATABASE_URL"` (owner role) from `.env.local`. Source of the commands: the QA reports of 2026-11-03 (`a828b767-sql-writes-ledger.md`, `scratchp-ENV_CHANGES.md`).
  1. **The 10 000 volume conversations** of store QA-PASADA-2 (`4f09d705-0ad3-4034-b074-85b86e087d70`; `messages.content LIKE 'QA2A volumen %'`, no other message in those conversations; conversation `49feaeca-21dd-4cfc-9940-78e64fa2d02d` must stay):
     ```sql
     BEGIN;
     CREATE TEMP TABLE _vol AS
       SELECT DISTINCT conversation_id AS id FROM messages
       WHERE store_id = '4f09d705-0ad3-4034-b074-85b86e087d70' AND content LIKE 'QA2A volumen %';
     SELECT count(*) FROM _vol;                                      -- expect 10000
     DELETE FROM messages WHERE conversation_id IN (SELECT id FROM _vol);          -- expect 10000
     DELETE FROM conversations WHERE id IN (SELECT id FROM _vol)
       AND store_id = '4f09d705-0ad3-4034-b074-85b86e087d70';                      -- expect 10000
     COMMIT;  -- or ROLLBACK if a number differs
     ```
  2. **The two unauthorized updates of the first functional tester** (dates of two resolved conversations of the same store, and a message forced to failed and re-sent): `UPDATE conversations SET last_message_at = '2026-11-03 13:15:18.266' WHERE id = '259c1b28-8f3c-45e1-9e11-ea515e8852c7' AND store_id = '4f09d705-0ad3-4034-b074-85b86e087d70';` and the same for `a51b384c-e576-4e7c-bbb3-a66ddf7edb6c` with `'2026-11-03 13:15:33.881'` (the originals were not saved to the millisecond); optional: `UPDATE messages SET resent_at = NULL WHERE id = '022fdaa2-2098-415f-8668-1e70a17376a5';`.
  3. **The five updates that the owner authorized to the second tester (U1 to U5, one row each; ids are the first 8 characters the report kept, so look each row up by `id::text LIKE '<prefix>%'` and `store_id`, and expect exactly one row before changing it):** U1 `conversations ef9d64ce`: `last_message_at` and `resolved_at` were moved 3 days back, put them forward by `interval '3 days'`. U2 `conversations 897c18da`: the same. U3 `exports d2d085e6`: `expires_at` is now `2026-11-03 15:53:48.693`, the original was `2026-11-10 12:39:19.66`. U4 `messages 86f767c4`: `deleted_at`, `deleted_by_type`, `deleted_by_id` were NULL and the purge job has already erased the content: it cannot be restored, leave it. U5 `messages 1d2f636e`: `delivery_status` was `sent` (now resent), `channel_message_id` was `1d2f636e...` (full value not saved), `created_at` was `2026-11-03 12:59:17.78`; only the date can be restored. Reverting these has no value beyond tidiness: the rows are test data of a test store, and deleting the store's rows (item 5) removes them.
  4. **The expired invitation** `qa2inva@example.com` (`invitations.id = '32b20ac7-0473-46a5-b990-1927723abbad'`, expired on purpose by an update the owner ran; the old `expires_at` was not saved). It does no harm; if it should go: `DELETE FROM invitations WHERE id = '32b20ac7-0473-46a5-b990-1927723abbad' AND email = 'qa2inva@example.com';`.
  5. **Eight QA stores:** QA-PASADA-2, QA-PASADA-2B, QA-B-propia, QA-C-propia, QA-FUNC, QA-SEC B, `<b>x</b>` and QA-TEXTOS. The product will not delete a store that holds a conversation, and there is no script that deletes the rows a store holds. The owner chooses between (i) leaving them, which costs nothing now that the suites no longer write to this database, (ii) `pnpm infra:reset` once Q8b builds it, which also drops Mi Tienda and Tienda Escalon unless he exports them first, and (iii) asking for a cascade script (a new PR that deletes by `store_id` in foreign key order, with a dry run). Nothing here decides for him.
  6. **About 60 roles `QA2P-*` and `QA2R-*`:** `SELECT id, store_id, name FROM roles WHERE name LIKE 'QA2P-%' OR name LIKE 'QA2R-%';` and, if the count is the expected one, `DELETE FROM roles WHERE (name LIKE 'QA2P-%' OR name LIKE 'QA2R-%') AND is_system = false;` (a role still given to a member makes the delete fail on its key: unassign it first). Test rules, widgets, tags and snippets of the QA stores go with item 5.
  7. **About 200 emails in Mailpit:** open `http://localhost:8025`, select all and delete, or send `DELETE /api/v1/messages` to Mailpit (it only empties the mail catcher).
  8. **The orphan upload of the second tester** (`uploadId 4f51899b-0821-4ccb-95f5-92dc6cc8cd2c`, uploaded at 13:14Z on 2026-11-03, TTL 24 hours): from 13:50Z on 2026-11-04 the orphan cleanup (`lock:clean_orphan_media`, minute :50) may have removed it. Reading the bucket for that key tells if W.06 holds; nothing needs doing if it is gone. A note, not an action.
- [ ] **Currency `ZZZ` is accepted and the list holds obsolete currencies.** Step 7.1 (input validation pass) (task in Step 7.1)
- [ ] **`media.processed` arrives twice; the dead-letter queue holds 37 jobs with no consumer.** Step 7.4 (monitoring of queues) (task in Step 7.4)
- [ ] **Preferences stored and never read:** `crossStoreEnabled` and `crossStoreSupervisorOnly`; the api accepts turning off the in-app channel. Step 7.3 with the review debts of Step 3.5 (task in Step 7.3) **NOT-14 (QA, 2026-11-07), decided by the coordinator while the owner is away: option A, the literal TDD (`TDD_06 §6.5`: "Persist - always, so it appears in the notification center"); the in-app channel switches off the live event and the sound, and a row is still saved and counted as unread.** No service change; the preferences page must say so (task for the web). **Option B is the OWNER'S DECISION:** switching in-app off hides the row from the center and the unread count. Design: `Notification.center_hidden_at` (migration with its rollback), set at creation when `prefs.inApp` is false (the row must still exist for the email digest, which reads `email_pending_at`; with no email wanted, no row at all), excluded by the list, the unread count, mark-all-read and the live event; plus the isolation case and a test of the digest with in-app off. It contradicts that explicit sentence of the TDD, so the TDD changes with it.
